# Changelog

All notable changes to this repository are recorded here, newest first, in the
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) form. Every pull request adds its line as a
fragment `changelog.d/<branch>.md`; the lead folds the fragments under **Unreleased** with `bin/changelog-fold`
(AGENTS.md, code review rule 10, since 2026-10-20, fragments since 2026-10-31); a tag moves that section
under a dated heading. `ade update` (proposal 4.4 item 9) prints the entries between the commit a
machine last applied and the head it is moving to, so this file is what tells an operator or an
agent what changed and what is new. Entries end with the pull request number.

## Unreleased

### Added

- `bin/ade-job-run --lock-dir DIR LABEL -- COMMAND...`: every ADE launchd job in `config/launchd/com.djbclark.*.plist` now starts through
  it. Each run holds a shared `flock` on a per-label admission lock (`DIR/LABEL.lock`, DIR an absolute argv value) for the whole
  run, and skips (exit 0, one log line) while `ade-sched reconcile` holds the lock exclusively. `reconcile` can now reload
  or remove a LOADED job whose installed plist provably routes through the wrapper, is ledger-owned with a matching hash
  and was loaded from that plist with that lock directory in its LOADED argv and a wrapper that is this checkout's own `ade-job-run` (path, bytes, `--admission-protocol v1`): it takes the lock exclusive without waiting (a run in flight defers the step, nothing is
  killed), keeps it across `bootout`, write, `bootstrap` and read-back, restores the prior bytes if the new ones fail, and
  leaves a durable recovery record that the next `reconcile` acts on after a kill. Jobs not routed through the wrapper,
  including every existing install, keep the exit-6 deferral until migrated once (docs/scheduling.md). Every other ADE plist writer (`ade-sched install`/`uninstall`, native install, recovery) takes the same lock without waiting and refuses while a run holds it, and every locked reconcile/recovery replace, unlink and restore re-checks the plist's inode and bytes right before the syscall. The lock directory is one canonical path from the password database (`<real home>/.local/state/djbclark-ade/admission`), never `$HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`ADE_JOBS_STATE_DIR`, so no two ADE writers can lock one label in two places; low-level `ade-sched install`/`uninstall` of a LOADED job first prove from `launchctl print` that it runs the canonical wrapper (realpath bound to the selected ADE root or the checkout beside the scheduler, bytes, protocol v1) and refuse otherwise, and `ade-sched migrate-wrapper PLIST` is the explicit, idle-only migration of a pre-wrapper job (its new render must itself be wrapped); a loaded canonical job reinstalled by `ade-sched install` takes the same record-first reload path as reconcile; `__ADMISSION__` cannot be overridden. (#622)
- `claude/hooks/adversary_marker_gate.py` (2026-11-14, am-01) is a Claude Code `PreToolUse` Bash hook, registered in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl`, that denies a `gh` write (`gh pr|issue create|new|edit|comment|review|close|reopen`, behind shell keywords, wrappers, `$( ... )`, `bash -c`/`-lc`/`eval` or a heredoc fed to a shell, or `gh api` with a body flag at any endpoint) that sends a live `adversary-verdict:v1` APPROVE marker: in its words, its body files, its stdin and the commands piped into it, its heredoc bodies, the files a filter reads (`$(grep M report.md)`, `grep M report.md | gh ... -F -`: the filter lifts the marker out of its fence), variables and `$( )` the same command sets or holds (`BODY="$(cat raw.md)"; gh ... "$BODY"`), and JSON-decoded `--input` payloads (`\u003c`). Commands that only mention a gh write or a marker (`rg "gh pr edit"`, a commit message, prose) pass. It reads "live" with `bin/adversary_marker.py`'s own parser, so a BLOCK, a fenced or inline-code example, the docs template and a placeholder head pass; it fails open on bad stdin, other tools, unreadable bodies, a missing checker and its own errors. It is inert until `~/.claude/hooks/adversary_marker_gate.py` is symlinked into this checkout and `~/.claude/settings.json` is re-rendered. Contract, failure modes and the `ADVERSARY_MARKER_GATE_OFF=1` operator escape hatch are in `docs/code-review.md` rule 14 (#724).
- `bin/agent-id <AGENT_ID> [--json]`: prints the agent id, workspace name, and the window/tab and tab/pane placement fields for the harness the agent runs in, so any agent mentioning another to a human can name it fully (docs/agent-identity.md, operator rule 2026-11-20). Fields that do not apply or resolve print `?`. The workspace is read from `workspaceId` in the Paseo state file, the Paseo tab from `paseo agent inspect`'s `Name`; the id is validated (UUID or hex prefix, never a flag), every subprocess call has a timeout, and a missing binary or odd herdr JSON gives `?`, not a traceback. `bin/agent-id` also resolves a local Claude Code session UUID (or unique prefix, dead pids skipped) from `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` to the `ListAgents`/`SendMessage` name (docs/agent-identity.md, docs/orchestration-practices.md 5a item 6). (#842)
- Added `--lead-ack` and `--check-ack` to `bin/completion-notice` supporting dual-route delivery proof with unique UUID message IDs and atomic disk acks per operator ruling 2026-11-12 (#573).
- Added fixed OpenAI/OpenRouter metadata verification commands to the existing
  key engine, with environment-only credentials, bounded read-only GETs and
  exit-code-only results; metadata authentication did not establish inference
  quota, entitlement or provider acceptance. (#552)
- Extended fixed key metadata verification to Anthropic, Gemini and DeepSeek
  authenticated model-list GETs; keys stayed in documented headers and results
  did not establish inference entitlement, quota or pool acceptance. (#554)
- Gemini 4 Argon is a routable `frontier` model, fallback only (2026-10-31, operator; needs aiuse 1.5): `config/routing.toml` lists `gemini-4-argon` in `[dispatch].frontier_models` (so it runs only with `--kind frontier`) and gains the `antigravity-argon` service (`acp-run agy --model gemini-4-argon --effort high`, rank 5 in `frontier`, `pool = ["antigravity", "gemini_argon"]`, `fallback_after = ["codex", "claude"]`). It is used when astra OR Fable is short (codex below its 60% estimate floor, or the Fable sublimit below `frontier1_min_left`, where it replaces the Opus downgrade), never while both have quota: `route_agent.py plan --kinds frontier` skips it then and `acp-dispatch` refuses it (exit 2, `route-override` escapes). It fails closed: no fresh, unfrozen reading of the Argon window means the planner never picks it and a dispatch exits 5 naming the window. aiuse 1.5 files Argon as its own pool family `gemini_argon`, so `route_agent.antigravity_family` (shared by `acp-dispatch` and mirrored in `bin/failover.py`) maps `gemini-4-argon*` to it and other `gemini-*` to `gemini`; `acp-dispatch`'s reset-time and live-headroom readers and the exhaustion note use the same family. A quota refusal names the binding window (`binding window: Gemini 4 Argon weekly, N% left`), and `--dry-run` prints it on its `would refuse` line. The frontier allowance of `--frontier-reason` stays claude and codex only, and the record says so in `frontier_judged_note`. Carbon is not reachable through agy yet (Google's staged rollout, 2026-10-28). `tests/test_argon_frontier.py`. (#224)
- `ade-window-name` Paseo plugin and `bin/ade_ws_autoname.py`: a default-named window gets a `<KIND> <repo> (<work>)` name when its first agent is
  created or next ends a turn (WORKERS for an agent with a parent or `ade.lead` label, else MAIN), but only while the window has no title of its own (Paseo's prompt pre-title counts as none); a window named
  on purpose is never renamed. Operator-installed (`paseo plugin install`), dry run with `ADE_WINDOW_NAME_DRY_RUN=1`. (#666)
- Bash habits gate denies a job-control background `&` (`cmd &`, `nohup cmd &`, `( cmd & )`): the reason points at the Bash tool's `run_in_background` parameter and says nothing ran; `&&`, redirections (`2>&1`, `&>`), quoted text, heredocs and comments pass. `docs/bash-habits-gate.md`. (#752)
- Added no-argument `ade-install` first-run guidance (exit 0, no probes or changes)
  and an early Python 3.11+ prerequisite diagnostic (exit 2); automatic Python/Just
  bootstrap remained planned work. (#544)
- `skills/bottom-config` (2026-11-01): edit, strictly validate (`scripts/validate.py`: bottom ignores unknown config keys silently), preview (`scripts/preview.py`, private tmux server) and rebuild-install (`scripts/install_btm_mt.py`, CI artifact for the exact fork branch head only) the config of bottom (`btm`) and djbclark's fork build `btm-mt` with the middle-truncated Command column (`processes.truncate_cmd_middle`). (#221)
- Added opt-in `ade-browser-task track/status/verify` for pr-access, with fixed deadline registration and bound repository API completion proof; preserved pure tracking previews and left unsupported templates unverified. (#553)
- `skills/btww` (2026-11-01): `/btww <question>` answers a side question from context in every TUI. It reaches the native `/btw`
  where one can be aliased (omp `side: true` command, Hermes CLI `quick_commands` alias) and runs the skill elsewhere;
  zcode gets `zcode/commands/btww.md`. (#236)
- `config/install.toml`: `paseo-plugin-github-board` (gpambrozio/github-board) is a chosen, offered Paseo plugin (no `paseo_plugin` key: doctor reports it `unknown`, apply `blocked`, like the other offered plugin rows; nothing runs); `docs/install-catalog.md` section G records what adopting it trusts. (#681)
- `bin/changelog-fold [--check] [--repo DIR]` (2026-10-31) folds the `changelog.d/<branch>.md` fragments into `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased` (new entries at the top of each subsection in file-name order, missing subsections created in Keep a Changelog order, an entry already anywhere in the file skipped so a rerun cannot duplicate), writes the file atomically (fsynced) and deletes the folded fragments; `--check` validates headings and the `(#N)` ending without writing; exit 0 folded, nothing to fold or valid, 1 invalid fragment (nothing written or deleted), 2 usage error, `--repo` not a directory or no `CHANGELOG.md` (`--check` included). One writer, the lead, folds through a normal PR (#190).
- `bin/ade-update` lists the entries of `changelog.d/` fragments present at its target and absent at its base (under "Unreleased (changelog.d)", once even when a fold moved them into `CHANGELOG.md` between the two revisions, and never again after the fold for a machine that already saw them as fragments; `--json` `changelog.entries`) and warns, with `changelog.stale_fragments` in `--json`, about a fragment older than 14 days on the revision it updates to; it reads fragments through the `changelog-fold` beside it, never one inside a non-canonical `--repo`, and records `<sha> fragments-v1` in its state file so an install last updated by the older, fragment-unaware `ade-update` is shown its fragments once instead of never (#190).
- `chrome-devtools` MCP server (user scope, Claude Code) for introspecting and driving the operator's running Chrome, with setup, verification and security notes in `docs/mcp-servers.md`. (#768)
- `ade-install doctor` checks stdio MCP servers (a `command` and `args` row in `config/mcp-clients.toml`, Claude Code only), starting with `chrome-devtools`; `docs/threat-model.md` gains entry 4.12 for the logged-in browser. (#768)
- `docs/mcp-servers.md` documents chrome-use (Chrome extension plus local CLI, no remote-debugging dialog) as the unattended route for tab control, with what was installed, what was verified, the side effects to re-check and the Pi panel gap; `docs/threat-model.md` 4.12 covers it. (#768)
- `skills/ci-resources/SKILL.md`: the single reference for picking and operating every VM and CI resource (Apple container, tart, Forgejo/Hyades, GitHub Actions, WarpBuild, local Linux gate), including the container apiserver start, the tart-vs-container exclusion, the Tailscale route trap, and how to confirm a run actually ran rather than trusting exit 0. (#845)
- `ade_claims.py successor-of PRED --record R` and `ade-self-session finish`: a self-handoff successor inherits its predecessor's active claims (same claim id, `history` line), verified against the archived handoff record; a caller the record does not name, an unarchived or unreadable record, or an expired claim is refused. (#757)
- Added `docs/slim/`: the slim-down plan of record (v2), the agent check-in prompt, the rulings on 42 agent check-ins and 17 open PRs, and the Paseo recovery evidence taken from PR 290; collected the batch-1 check-in reports under `docs/checkins/` (#295).
- Added `bin/claude-cost-whatif` and `tests/test_claude_cost_whatif.py` to simulate cache TTL policies, compaction thresholds, and fresh-handoff savings from local Claude Code transcripts. (#577)
- Added `bin/review-launch`, the permanent review boundary: one `claude -p` with `--tools Read,Grep,Glob` (`--mode read-only` adds WebFetch and WebSearch), `--permission-mode default`, no setting sources, no MCP, no hooks and `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`; it refuses bypass flags and variables, proves the boundary from the run's init event, ends the run with exit 77 on any tool or path outside it, and refuses every launch until its cached per-version self-check (`review-launch --check`) has shown outside reads refused (slim-down E8) (#297).
- Added `bin/review-pr PR`, rule 11's local review: Claude through `review-launch --mode review` on the PR's exact head in a clean detached worktree; it prints the `adversary-verdict:v1` marker to paste, refuses an untrusted author unless `--allow-untrusted`, and never posts (slim-down E8) (#297).
- Added the `adversary-marker` Action (`.github/workflows/adversary-marker.yml`, `bin/adversary_marker.py`): on `workflow_run` of Tests and on `gh workflow run adversary-marker.yml -f pr=N` it judges the PR body's marker against the head and posts the `adversary-marker` commit status from the `ade-merge-gate` App, counting an APPROVE only in a body last edited by the repository owner or an OWNER, MEMBER or COLLABORATOR author (the operator approves an outside PR by editing its body); the marker parser moved unchanged from the PR waiter with its tests (slim-down E8) (#297).
- Added the `fan-out` skill: one page on the lead's Agent/Workflow fan-out, `acp-dispatch` or `paseo run --no-wait` slices in their own worktrees, collecting with `acp-dispatch wait`/`check` and `paseo ls`, and the footer (slim-down E6) (#297).
- Added `bin/ade-roster`: prints README's skills and `bin/` roster from each `SKILL.md` frontmatter and each tool's first docstring or comment line; `--write` updates README's `<!-- roster:start -->` block, `--check` exits 1 when it is stale (run in CI's context-policy job) (slim-down E11) (#297).
- `bin/ade_settings_apply.py`: `--allow-env-add NAME` and `--allow-env-remove NAME` (each repeatable, empty by default) let a caller-named `env` key be added (in the template, absent from live, also when live has no `env` block) or removed (in live, no longer in the template; a template that still has it removes nothing). The `--apply` "live key would disappear" refusal exempts exactly the removed `/env/NAME` paths, so the #844 case (drop `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS`, add `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE`) applies in one run; no value is changed or printed. The `env` block is still never auto-added (mirrors `codex-env-policy-apply --allow-set-keys`); `ade update` passes neither flag. (#846)
- `bin/ade-sched` and `bin/ade_sched.py`: one scheduler for the repo's system jobs that installs a checked-in launchd plist as a launchd agent (macOS), a systemd user service and timer (Linux; refuses without linger unless `--allow-no-linger`) or a marked crontab block; `backend`, `render`, `install`, `uninstall`, `status`, exit 0/1/2/3/69; [`docs/scheduling.md`](docs/scheduling.md). (#291)
- `deadline install` and `pr-policy install` work on Linux through `bin/ade-sched`, with `--backend` and `--allow-no-linger`; `deadline install --agents auto|on|off` decides whether the sweep runs the Paseo agent scan. (#291)
- Added `bin/instruction-lint`, a deterministic lint for `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md` and `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` (directory-tree
  blocks and cross-file duplicates are errors; phase D7 sweep terms and skill-name headings warn); CI's `context-policy`
  job runs it and `context-budget-check --instruction-lint` adds its counts to the nightly run (#289)
- Added `bin/post-edit-check`, a single-file fast lint (ruff syntax selection, `bash -n`, `jq empty`, `tomllib`) that never
  runs tests, registered as a Claude PostToolUse hook in the settings template (#289)
- Added `/steps plan`, an explicit interview-before-plan mode of the `steps` skill (#289)
- Added `bin/ade-retro` and the `retro` skill, read-only material for a periodic self-review (merged PRs, changelog
  entries, handoffs since a date) (#289)
- `bin/codex-env-policy-apply --allow-set-keys KEY[,KEY...]` (operator, 2026-11-21): a deliberate template change to the explicit
  `[shell_environment_policy.set]` keys can reach the live Codex config. The default is unchanged: any add, drop or change of a set key
  is refused; the flag permits exactly the keys it names and still refuses the rest, and the machine's browser digest key can never be
  allowed. (#844)
- `bin/codexbar-cpu-watch` and its launchd job (2026-10-31): every 5 minutes one 3 s CPU sample of CodexBar's process tree into `~/.local/state/codexbar-cpu-watch/samples.jsonl` (0600 in a 0700 dir, one O_APPEND line per sample, rotated past 5 MB). No command line is stored, only pid, percent, RSS, a name and flag names; a spike lists the top 15. `report` lists the last samples; `install [--ade DIR]` refuses a linked worktree or cow pasture; `uninstall` removes the job (#202).
- Added `bin/coding-bot` and the `tell-coding-bot` skill (2026-11-01): any project's agent talks to the operator's cloud Grok Bot "Coding" like `acp-run` or `hermes send`. `ask` POSTs to the bot's webhook routine (`CODING_BOT_WEBHOOK_URL`/`CODING_BOT_WEBHOOK_KEY`, Bearer key sent only to `api2.cursor.sh`, ids `ade-N` with `re=` as in the Dann -> Eve channel) and blocks for the reply (exit 4 BLOCKED, 124 timeout); `send`, `wait`, `deliver` (the one sink for the bot's reply: stdin or `--clipboard`), `inbox`, `status`; `prompt` (and `send`/`ask` with no webhook, exit 10) put a self-contained prompt on the clipboard for the operator to paste, for web pages that need UI interaction; `bootstrap` asks the bot to create its inbound webhook routine (#233).
- Added `cow-pasture check <pasture-path>`, which lists those symlinks for an existing pasture (exit 1 if any, 2 if the path is not a pasture in `cow list` or a directory in it is unreadable); `skills/cow-workspaces` gained how to check and fix an older pasture and how to rebuild a copied venv (#228)
- `bin/ade-backup-stores` gained `--keep N` (retain the newest N successful generations) and `--clean-parts` (remove leftover `<stamp>.part` dirs that may hold unscrubbed credentials), plus a daily `config/launchd/com.djbclark.ade-backup-stores.plist` for `bin/ade-sched` (`--strict-stores --keep 3 --clean-parts`). (#402)

- `ade-install apply --print-system-argv` dumps platform `system_argv` print-only; `--yes --system --apply-system` opts into live `system_argv` (default still `refuse-system-exec` / exit 2). (#395)

- `ade-merge-ready approve-post` posts owner `adversary-verdict:v1` APPROVE markers for selected green `needs_approve` tips after `--i-reviewed --yes` (dry-run by default; refuses non-owner `gh`; optional `--trigger-marker`); `--via auto|body|comment` (default auto: body for owner-authored tips, comment for outside); `--yes` requires `--expect-head N=40hex` matching the live tip so a substituted head cannot be approved (#493).
- `ade-merge-ready merge-now --prs …` squash-merges only tips classified `merge_now` (pytest-linux pass + adversary-marker success); refuses otherwise so a raw `gh pr merge` cannot bypass the gate (#481 incident, 2026-11-10); falls back to GitHub `merge-async` when the tip is in a native stack (#493).
- `bin/ade-merge-ready` classifies open ADE PRs against pytest-linux, adversary-marker, and live owner APPROVE (read-only; prints paste-ready marker templates, never invents a verdict) (#371).
- `ade-merge-ready retrigger-marker` re-runs `adversary-marker.yml` for selected open tips (`--prs`, `--bottoms-only`, and/or `--class needs_approve`; dry-run by default, `--yes` to fire); never invents APPROVE and never merges (batch re-dispatch after owner-trust #484) (#493).
- `ade-merge-ready stack-rebase` dry-run / `--yes --repo-dir` restack for open stack children after a bottom lands (conflict refuse; no APPROVE invent; no merge) (#512).

- `bin/render-secret-deny` now writes `sandbox.filesystem.denyRead` into `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` from

  an operator order-11 step. Docs: `docs/secret-deny.md` C3/D2–D4; queue Carried #1. (#405)
- Documented the Claude `sandbox.filesystem.denyRead` operator trial (proposed entries mirroring

- `bin/render-secret-deny` now writes an inactive Codex `[permissions.ade-secret-deny]` deny-read profile into

  openai/codex#22179 tracked early deny-glob docs that did not enforce — do not claim it landed (#446).

  GraphQL `reviewThreads.isResolved`; never resolves, replies or posts; bot comment text is untrusted data (truncated
  title only). `--json`, `--skip-outdated`, `--include-open`. Docs in `docs/review-boundary.md`. (#443)

- `load-watch` mds probe ported from #231 onto Wave 6 master (2026-11-10): when root's Spotlight `mds` has used 0.5+ cores over consecutive samples spanning 2 minutes (`LW_MDS_CORES`, `LW_MDS_SUSTAIN_S`; a gap over `LW_MDS_MAX_GAP_S`, 90 s, restarts the span), one line per 30 minutes (`LW_MDS_COOLDOWN_S`) goes to the load-watch log with mds, Finder and the six busiest other processes by program name only. No root required, no pings, no signals. Master's Paseo agent-counting path is unchanged. Test: `tests/test_load_watch_mds_probe.py`. Close #231 after merge (#517).
- `config/model-tiers.toml` plus `bin/model_tiers.py` / `bin/ade-model-tiers` so frontier/judgment/everyday/bulk roles are data; `promote`/`set` update the file atomically (no secrets); `route_agent` and `ade_config.load_effective` read it (#401).
- `bin/open-clean`, a drop-in for macOS `open` that runs `/usr/bin/open` with only HOME, USER, LOGNAME, a fixed PATH, LANG/LC_* and TMPDIR, so an app an agent launches no longer inherits the agent's session variables (`CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION` had reached Ghostty and its shells through Quicksilver); `claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py` (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) denies a bare `open` and names it, and `env -i ... open` passes; dispatch-footer rule 23 and the apply-toolchain roster list it (ported from #256) (#522)
- `plugins/ade-yolo`: on `agent.create.before`, set Claude/Codex/OpenCode/omp creates to Bypass / Full Access / build (+ OpenCode `auto_accept`) unless `plan`/`read-only` or metadata `ade-yolo.skip` (operator install; restores YOLO after Always Ask drift) (#526)
- `bin/paseo_yolo.py`: shared provider → unattended mode map for launches (#526)
- Added `config/pi-pool-acceptance.toml` and `bin/ade-pi-pools` so automatic Pi routing (`ade/auto` / `bin/pi-default`) refuses pools the operator has not flipped to `accepted=true`; `check` exits 2 while any routing Pi service is unaccepted (#418).
- Push on completion for delegated slices: dispatch footer rule 22 and `skills/fan-out` Collect require a finished slice to tell its requester `done: <title>, <PR URL>` (or status/report) via `paseo send --json --no-wait` with a named ack (never relay); `bin/completion-notice` formats and optionally sends that shape (#397)
- Made `research_check.py claims` namespace-aware across sibling runs under the same parent (bare unique cross-run ids and `[slug/E####]` cites); `claims` rejects duplicate ids inside a cited sibling, and `numbers` accepts figures quoted only in cited-sibling evidence (#387).
- Documented and inventoried the optional monthly Paseo schedule `routing-recalibrate` (Day 1 09:00, `--mode auto`) for `good_at` re-rank from `route_agent.py calibrate`; retired launchd label stays as `routing-recalibrate-launchd` (#434)
- `config/install.toml` `[install.jobs]` / `[install.retired_jobs]` ADE jobs inventory (S4b) with schema and validation (#433).
- `ade-sched list` and `ade-sched diff` (and `just sched-list` / `just sched-diff`) compare the inventory to live launchd/systemd/cron/paseo schedules; doctor includes the same drift and never unloads foreign owners (#433).
- `ade-sched reconcile` (and `just sched-reconcile`) installs/reloads ADE-owned `ade-sched` jobs on systemd user units, writes the ownership ledger at `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/jobs.json` (`ADE_JOBS_STATE_DIR`), and removes only ledger-owned labels; default dry-run, `--yes` executes; never unloads foreign/unknown owners or root/`--system` units (#461).
- Added `docs/s9-cloud-paseo.md` (2026-11-10): design for setup-plan **S9** (cloud agents ↔ Paseo) — item-24

  routing, or live wiring (#463).
- Extended `ade-sched reconcile --yes` to the user crontab write path (with systemd) so ADE-owned cron blocks install/remove through the `jobs.json` ledger; launchd remains refuse-backend (#511).
- `ade-sched reconcile --yes` installs and removes ADE-owned launchd LaunchAgents with ledger `backend="launchd"` (S4b; completes host write backends alongside systemd and cron) (#514).
- `ade-sched reconcile` creates and deletes ADE-owned `kind=paseo` schedules via `paseo schedule create|delete`, with ledger `backend="paseo"` (S4b; orthogonal to host systemd/cron write backends) (#513).
- Declared agents/ template secrets in `secrets/secretspec.toml` and companion field homes under `companion/` (Phase 1). (#396)
- Owned-key `modify_` merge helpers for Claude and Codex under `agents/claude/modify_merge.py` and `agents/codex/modify_merge.py` (keep unowned live keys; refuse live home paths). (#396)

- Accepted `ade-update --auto` / `just update --auto` as an S4 CLI stub that runs observe-only (`--check` + `ade-install keys`) then exits 5 naming full auto-apply not implemented; no scheduled apply plist (PR 359 check-only gate stands) (#510).
- `bin/wave6-mac-proof-capture --linux-safe` runs Linux-safe pgrep (+ optional paseo) probes with launchctl SKIPPED and `LINUX-SAFE — NOT Mac live-cycle` labels (Mac live cycle stays blocked) (queue Carried item 1) (#470).
- `bin/wave6-delete-check --inventory` lists every remaining gated §A `relay.py` / `acp-dispatch` / `acp-run` reference untruncated and reports `gate_exits` proving callers are covered while Mac live stays blocked (`ready` false; queue Carried item 1) (#472).
- Documented the Wave 6 follow-up deletion checklist for `relay.py`, `acp-dispatch` and `acp-run` (`docs/slim/wave6-follow-up.md`), linked from PLAN, queue Carried 1 and AGENTS.md (#327).
- Documented Wave 6 shim-deletion readiness (`docs/slim/wave6-follow-up.md`) and `bin/wave6-delete-check` (refuses delete until callers, C3 and Mac live-cycle gates pass; queue Carried item 1) (#398).
- `bin/wave6-mac-proof-capture` Mac live-cycle proof harness (Darwin `--live` only; refuses invented Mac stdout) and labeled FIXTURE `mac_proofs` bundle under `tests/fixtures/wave6-mac-proofs/` for CI shape until the Mac live cycle replaces them (queue Carried item 1) (#441).
- `wave6-delete-check --dry-run` FIXTURE-only FAILCLOSED shape regression (labeled proofs; never deletion-ready / never invented live Mac stdout) (queue Carried item 1) (#441).
- `docs/dated-quota-overrides.md` (design, 2026-11-21, no live wiring) covers four things: which agy models share a
  Google quota window on Ultra 20x (one Gemini pool, one Claude/GPT pool, no per-model window, so the
  `antigravity-argon` row has no reading); operator-entered dated price, menu and floor changes in a proposed
  `config/dated-changes.toml` with a separate-pool guard; a proposal for Claude 5.5 via agy as an Anthropic weekly
  relief pool; and the operator questions. An AGENTS.md row points to it. (#861)
- `deadline start --holder PID` (notify/relay records only): the process whose heartbeats keep the record. When it is gone (pid and start
  time verified) and `done` was never called, the sweep retires the record silently as the new terminal state `abandoned` (one `abandoned
  (holder N gone)` line in `sweep.log`, no owner notice, no Hermes ping, no recovery); a record without `--holder` behaves as before. The
  holder is never signalled, so the ancestor-pid refusal does not apply to it. (#266)
- `bin/deadline` (2026-11-01): register a maximum run time (`start`, `beat`, `extend`, `done`, `ls`, `run`) before any command or wait that may exceed ~2 min; `deadline sweep` (launchd `com.djbclark.deadline-sweep`, every 60 s, `deadline install`) enforces it from outside: SIGTERM to the process group, SIGKILL after the grace, then the recovery (`kill`, `notify`, `relay`, `reroute:RECORD`) once, guarded against pid reuse by the recorded process start time; it also alerts (never kills) once an hour on a running Paseo agent with no activity for 30 min and no live deadline. Rule and commands: `docs/deadline.md`. (#223)
- `bin/pr-wait`, `bin/load-watch admit --wait S` and `bin/acp-dispatch-wait` (2026-11-02) register their own deadline by default, as
  `pr-wait-<repo>-<PR>-<pid>`, `admit-wait-<pid>` and `dispatch-wait-<record>-<pid>`: owner `$PASEO_AGENT_ID` (none: the sweep pings Hermes), budget
  min(own timeout, class budget: 60 min, admission 30 min, dispatch slice 2 h) + 60 s, a heartbeat from the poll loop, `done` on every exit. Past it the
  enforcer kills the waiter's own process group only (exit 124; `admit` answers its 75 "still too hot" because callers fail open on an unknown exit; `acp-dispatch-wait` exits 76, never the
  slice-timeout 124, so a lead re-arms instead of re-tasking a running slice;
  a waiter holds no work). `--no-deadline` opts out; a waiter under
  `deadline run` (`DEADLINE_NAME` set) does not register twice; a missing or failing deadline tool is one stderr warning and the wait
  goes on. Shared code in `bin/deadline_wait.py`; `docs/deadline.md` "Waiters register themselves". (#257)
- Added pure `ade-install update-plan` and `just update-plan` candidate reports
  from recorded dependency update commands and choices, with explicit unknown
  runtime/ownership state and no probes, execution or state writes. (#546)
- Added `skills/diskfree/scripts/bigteam-prune.py` (2026-10-31): removes finished fan-out checkouts under `~/.local/state/bigteam` (dry run by default, `--apply` to act, exit 3 when another run holds `bigteam-prune.lock`), failing closed: only clean, pushed clones with no other worktree and no commit in any ref or reflog missing from a remote-tracking ref, and clean linked worktrees (removed with `git worktree remove`) with no per-worktree refs or HEAD-reflog-only commits; keeps any checkout with ignored files other than known generated dirs, non-standard git dir content, submodules, in-progress operations, activity in the last 7 days or a local-path remote inside the root; never follows symlinks or `GIT_*` variables. `skills/diskfree/SKILL.md` step 7 lists the same rules; `tests/test_bigteam_prune.py` covers them (#196).
- `antigravity-neon` in `docs/model-routing.md` B7a and `skills/model-routing/SKILL.md` C7a (512K Antigravity context vs the 1M API, the Argon tie-break, canary); B9a records the 27-model Antigravity list, the 2026-11-30 removals and the 2026-12-01 Carbon/Argon API price change (#873)
- Carried 80 and 79 in `docs/queue.md`: map the aiuse clinepass sub-accounts in `bin/ade-pi-stuck` so a
  `clinepass-t-e/<model>` agent is judged instead of reported as unmapped, and write `bin/ade-supervisor-scan`'s
  `.redirect-noted` marker when a handoff is first resolved so a replaced lead does not log the hop every 300 s on quiet
  runs. Both were found by the merged supervisor work and its review. (#799)
- Added `bin/codium-ai`, which started VSCodium with Continue's Anthropic key from `sudo-secretspec` as `CONTINUE_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, refusing while VSCodium ran (without `--restart`) or while its settings let terminals inherit the key; `docs/apply-toolchain-prompt.md` 5.13 installed VSCodium with ANSI Colors, Claude Code and Continue and a Ghostty ⇧⌘C colour-code copy, without overwriting existing settings (#253)
- New `eli5` skill: house rules for ELI5 and plain-language explanations (STE-based sentence, word and paragraph limits,
  a fixed structure with an everyday analogy and "where the analogy breaks", an accuracy check), plus an `AGENTS.md`
  pointer and a `docs/skills.md` row. The skill reaches every TUI through the skill links. (#690)
- Manual ClinePass models in Pi using the managed primary and t-e keys; automatic routing remains reserved. (#762)
- `plugins/ade-claim` + `bin/ade_claims.py`: on `agent.create.after`, a claim whose owner agent is archived (or gone, `AGENT_NOT_FOUND`) is rebound to the newly created agent in that workspace through the new compare-and-swap `ade_claims.py rebind-owner` (plugin use only), one log line per rebind; a live owner, a harness-label or session-keyed owner, a released claim or any owner-lookup failure rebinds nothing (operator ruling Q4, 2026-11-20). (#841)
- `bin/ade-update` links the repo tools the instructions name bare (`git-commit-own`, `load-watch`, `agent-id`; the `BIN_LINKS` table) as `~/.local/bin/<name>` -> the canonical checkout's `bin/<name>`: a missing link is created, a stale link into a djbclark-ade checkout or worktree is repaired, a regular file or foreign symlink is never touched (reported, exit 10), a tool absent from `bin/` is skipped with an info line, and `--check` writes nothing. (#860)
- Versioned read-only effective config loader (`bin/ade_config.py`), `bin/ade-config
  check|get|diff`, checked-in `config/ade.schema.json`, thin `just config-check`, and
  `docs/config-migration.md` for schema_version=1 legacy routing/gates projection (no
  reader adoption, no live config writes). (#308)
- GUI-hog auto-freeze list: `config/gui-hog.toml` `[freeze.auto] order` (shipped: Grok Bot) names the apps the load-watch sampler freezes, first listed first and one per 20 s tick, while a build has been held for 3 min on a gate desktop apps can relieve. The freeze is timed with the usual thaw paths and logged `by: "auto"`; protected apps are never auto-frozen, an operator's Resume sticks for an hour (`refreeze_s`), and `LW_GUI_HOG_AUTO=0` or `[freeze.auto] enabled = false` turns it off. Design rule B6 ("never automatic") is amended for the listed apps only. The user overlay may set `[freeze.auto]`. (#859)
- GUI-hog `[freeze.keep_running]`: paths inside an app's bundle (or absolute) that a freeze never stops, matched on the kernel executable path; the entry, `gui-hog frozen --json`, the event log and the dialog's Frozen line record them. CodexBar's `CodexBarCLI` HTTP server (aiuse's collector) now keeps running when CodexBar is frozen. (#859)
- `skills/herdr/SKILL.md`: the official herdr skill, tracked from `herdr --skill` and linked into every TUI by `bin/skill-everywhere --all`. It is the loosened description (upstream's "do not use merely for delegation or parallel work" kept), an ADE rules overlay that overrides the upstream text (messages go by `paseo send` with a named ack or SendMessage with two reply routes, never typed into an agent's pane; agents launch through `route_agent.py plan` and `ade-worker-run`; pane builds go through `bgrun`; `HERDR_ENV` may be inherited), and the untouched upstream body. `bin/herdr-skill-refresh` regenerates it (`--check` reports drift, writes only from a linked worktree, atomic write, clear errors). Listed in `docs/skills.md`. (#855)
- Restored archive-only `/session-finder` searches and explicit data-only refreshes for retained omp, OpenCode, Crush and Pi history without requiring their executables. (#310)
- Added task-boundary Pi automatic model selection using the existing quota-aware router, persistent virtual-model choices and explicit physical pins. (#315)
- Added a Cursor CLI launcher for unattended Pi bridge requests and a SIPB-only skill-catalog reduction that retained project instructions and tools. (#311)
- `bin/skill-everywhere` links Paseo's daemon-synced skill (`~/.agents/skills/paseo`) into the Hermes skills dir as `paseo`,
  idempotently and without touching any Paseo-managed file (a foreign real dir or a missing source is left alone and reported),
  and `--check` exits 1 with an `error: paseo skill is missing on <tui>` line when `paseo` is absent from a live target
  (claude, codex/pi, hermes). (#871)
- `watch` skill (`skills/watch/SKILL.md`) and `/loadwatch` alias (`skills/loadwatch/SKILL.md`): low-token on-demand load and system triage from any terminal window (#539).
- `ade-browser-task` completion readbacks for `issue-access` (repository GET:
  `issue_creation_policy` collaborators-only with issues still enabled; a missing
  REST field records `field-missing` and stays not done) and `github-app-create`
  (App slug/permissions via `GET /apps/{slug}` plus the operator-visible
  installation list restricted to exactly the configured repository), and a
  bounded `poll` loop that reruns the readback on an interval until
  completed/expired. Browser replies and deadline registrations still never prove
  completion (2026-11-12). (#578)
- `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl` registers the CFEngine upstream write gate for native Codex hooks: after applying the template and trusting it, Codex shell calls that write to a `cfengine` or `NorthernTechHQ` upstream are denied, the same protection the Claude hook (`claude/hooks/upstream_review_gate.py`) already gives. Contract and limits in `docs/upstream-review-gate.md`. (#788)
- `bin/fable-wait`: the list of work that must run, or be re-checked, on Fable 5.2 (`add --title T --lead AGENT_ID [--brief-file F] [--note N] [--kind deferred|degraded]`, `close ID`, `ls [--json] [--all]`; an append-only `~/.local/state/ade/fable-wait/queue.jsonl`) and its one-shot wake: `arm` reads the Fable window's reset from `paseo usage ls --json` (claude `weekly_model_fable`, else `weekly`), refuses when it is unknown or past, and installs ONE wake through `bin/ade-sched` at reset + 60 s (re-arming replaces it); `fire` sends each lead its open items with `paseo send --json --no-wait` and one `hermes-ping`, starts nothing itself, and disarms on every path (a corrupt queue line is skipped and reported, an unreadable queue is named in the ping, and a wake more than 24 h late is dropped without sending, because the calendar schedule has no year). Item text reaches peers as quoted data, and `add` refuses control characters and line or paragraph separators (the queue is written ASCII-escaped and split on newline only). A disarm started for a wake `fire` could not identify (`--expect none`) never removes a readable wake armed later. (#652)
- Added opt-in SIPB request-shape diagnostics without retaining prompts, tool arguments, headers or credentials. (#312)
- `bin/ade-pr-open`: opens a PR with `gh pr create` and fills the `(#PR)` placeholder in the branch's own changelog fragment with the new PR number, checks it with `changelog-fold --check`, then commits and pushes that file, so a fragment written before the PR exists no longer turns CI red; `docs/dispatch-footer.md` item 25, `skills/fan-out/SKILL.md` and `changelog.d/README.md` now say to write `(#PR)` first (#858)
- Add a deny-by-default ADE controller pilot with protected enrollment, bounded ZIP verification, immutable evidence retention and existing release Journal orchestration; native production integration remains held. (#796)
- Discover protected evaluator outputs automatically and select qualified master ancestors with retained baseline fallback, ancestry checks and stale-policy refusal. (#796)
- Expose fixed protected apply/recover controller commands with structured refusal until genuine native support is available. (#796)
- Bind the finite Linux controller facade to the producer manifest and complete executing cohort, mapping retained receipts through exact runtime profile identities. (#796)
- Parse explicit bounded versioned standing grants with separate recorded recovery selectors, exact resume/release scope, and matching-denial precedence. (#796)
- Bind repeated standing promotions, recorded rollback and exact resume/release reconciliation to the same Journal transaction API with separate native counters and gated same-bearer recovery. (#796)
- Parse explicitly opted-in tagged V2 directory and fourteen in-process builtin observations against independent protected/manifest identities; retain production HOLD until the genuine native bridge exists. (#796)
- Bind committed V2 observations to the producer's adapted builtin factories, canonical module table and current fixed protected host profiles, keeping unintegrated system namespaces on preexecution HOLD. (#796)
- Validate committed retained system-closure metadata and module-table binding while holding unproved namespace execution; consume native scoped same-bearer recovery with separate verification and no retry of unknown failures. (#796)
- Invoke the authenticated shared read-only namespace verifier immediately before retained CLI execution, with controlled import and refusal before subprocess start on missing or changed proof. (#796)
- Consume the committed strict finiteGit G data only for worktree-include, binding independent recipe and retained tool identities without granting helper admission or changing ordinary Git configuration. (#796)
- Add a private fork inventory projection with paginated owner discovery and explicit incomplete coverage for upstream release watching. (#786)
- Added exact-run GitHub evaluation verification and immutable qualification archive retention without granting deployment authority. (#792)
- Added read-only fork enrollment status and readiness checks with explicit unknown runtime and qualification state. (#782)
- Add immutable fork qualification evidence and a durable binary-only deployment core with trusted fake-adapter coverage; managed installer holds remain in force. (#783)
- Add native Git fork integration API, deny-by-default CLI and synthetic Linux workflow template; mutation remains held pending trusted policy enrollment and App provisioning. (#784)
- Add offline exact-object ADE plugin snapshots with sealed content-addressed retention and a CI-only behavior pilot; no live activation authority. (#793)
- Add a tendcf eligibility adapter that holds missing owner contracts, coupled evidence and permanent retention without granting pin or deployment authority. (#794)
- The Bash habits gate denies `gh run watch` and `gh pr checks --watch` unless `--interval`/`-i` is 60 or more, and warns on `xargs -P`/`parallel` around `gh` calls: four default-3 s watchers emptied the shared 5000/h GitHub quota for an hour on 2026-11-14. dispatch-footer item 26 and orchestration-practices rule 9 budget bulk GitHub API use (`gh api rate_limit` first, stop at remaining < 500, batch with GraphQL). (#672)
- The graft/Trail helper now answers Cursor's `sessionStart` too: `.cursor/hooks.json` now has a `sessionStart` entry, and
  `graft-hooks.cjs` (both the `.claude/helpers` and `.cursor/hooks` copies) treats `cursor-session-start` like
  `session-start` and writes Cursor's `additional_context` shape instead of Claude Code's `hookSpecificOutput`. When an
  installed trail/graft only knows the Claude event, the helper runs its `session-start` and re-shapes what it printed,
  so the hook works with trail installed instead of only down the no-trail fallback. The Trail
  offer also says what to do when `npm install -g` fails with EACCES: point npm at `~/.npm-global`, never `sudo`. The
  `AGENTS.md` graft section now carries the tool-by-tool guidance `skills/graft/SKILL.md` already states. (#798)
- Skill `greptile`: Greptile AI code review through the CLI with `GREPTILE_API_KEY` from sudo-secretspec (never stored by `greptile login --api-key`), review status exit codes, credit costs, MCP OAuth-only; the official greptileai/skills (`check-pr`, `greploop`, `cli-review`) are installed unmodified alongside it (#229).
- GUI-hog dialog, PR 3a (data and a dry-run trigger, no window; docs/gui-hog-dialog.md section M): `bin/gui_hog.py` and `bin/gui-hog status`. `load-watch admit --wait S` keeps a hold file under `~/.local/state/load-watch/held/` while it is refused (best effort, never changes the verdict or exit code); the 20 s sampler keeps a 5-minute per-app window (from `lsappinfo`, build subtrees and protected apps left out, `config/gui-hog.toml`) and, when a hold has lasted 3 minutes and desktop apps use 0.75 cores or 2 GB in sum, logs "would-show" to `gui-hog/dry-run.jsonl` at most once per 30 minutes. `load-watch admit-log summary` counts those lines; `LW_GUI_HOG=0` turns it off. Nothing is quit, frozen or signalled. (#722)
- GUI-hog dialog PR 3b: the native window. `macos/gui-hog/` (Swift, a thin view over the Python core; `ade-gui-hog --self-test` checks its logic without a window) and
  `bin/ade-gui-build` (compiles it into `~/.local/libexec/djbclark-ade/ADE GUI Hog.app`, ad-hoc signed, stamped with the sources' SHA-256; `ade update` runs it with `--if-stale`
  and fails open without `swiftc`). The window shows the load header and one row per desktop app (Quit, Force Quit… with a confirm sheet whose default is Cancel) and the bottom
  row Wait (leftmost, the default, does nothing; Return and Escape are swallowed), Ignore 1 h and Quit All… (confirm sheet, Cancel default; never Force Quit). Every Quit runs
  `bin/gui-hog vet` (live protected-list, identity and build-subtree checks, exit 0/3/4/5/6) and the dialog's own pid-plus-start-time re-check before it calls
  `NSRunningApplication`. New `gui-hog` subcommands `vet`, `snooze`, `record-exit`, `log`, `show` (manual run on real apps) and `demo` (fixture rows, touches nothing). (#729)
- GUI-hog dialog PR 3c, Freeze: `gui-hog freeze | resume | thaw | frozen` stop an app's whole process tree on an operator click only (never automatic), with a write-ahead record, a four-field identity check before every signal, the protected list on the root and every member, and a thaw on every path (timed 10 min default, Resume in the window and the new menu-bar item, the sampler's first step each tick, crash recovery, load-watch's removal, `gui-hog thaw --all`); row buttons Freeze N min and Freeze…, a Frozen section with Resume, and `[freeze] enabled = false` to hide the buttons; the dialog window can now be minimized to the Dock (it acts on nothing and keeps refreshing) (#730)
- `docs/gui-hog-dialog.md`: design for the GUI-hog dialog (PR 3 of the load-gate retune). It covers why Swift, the sampler-side trigger with the `admit --wait` hold registry, a thin Swift view over a stdlib-Python core, Freeze safety (write-ahead state file, pid-plus-start identity, thaw paths, protected list, escape hatch), Quit semantics, testing, phasing and threat notes. No code yet (#717)
- `bin/gui-hog suggest`: reads the history of measured freezes and the dialog's would-show lines and proposes apps for the never-auto list (`[freeze.auto] skip`: a `[freeze.depends]` note such as Beeper's MCP API or CodexBar's aiuse readings, or negligible savings) and for the new always-freeze list; read-only, it prints its evidence and edits nothing. (#872)
- GUI-hog measured savings: every freeze records the app's freeable cores and RSS and the system window just before it, and its resume writes a `freeze-result` event (load, compressor, pageins, swapins over the freeze against the window before). A SIGSTOP frees CPU, not RSS; the docs say so (`docs/gui-hog-dialog.md` Q1). (#872)
- `[freeze.auto] always` (ships empty): apps frozen without asking, with no dialog row; Resume stays in the Frozen list and menu, the never-auto list wins, and a protected app or one hosting a build or terminal is never quiet. (#872)
- The dialog and `gui-hog status` list the real load sources (builds and test jobs, agent sessions, apps, system processes) with cores, RSS and share, a build's tree grouped under its root and owner; display only, never a Freeze button for a build or a protected app. (#872)
- `hand` skill and per-TUI `hand` command wrappers (omp, Pi, zcode, opencode): `/hand` runs the `handoff` skill everywhere, because omp's built-in `/handoff` only compacts context and shadows the skill (#277).
- Queued the provider-quota strand + broken-self-handoff incident as Carried 82: a quota-walled agent cannot self-handoff, so the lead archives it and records why, and the paseo-merge-ruling instruction edit must be redone on a working provider. (#836)
- `bin/ade-tidy prune [--apply] [--json|--json-line] [--min-idle S] [--max-kills N] [--orphan-grace S]` archived idle, finished Baton `[Handoff writer]` helpers machine-wide (observe-only without `--apply`) and logged one JSON line per action in `~/.local/state/ade/helper-prune.log`; the `config/launchd/com.djbclark.ade-helper-prune.plist` template (every 30 minutes, not installed by merging) and the `ade-helper-prune` inventory row ran it (#718)
- `ade-tidy prune` added `--exclude ID` and `~/.local/state/ade/helper-prune.exclude` (`ADE_HELPER_PRUNE_EXCLUDE`), re-read on every run and again right before each archive or kill (#718)
- `ade-tidy prune` reported orphan provider processes of archived, closed agents (session-id mapping only, unprovable ones `unmapped`) and, with `--apply`, sent SIGTERM by pid to the provider and its direct `graft mcp` children after a pid, start-time and session re-read, within a per-run kill budget (`--max-kills`, `--orphan-grace`, `kill_plan` and `deferred[]` in the summary); a process counted as Paseo's only when its parent was the Paseo daemon and its `callerAgentId` named the mapped agent, never on `--input-format stream-json` alone; each helper's last activity was re-read before its archive, a provider's graft children were signalled only after the provider's own checks passed, an unreadable agent record was an error (`records_unreadable`), and the run refused to act when its log could not be opened (#718)
- `skills/agent-surfaces/SKILL.md`: resolves a named tab/agent across Paseo and herdr together and routes by harness: `paseo send` to a Paseo agent; to a terminal agent SendMessage or an atomic reply file, never keystrokes into its pane. The herdr skill itself moved to #855. (#847)
- `docs/cross-harness-messaging.md` and `bin/xmsg`: the verified two-way path between a Paseo agent and a local Claude/herdr session (paseo send one way, atomic reply file the other). A sender outside Paseo must offer two reply routes (SendMessage and a reply file); an ack file is written only by its receiver, the sender's own `queued` status goes to `PATH.status`; `to-paseo` exits 0 delivered, 5 accepted, 1 failed. (#847)
- Added dry-run-default `ade-install bootstrap`; explicit `--yes` acquired only
  missing user-scoped Just, verified it, and continued to `just install --dry-run`,
  stopping on acquisition or verification failure without claiming full setup. (#550)
- Added `ade-install keys-collect` / `just keys-collect`: one interactive batch of
  `sudo-secretspec set` on inherited terminal stdio for the selected keys that are
  missing, failing or expiring within 14 days, with immediate re-verify, retry/skip,
  a summary table and launch-time delivery through `sudo-secretspec run`; without a
  terminal it lists key names and exits 3. Offline-tested only; no live vault. (#597)
- Added cached selected-key worklist planning with explicit unknown coverage and no probes, credential entry or writes. (#559)
- `ade-install verify-key`: three more fixed metadata probes — cursor `GET /v1/me`, telegram `getMe` (documented token path) and github `GET /rate_limit`, whose probe reports the token expiry date GitHub returns via the allowlisted expiring marker; catalog rows `CURSOR_API_KEY`, `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` and `FIRECRAWL_GITHUB_PUBLIC_TOKEN` now carry `verify_argv`, while ZAI/Exa/Jina/Greptile stay deliberately probe-less with dated reasons (#580).
- Added per-job CI attempt history and load-gated macOS local fallback after slow or failed CI. (#586)
- `ade-install doctor` / `just install-doctor` reports, per current TUI (the `bin/skill-everywhere` targets table), whether the shared MCP servers in `config/mcp-clients.toml` are registered in its config file and whether every skill is linked (`bin/ade_client_wiring.py`): ok, or a warning with the exact fix command; observe-only, TUIs without readable MCP config report unknown (#633)
- `docs/mcp-on-demand.md`: a design for reaching MCP servers on demand: the servers in every client, with measured cost and 30-day usage; a registry schema; `bin/mcp-find` capability lookup; the Google Photos verdict; a phased rollout. Also `bin/mcp-usage`, a read-only, unwired transcript counter. (#865)
- Added atomic scalar edits for numeric context budgets and four existing-key metadata fields across local, unified and legacy inputs, with final effective validation and preserved array/credential boundaries (#555).
- `libexec/agent-bin/gh` + `bin/ade-gh-guard` (mg-52, queue Carried 62; operator ruling 2026-11-16, B2 + B5): a `gh` PATH shim that judges a `gh` write (`pr`/`issue` create, new, edit, comment, review, close, reopen; `api` with a body flag) for a live adversary APPROVE marker in the `gh` process itself, from its real argv, cwd, operand files and stdin, with the hook's own `judge` (one module, no copy). A refusal is exit 4 and nothing runs; everything else is `os.execv` of the real `gh` with the same argv, stdin, exit status and environment. It skips every copy of itself, counts re-entries only when the real `gh` is a script, and fails closed only for a write it cannot read. `docs/adversary-gh-shim.md` has the install and what it never catches; `docs/code-review.md` rule 14 now promises only that the gate stops an agent's careless marker text, not deliberate obfuscation (`docs/threat-model.md` section 5 item 9 says the same). (#761)
- `bin/ade-model-rankings` (zero-token script): refresh `docs/model-rankings.data.json` and section A of `docs/model-rankings.md` from the two Artificial Analysis boards via the self-hosted Firecrawl API, with a `check` drift guard; a weekly `config/launchd/com.djbclark.ade-model-rankings.plist` job runs it. (#818)
- Added explicit checkpointed native self-compaction requests with duplicate protection and unconfirmed completion reporting. (#567)
- Added same-host self-handoff with durable successor acknowledgement, native mode readback, ambiguous-outcome reconciliation and idle-only predecessor retirement. (#567)
- Add explicit role enrollment, broker-backed claim projections, durable completion intake, bounded optional plugin controller, shared operation intents and a controlled local retirement gate. (#779)
- Added the read-only `ade-coord agent relationships` JSON query for explicit configured agent/controller/worker edges, with unknown or ambiguous ownership kept non-contactable and fresh online owner verification required before any send. (#813)
- Add durable SQLite delivery state, verified NATS Object Store artifacts, authority-checked intake, operation reconciliation and a secret-safe JSON CLI. (#779)
- Exercise real coordination and Object Store capacity rejection, bounded Linux tmpfs ENOSPC, established TCP partition/reconnect, broker/client restart, handoff with late acknowledgement, and older-store rollback fencing in scoped CI. (#779)
- Claim-attached durable managed-job evidence for the opt-in NATS coordination pilot. Unknown launches and live process groups block quiescence; no idle timeout grants ownership. (#779)
- Persist fenced role lifecycle transitions and checkpoint provenance before ownership transfer, requiring active authority for receipts. (#779)
- Add shared NATS coordination protocol library and Linux broker conformance proof for roles, immutable reports, and fenced receipts. (#779)
- Recorded the operator's record-only ruling for NATS in `docs/nats-record-only-ruling.md`: the host carries the prompt, NATS is the record; cross-agent re-handoff proceeds on `paseo send`; directed delivery, dynamic enrollment, wake/reaper and retirement-ruling changes are not authorized. (#831)
- Network stream snapshots with integrity and generation checks, isolated restore verification, and a bounded local Temporal workflow experiment without cloud enrollment. (#779)
- Prepare a portable CI coordination runtime with dependency provenance, native NATS CLI checksums and atomic artifact installation without enrolling roles. (#779)
- Include the enrolled-role instructions in the portable coordinator runtime (#779)
- Verify the current coordination source against the broker package from published hub configuration. (#779)
- Audit only installed production dependencies when building the runtime bundle. (#779)
- Supply an isolated bounded filesystem for deployment-package disk-full conformance. (#779)
- `antigravity-neon` service: route Gemini 4 Neon through Paseo's antigravity provider on the shared Gemini 5-hour/weekly window, as a frontier fallback (behind gpt-6-astra and Fable) and last-resort research pool; `[dispatch].frontier_models` now names `gemini-4-neon`. `bin/route_agent.py` maps `gemini-4-neon*` to the `gemini` pool family (Neon is not Argon's separate weekly window). (#815)
- Added `ade-agent-mode check` and dry-run-default repair planning with exact native readback and refusal to apply without an atomic live-only native mode API. (#568)
- A `paseo` PATH shim enforces the archived-send guard in the send path itself: `libexec/agent-bin/paseo` (a POSIX sh front door; the real paseo is found past its own directory, `ADE_REAL_PASEO` overrides) hands every `send` to the new `bin/ade-paseo-guard`, which parses `paseo [global opts] [agent] send [opts] TARGET` with the hook's own parser and then execs the real paseo as asked, with the target rewritten to its successor (a completed self-handoff replaced it), or not at all (exit 4: archived with no successor, a cyclic ledger). It closes the hole of the Claude hook, which never saw a send built in a Python `subprocess.run(["paseo", "send", ...])` (a send to a replaced ex-lead went through that way on 2026-11-14); it sees only a `paseo` that PATH resolves to `libexec/agent-bin`, so on the operator's Mac a bash child (`bash -c`, a bash script, `bash <<EOF`) still reaches the real paseo because `BASH_ENV=~/.bashrc` reorders its PATH (call the shim by its path, or `env -u BASH_ENV`), it fails open on any error of its own, skips every copy of itself when it looks for the real paseo (two checkouts on one PATH do not loop), never judges the message text, and is not installed by this change (put `libexec/agent-bin` first on PATH; the ade-claim plugin's W4 hook does it only in `enforce` mode). The hook parser now reads `-o/--format` and the global options after `agent` and after `send`, and does not judge a send with an explicit `--host`/`--home` anywhere. New operator override `ADE_ALLOW_REPLACED_SEND=<its full id>` sends to a replaced agent that is still live (not archived, sound ledger), logged to `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/send-guard-overrides.jsonl`; the hook accepts the same assignment before the command. (#696)
- `claude/hooks/peer_trust_gate.py` (UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop; registered in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl`
  with a fail-open command): a `paseo-system` agent-message that Paseo delivered to the session (the prompt, or a no-origin
  `queued_command` transcript record; never tool output, never text quoted inside other text) gets "Verified peer message from ID
  (NAME): full internal trust, answer it with `paseo send`" when the source id is a listed local agent (`paseo ls -g --json`,
  offline), or "UNVERIFIED: ask the operator" otherwise; agent-notifications are informational; Stop blocks once if an unannounced one is pending. Agents had refused peer questions as
  prompt injection (operator, 2026-11-13). AGENTS.md Trust, docs/threat-model.md section 5 item 9 and docs/code-review.md rule 13 record
  the accepted weakness. (#645)
- `docs/persistent-progress-reminder-design.md` (design only, 2026-11-12): a zero-token `ade-progress` scanner run by `ade-sched` that, for an enrolled idle agent with declared open work, writes a local inbox record, raises a Paseo Progress-panel item, sends one `hermes-ping` and wakes the agent with `paseo send --json --no-wait`; a closed, errored or archived owner gets an operator notice and no wake; the Paseo built-in survey (heartbeat, schedule, wait, plugin hooks, Progress, Herald) is recorded, and `docs/scheduling.md` points to it (#565)
- `acp-run` and `acp-dispatch` target Pi through `pi-acp` (independent OpenAI API routing profile, `--perm all` only), with paid-capacity admission that refuses identity-changing pass-through options (`--model`, `--set`, `--meta`, `--agent-arg`, `--agent-env`, `--auth`) and persists the admitted minimum headroom across resumes (#273)
- Pi session history, fleet detection, resume hints and `ade-backup-stores` coverage; exact liveness requires a session-file identity (#273)
- Native Pi session naming (`skills/autorename/pi-bridge.ts`, `pi_native.py`), the only native Pi control: titles with C0/C1, line-separator or bidi control characters are refused. `self-slash` on native Pi rejects `--finish`, `--then` and successor options, and `/compact` always refuses (#273)
- `bin/pi-skill-dedup`: a manual, claim-gated tool that adds exact `SKILL.md` exclusions to Pi's settings for an explicit duplicate pair (reversible with `undo`; refuses when Linux inode flags, macOS xattrs/flags or ACLs cannot be preserved). No existing duplicate is removed or hidden until an operator runs it (#273)
- `skill-everywhere` gains a `pi` target (Pi reads `~/.agents/skills`, shared with codex and goose); a `pi` routing service (OpenAI API billing, `api-only` eligible alongside omp, `measured_only` so it fails closed without a measured reading and prints no RESERVE advice); committed canonical `.pi/agents/` profiles (`astra-worker`, `frontier-astra`; installing them is manual, see `docs/pi-agent-profiles.md`) (#273)
- Pi subagent extension with per-invocation `model` and `thinking` overrides (invocation, then frontmatter, then Pi default), documented in `docs/pi-subagent.md`; project-local agents always prompt in a UI session and are refused headless unless `PI_SUBAGENT_ALLOW_PROJECT_AGENTS=1` (#273)
- Three named manual OpenCode Go accounts in Pi, resolving managed keys independently without cross-account failover. (#763)
- `bin/ade-pin-interactive` tags non-worker (human-interaction) agents `ade.interactive=1` so `paseo ls --label ade.interactive=1` finds them. (#839)
- `bin/ade-pin-workspaces` applies Paseo's native sidebar pin to every non-worker window (`LEAD`/`MAIN`/`OTHER` and operator-started windows; `WORKER`/`WORKERS` boxes excluded) by driving the UI's own `workspace.pin.set` RPC through the bundled CLI client — the same mechanism as `bin/paseo-set-model`, since the `paseo` CLI and plugin SDK expose no pin command (upstream draft 23). (#839)
- `bin/pr-policy` (2026-11-04): only djbclark and the collaborators he invited open ready pull requests on his repositories. Every live GitHub repository of `djbclark` and `frdminc` is set to `pull_request_creation_policy=collaborators_only`, and an open pull request from anyone else is kept a draft (GitHub `convertPullRequestToDraft`; Forgejo, which has no collaborators-only setting, a `WIP: ` title prefix). `plan`, `apply --yes [--notify] [--quiet]`, `install`/`uninstall` for the launchd job `com.djbclark.pr-policy` (every 600 s), which applies the policy to repositories created later. Config `config/pr-policy.toml`, doc `docs/pr-policy.md`. (#285)
- `bin/pr-radar-route`: the routing adapter for the pr-radar Paseo plugin (JSON context on stdin, launch overrides on stdout). It maps the PR signal to a route kind (review requested or security: judgment; bot author: bulk; else code, operator ruling 2026-11-14), asks the `route_agent.py` beside it, and, when the router or the adapter fails, exits non-zero (the router's own exit code when it had one, else 1) with one stderr reason and nothing on stdout so pr-radar falls back to its defaults; documented in `docs/model-routing.md` A2b (#685).
- Catalog component rows take optional `probe_warn_exit` (exit codes that mean advisory findings) and `probe_summary_re` (the
  probe output line shown as the detail). A listed exit whose stdout carries the summary line is `warn` (doctor exit 0), not
  `error`; a crash without that line stays `error`. Hermes sets both, so `ade-install doctor` no longer fails on `hermes doctor`'s
  advisory exit 1. (#642)
- Documented in `docs/scheduling.md` that un-choosing a ledger-owned job makes the next `reconcile --yes` unload it, and added `plan_reconcile` tests for the `ade-progress` row (unchosen skip, installed-unchosen report, ledger-owned unchosen removal, chosen install). (#617)
- Added the `ade-progress` row to `config/install.toml` `[[install.jobs]]` (slice 1b of `docs/persistent-progress-reminder-design.md`, 2026-11-12): `kind = "ade-sched"`, label `com.djbclark.ade-progress`, `class = "optional"`, `chosen = false`, so `ade-sched diff`, `just doctor` and `reconcile` stay quiet and install nothing until the operator installs the job and sets `chosen = true`, after which doctor expects the label. Merging loads nothing. Updated the plist template comment, the design doc status (A0, G3) and `docs/scheduling.md` (install and uninstall commands), and replaced the "not inventoried" test with row and doctor-expectation tests. (#616)
- Added `plan_reconcile` tests that pin the ledger-id equality in the "inventory row ... is not chosen" remove reason, the `retired` reason winning over it, and the `ade-sched`/`paseo` kind filter on the installed-but-unchosen report. (#618)
- Queued the quota-reset-boundary gap as Carried 83: routing must treat a past-reset row as refilled, and the lead must re-probe quota before a quota-sensitive dispatch; a zero-token reset nudge will name a pool whose window just reset. (#838)
- Added `bin/ade-net-resume --quota` (off by default), a second pass that resumed our own Pi agents (z.ai `zai/` models) stopped by a provider quota window once a fresh `aiuse` sample showed the window reset. The pass recorded `quota-wait` in the shared ledger, failed closed on a stale, missing or unparseable `aiuse`, refused while any window over 360 minutes was exhausted, kept the ledger, cap, lock, ancestry and re-inspection guards of the network path (the endpoint probe is not used), skipped agents that session-guard held, resumed at most one agent per pool per scan and per 600 seconds, never re-read an agent's logs before the reset check once its error event was in the ledger, and accepted a `closed` agent only with an unchanged ledger entry. Added `--quota-per-pool`, `--quota-grace-seconds` and `--only-agent` (restricts both passes). Documented in `docs/net-resume.md`; the launchd template was left dry-run, without `--quota`. (#613)
- Added an upstream bug draft for the Paseo daemon worker crash from an uncaught `kill EPERM` in a plugin timer (`docs/upstream-issues.md` item 20, not filed). (#613)
- `ref lookup ID` (`CLAUDE-74` or `74`): prints the bot, issue time and agent hint of an id from `issued.log` under a shared flock, creating nothing; exit 1 for an id never issued or issued to another bot. `claude/hooks/ref_integrity_gate.py`, a Claude Code Stop hook (registered in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl`, linked by `ade-update`): when the final post's last line is `Ref:` it checks the form `Ref: <BOT>-<base36>`, that the id is in `issued.log`, that the bot prefix matches and that the id was not used earlier in the transcript, and otherwise blocks once with an instruction to run `ref <BOT>`; a long post with no Ref line gets a non-blocking note. It honours `stop_hook_active`, skips sub-agents and fails open. Tests in `tests/test_ref_integrity_gate.py`, `tests/test_ref_labels.py`. (#863)
- `bin/ref` and `bin/ref-append`: script-issued `Ref: <BOT>-<id>` lines from one global base-36 counter (`~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/refs/`, flock-locked, append-only `issued.log`). `docs/ref-labels.md` records the standing reply-label convention (Roman sections, bold-italic items, script-issued Ref on every operator post) and the per-bot prefix table. `ref_audit.py` is not yet wired to a Mac transcript store. (#775)
- `relay.py` pings the operator (one `hermes-ping`, naming the target, the card, the sender and the message id) when a message has waited 30 minutes behind a question or permission card in its target; once per card and target, bounded and never fatal to the message, `RELAY_CARD_ALERT_S` sets the delay (0 = off), and `--pending` shows `[card alert: ...]` (#248)
- `relay.py` also tells the sender (one notice) and the operator (one `hermes-ping`) when a text has waited 30 minutes for a merely WORKING target (`RELAY_BUSY_ALERT_S`, 0 = off); a text queued behind an earlier one for 30 minutes tells its sender (one notice, no operator ping: what holds the head pings for itself); it never steers by itself, and the usage text and `skills/session-finder/SKILL.md` name the `--steer` re-send and its lost-work risk (#248)
- Rules in `docs/orchestration-practices.md` and the dispatch footer: never block on a job over about 2 minutes unless the next step depends on it (a blocking agent is deaf as well as silent), never wait on CI, one progress line per fix-and-review round, a reply stuck behind a card is the operator's to unblock (#248)
- `bin/review-bot-findings` and `config/review-bots.toml`: a must-triage gate for listed AI reviewer bots (CodeRabbit, Codex, Greptile, Cursor Bugbot, Copilot code review): unresolved bot threads and actionable review bodies must be fixed or answered with a reason by `djbclark`'s account; every actionable review body counts (a later 0-count review does not erase an earlier one); a running bot check run or commit status (`status_contexts`) blocks as pending; a repo adds or drops bots in `.github/ade-review-bots.toml` on its default branch; the bots' text stays data. (#713)
- `config/routing.toml`: `[dispatch.repo_overrides.REPO] frontier_order` declares a repository's frontier reviewer order, and `route_agent.py plan --kinds frontier --repo REPO` sorts the pools that already passed every gate by it (tendcf: `codex`, `claude`, `antigravity-argon`), so a claude burn-first bonus no longer puts Fable ahead of a usable Astra there; a name that is no service, a repeated or empty list, or an upper-case repo key fails the policy load (legacy file read and runtime config alike). (#708)
- `skills/model-routing` item 14 "cost tiers by kind of work" (2026-11-01): tier 1 code on Sonnet 5.5 / GPT-6.1 Sol, tier 2 never new or changed logic (output reviewed by a stronger model), tier 3 judgment on Opus 5.5; no cheap-first cascade for code, with the Terminal-Bench 4.0 evidence (Vals AI, 2026-10-29); and a Copilot use-it-up exception to item 6 (floor, `github`-only limit and `max_parallel` lifted until the next 00:00 UTC on the 1st, included allowance only, per-token AI-credit billing, $0 budget stop confirmed first) (#207).
- `ade-install update` (and `just update` with no arguments) now executes each
  catalog candidate's own recorded `update_argv` with evidence: default
  `--dry-run` previews candidates with pre-observation and exact argv; `--yes`
  runs the argv (argv lists, never shell strings; `system_argv` and
  `paseo_plugin` rows are never touched), runs a scoped doctor over the updated
  ids, retries each doctor-failing component once and reports it (never rolls
  back), and writes outcome evidence atomically to
  `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/updates.json`. Exit 1 when an update command
  fails or an updated component still fails doctor after its retry. (#583)
- Added the item-24 signed-agent-message contract as a design page
  (`docs/signed-agent-message.md`, queued unit W26, operator ruling OQ3):
  versioned envelope, key registry, expiry/replay rules, fail-closed verify
  order and operator approval tokens for high-impact actions; paper only —
  no code, tool, key or transport. (#575)
- Added `ade-install doctor --id ID` and `just doctor hermes` for component-only
  checks with explicit result scope; unchosen components never probe and full
  doctor behavior stayed intact. (#540)
- `ade-self-session handoff --thinking LEVEL` sets the successor's thinking level (passed to `paseo agent run --thinking`; a malformed value is refused before any native call; recorded as `thinking` in the transaction record); without it the successor inherits the predecessor's own level when `agent inspect` reports an explicit one, and an unknown or `auto` level launches with no flag as before (2026-11-14: a `high` lead handed off to an `auto` successor). `skills/handoff` Step 9 and `docs/self-session.md` document it. (#663)
- `bin/skill-everywhere` links a fourth shared skill root, `~/src/chrome-use/skill-data/<name>` (the third-party `leeguooooo/chrome-use` repo's skills, e.g. `real-chrome`, `core`; `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_CHROME` overrides it). It resolves after public, private and site, so a same-named ADE skill wins. `docs/skill-everywhere.md` documents it. (#773)
- `skills/barnowl` section "Snipe on pastebin" (2026-11-02): the second Zephyr client on pastebin (asedeno/snipe on branch `latest` of djbclark/snipe, which merges asedeno/snipe#2 so it runs on the system Python 3.13 and #3 so roost retries when roost-api is down; launcher, the roost-api.mit.edu outage, the `R l` subscription load still to do, signup left to the human) (#252)
- `ade-self-session handoff` ends `creation-refused` (terminal, CLI exit 3) when Paseo refuses `agent run` with a code it raises before creating anything (`INVALID_THINKING_OPTION`, `INVALID_OPTIONS` and the like), returns no agent id and `agent ls --label ade.handoff=TOKEN` lists none, instead of `creation-unknown`: no reconcile is needed, the predecessor keeps working, the send guard and the succession ledger treat it as dead, and a new `handoff` replaces it (the earlier reason kept as `refused_before`). A retry first re-probes the old token and is refused if an agent carries it. A timeout, a missing code, any other code (`AGENT_CREATE_FAILED`, `DAEMON_*`, `UNKNOWN_ERROR`: Paseo raises them after creating the agent too), a labelled agent or unparsable output stays `creation-unknown` (#748).
- `bin/ade-settings-apply` (run by `bin/ade-update`; `--check` is a dry-run) applies the ADE-owned parts of `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` to the live `~/.claude/settings.json`: ADE hooks whose script exists in the djbclark-ade tree, matched by script path (live form wins), a `permissions.deny` union, and template keys absent from live (such as `sandbox`); it backs up first, writes atomically, never removes or overwrites a live value, and refuses if a live key path would vanish. The template was re-refreshed from the merged result (docs/claude-settings-apply.md). (#759)
- `bin/ade-supervisor-scan`: each scan now writes the re-handoff it would perform for a stuck Pi agent (who, which exhausted account, which healthy alternative) and logs it. The default is a dry run that archives and relaunches nothing. `ADE_SUPERVISOR_REHANDOFF=live` calls `bin/ade-supervisor-rehandoff`, which is not yet built and refuses, so no agent moves until the ade-self-session handoff path is wired (Carried 70). The plan file is written temp-then-rename, the plan line is logged only when the plan changes (an empty plan never), and `ADE_SUPERVISOR_REHANDOFF` is documented in docs/scheduling.md and the watch skill. The plan names only Pi agents (each entry carries `family`), so an errored Codex or Claude agent is never in it. (#822)
- `bin/ade-pi-stuck` (read-only) lists every live Pi agent, maps it to its billing account and flags the ones on a
  quota-exhausted account, printing a comparable healthy alternative model under another account for the re-handoff.
  A stale or absent quota reading never clears an account the operator has confirmed empty; only a fresh positive
  balance does. `skills/watch/SKILL.md` section 5 is the `/watch stuck` mode that runs it (the re-handoff — archive,
  relaunch, tell it to continue — stays a separate gated action). (#785)
- `bin/ade-supervisor-scan` + `config/launchd/com.djbclark.ade-supervisor-scan.plist` (every 300 s, `ade-job-run`
  wrapped, inventoried in `config/ade.toml [install.jobs]`, install command in `docs/scheduling.md`): the scan notices
  the lead named in `ADE_SUPERVISOR_LEAD` or in `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/supervisor/lead` (a file, so no agent id
  sits in the plist or the inventory and a handoff rewrites one line) once per newly stuck Pi agent, in at most one
  message per run, and stays quiet while that agent stays stuck, its dedupe set in
  `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/supervisor/notified`. A scan that breaks or cannot read quota alerts the
  lead once rather than going quietly blind, never messages an archived lead (a delivery would un-archive it) and
  reports that to the operator once through `hermes-ping`. (#785)
- `skills/tidy/SKILL.md` and `bin/ade-tidy`: tidy one repo's Paseo agents and windows. Observe-only by default; `--apply` prints the plan, then acts with every precondition re-read right before its action. It inventories agents (owner class, window, status, pending permissions, a WAITING verdict), reports workers waiting on the lead and agents waiting on the operator, sends at most one plain reconnect message per agent per hour (delivery proven only by `ack`), renames windows to `<KIND> <repo> (<work>)`, and archives only verified-done own workers and provably-ours empty windows (never MAIN, never a running agent). Paseo has no command to move an agent between windows, so misplaced tabs are reported, never relaunched. (#651)
- Added an installed Trail matcher diagnostic that detected inert-data false positives and unsafe exemption drift after package upgrades. (#594)
- `claude/hooks/upstream_review_gate.py` (2026-11-12) is a Claude Code `PreToolUse` hook that denies writes CFEngine upstream would see, and only those: `gh` writes and `git push` whose target is a GitHub repository of `cfengine` or `NorthernTechHQ` (explicit `-R`/URL/`gh api` endpoint first, then the directory's remotes), mutating requests to `*.atlassian.net`, and Composio Jira writes. It replaces the unregistered shell hook `~/.claude/hooks/upstream_review_gate.sh` and closes its two gaps (a `gh api` path was never read; `gh api -f/-F/--field/--raw-field/--input` without `-X` is a POST and was not seen); reads and other third-party repositories pass. Contract and limits in `docs/upstream-review-gate.md`; registering it in `~/.claude/settings.json` is a separate step (#563).
- Added offline nightly-learning token reservation accounting and reviewed-effect proposal classification; uncertain injection holds proposals and adoption remains disabled. (#584)
- Added `ade-web` search/fetch/check failover with provider attempts, wall deadlines, archive recovery and explicit all-failed/truncated pause exits. (#582)
- `ade-config set` now writes string arrays and choices: `keys.NAME.verify_argv|components`, `context_budget.dirs`,
  and existing install rows by identity (`install.component.ID.chosen` and its argv/platform arrays,
