  `install.jobs.NAME.chosen|set|platforms`, repo target only), plus `--create-key` for new key identities. Every edit
  is proved against the whole reparsed tree, the catalog schema and semantics (docs/config-migration.md C4). (#602)
- `ade-config export-unified [--write|--check]` generates `config/ade.toml` from the legacy domain files with their
  comments and proves the effective tree is identical; the tracked file waits for in-flight catalog edits (section F
  of docs/config-migration.md). (#602)
- `ade-install python` and the POSIX-sh cold-start bridge `bin/ade-bootstrap`: find or obtain Python 3.11+ with uv
  (never pip/sudo, no system-Python writes, refused as root, preview by default), serialized by the install ledger
  lock with a pending sentinel and an explicit `--recover`; uv itself is never fetched, Just and full setup stay
  separate. (#604)
- `ade-install doctor` now reports crash leftovers of an interrupted install as `install-state` rows (an orphaned
  `pending` ledger entry, a killed lock holder, a half-written temp file, a dead Python acquisition), judged by pid plus
  process start time, and `ade-install recover [--yes]` is the explicit repair: it relabels orphaned `pending` entries
  `interrupted`, quarantines temp files and appends everything to `recovery-evidence.jsonl`, deleting nothing, never
  waiting on a running installer and refusing as root. `python-acquire.pending` no longer reports `owner-active` for a
  reused pid. (#609)
- Running the installer as root now walks the state directory from `/` with directory file descriptors and refuses a
  symlink, a non-root-owned component or one writable by others (sticky excepted) before creating or opening anything, and
  opens `ledger.lock` and the evidence log relative to that descriptor, so a root-owned link into a user-controlled
  directory or a swap after the check cannot redirect a root write. (#609)
- `just install` asks the three setup-plan S3a Hermes paths on a tty
  (`ADE_HERMES_CHOICE=skip|self|ai` otherwise) and `ade-install hermes-choice`
  records the answer in the install state dir without editing tracked config.
  `skip` prevents Hermes apply/probes; `self` and `ai` apply, then with `--yes` run
  `just hermes-setup --self` or `just hermes-setup`. Scoped `doctor hermes`
  adds observe-only checklist probes after the catalog probe passes
  (config, skills root, claude-collaboration, `claude mcp list`; the
  tell_chief_of_staff pointer stays optional) (#591).
- Add scoped automatic Paseo permission sweeps, explicit unattended fan-out modes, and an unloaded scheduler template. (#585)
- Render permission sweeps across scheduler backends, check watch expiry before each sweep, and exclude the lead without ancestry probes. (#585)
- Added dry-run network recovery for a lead's descendants with connectivity probes, bounded watching, durable resume limits and an unloaded scheduler template. (#596)
- Added `review-launch --mode trusted` (YOLO: `bypassPermissions` with Bash, no read-boundary claim, needs `--trusted-author` and `--head`) and `review-pr --runtime auto|restricted|yolo`, so a PR the repository owner authored is reviewed by default in the trusted runtime while every other author, `--allow-untrusted` and `--runtime restricted` keep the restricted review boundary. (#574)
- `ade-sched reconcile --yes` and `ade-install apply` enable only the launchd writes that cannot kill or overwrite under concurrency: an absent job is created exclusively (`O_EXCL` temp + `os.link`) and bootstrapped, an unloaded job whose plist byte-matches the ADE render is bootstrapped without a write, and a loaded job whose plist matches is adopted ledger-only; no path calls `bootout`. Loaded jobs, any existing non-matching file and every removal defer with exit 6, untouched (a probe on macOS 27.2 showed `launchctl disable` does not stop a loaded StartInterval job, and a check followed by `bootout`, replace or unlink is not atomic); systemd, cron and paseo keep the conservative deferral (#619)
- `ade-sched install` and `ade-sched uninstall` take `--restart-service` (low-level `install(..., restart_service=True)` /
  `uninstall(..., restart_service=True)`): the one way to replace or remove a LOADED launchd job that is not wrapped in
  `bin/ade-job-run`, and only for a KeepAlive service that does not run through `ade-job-run` (the `launchctl print` of the
  LOADED job must itself show KeepAlive and its path must be the installed plist; the installed file must provably be the
  loaded definition and have a truthy KeepAlive; an install also needs the new definition's KeepAlive; any loaded argv that
  contains an `ade-job-run` under any name is refused, use `migrate-wrapper`). The flag is accepted before or after the
  subcommand, and acts on any loaded KeepAlive user agent the operator names. It is a deliberate service restart: the
  `bootout` stops the running service. The label's canonical lock is still taken exclusive without waiting (a busy lock
  refuses), the recovery record and the prior bytes are saved before the `bootout`, the unload is proven, and the prior
  definition is restored if the new one fails (the record stays if the restore fails). Without the flag, and for a non-KeepAlive
  unwrapped job, the refusal is unchanged. (#626)
- `ade-harden` S6 coverage: Forgejo read-back through `forgejo-api`
  (`[hardening.forgejo]`), an operator drift notice printed by `observe`,
  `ade-harden renew` for the expiring interaction limit (dry-run unless
  `--apply`; the only mutation a check authorizes, the read-back must show the
  expiry moved, and a failed PUT exits nonzero), and the S5a gate canary
  (`[hardening.canaries]`, corpus outside the repo with `~` expanded; a
  configured but missing corpus fails the check). A malformed Forgejo
  `base_url` is a validation error, not a traceback. `observe` stays
  read-only and CI runs the local check (#592).
- Added `bin/ade-progress` (slice 1 of `docs/persistent-progress-reminder-design.md`, 2026-11-12): a zero-token reminder for agents that went quiet with declared open work. `declare` / `retire` (key `{paseo_home_realpath, agent_id}`, generations, stale generation rejected), `scan` (`due`, `idle_with_work`, `stopped_with_open_work`; re-inspect before any wake; a plain `paseo send` only to an idle agent, never to a closed, errored, archived or permission-pending one; one aggregated `hermes-ping` per pass, three per hour; a Progress-panel item with `no-run` degradation), `inbox` and `ack`, with a locked atomic local inbox, a 3-hour cooldown, crash reconciliation and `--dry-run` by default; offline tests with fake `paseo`, `hermes-ping` and `paseo-progress`, and an uninstalled `config/launchd/com.djbclark.ade-progress.plist` template (no `ade-sched` row, nothing loaded) (#599)
- Add advisory per-pool conservation and expiring-surplus JSON forecasts with
  per-pool freshness, conservative Paseo caps and fixture coverage. (#579)
- Added `route_agent.py adjust`, a launch-time pool adjustment with restoration (W8, 2026-11-12): a kind's last
  routed pool that falls below its floor is held off and new launches go to the next eligible pool, and it returns
  after recovering `restore_margin` points above its floor and a minimum dwell (hysteresis), with one change-log line
  per switch, restore, expiry or refusal. A `frontier` slice that would be downgraded is refused (exit 3), omp is never
  a target, and running agents are never touched. Config is `[routing.adjust]`; `plan` gained a soft `kind_exclude`. (#598)
- Add per-pool advisory quota levels, batch reminders, and expiring operator spending profiles visible in the router show command; routing and gates remain unchanged. (#587)
- `docs/ci-runners.md`: the rule that CI jobs over 10 minutes run on WarpBuild runners, the label mapping, how to switch a repository, the MCP install line and the finding that personal GitHub accounts are not supported. (#655)
- `/helm lead` and its alias `/watch lead` (2026-11-02): added helm's lead mode (`skills/helm/lead.md`). A lead session surveyed every running Paseo and herdr agent without spending their turns, briefed each one on what it needed to continue or to be safe to quit, answered only what written rules and probes settled, batched the operator's decisions into one picklist prompt, relayed each pick back, and stopped nothing in flight; agents asked the lead through `relay.py` instead of raising cards. (#258)
- Web search first and last (operator, 2026-11-13; former queue Carried 34): `docs/dispatch-footer.md` rule 24 and the `skills/fan-out` brief section require a `Prior art / docs checked:` line in every non-mechanical brief (what was searched, with which tool, what was found or `none`; `exempt: mechanical` for renames, date bumps and typo fixes, or `exempt: <reason>` when no search could change the answer) and a `Closing search:` report field above the final `DONE` line (a search run last against what was built: current docs, changed APIs, upstream fixes, prices and model ids); tools in order: shared `mcp__firecrawl__*`, Context7, `WebFetch`. `docs/code-review.md` rule 12 makes a non-exempt PR body without the line a LOW finding (AGENTS.md now says rules 1–12) (#634).
- `bin/ade-worker-run`: launches a lead's worker into the lead's one `workers <middle> <lead id>` window (strips
  `PASEO_AGENT_ID`/`PASEO_AGENT_CWD`/`PASEO_WORKSPACE_ID`, finds the window by exact name and refuses two matches, mints and
  renames it on the first launch reading its id from the launch and failing closed otherwise, adds `--label ade.lead=` and
  `--label slice=`, renames the tab `worker <NAME> <lead id>`, prints `{agentId, workspaceId, windowName, tabName}`), and
  `ade-worker-run archive-window` archives the window only when no live labelled or other agent remains; `skills/fan-out` and
  `docs/workspace-layout.md` use it instead of the five hand-run steps, and `tests/test_ade_worker_run.py` covers it
  against a stub `paseo`. (#629)
- `bin/ade_ws_names.py`: the importable window-name builder and parser (`build`, `parse`, `parse_legacy`, `migrated_name`,
  `repo_of`) that the tidy skill reuses. (#643)
- `ade-worker-run --placement main` (alias `--interactive`): launches a worker the operator asked for into the lead's own MAIN
  window, resolved from the lead's agent record; `ade-worker-run rename-main` names that window `MAIN <repo> (<work>)`;
  `archive-window` accepts current and legacy names, never archives MAIN or USER, and ignores a MAIN-placed worker. (#643)
- `ade-worker-run` appends `Also follow every item of <repo root>/docs/dispatch-footer.md.` to the brief it launches
  (`--no-footer` to skip), and `docs/dispatch-footer.md` item 25 requires `python3 bin/changelog-fold --repo . --check` before a
  changelog fragment is committed or pushed (a worker's fragment with no `###` heading turned #637 CI red). (#643)
- `plugins/ade-yolo/README.md` canary and rollback procedure; `tests/test_ade_yolo_plugin.py` runs the plugin's offline node suite under pytest (#605)
- The Claude session-start hook (`claude/hooks/cswap_quota_context.sh`) also ran `~/.config/zsh/selftest.sh` (the zsh port of the bash shell-config selftest, installed 2026-11-02 when zsh became the login shell) when a zsh config file changed since its last passing run, with its own mtime stamp; skipped where the zsh port is absent (#250)
- `relay.py --message-file PATH|-` reads the message from a UTF-8 file or stdin instead of the positional argument (exactly one of the two; an empty file or both given is exit 2; a leading BOM and one trailing newline dropped), so a message with backticks, `$( )` or quotes never passes through a shell-quoted string (the text is still folded to one line like any relay text: every whitespace run, newlines and indentation included, becomes one space); `relay.py --ack PEER --message-file -` is a send to PEER, not the `--ack ID` verb; the usage text and both `session-finder` and `peer-relay` SKILL.md say so first (a lead's message lost text to a double-quoted backtick on 2026-11-01) (#211).
- `session-history.py --status` (read-only index lag per agent), `--install`/`--uninstall` (a 10-minute launchd refresh of the history index, primary checkout only), `SESSION_INDEX_DIR`; history adapters for omp, grok, goose and crush, so their 2,300 sessions are searchable; history hits of agents without an exact `live()` print `LIVE` (fleet knows the native id) or `LIVE?` (a running fleet row in the same directory and a touch in the last 10 minutes) instead of always `ended`; a title-only match is a hit; the crush adapter also indexes every registered project's `.crush/crush.db` (project path as cwd, resume command with its `-D` data dir) (#191).
- `skills/paseo-screen` and `/paseo-screen` (2026-10-31, djbclark's ruling): the one screen-control tool, the Paseo plugin `paseo-screen` (`paseo plugin add kevguy/screen`; viewer "Screen · mac"; RPCs `screen.status`, `screen.frame`, `screen.input`), with the 2026-10-30 verification (live desktop at about 10 fps, a keyboard chord delivered; clicks, drags, clipboard and multiple displays not tested), the focus-without-clicking driving method and the safety rules; it replaces the retired Orca `orca computer` / `computer-use` route and stays enabled when idle; `docs/apply-toolchain-prompt.md` (5.12) installs both Paseo plugins, `paseo-screen` (`paseo plugin add kevguy/screen`, no version pin, macOS only, Screen Recording and Accessibility granted once, enable only after asking djbclark) and `questions-please` (`paseo plugin install github:djbclark/paseo-plugin-questions-please`, plugin id `questions-please`, v0.1.2: Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+. jumps to the next agent waiting for input, confirmed by djbclark in the macOS desktop app and a browser; it replaced Cmd+Shift+U, which did not work in the desktop app) (#180).
- `relay.py --status ID` (alias `--show`) says what became of one deferred record without changing anything (state, target, age, the deliverer's last look with the pending card by id and tool, the `deferred.log` events, any expectation sharing the id; exit 0 found, 1 not); `--pending` gains `--queued-only`, `--older-than S` and `--summary` (plain `--pending` is unchanged); `--sweep` prints `STUCK 14m ID -> [target] what holds it [why: ...]` for every non-final record older than `--stuck-after S` (default 300), the hook for the five-minute rule; the `queued:` line and `--json` name a blocking card by id and tool; `deferred.log` is capped at 1 MiB (then `.1`, `.2`) and one corrupt record no longer stops the week-old prune. `--cancel` is documented as destructive, never a state check (#177).
- `relay.py --ack ID` alone answers an expectation: it sends `ack ID` to the expectation's sender by the ordinary send (open expectation, caller is its target; else refused naming `relay.py <sender> "ack ID"`); an `ack ID` that begins ANY line of a relay-verified send from the target to the sender now closes the expectation, so a queued reply that ends with the token closes it when delivered (for a sender with no transcript: a mid-line quote or a third party closes nothing; a transcript-backed sender's sweep still accepts the token anywhere in a line carrying the target's relay header); `--status` refuses any option but `--json` (exit 2, nothing runs); `--ack ID` carries `--wait/--json/--no-defer/--no-ack/--ack/--steer/--via/--defer-for/--idle` and refuses every other option (exit 2); an open expectation whose transcript cannot be read reads `uncollected`; a plain `--pending` runs a bounded ack sweep first and marks rows it could not collect `open (uncollected, last swept WHEN)`; the `queued:` line of a queued ack says it closes only when delivered (or on `--sweep`) (#177).
- `README.md` and `docs/skills.md` rows for `skills/barnowl` updated (2026-10-31) to the sandboxed-reads design of #197: `bo log`/`screen`, the always-on human confirmation, and the restricted `pastebin-bo` key (they still described the pre-#197 `capture`/`run` skill).
- `skills/barnowl` sandboxed reads (2026-10-31), the answer to the open security finding on #193 (Zephyr text reaches yolo agents). `scripts/bo-log.py` (stdlib Python) is the only read path: `bo log` reads `~/zlog` (validated names, `O_NOFOLLOW`, regular files only, fields before bodies, literal `--grep`, linear-time header parsing, a byte-offset `--new/--mark` cursor that pages forward and cannot be moved by a forged header, `history_cut` plus exit 5 when the log is bigger than the 8 MiB window) and `bo screen`/`run`/`perl`/`locate`/`file` output is framed the same way: printable-ASCII JSON lines, NFKC plus removal of controls, format characters, the Unicode tag block, private use, variation selectors, caps on every dimension, an `untrusted` preface, errors as fixed reason codes, the external command's exit status kept. `bo perl` and every `bo run` that is not `show|help|getvar|getsubs|getview` ALWAYS ask the human at the live client first with `BarnOwl::start_question` (injection also arrives through web pages, PRs and files, so there is no "only after a Zephyr read" taint), showing the whole command (refused, never clipped, when it does not fit one line at the pane width minus 25, has a non-ASCII character, or has `{ } \`; measured on BarnOwl 1.10: a longer prompt shows only its last line), only when BarnOwl is the active window of an attached tmux client (the pane is pinned by id and must run `barnowl`; width and visibility are checked again when the answer arrives), and type nothing without a `y` (exit 4). `scripts/bo-ssh` is the forced command for a restricted key (`restrict,command=...`): only `bo log|screen|capture|subs|file|run|perl|locate|help`. The confirmation is a boundary only for an agent that holds that key and not the operator's main one (SKILL.md says so). Designed from a codex `gpt-6-astra` and a Claude Fable frontier consult, reviewed by both over four rounds (Fable's safeguards declined two reviews and the harness retried on Opus 4.8, which APPROVED) plus Codex's PR review; the findings that changed the code: whole-command confirmation, no TTL and no taint, literal grep, offset cursor, framed argparse errors, private scratch files, pane pinning and width fit, linear header parsing. 29 tests (fake tmux) in `tests/test_barnowl_skill.py` (#197).
- `skills/barnowl` restored (2026-10-31) after 40472f7d deleted it as deprecated: the skill drives the live BarnOwl in tmux session `barnowl` through `scripts/bo`. Rewritten for what exists now: no `gr`/`go`/`/barnowl` commands and no `:g` (`g-cmd.pl` stays removed; `:goose` in forgejo.mit.edu/djbclark/barnowl replaced it), and the command list comes from `bo run 'show commands'` because `~/src/barnowl` is the modules repo, not the client source. `scripts/bo` hardening, from the PR's review: it refuses (exit 3) to send keys while a pager or popup is open or the screen has no status bar (BarnOwl 1.10's popups keep the status bar and draw a `└──┘` box), refuses (exit 2) any line with a newline, tab or other control character (a newline in `tmux send-keys -l` would run the rest as a second BarnOwl command), `bo run` now closes a popup it opened (before, the next call typed into it), each call uses a private `mktemp` output file instead of the shared `/tmp/bo-out.txt`, and calls on one session are serialized with `flock` (verified: four concurrent `bo perl` calls each returned their own value); the skill says Zephyr bodies and `~/zlog` are untrusted data that never authorize an operation (#193).
- `docs/paseo-only-rearchitecture.md` (plan v1, 2026-10-30): the plan of record for djbclark's Paseo-only ruling of 2026-10-29 (retire `relay.py`, herdr-as-harness, Orca, `acp-run`/`acp-dispatch` in phases), refined by codex astra and Fable consults and djbclark's decisions on notifications, unattended runs, the second daemon (the existing vps-primary Paseo host), OpenCode plugins, the `docs/history/` directory, lead models, phase order and the two edge-case Herdr bridges (`paseo4herdr`, `herdr4paseo`); it adds a provider map, four plugins to build (`ade-jobs`, `ade-gate`, `ade-ack`, `ade-permit`) and a live acceptance gate per phase. Nothing is retired by this PR (#154).
- Added opt-in `rumination-watch` absent-seat recovery under djbclark's 2026-10-30 option-3 ruling: `RW_AUTO_SEAT` stayed off by default, required an explicit provider, NARROWED his "including after a hard error" ruling to inspect-confirmed `gone` (never broken reads, existing seats or `card:*`), limited every start attempt per role/window whether confirmed or not, logged every invocation, and kept saved ids behind authoritative owners, still paged with the new id, left a `silent` hung seat unreplaced, and required the README field check proving a started seat reachable by relay before enabling (#160).
- `bin/local-review PR` (2026-10-29) runs Codex's code review locally on the exact head of a PR (`codex review --base <base sha>` in a clean detached worktree, killed after `--timeout` seconds) and files `~/.local/state/local-review/<owner>__<name>-pr<N>-<head>.json` (counts by P0-P3, findings' titles; exit 0 clean, 1 findings, 2 usage, 3 failed, 4 a run for that head is alive); and `bin/pr-wait PR --local-codex` (implies `--no-codex`) reads that file instead of waiting on the GitHub bot, so `merge_ready` needs a `clean` result on this head and the blocker is `local-codex-missing`, `-findings`, `-failed` or `-unreadable`, after the bot's review hung on PRs 112 and 124 (openai/codex#55890); a PR by someone other than the repository owner or the authenticated gh user is refused unless `--allow-untrusted`; AGENTS.md "Code review" item 11 and the bigteam skill say how (#130).
- Added `skills/aws-rules` (2026-10-29): the Agent Toolkit for AWS rules (`aws-agent-rules.md`) as an on-demand skill instead of an `AGENTS.md` append, with the local setup facts (profile `aws-agent`, Region `us-east-1`, `AWS_MCP_PROXY_PROFILES` on every `aws-mcp` entry) (#138).
- `relay.py --retire ID --reason "..." [--evidence "..."]` closes an expectation that will never be answered (both ends gone, answered out of band, superseded by a re-send) as state `retired`, keeping the reason, the evidence and the retirer IN the record instead of deleting it or bare-cancelling it, so `--pending` shows why instead of OVERDUE forever. Only an `open` expectation retires (`acked` is settled, `reserved` is a live send: exit 1); a blank reason is a usage error (exit 2) even for an unknown id; re-retiring is a no-op exit 0 that keeps the first reason; the expectation's own TARGET may not retire it (it owes the ack, and retiring would silence fleet-watch's page) but answers `ack ID`; the retirer is the fleet `self` row, else a sanitized declared label, else `?`; reason and evidence are one line of at most 500 characters from the start; a reused id takes the retirement back, so a re-send takes a new one (#133).
- `skills/oc-go-key` (2026-10-29): the OpenCode Go quota wall is not a crash. `scripts/go-status` prints every server, every credential label with the active one starred, the failures in the log window classified as `go-limit` / `no-funds` / `go-429` / `region` / `auth` (`go-429` is the UI-side error name, with no live occurrences observed on 2026-10-30), and rc 2 when one is recent; the skill says which credential actually serves `opencode-go/*` (the Console integration `opencode`'s active one, **not** the `opencode-go` key — a day went into that on 2026-10-29), how to swap it for running sessions with `oc-go-key use <label>`, and the step usually skipped: a reload changes the key, **not the prompt**, so an aborted turn is resumed by `relay.py` into the same session rather than by opening a new tab, with the resume VERIFIED rather than assumed: relay's exit code is the verdict (`0` delivered, `1` not delivered, `3` unverified — do not resend, read the seat with `relay.py --why`, `silent` or `card:` means a human is needed), and first contact can be closed by the session's own `ack` through `--expect-ack` (#132).
- goose joins the per-TUI skill set: `bin/skill-everywhere` gained a `goose` target (its global skills dir, `~/.agents/skills`, shared with Codex), so goose discovers the shared djbclark-ade skills.
- `relay.py --why IDENT [--json]` (exit 0 = can take a text now, 1 = something is in the way) says why a session cannot be sent text yet, from two methods compared: a Paseo agent by `paseo agent inspect` (pending cards by tool: question / permission / plan), `paseo permit ls` (the card's own text, shown by `--why` only and stripped of control characters) and `paseo logs` (its last activity), a pane TUI by the multiplexer status and a classification of the bottom rows of its screen (picker, permission prompt, open slash menu, turn running); states idle, working, silent (running, nothing new for 15 min), card:question|permission|plan|picker|unknown, archived, gone, error, refused (a hard stop such as the caller's own session), and a disagreement between the methods is reported with the blocking reading winning. Card refusals and queue lines name the kind (`a QUESTION (AskUserQuestion)`), the deliverer files its latest reason (kind and tool, never the card's text) on the record and in `deferred.log`, `relay.py --pending` shows it as `[why: ...]` (`look` in `--json`), and an expired record's outcome carries it (#124).
- `relay.py` now uses a second witness, the Paseo agent's own timeline (`paseo logs ID --tail 80`), alongside its Claude transcript to prove a delivery to a Paseo target (either proves), so a codex, omp, opencode or cline Paseo agent is "delivered" instead of "unverified": only a received-message entry (`[Message received]` or `[User]`) counts (never the agent's reasoning or output), the text must not already be in the timeline before the send, must be at least 10 characters, is polled 6 s at most, and a caller that wants no proof (`--no-ack`, a launchd job) gets the instant "accepted" it always had; and `relay.py --sweep` reads a transcript-less sender's own timeline for `ack ID` and the first-contact `ack <id>` (the entry's own sender title or a leading relay header must name the target; an ack already there when the question was sent is ignored via `tl_base`), so expectations of omp and codex senders close; expectation and link records now carry `sender_paseo` and `tl_base` (#124).
- `bin/watch up` (2026-10-28) archives each retired `x watch <domain> <n>` agent once it is idle, right after the replacement registers and on every later `up`; one with a turn in flight is kept until idle; any other idle agent titled `x ...` is archived the same way; a holder replaced by `up --force` is renamed `x ...` but never archived (#100).
- `bin/paseo-new-seat --probe` (2026-10-28) waits for a throwaway seat's turn to end and archives it, so a live test no longer leaves a "zz probe" tab in the shared `watch` workspace; exit 5 when the seat was created but could not be archived (#100).
- `bin/pytest` (2026-10-28) runs the suite with the dependencies CI installs (`uv run --with pytest --with pyyaml --with agent-client-protocol`); the bare `python3` has no pytest; use `~/ops/site-private/bin/bg bin/pytest -q tests/test_x.py`; `tests/test_bin_pytest.py` keeps it in step with `.github/workflows/test.yml` (#100).
- Added an OpenCode V2 Cargo permission gate that required compile/test commands, including release builds, to use the full bg path and preserved native permission decisions (#113).
- Automatic handoff trigger (2026-10-29): the native Go context guard now escalates an over-long OpenCode Go session into a mandatory handoff directive at a task boundary. `config/go-model-policy.json` gains `routine_context.handoff` (112000) / `advisory_context.handoff` (80000), `handoff_after_compactions` (2), `handoff_cooldown_seconds` (3600) and `handoff_max_wakes` (3). On `session.idle` (handled before the `created` staleness guard, since it carries none) the guard wakes the model with `ctx.session.prompt({ prompt: { text }, delivery: "steer" })` when the measured input context meets the handoff ceiling or the compaction budget is exhausted, escalating the wording across retries, and `onContext` surfaces a handoff-level notice into the next request's system messages. The policy is validated once at load and fails closed on a bad cooldown (below a 300 s floor), `handoff_after_compactions`, `handoff_max_wakes` or either `handoff` ceiling, as does a malformed or unreadable persisted record; a wake is capped at `handoff_max_wakes` (3) and re-checked against the cap and cooldown after the awaited live read, refused for child (subagent) sessions and live-model mismatches (re-checked with `ctx.session.get`, which also caches `state.child`), and never sent in a dispatched slice (`ACP_RUN_AGENT=1`). A child or dispatched session also gets only a softened, warn-only context notice — "finish and report back to your parent" with no "start a fresh session" or lifecycle command — and is never steered toward `/handoff` or `/new`; before the first idle confirms a primary the notice is neutral, and only a confirmed primary re-surfaces the full directive. A far-future persisted `handoffAt` is clamped per record rather than rejecting the whole store. Handoff outranks `blocked` in the context notice because an ineffective-compaction session is the one that must hand off rather than loop compactions. The directive routes through the handoff skill and `self-slash "/new"`, never a raw `/new`/`/clear`/`/reset` or `--finish --successor`, and always carries "Never clear a parent session" (#123).
- `relay.py` now queues a message for a target blocked on a permission or question card (a Paseo agent with a pending permission, a pane TUI showing a dialog) instead of refusing it, and the deliverer waits for the card with capped exponential backoff and full jitter (5 s ceiling doubling to 2 min, floor one 5 s poll, reset by a look that finds no card) rather than a flat 5 s poll; text is never typed into the card, a record still blocked at expiry is `expired` with "blocked on a permission or question card", and `--no-defer`, `--plan`, `--idle` and another `--via` keep the plain refusal (#112).
- `self-slash` waiter on herdr: when the last 12 rows show no prompt it also reads the last `SELF_SLASH_READ_LINES` (default 60) for a Claude Code or Codex pane that herdr calls idle or done, and takes a Claude marker row there only at the line start inside its input box (`───` rules above and below); a stale `❯` row, an answer option, a blocked pane or another TUI gets nothing typed (exit 5). While a pane with no prompt is busy (herdr status `working`, or for another TUI a spinner in the last 12 rows) it waits up to `SELF_SLASH_WAIT` of wall-clock time instead of giving up after ~45 s; spinner text in the scrollback of an idle Claude or Codex pane no longer holds it. `--finish --successor claude/MODEL --mode X` asks the installed claude CLI whether X is a permission mode (`claude --permission-mode X --version`; `SELF_SLASH_CLAUDE_BIN`, `SELF_SLASH_PROBE_TIMEOUT`) and exits 2 before anything starts when it is not; without a probe it uses a static list (plan, default, acceptEdits, auto, bypassPermissions, manual, dontAsk) (2026-10-23/24 failures) (#110).
- Added `bin/context-watch` (2026-10-28), a read-only per-agent context readout: for each running Claude Code agent (`paseo ls -g --background --json`, else transcripts modified in the last 30 minutes) it prints `<id8> <title> 214k/267k (80%) wall in 53k`, fullest first, from the newest non-sidechain usage block (the nudge hook's `_last_context_tokens`, imported) against each agent's own compaction ceiling (the smaller of its `autoCompactWindow` and its model's window, minus the 33k buffer); `--json`, `--min-pct`, `--source`, `--recent-minutes`; an unreadable size prints `?`; exit 0; not wired into fleet-watch or settings.json; `docs/context-hygiene.md` section 6 (#107).
- Added a private rotating context-nudge log with absolute-path overrides and symlink-safe opens where supported, event-aware rate-limited PostToolUse support, bounded symlink-safe state locking with atomic saves and bounded day-old orphan cleanup, and a 64-candidate tail scan without duplicate counting; left PostToolUse unregistered pending operator approval (#117).
- `relay.py --sweep` and `--pending --json` (2026-10-28): an `--expect-ack` still open 6 hours past its due time printed `ASSUMED LOST` and carried `assumed_lost`; `skills/peer-relay/SKILL.md` rule 13 and `skills/session-finder/SKILL.md` told the sender to re-route its own work once without asking, the row stayed open, and a negative hand-edited `within` read as the default (#106).
- Added `relay.py --transfer-expectations OLD_SENDER [--id TOKEN ...]` to settle unswept old-transcript acks before adopting a finished sender's open ack expectations into the successor identity and transcript, preserving their status and age; reported skipped tokens and exited 1 with `missing` for requested tokens not transferred (#101).
- `self-slash --finish --successor` on Paseo reads its own workspace id from the agent state file (`~/.paseo/agents/*/<id>.json`, `PASEO_HOME` honoured) when several workspaces share its cwd (2026-10-28: three shared `~/src/djbclark-ade`, the successor was refused and no tab opened); the id only places the successor and the shared workspace is never archived. `skills/handoff` Step 9 now says to verify a successor started (log line, `paseo agent ls`) before reporting it, and how to hand a slice to a new tab while keeping the current session (`paseo-new-seat --workspace ... --prompt "/baton CHAIN"`, no autorename) (#103).
- The frontier Agent-tool hook entry in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` now sets `"timeout": 15` and `"onFailure": "block"` (2026-10-27), so a stalled, timed-out or unstartable `frontier_agent_gate.py` blocks the Agent/Task call instead of letting it through (#96).
- Away frontier allowance (2026-10-27): a third `[frontier]` gate in `config/gates.toml` (`frontier1_judged`, `frontier2_judged`, `frontier1_per_wave`, `frontier2_per_wave`, `frontier1_agent_calls_per_day`, `frontier1_min_left`, `frontier2_min_left`, floors, baseline 0 = today's rule) and an `away` profile table that lets an agent use a frontier model where it judges it significantly better than judgment, with a one-line reason: `acp-dispatch --kind frontier --frontier-reason TEXT` (10-200 chars; recorded with the grant in the dispatch record), a `frontier-reason:` line in an Agent-tool prompt for `claude/hooks/frontier_agent_gate.py` (guard state, cap floor and a per-New-York-day call cap checked, double-counted in `agent-calls-day.json`; every failure asks), away rows in the quota guard's `watch` advice and `quota_nudge.py`'s daily line, `reasons` and `frontier_allowance` in `quota-guard.json`; `frontier_per_wave` and `fable_min_left` moved from `config/routing.toml` to the gate; `docs/gates.md` "Frontier allowance", `skills/model-routing/SKILL.md` item 1e (#95).
- AGENTS.md rule 1, `claude/agents/adversary.md` and the bigteam adversary brief now describe the
  verdict marker as `bin/pr-wait` enforces it (2026-10-27): a BLOCK counts by the full sha **or a 7+
  hex prefix** of the head, with or without a trailing `…`/`...`, while the `verdict` value must be
  exactly `APPROVE` or `BLOCK` in capitals and an APPROVE carries the **full 40-hex** head (an APPROVE
  with any shortened sha, or a BLOCK whose shortened head is not a prefix of this head, is `malformed`;
  a full sha of another commit is other-head and report-only); the marker's JSON is delimited by its
  own braces, so a `note` may quote the format and a marker whose JSON is partly inside code is
  `malformed` rather than approved; an APPROVE inside a fenced block or a single-line inline span — the
  only shapes `pr-wait` blanks — or with the template placeholder is documentation; `malformed` and
  `adversary-fetch-failed` always block, and the opt-in is named (`--adversary-required`, or an
  `adversary-required` label). The adversary now emits the marker as a raw line for the lead to paste,
  since it never edits the PR itself (PR 67).
- `bin/paseo-bypass-watch` (2026-10-29): a no-model watcher (`--once` default, `--loop SECONDS` with a 1 s floor, `--dry-run`, `--state`, `--max-age`, `--json`) that sets a Paseo Claude agent created in the last 120 s to `bypassPermissions` once, but only when its mode is exactly `default` or `auto` (home rule 16: Paseo has no default-mode setting or create-time hook, so a UI-opened or hand-typed `paseo agent run` seat starts in `default` and prompts); the flip lands roughly 10-25 s after creation under `--loop 10` and does not answer a prompt already pending, so `--loop 10` or longer is advised. Every pass reads `paseo agent ls --json` first and then `paseo agent ls --label perm=ask --json` (the CLI prints no labels; in that order so a seat created between the two calls is never missing from the exemption set; never cached), exempts the agents the second returns, and aborts the whole pass if either call fails; both use the default `active` `ls` scope, so a seat outside an active project or in a background workspace is never listed and never flipped; it takes a per-pass `flock` on `<state>.lock`, never touches non-claude, archived, closed or agents in any other mode (plan, acceptEdits, bypass, unknown; `auto`, the classifier mode this build's claude provider advertises as its default and the UI may preselect, counts as a mode that can prompt, and an `auto` seat gets the same ~3 s window with no compare-and-set), never re-touches a handled agent (a later `plan` sticks if picked after the watcher has acted or more than about one CLI start-up, ~3 s, before; there is no compare-and-set, so a `plan` picked inside that window is overwritten and the seat recorded `set`; retries re-check the mode), retries a failed or unverified set 5 times, and keeps its handled ids fsynced and atomically in `~/.local/state/paseo-bypass-watch.json`. Not installed anywhere; a launchd job is the operator's decision (#121).
- `config/gates.toml`, `bin/ade_gates.py` and `bin/gate-profile` (2026-10-26): the Claude quota gates' thresholds (`claude-fleet-governor`, `claude-quota-guard`) now load from a versioned `gates.toml` baseline (code `THRESHOLDS` stay the fail-safe), and `gate-profile set away --for 72h --reason TEXT` applies a temporary, expiring, floor-checked loosening (ignored entirely when expired, corrupt, over `max_profile_hours` or outside its floors; it can also cap the aiuse `conserve` alert at `watch` through `conserve_alert_level`) with an audit log, one ping at set/clear/expiry, `profile` in both state files and an immediate recompute; `docs/gates.md` (#84).
- Gate profile reminders (2026-10-26): while a profile is active the first `claude-fleet-governor run` of each America/New_York day sends one `hermes-ping` ("away profile active until ..., N days left; restore normal routing now: gate-profile clear"), `gate-profile extend --for 24h` moves `until` within `max_profile_hours`, `gate-profile status` shows the next nudge, and `quota_nudge.py` prints one `[gates]` line per session per day while the guard state names an unexpired profile (#84).
- Gate profile hardening (2026-10-26, adversary round 1): `gate-profile set`, `extend` and `clear` now take the state lock around their read and write (waiting at most 120 s, then refusing with exit 1 and a message; the governor and guard skip a notice after 10 s and retry it next run), so a failed expiry or daily ping can no longer bring a cleared profile back or overwrite a new one (its restore is compare-and-restore); a `gates.toml` baseline looser than the code default in a key's floor direction is replaced by the code default at run time and logged; a huge integer cannot crash a gate; the profile file must be a regular file of at most 64 KiB (no symlink, FIFO or directory); `schema` must be the integer 1 and the profile name ASCII `[a-z0-9_-]` at run time and in the hook; `--by`/`--reason` are bounded; the governor's tight triggers must stay at or above `critical_left`; `clear` warns when the guard recompute fails The guard now recomputes before the governor runs after every `set`, `extend` and `clear`, so the governor never acts on the replaced thresholds (Codex review) (#84).
- `claude/hooks/relay_prose_nudge.py` (2026-10-25), a Claude Code Stop hook registered in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl`: when a turn began with a message from a relay sender (the `[relay from KIND ID ...]` header, or on the socket route, which writes none, the `from-name` label of relay's `<cross-session-message>` envelope, read from both real transcript forms of a socket frame: a busy receiver's `queued_command` attachment holding the bare envelope, and an idle receiver's `isMeta` peer user entry with Claude Code's prefix before and suffix after the envelope, the second [INFERENCE] for relay since no relay frame to an idle session was observed; a deferred send's `[queued Nm ago]` after it is skipped) that asks for an answer (it ends with relay's own `[reply through relay.py ...]` or first-contact footer, or has a `?` outside a URL unless relay enforced footers on that sender, which only a herdr pane id with a `(DIR)` suffix shows) and made no `relay.py` call (and no `SendMessage` to that sender), it blocks the stop once with the reminder to answer through `relay.py <ID>`, or to end the turn if no answer is needed; silent for statements, `ack <ID>` texts with no `?` (also with an answer after the ID, so acks and acks of acks never provoke messages), `no reply needed`, `no need to reply`, `no response needed` and `FYI` texts, a `--fyi` question from a herdr pane with a `(DIR)`, a header with no usable ID, a repeated Stop and any parse error; known limit (deferred): an `ack ...`, `no reply needed ...` or `FYI ...` text that goes on to ask for an action without a `?` is read as an exemption, and an `FYI` text is silent even with a `?` (#75).
- `bin/paseo-new-seat --mode ID` (2026-10-26): a claude seat now starts in `bypassPermissions` unless `--mode` says otherwise (it set only the `auto_accept` feature, which leaves a claude seat in `default`, so a new seat could prompt); other providers keep their own default. (#83)
- `acp-run kiro` (2026-10-25): `kiro-cli acp`, started with `--trust-all-tools` under `--perm all`, `--perm deny` and `--perm read-only` refused (Kiro runs workspace reads and edits without asking), a 120 s handshake limit for its first-run model download; `[discovery].never_route` keeps `route_agent.py` from handing it slices. `~/.bashrc` has the matching `kiro-cli()` YOLO function (#76).
- `claude/hooks/prior_art_nudge.py` (2026-10-26): a shadow-mode PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure and UserPromptSubmit hook that records one JSON line per tool event (category: own code, external tree, memory, web, other; output bytes and a token estimate; external-read runs, duplicate and retry calls, shell quoting failures; task changes; `schema` and `classifier_version`) to `~/.claude/state/prior-art-shadow/<session id>.jsonl` so the web-prior-art nudge (research decision C1) can be judged on numbers; Bash is parsed (pipe filters, redirects, heredoc bodies and comments ignored, `cd`, `sudo`, `bash -c` followed, `cd` inside `( )` scoped; a stdin reader such as `cat` or `wc` with no path is not a read unless it has `< file`; same-command literal assignments and for-loop lists are resolved and a path that still needs an unknown variable is `private_other` with `path_class: unresolved`, never the cwd (`classifier_version` 5); a command substitution inside double quotes is not scanned (`echo "$(find /nix/store)"` is a miss; a heredoc inside `"$( )"` is text of the outer command); `gh api repos/<other owner>/...` is web only for code, docs and releases paths and a variable owner is never web), MCP web and memory servers are matched by name, and subagents keep their own carry-over state; it prints nothing and blocks nothing, never stores a command, path, query, URL, prompt or output (a URL is bucketed as `host_class` plus an allowlisted registrable domain, a read path as a fixed `path_class`; `event`, tool and model only from fixed lists: an MCP tool is its server alone, a model its family, Claude tier and up to three version numbers), opens its files `O_NOFOLLOW`, prunes only its own file names older than 30 days (a session's lock is touched on each event), bounds its lock wait to 0.5 s of wall time and counts the events lost to a held lock, and `PRIOR_ART_SHADOW=0` disables it; wired in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` (#85).
- `bin/pr-wait` reads the **adversary verdict from a machine-readable marker** in the PR body, because the house rule that a teammate's PR carries an adversary verdict and the head it reviewed had no enforcement (PR 61 merged with no quote and nothing noticed): `<!-- adversary-verdict:v1 {"verdict":"APPROVE"|"BLOCK","head":"<40-hex>"} -->`. The `:v1` suffix is part of the match, the way AGENTS.md rule 9 reads Codex's `codex-review-state:v1`. A missing or unknown `verdict` fails closed; `head` must be 40 hex **and** equal the PR head (another head is not a pass); a malformed marker is its own outcome so a typo reads as a typo; and the rule is **asymmetric**: an APPROVE must be live, while a BLOCK naming this head — full 40-hex, or a 7+ hex prefix of it with or without a trailing `…`/`...` — is read from the **raw body**, so no code fence or strip bug can hide it (adversary review of 4a56c01, B1; the raw-body rescue used to be full-sha only, and four ordinary Markdown shapes made `_strip_code` over-strip and hide a shortened-sha BLOCK). The summary gains `adversary` (whose own keys include `conflict`), `adversary_warnings` and `adversary_required`. It **reports by default and blocks only when opted in** (`--adversary-required`, or an `adversary-required` label), because `merge_ready = not blockers` and a global default would block every PR in the house. **Prose verdicts are deliberately NOT read** — the first version parsed English and **two** adversary rounds found false approvals and false misses, including a BLOCK silently deleted by a prose filter, so the parser was deleted rather than patched (a third round reviewed this marker design and found the shortened-sha hole above). **A malformed marker is never OK**, even beside a valid APPROVE, and it blocks as `adversary-malformed`; a duplicate JSON key, an odd-cased verdict, a near-miss spelling (`adversary-verdict`, `adversary_verdict`, a dropped `:`) and an unclosed `<!--` all count as malformed, and the detail names which mistake it was. **70 new test functions** (77 → 147 in the file; all 165 collected tests pass), including gate-level tests through `assess()` and end-to-end runs of `main()` against a fake `gh` (none of the new ones are parametrized). Fenced blocks and inline code spans are stripped before matching, CommonMark-faithfully (a fence opens only at indent ≤ 3, remembers its character and length, and closes only on the same character at ≥ that length; spans do not cross a line), and only an APPROVE may be skipped as documentation — the template's placeholder head (`<40-hex>`), never a BLOCK. The bundled re-review of 66/67 then closed five shapes in the raw-body/strip interaction, each pinned by its own test: the marker's BOUNDARY is JSON-aware (an unclosed start quoted in prose ran to the next `-->`, swallowed the BLOCK that followed and read as a live APPROVE; a tempered regex fixed that and then hid a BLOCK whose JSON `note` quoted `<!--`, so the boundary is now a string- and escape-aware scanner shared by the live path and the rescue, which also reads a marker the format itself quoted inside another marker's `note` as content rather than a second typo, with the tempered capture kept only as the fallback for JSON that does not balance), code spans are CommonMark's equal-length backtick runs (a double-backtick span holding one backtick is code, not a live marker), stripping only ever replaces characters with spaces and never deletes (so a typo cannot be "repaired" into different JSON) and never changes the length (so a marker whose JSON slice differs between the raw body and the stripped text is `malformed` rather than read as an APPROVE), a backtick in a backtick fence's info string makes it inline code and not a fence, a full 40-hex sha with a trailing `…`/`...` is a BLOCK and not a malformed head, a raw-body BLOCK now reports `malformed` from the live validation so the rescue cannot launder a typo beside it, and the rescue scans EVERY marker start rather than resuming after one it has accepted (a candidate was accepted on balanced braces before its JSON was validated, so a real same-head BLOCK nested inside a rejected outer candidate was skipped and the body read as a clean APPROVE — the two shapes the re-review of `eb90d20` found). Known limits (accepted): a mistyped BLOCK quoted inside code, whose head is not a prefix of this head, is documentation — the raw-body rescue only recognises a head that actually names this head; and three code shapes outside "fences and inline spans" still read as live markers (an inline span broken across a line, a 4-space indented code block, a fenced block inside a blockquote), which can only add an APPROVE, never hide a BLOCK (#66).
- `relay.py --ack` (2026-10-25): after a send that is only accepted or queued, watch the target for `--wait` more seconds and exit 0 delivered, 1 not delivered, 3 unverified (new code); without it the exit codes are unchanged, so fleet-watch, load-watch, session-load-watch and rumination-watch keep working (#74).
- `relay.py --expect-ack ID`, `--needs-reply`, `--fyi`, `--ack-within S` (2026-10-25): a message with a `?` outside a URL, or `--needs-reply`, is refused (exit 2) from an identifiable session without `--expect-ack`; the expectation is a record listed by `--pending` (`"kind": "expect"`, `overdue`) and resolved by `--sweep` when an `ack ID` reply reaches the sender's transcript; an overdue one prints `OVERDUE ack ID` for the operator (#74).
- First contact (2026-10-25): the first relay from a session to a peer appends a request to reply `ack <sender id>`; `relay.py` keeps `~/.local/state/relay/links.json` and `fleet.py list` shows `link: acked` or `link: ONE-WAY` per peer (#74).
- `relay.py --pending --json` expectation rows carry `created` (2026-10-26): the record's creation time as fractional epoch seconds (`0.0` when missing, non-numeric or non-finite; use it verbatim, never round), so a consumer keys an expectation by `ID@created` and a reused, closed ID is a new expectation (#82).
- `relay.py` header names the sender (2026-10-25): a relayed text starts `[relay from KIND ID (DIR)]` (`KIND ID aka NAME (DIR)` when the session has a name), e.g. `[relay from codex w2F:p45 (djbclark-ade-baseline-ci-fixtures-wt)]`, so the receiver can run `relay.py ID "..."` verbatim; `[relay from unknown]` is never sent: when nothing resolves relay refuses (exit 4) and lists what it checked (`PASEO_AGENT_ID`, `HERDR_PANE_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `HERMES_SESSION_ID`, `RELAY_SENDER_NAME`, ancestors, fleet `self` rows). A job's declared label (`[relay from load-watch]`) has no ID and cannot be replied to. The sender is read from the sending process, including the herdr pane and Paseo agent an ancestor runs in, because Codex's `shell_environment_policy inherit = "core"` strips both variables from the shell it runs `relay.py` in (#74).

- `bin/ade-git-guard` (PATH shim `libexec/agent-bin/git`; `install [DIR]`, `--real-git`) refuses with exit 77 destructive git by an agent (`reset --hard`, `stash`, `checkout <path>`, `clean`, `rm -f`, `commit -a`, `add -A/-u`, autostash, `rebase -x`, `worktree remove|move|prune` of a claimed workspace, the destination of `branch -m|-M|-c|-C`) in a workspace another claim owns or in an unclaimed checkout, also by abbreviated option and through aliases; in a workspace another agent claims (the cwd, or any `-C`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree`/`GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` value) only a read allowlist runs (`status`, `log`, `show`, `diff`, `rev-parse`, `ls-files`, `ls-tree`, `cat-file`, `blame`, `describe`, `rev-list`, `for-each-ref`, `grep`, `shortlog`, `name-rev`, `merge-base`, `branch --list`, `remote -v`, `config --get`, `worktree list`); it fails closed on an unknown leading option or subcommand, on command-line or environment config that sets `core.hooksPath` or a `hook.*`/`include.*`/`includeIf.*` key (also a `git config` write), and on `core.hooksPath` set to any value, an empty one too, in the command, global or system scope as `git config --null --show-scope --get-all core.hooksPath` answers under the caller's own environment (`HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, includes; no size cap on `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` or on a config file; when git does not answer, the call is refused as `hook-config-unknown`; a `git commit-own` call is refused as an external program, run `git-commit-own`), and on `push --no-verify` (any `--no-v` prefix) and `send-pack`; the owner's own workspace and a human shell are untouched; each refusal is logged to `~/.local/state/ade/guard.log`; `bin/ade_claims.py` holds the claim schema (`~/.local/state/ade/claims/<id>.json`, pointer `.git/ade-claim`; `new` exits 3 for an existing id, 4 for a path another owner holds) and its CLI; a guard crash fails open; `ACP_OPERATOR_APPROVED=git-guard` allows and logs one call; the shim parses under macOS `/bin/bash` 3.2; nothing wires it into a live session yet (isolation slices W2-W4); reference: `docs/git-guard.md` (#59).
- `libexec/git-hooks/reference-transaction` and `pre-push` (bash 3.2) refuse, by git's own hook and the ref git resolved, an agent's update of a branch another agent's active claim names (creation, update, deletion, rename source, however the argv spelled it: `fetch . main:heads/cb`, `branch -f`, `update-ref`, `push . main:cb`, `checkout -B`, ...), a `HEAD` or branch update while the hook runs in a workspace another agent claims, a `refs/stash` update in a linked worktree, and a push to a claimed branch; the `committed` state logs every change to a claimed branch to `guard.log`; the installed stubs run the checkout's hook as a child and pass on only its exit 77, so any crash of it, of python or of bash lets git proceed (2026-10-25); the claim match uses the URL `git config --get remote.origin.url` answers; `git pack-refs` and `gc` pass, decided by the parent's real executable and exact argv, never its `argv[0]`; `ade-git-guard install [DIR]` writes chain-preserving stubs into the common git dir (an existing hook becomes `NAME.pre-ade`; exit 78 while `core.hooksPath` is set to any value, an empty one included, or git does not say) and the shim installs them lazily on its slow path (#59).
- Added `judgment` to Codex's `plenty_kinds` (`gpt-6.1-sol --effort high`, rank 3 behind claude and omp), so `acp-dispatch codex --kind judgment` is accepted while every Codex window has at least `plenty_min` left; below it, with no fresh reading, or with any window unread (a null `remaining_percent` no longer hides behind a known 95% window), it is refused as before, and `acp-dispatch` judges that gate again on the reading taken after the load-watch wait (the detached runner too, finalizing `unavailable/headroom`, exit 5) and prints the reading's decimal (49.9% no longer reads "50% left"); the dispatch estimate now matches history on kind and effort too (rows without those fields still count) and `[dispatch.estimate_floor] "codex:judgment" = 25.0` holds until three comparable slices (the same kind and effort, both recorded; a request without `--effort` has none; each quota window needs its own three, else it is estimated at the floor) are measured, a rerouted slice estimates afresh when its kind, model or effort changes, and the detached runner re-checks the plenty gate right before the spawn too, each of those post-wait looks reading aiuse afresh instead of its 30-second memo (2026-10-25) (#73).
- Added Go guard installation and zero-inference verification to standard setup instructions (#68).

- Added the native Codex `create-hook` guide, verified against Codex 0.164.0, with exact hook trust review, conservative installation, approval semantics and coexistence with legacy notify (2026-10-25; direct integration).
- Added exact acp-dispatch record paths to `relay.py`, including headless workers outside the default state tree; preserved queued versus delivered status and named the acknowledgement files (2026-10-25; direct integration).
- `relay.py` recognizes the transcript created by a fresh Claude TUI on its first prompt, with exact text, session and timestamp checks. `fleet.py` recognizes the native Hermes `python -I -c` bootstrap from its anchored entry markers and excludes service, ACP and single-shot runs (2026-10-25; direct integration).
- Added a load-watch notice when one Google Chrome process uses 0.9+ cores for 5 minutes (`LW_CHROME_CORES`, `LW_CHROME_SUSTAIN_S`): warns the watcher hourly per process and never kills (2026-10-25).
- `helm.py show` offers a screen fingerprint for a local Herdr TUI that is idle, or is undetected with unknown status. After inspecting an empty composer with nothing pending, use `helm.py send <pane> --screen-fp FP "<text>"` to submit through the pane APIs. The same captured screen is displayed and hashed; failed or blank reads, changed screens and known busy or blocked states refuse. The fingerprint binds the pane and, where fleet knows it, the pid. Typing failures never press Enter or retry. Verify the submit on screen or by an acknowledgement. Duration-only changes are masked; the plain idle send retains `agent prompt` for Codex paste handling (2026-10-25; direct integration).
- Added `bin/herdr-server-run` and `config/launchd/dev.herdr.server.plist`: the herdr LaunchAgent wrapper that turns "server is already running" after a handoff into a clean exit, so launchd no longer loops (2026-10-25; not yet applied to the live plist).
- `watch` skill and `bin/watch` (`status`, `up [--dry-run] [--force] [--probe]`, `down`; table `config/watch.toml`): starts one watcher agent per domain (CFEngine PRs, Paseo contributions, load) in its own Paseo workspace, only when nobody live holds the role, with model, effort and context from `route_agent.py plan` and recent event load, per-domain lock and state record, registration verified (exit 1 `not registered`, 3 no eligible route); `paseo-new-seat` gained `--thinking`, `--label` and `--json` (2026-10-25).
- `paseowatch` skill and `fleet-watch upstream --profile <name>` (site-djbclark; profiles `cfengine`, the unchanged default, and `paseo`): the upstream watcher now runs once per profile with its own state, relay target and ping prefix, reads Discussion threads through GraphQL, and the scheduled tick runs every profile; the `paseo` profile has no worker fallback (a Hermes ping only) (2026-10-25).
- Added `skill-everywhere --all` to discover and reconcile every public/private shared skill, strict `--check --all` verification, and an all-skills default for `/skills-everywhere` with an explicit Codex coverage check (2026-10-25).
- `launch.py successor` and `self-slash --finish --successor claude/MODEL` on herdr (2026-10-25): the herdr half of the shared handoff contract with Paseo. It waits for the pane's agent to be idle, opens a tab in the same herdr workspace and cwd, starts a real claude TUI there (`herdr agent start --kind claude -- --model M --effort E`), prompts `/baton`, checks that the pane runs claude under another session id, and leaves the old pane idle for `herdr-tidy` (exit 11 and nothing touched on any failure). `launch.py start --baton` on herdr now starts a real TUI for claude and codex by default (djbclark, 2026-10-25: an `acp-run` pane shows only "⚙ Terminal" lines, nothing a human can read or steer): `herdr agent start --kind K -- <yolo, model, effort>` then `herdr agent prompt`; `--acp` keeps `acp-run`, `--tui` forces a TUI without `--baton`, `--no-interactive` slices are unchanged. A TUI session can take `/new`, `/compact` and a handoff; an `acp-run` one cannot (`self-slash` exits 9). `launch.py` also maps `claude-<family>-5-5` to what `acp-run claude` takes and probes `--model` before any tab opens (a bad id used to open a tab and then die). skills/handoff Step 9, skills/baton and skills/session-finder carry the contract.
- `bin/seat-name` (`next`, `peek`, `issued`, `check`): one **ever-increasing base-36** name for every Paseo seat — `<role> <n>`, counting 1, 2 … 9, a, b … z, 10 — allocated atomically under an `flock` so two agents creating seats at the same moment cannot receive the same number, and monotonic so a name is never reused even after the seat it named is archived. This exists because names are derived from the first prompt, so two unrelated sessions that opened with the same words get the same name: on 2026-10-25 there were two live tabs named `◈ Dann` and two named `/baton`, and one was draining the other's mail because a peer cannot tell them apart. Base 36 (djbclark's choice) keeps a name short for a long time — one character covers 36 seats, two cover 1296 — and a bare counter reads as an identity rather than a version (2026-10-25).

- `bin/paseo-new-seat` (`--title`, `--prompt`, `--provider`, `--model`, `--cwd`, `--workspace`, `--no-auto-accept`, `--dry-run`): creates a Paseo seat with `config.featureValues.auto_accept = true` **in the create call**, then verifies the shield from the agent's state file (exit 4 when it did not come up). This exists because `paseo agent run` cannot do it: Paseo's `resolveACPCreateConfig` defaults `auto_accept` only for an *unattended* create and `agent run` has no `--unattended` flag, so a create-then-set sequence leaves a window in which the seat's first tool call prompts the operator — which fired for real on 2026-10-25, when a new seat was told to read its brief immediately and hit a permission prompt before the feature could be set afterwards (2026-10-25).

- Added Go model cost classifications and bounded read-only advisory gates, economical execution defaults, binding monthly substrate limits, and native V2 context nudges/compaction with one shared admission guard; moved Claude growth/boundary nudges earlier, documented durable handoff cycles, removed shell command rewriting, preserved compaction admission across model changes, retained the trusted guard in sanitized advisory launches, and supported standalone ACP installation with an explicit Go bundle; fixed costly Go advisory deadlines: `acp-dispatch extend` refuses them and `acp-run` ignores a hand-edited `.extend` file for them (#64).
- `bin/bm-lint` (2026-10-24, Basic Memory design slice W3): a read-only lint of the Basic Memory store (default `~/ops/site-private/memory`, regular-file reads only, never the SQLite index) that reports missing `type:`, empty projects, dead subtrees, handoffs older than 30 days outside `handoffs/archive/`, orphan notes, duplicate titles (frontmatter title else file stem, as Basic Memory does), undated rulings and unlinked decisions, unresolved relations (wiki-links outside code; Markdown `.md` links count as relations), unverified status, oversize notes and dead path references; findings carry paths, line numbers and counts only (never note text) with a unique stable `key` (`check:path`, or a 12-hex hash for titles, link targets and rulings), `--json` is local-only, symlinked and special files are listed under `skipped` and never read, exit 0 clean, 1 findings, 2 error (including a bad `BM_LINT_*` value and a run that read no note at all, such as an unreadable store root; notes and subdirectories it cannot read are listed under `skipped`, never dropped, and an unlistable config project directory is skipped rather than reported as empty; dangling or looping symlinks are listed too); a note whose parse raises is skipped alone under `skipped` (`notes: not parsed`), a link whose host NFKC-normalizes to `/ ? # @ :` is read by hand (no `urlsplit`) and no longer aborts the run, and an internal error prints only the exception class, never note text; the weekly fleet-watch hook-up is a separate change in site-djbclark (#55).
- `relay.py` deferred send, adversary round 2 (2026-10-24): a herdr pane whose TUI herdr itself detected (omp, codex, opencode, pi: its fleet row has no session id, pid or transcript) is identified by the pid of the pane's one foreground process named like the agent (the herdr label or a `fleet.PROC_AGENTS` binary; none or several matching: not deferred, exit 1) and rechecked by membership among the pane's foreground pids at each look (tool and MCP children come and go; an unreadable pane is an error, 3 in a row fail), so a pane id reused by a new TUI of the same kind fails the queued message ("target changed"), and a pane with no way to tell its TUI apart is not deferred (exit 1); a caller with no identifiable sender (no verified Paseo id, no fleet `self` row: fleet-watch, load-watch, a cron script) gets exit 1 "deferring needs an identifiable sender" instead of a queued message nobody is told about; a failure notice to a busy sender is deferred once as a `kind: notice` record (its own cap of 20, an hour, owes no notice, never collapsed with a normal record); a failed record and its owed notice are written in one transition; a Paseo daemon that cannot answer fails a queued message only after 3 consecutive errors, counted apart from "not found", and a pending permission card is waited for; the deliverer keeps `PASEO_HOST`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `HERDR_BIN_PATH`, `SESSION_FINDER_STATE`, `HERDR_PLUGIN_STATE_DIR`, `HERDR_SLEEPER_STATE`, `BIGTEAM_STATE` and no session id; a deliverer alive past the 15-minute "unverified" rule stops sending, and its log line and notice say "unverified"; the deliverer no longer sets umask 077 (its children would inherit it; its records and log are 0600 already), and `bin/session-load-watch` passes `--no-defer` as `load-watch` does (#57).
- `relay.py` deferred send: a send whose only obstacle was a busy target (a Paseo agent whose provider cannot steer, a codex/omp/opencode/pi agent without `--steer`, a working non-Claude pane TUI) was queued in a 0600 record under `~/.local/state/relay/deferred/` and exited 0 with `queued: <id>` (`"queued":true` with `--json`), then delivered by a detached `relay.py --deliver` when the target went idle, prefixed `[queued Nm ago] `, in order per target, never twice (a `sending` record whose deliverer died, whose send raised or that sent for 15 minutes became `failed` "unverified, do not resend", never resent, and never blocked the records behind it); the deliverer ran in a new environment with none of the session ids and was a double-forked grandchild, so a sweep started by any agent (the target included) still sent under the real sender's label and Paseo id, and a failure notice reached a sender in a herdr pane; at most 20 queued per target, 20 per sender and 100 in all (exit 1 "deferred queue full"); a pane id reused by another session failed the message; the stored sender label went through the label sanitizer and the Hermes forgery rule; the opportunistic sweep only changed states and started detached deliverers; `--no-defer` kept the old refusal, `--idle S` still blocked and won, `--defer-for S` (default 6 h, cap 24 h), `--pending [--text] [--json]` (the text only with `--text`), `--cancel ID`, `--sweep` (also run at the start of every relay); a failed, expired or unverified message sent its sender one notice and every event went to `~/.local/state/relay/deferred.log` (#57).
- `bin/claude-fleet-governor` (`check`, `run [--dry-run]`, `status [--json]`, `--install`): projects the Claude 5-hour and weekly aiuse windows to their resets and, at `tight` or `critical`, moves every live Paseo `claude` agent Opus/Fable → Sonnet 5.5 in place (Fable → Opus at any level), at critical steers running agents to hand off to omp API credits once per reset period, restores after the reset, and pings Hermes once per changing run; exit 0/1/2, 10 stale data (each Claude account is judged on its own readings and its own burn rate, the account in the worst state binds, and an account whose last reading of a window is older than 45 minutes by its own time takes no part; exit 10 when none is fresh), 11 a call failed, 12 internal error; state `~/.local/state/ade/fleet-governor.json`; launchd job via `--install` (2026-10-23, #31).
- `bin/paseo-set-model` (`set <agent> <model> [--dry-run]`, `list --json`): switches a live Paseo agent's model in place through the daemon's own `set_agent_model_request`, which the Paseo CLI lacks (docs/upstream-issues.md section 17); exit 3 no such agent, 4 model not applied (2026-10-23, #31).
- `bin/pr-wait` requested the Codex review itself: when the head had no Codex marker and no `@codex review` since its push (decided by the server-stamped push time, the earliest check-suite `created_at` or a force-push event, against each request comment's server `created_at`, never by a commit date or timeline position, and never counting Codex's own receipts that quote the phrase) it posted one `@codex review` comment, and once more after `--rerequest-after S` (default 600) of `running`, never a third; at most two POST attempts per head, an errored POST counted and saved before it is sent, and an errored POST that landed adopted once, by its server `created_at` (the attempt time less 120 s of clock skew, at most 240 s after it, and later than the push stamp when there is one, so a request for the previous head is never adopted; adopted comment ids are saved) even on a repo with no check suites, and a re-request forgone because of the attempt cap said so in `codex_request_note` (state in `request-<repo>-<pr>-<head>.json`; `--no-request` only waits, `--no-codex` never requests); the summary gained `codex_requested` and `codex_request_note` (which points to openai/codex#55890 when a re-requested review stays stuck) (#58).
- Added `bin/pr-wait`, one waiter per PR head that waited for the CI checks of the exact head (check runs and commit statuses through `gh api`) and for Codex's `codex-review-state:v1` marker for that head (`completed` and `failed` both terminal; a pass still `running` or absent past `--codex-stuck` ended the wait as `codex-stuck`), then printed one line and wrote `PREFIX-{checks,codex,findings,summary}.json` with Codex's inline findings for the head (selected by head and author, never a time window; only the Codex app's comments and state markers are believed); it refused a second waiter for the same repo, PR and head (exit 4, naming the live pid and prefix), treated a `gh` failure as unknown rather than done, and ended on a moved head (exit 5), a plain timeout (1) or a timeout while `gh` failed (6); it also read the PR's review threads through GraphQL and wrote `PREFIX-open-findings.json` (every unresolved Codex thread of any head, outdated or not) so an earlier head's open finding shows (PR 32, 2026-10-23: a P1 left open past two pushes while a head-only filter read 0), it treated Codex security findings (`codex-security-review-finding:v1`, the marker's `security_review`) like code findings (each finding and open thread has a `kind`; the wait ends only when both reviews are terminal), and its summary carried `merge_ready`, true only with at least one CI check that succeeded and none failing or pending, Codex's code (and security, when the marker has it) review completed on the head, no unresolved Codex thread of either kind and no marker/thread mismatch; `merge_blockers` names why not, one of `codex-<status>`, `security-failed`, `security-running`, `security-other-head`, `findings-not-collected`, `marker-unverified`, `security-marker-unverified`, `no-checks`, `ci-pending`, `ci-failed`, `ci-none-succeeded`, `open-findings`, `findings-mismatch`, `codex-skipped` (2026-10-23) (#34).
- `bin/app-tamer-ab`: a 60 s sampler (load per core, compressor churn, swap, App Tamer's own CPU) with named phases and a per-phase `summary`, for the with/without App Tamer comparison (2026-10-23).
- `bin/change-hermes`: numbered picker that points every Hermes side-model slot (fallback, compression, MoA, per-model effort and context cap) at one model, listing only options whose quota a live call or `aiuse` confirms (2026-10-23).

- Added `bin/ade-update`, one command that keeps a machine current: it fast-forward pulls (refusing with exit 2 when a
  path the pull would change or create has a local modification, an untracked or an ignored file, or a parent or
  child path of one), relinks missing skills
  (only in the canonical checkout; any other `--repo` is report-only), checks the `CLAUDE.md` and `~/.claude` symlinks,
  and prints the changelog entries new since the revision it last recorded; `--check` reports only and exits 10 when
  behind (2026-10-20, #20); `--check` also lists `claude/{commands,agents,hooks}` entries that upstream adds as "new upstream" links, and apply fast-forwards to the exact commit it checked instead of pulling again; a fast-forward that landed before a post-merge hook timed out is recorded as a `merge warning` problem (exit 10), and a non-canonical `--repo` without `--state` records under its own `~/.local/state/ade/applied-<12 hex>` instead of the shared record (#20).
- `load-watch sample` also watches compressor churn: above the `admit` limit (60000 pages/s) for `LW_CHURN_SUSTAIN_S` (180 s) it sends one line with the top
  three commands to the load watcher (at most hourly, cooldown `churn-watcher`) and to Hermes (cooldown `churn`), adds the Grok Bot/Chrome standing exception
  when they lead, never signals anything; `status` lists both cooldowns; test hook `LW_FAKE_COMPRESSOR` (2026-10-23).
- Added `bin/claude-quota-guard` (`check`, `status`, `--install`), which turned the Claude weekly, 5-hour and Fable windows (read from the snapshot when `aiuse --available` drops an exhausted Claude or the exhausted Fable pool, and at least `watch` when aiuse dropped a Claude the snapshot still shows) and aiuse's Claude `conserve` alerts into a level (`ok`, `watch`, `tight`, `critical`; exit 0-3, 10 when Claude appears nowhere, the data is malformed, aiuse is unreadable or another check holds the lock, 64 for a bad thresholds env, 69 for `--install` off macOS), wrote `~/.local/state/ade/quota-guard.json` under a lock, pinged once per level per reset period, and treated data older than 3 hours (the oldest of the payload, each pool and the snapshot) as stale: at least `watch`, no level pings, one ping per stale episode; added the `claude/hooks/quota_nudge.py` UserPromptSubmit hook, which printed the level's advice once per session and level (#29).
- `bg` (site-djbclark `bin/bg`, new helper `bin/bg-offload`) offloads a targeted test run to CI once the load gate has held it
  `BG_OFFLOAD_AFTER` seconds (default 60) in a repo recorded `ci` (djbclark, 2026-10-23: a 2 s pytest file waited over an hour at
  load 25-93). It runs the repo's tracked, executable `scripts/bg-offload-run` or `scripts/github-ci-run --no-wait REF` (an untracked script is never used) (the `pushed https://...`
  or ShizukuTendCF's `branch: https://...` line is accepted), polls `gh run list -R OWNER/REPO` and exits with CI's verdict: 0 CI's
  workflow passed on this tree (not "your named test passed"), 1 failed (failed-step log tail and
  `~/.local/state/bg/offload/<run>.log`), 76 still running at `BG_OFFLOAD_WAIT` (2400 s), 77 no verdict (also when every run was
  skipped); 3 and 75 are unchanged. It publishes nothing that is not already on the remote (djbclark, 2026-10-23, after six
  review rounds): it offloads only when no tracked file is modified (working tree or index; untracked files do not block and are
  never pushed) and every commit of HEAD is reachable from a ref the real push target advertises: the remote (`GITHUB_CI_REMOTE`
  or origin) must have one fetch-and-push url, and `git ls-remote --refs <remote>` by name, resolved exactly as the runner's push
  (60 s, no prompt; any failure refuses), is the proof, not the local remote-tracking refs, which go stale after a `set-url`, a
  `pushurl` or a branch deleted by another clone. History is walked without replace refs (`GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, for the
  runner's push too) and a graft file refuses. The `ci/base` the runner overlays must be on the remote too; in a partial clone
  the objects of HEAD's tree and the overlaid `ci/base` tree are fetched first, and any that cannot be fetched refuses (so the
  overlay is never silently skipped), and the runner's git
  config is forced to `push.followTags=false` and `push.recurseSubmodules=no` (appended to `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, which beats
  config files and a caller's `GIT_CONFIG_*` env) so no local tag rides along. If any check fails it prints one line per reason and keeps waiting at
  the gate as before: dirty or unpushed work is not offloaded, so commit and push, then bg offloads. There is no opt-in and no
  scan. A missing GNU tool refuses (it never skips a check). A named test path that is missing, untracked, a directory with no
  tracked file or outside the repo stops the offload. Killing bg stops an in-flight push. No route, no `gh`, a failing push or
  `BG_OFFLOAD=0` keep the old waiting. No `load-watch` change: the runnable route is the script convention
  (PR pending).
- `load-watch admit` also gates on memory: it is hot when compressor churn (macOS `vm_stat` Compressions +
  Decompressions, pages/s) exceeds the new `--max-mem-churn N` (default 60000, calibrated 2026-10-23 from 215
  samples: calm 11-22k, load >=25 median 80k; the first guess, 20000, was below the load<10 median of 34k), so `acp-dispatch` (which calls `admit`) holds new slices during a thrash that load alone does not show; `bg` (site-djbclark `bin/bg`, f370ad7) now also asks `admit` after its own load check and fails open if `load-watch` errors.
  The result and `--json` carry `mem_churn_pages_s`; no `vm_stat` (Linux) means the gate never blocks (2026-10-23).
- `relay.py` also reaches a Hermes gateway conversation (a `hermes-gw` row with a session id; route `hermes`, its only route): `hermes --resume <sid> -z` runs one turn on that conversation's stored transcript, text tagged `[relay from <name>] `, in its own process group. The child loses the caller's session, Paseo, ACP-approval and Hermes routing variables and gets `RELAY_HOPS` and `RELAY_SENDER` (a relay made inside the turn is tagged as Hermes; only nested `hermes -z` turns are counted, a fourth is refused). The reply goes to a 0600 file under `RELAY_HERMES_DIR` (pruned after 7 days) and is printed as untrusted data (control, format and escape characters stripped, 4000 characters, a closing marker). It is never sent to Hermes's own conversation (session id, session key, or the same platform, chat and thread), and not while an earlier relayed turn in that conversation still holds its lock. Only rc 1, an empty stdout and the single exact "session not found" (or "cannot resume session") stderr line count as not sent, every other failure is "unverified, do not resend"; a "turn started, do not resend" line is printed to stderr first, because the caller's own tool timeout can end `relay.py` before its verdict; a turn that outlives max(100, `--wait`) seconds from the start is left running (relay.py never kills it, but a caller's tool timeout that kills the whole process tree does: give the command at least max(100, `--wait`) + 50 s or run it in the background). `--wait` must now be finite and not negative; `RELAY_HERMES` overrides the `hermes` binary. Checked on a throwaway `oneshot` session and end to end on a real Telegram topic (delivered, tagged user row and reply in its stored transcript, lock free afterwards; 2026-10-23).
