New skill `peer-relay` teaches Hermes to run `relay.py` itself (drafted here; Hermes installs it).
- `fleet.py` keeps a Hermes gateway conversation listed while its row is closed with `agent_close`, which every `hermes --resume -z` turn does until the gateway reopens it by session key a few minutes later (the log line `gateway.session: reopened ended session ...; keeping route`); a row replaced by a newer one for the same session key, or ended by a reset, stays hidden. Before, a second relay minutes after the first found no session (2026-10-23).
- `relay.py` picks the best route for the session (Paseo `send`, `acp-dispatch tell`, a live ACP launch's inbox, keys into
  its pane, then the Claude messaging socket) and falls back to the next itself when one fails before sending; it errors
  only when every route has, listing each reason. A send it cannot rule out (text typed but unseen, a `paseo send` that
  may have reached the daemon, a socket frame written) never falls back, so nothing is delivered twice. New `--plan`
  and `--via ROUTE`; `--idle S` now also waits for a working non-Claude pane TUI; the Paseo gate runs whatever `--via`
  says. Text starting with `/` or `!` is no longer typed into a pane (only a socket frame carries it); a Claude with a
  draft in its box now gets the message by socket instead of exit 1, and an idle one is checked in its transcript.
  Text a pane relay left unsent is remembered for an hour with the draft it showed; a retry of the same text types
  nothing (it submits our own leftover, never the operator's draft or paste) and proves delivery in the transcript or
  reports it unverified ("do not resend"). Exit 1 means not sent or sent but unverified; read the message.
  Home item 24 and `session-finder` say agents message peers only this way (2026-10-23).
  Adversary round 7 (2026-10-23): a box whose ❯ row or bottom rule is outside the 14 rows read is "unknown", never
  empty (nothing typed or submitted blind; a retry of our text is unverified), a styled row starting `Try "` is the
  operator's text, only the placeholder seen right after typing is ours, a delivered text recalled into the box is
  never submitted again, a `---` row or a ❯ inside the box no longer hides rows, entries purge after a day.
  Round 8: the input box is the one rule row directly followed by a ❯ row (a rule or ❯ typed into a draft makes it
  unknown, not a smaller empty box), the box is read once, an unsent entry is forgotten only on proof, a delivery is
  a whole user or queued message in the transcript (never a substring).
  Round 9: a collapsed paste is recorded wrapped in `<pasted_content id=N>` (seen in real transcripts) and now counts as
  delivered, the placeholder in the first read after typing is ours (no time window), a copy that returns after ours left
  the box is a recall and is never submitted, hook-injected (`isMeta`) and compact-summary lines are not deliveries.
- `bin/self-slash --finish --successor PROVIDER/MODEL [--thinking ID] [--mode MODE] [--title TEXT]` (Paseo only; exit 2 on herdr and Orca): after the turn it starts a new top-level agent in the same workspace (`paseo run "/baton"`, default mode and `--thinking high` of the old agent), checks it is not a subagent and runs the model asked, then archives the old agent; any failure exits 11, archives nothing and leaves this session usable. The successor always lands in an existing workspace (`--workspace ID`, default this agent's; never `--new-workspace`, and in another workspace it runs in that workspace's own cwd) with a short title. The old workspace was archived after the old agent only when proven unneeded: one cwd match with no workspace above that cwd (never an override of another row or a tie-break), local isolation, no terminals, no other agent in it by workspace id or cwd, and a cwd that neither equals nor contains the successor's (`--keep-workspace` skips that). The successor is polled for up to 20 s and must have a new id, a null `ParentAgentId`, the model asked and status idle, running or initializing, else exit 11. A malformed `SELF_SLASH_SUCCESSOR_SETTLE` is refused before `paseo run`, an inspect that fails after a good look exits 11, and no aborted successor path can fall through to the bare archive (#30).
- `skill-everywhere` gains a `grokbot` target: a rendered copy `ade-<name>/` in `~/agent-data/workflows/` on the
  Grok Bot box (banner, `.skill-everywhere` marker, only marked dirs touched), plus `--dry-run`/`-n`,
  `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_ONLY` and plain-GNU fallbacks so it runs on Linux (2026-10-23). (PR pending)
- `autorename` renames a Paseo-hosted session's workspace to the title in lower-case hyphen form plus `-pw`
  (`paseo-skill-sync-overwrite-fix-pw`; `--auto` keeps a workspace already ending in `-pw`); herdr tab labels keep
  `-t` (2026-10-23).

- `bin/ade-backup-stores`: Phase 0 backups, a SQLite online-backup copy of every live agent session database, the
  per-session trees and file stores and the Hermes JSONL export (run against a scratch copy of the Hermes
  database), with a hashed `MANIFEST.json`, built in `<dest>.part/` and renamed when complete; `--dry-run`,
  `--only`, `--json`, `--check-schemas`, `--strict-stores`. A configured store that does not exist is
  `"status": "missing"` in the manifest and on stderr (exit 0, so a partly provisioned machine still gets a
  generation; `--strict-stores` exits 1) and a Cursor session whose required `meta.json` is symlinked,
  excluded or gone during the copy is an error that marks its store `partial`. Credential file names and paths (inventory 5.5, plus `mcp-auth.json`),
  the `credential-bak` vault and symlinks inside a store (a linked Cursor `store.db` or a database under a linked session directory included; each skip is printed on stderr and leaves the store `ok`) are not copied (a store root that is a symlink, as Codex's sessions are on this Mac, a per-session tree root that is one, or a configured SQLite path that is one, as Codex's databases are, is followed under one root-link rule when it resolves under `$HOME` to a non-credential path and recorded as `root_symlinks`; a dangling, outside-`$HOME` or credential-path link is an error), OpenCode's credential tables are scrubbed from its
  database copy, and a schema guard fails the run on any other credential-looking table or column; session
  transcripts are copied as they are and can hold pasted secrets. Refuses a destination that exists, holds
  a source store or sits inside one, a configured store counting whether or not it exists; a source that turns into a symlink mid-copy is never followed, the Hermes export fallback obeys the root-link rule and a dry run survives a store pruned under it; Muse's `tui-history.jsonl` is backed up, the dry run skips an excluded-name database in a SQLite tree like the real copy (and lists `skipped`), and `--check-schemas --strict-stores` exits 1 on a missing database, tree root or file-store path and never opens a credential-named database (`E excluded`); Crush's `crush.json` (provider key and OAuth tokens) is not backed up; a sidecar, a single-file root or a SQLite source swapped for a symlink after its check is refused, never followed; a Cursor session with no `meta.json` is a `sidecar_missing` warning (db copied, store `ok`; an error only under `--strict-stores`), `--check-schemas` counts unopenable databases apart, the `crush-files` store backs up Crush's `projects.json` only, a store is walked by descriptor so a directory swapped for a symlink mid-copy is refused, a skipped optional sidecar is listed under `skipped`, and a store root reached through a symlinked parent directory gets the same root-link gate, and tree stores are opened through the anchored home descriptor too, the Hermes dry run reports a refused `state.db` link like the real run, and a failed per-session or bulk export leaves no partial file, and the dry run reports a missing `hermes` like the real run, and `--only hermes-export --check-schemas --strict-stores` checks the `state.db` source, a WAL-mode copy is made self-contained so the schema guard opens it under every SQLite build, and a symlinked directory in a tree glob is one `symlink` skip, never entered, a configured root of the wrong type (a directory where a single file belongs, or the reverse) and a subdirectory the dry run cannot list are store errors, not copied or silently skipped, and a non-regular Hermes `state.db` or any link above a glob's last component is refused or skipped without being followed, and the Hermes export reads `~/.hermes/config.yaml` anchored at $HOME, without blocking or following links and capped at 64 KiB, else uses the default limit with a warning, and a database or directory that vanishes or is unreadable between listing and `lstat` in a SQLite tree is an error (not silently omitted) while a dangling link at the last component stays a non-match, and `--json --check-schemas` prints one JSON document (the text lines go to stderr; exit status unchanged), `--check-schemas` validates a Cursor session's required sidecar like the copy (`sidecar_missing`, a failure only under `--strict-stores`; a linked one always), the dry run records an entry it cannot lstat like the copy, and a refused `config.yaml` or source leaks no descriptor (#24).
- `session-finder/relay.py` reaches Paseo-hosted agents (`paseo send --behavior steer -- <id> <text>`, labelled as an agent message from the sender; takes a `PASEO_AGENT_ID` as the ident, `--idle S` waits for idle first; refuses archived agents, pending permissions and text starting with `/`) and `where.py` reports `host=paseo agent=<id>` for them, so the "no terminal channel (unknown terminal)" refusal no longer hits a Paseo agent (#25).
- `agents/` templates for `~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.codex/config.toml`, opencode's `opencode.json` and
  `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (secrets, home paths and host or account ids as placeholders, listed in
  `agents/README.md`), `bin/ade-template-check` (credential scan, render-and-parse with plain and hostile values, `--diff` by key path) and its
  tests; CI now installs PyYAML for them (#23).
- `loadwatch` skill and `load-watch watcher set|show|clear|test`: one registered agent session (a Paseo
  agent by `paseo send`, else relay.py) also hears every load incident, unexplained sustained load and
  job-guard trip, and investigates; `set` replaces the old watcher and tells it (2026-10-20).
- `cfenginewatch` skill (with `fleet-watch upstream --relay-to|--relay-test|--relay-off|--relay-status`, in
  site-djbclark): one registered agent session hears every reviewer event on djbclark's upstream CFEngine
  PRs and works it through `/bigteam`, prepare-and-stop; `--relay-to` replaces and tells the old watcher
  (2026-10-23).
- `docs/ade-everywhere-inventory-2026-10-20-stores.md`: Phase 0 slice 1, every installed agent
  session store classified (52 stores, backup plan per store, differences from proposal §6.1) (#16).
- `CHANGELOG.md`, this file, and the rule that every pull request adds a line to it; README
  "Update (this machine)"; proposal v2.4 with `ade update` as a Phase 1 command and acceptance
  test T8 (2026-10-20).
- `self-slash --finish`: end the session; on Paseo it waits for idle and archives the agent without
  `--force`, and `self-slash "/quit"` there is mapped to it (Paseo runs no `/quit`); `/new`, `/clear`,
  `/reset`, `/quit`, `/exit` and `--finish` take a per-agent lifecycle lock, keyed on pid and start
  time (#19).
- `self-slash` exit 9: a dispatched agent (`ACP_RUN_AGENT=1`, set by `acp-run`) is refused every
  lifecycle action and `/compact` (#19).
- `docs/threat-model.md`, the threat model Codex's security review reads (#10).
- AGENTS.md "Code review" section: what a reviewer checks on every pull request here (#9).
- `bin/cursor-cloud`: launch and inspect Cursor Cloud Agents from the terminal, with unit tests
  and a doc page (#5, #8).
- Load gate: no slice starts on a hot machine (`load-watch admit` plus the `acp-dispatch` gate)
  (#7).
- `acp-dispatch`: a `cursor-cloud` place (record, poller, report) and the matching
  `route_agent` service (#11).
- CI runs the pytest suite on ubuntu-latest as well as macOS (#3).
- `herdr-tidy`: Orca host mode (`--host orca`, `/orca-tidy`) (#2).

### Changed
- `plugins/ade-claim/README.md`: links the upstream Paseo issue for the missing plugin directory ([getpaseo/paseo#9610](https://github.com/getpaseo/paseo/issues/9610)), so the install-record workaround can be dropped once Paseo fixes it. (#647)
- The six vendored Paseo skills are now `ade-paseo`, `ade-paseo-advisor`, `ade-paseo-committee`, `ade-paseo-handoff`,
  `ade-paseo-help` and `ade-paseo-plugin` (slash commands too): Paseo's skill sync owns the unprefixed names and kept
  overwriting the narrowed copies through the skill links (2026-11-01 again, by the 0.15.0-beta.1 bundle). (#243)
- `docs/review-boundary.md`: records that the master `merge-gate` ruleset is applied (id 27237596, App id 5655191, no bypass actors), so `adversary-marker`, `pytest-linux` and `context-policy` are required checks. (#661)
- `config/pi-pool-acceptance.toml` accepts the Antigravity pool for automatic Pi routing (2026-11-19), on verified fresh quota, `antigravity/gemini-4-neon` model identity and clean failure handling; continuation on Neon stays pending a quota reset and is recorded as such in `docs/pi-services.md`. (#819)
- `ade-merge-ready approve-post` (real run, `--yes`) now asks djbclark for fresh Touch ID/password through `sudo-ask -k` (else `sudo -k /usr/bin/true`) before its first post: the dialog names the repo and every `PR N head <40-hex>`; a refusal, failure, timeout or a selection too long for one dialog posts nothing and exits 3. Dry-run and the other subcommands are unchanged and there is no bypass flag or env var. It stops an agent approving its own PR through this tool, not one using djbclark's `gh` token directly (#731).
- Astra may run down to 20% of Codex quota left in tendcf and nix2cf only (2026-11-01, djbclark): `[dispatch.repo_overrides.tendcf]` and `.nix2cf` in `config/routing.toml` replace the 60% `codex:frontier` estimate floor and cap the estimate; `acp-dispatch` resolves the repo from `-C` through the origin remote (worktrees count) and applies it to the estimate, the in-flight reservation and the Argon fallback predicate; `route_agent.py route` and `plan` take `--repo NAME` (default: the cwd's repo). Other repos keep 60. (#224)
- The planner's frontier admission now uses `acp-dispatch`'s own arithmetic (2026-11-01, adversary R1-R3): one shared `pool_fits` with per-window estimates (`estimate_windows_from_rows`, `window_fit`), the same kind and effort filter, window-only rows and `ROOT/jobs` plus `ROOT/*/jobs` history, replacing the scalar `pool_floor`. (#224)
- `route_agent.py` reads a window `measurement_frozen` as exhausted for every provider when it reads the snapshot file (2026-11-01, adversary N7, needed for the Argon fallback): aiuse's own `--available` already did; before, only the file path trusted a frozen reading, so with a frozen Flash window the router now sees 0% where it used to see the frozen number. The planner also hands a `frontier` slice only to a pool at or above what `acp-dispatch` will demand of it (`pool_fits`: codex astra's 60% estimate floor until 3 slices are measured, then their median, shared with `estimate_pct` through `estimate_from_history`), and a fallback service (Argon) gets no burn-first bonus, so it never outranks a pool it falls back behind that is not short. (#224)
- A fallback service (Argon) is now ordered behind every pool it falls back behind that can take the kind, structurally and not by score (2026-11-01, Codex round 6: codex at 60% left and pace 1.6 scored 5, tied Argon and lost the name tie-break), in `route` and so `plan` and `reroute --escalate`; and `acp-dispatch` judges every admission a wait can change again right before the spawn, in the foreground and in the detached runner: the fallback-only gate (a primary pool that recovered during the load or capacity wait refuses Argon: exit 2, the same message as at dispatch, record class `fallback`), the plenty gate, and the pool's own quota (minimum headroom, estimate fit, a fallback with no reading fails closed). (#224)
- The planner and `acp-dispatch` now compute one slice estimate for every service and kind (2026-11-02, Codex round 7): `route_agent.pool_fits` no longer returns early for a pool with no configured estimate floor, so measured history binds Argon (three slices at 50% with 20% left is short, and the Opus downgrade stays) and every other pool, on the model and effort `plan` prints; the floor lookup (`floor_lookup`) and the history filter are shared code. Quota freshness at the spawn follows the fallback pool, not the `fallback_gate` stamp: a `route-override` Argon whose reading disappears or drains during a wait is refused (exit 5), as at dispatch; `route-override` still skips only eligibility. (#224)
- Docs follow-up to #710: `bin/ade-auto-permit` skips `ade.kind=external-review` agents and their Paseo descendants that carry no `ade.lead` or `--ade-label` of a lead in the lineage; docs/orchestration-practices.md section 3, docs/scheduling.md, skills/fan-out and docs/peer-trust.md rule 2 now state the limits (only the label counts; a top-level `bin/ade-worker-run` child of a labelled agent, and senders marked only by config ids or title patterns, are still approved under `--all-local`). (#719)
- `bin/ade-auto-permit`: an agent labelled `ade.kind=external-review` (one that judges untrusted GitHub artifacts for prompt injection) is never auto-approved, in any mode, even with `ade.lead`, as a descendant or under `--all-local`: its requests stay pending (`skip` / `sensitive-sender`), and neither is a descendant behind one; only the label counts (the config ids and title patterns the peer-trust gate honours are not read here, and no launcher sets the label yet, so this is a partial match of peer-trust rule 4). The sweep asks Paseo for that label when a request is pending and fails closed when the answer is missing or malformed. (#710)
- `bin/ade-auto-permit --all-local` was added: it approves tool-permission prompts of every local Claude, Codex, OpenCode, Pi and OMP agent, the lead included (audit reason `all-local`), so operator-started agents no longer stall; the launchd template passes it. In every mode the job now skips and logs requests that look like questions or plan approvals for the operator (`operator-question`, a best-effort name denylist), requests of agents of any other provider such as ACP-backed ones (`unclassified-provider`) and request ids that are a prefix of another pending id (`ambiguous-request-id`). (#653)
- `session-handoff`'s Reader protocol gained an **Owner check** (2026-11-01, djbclark; `/baton` points to it): before the first write or resume of a chain, its log's `session_id` is matched to a `fleet.py --json` row (`sid`, `paseo`, `id`, id prefix or transcript path) or a live `bin/watch` holder; a live owner other than you, or no match at all, is asked about first (the owner via `relay.py`, or the operator), with exemptions for your own chain, the named successor and your own chain after `/clear` or `/new`. A `/baton` after `/clear` had resumed the lead's live chain and merged PR 173 ahead of PR 177 (2026-10-31). ; every helper it runs is called by absolute path from `~/src/djbclark-ade`, never a cwd-relative script (#201)
- Migrated the build wrapper references from `bg` to `bgrun` (the wrapper was renamed so it no longer collides with the shell's `bg` builtin; djbclark/site-djbclark#281). `bin/load-watch`, `bin/pytest`, `claude/hooks/bg_test_gate.py`, `bash_habits_gate.py`, `opencode-plugins/bg-cargo-gate.*`, docs and tests now name `bgrun`, while `bin/bg` is still recognized as a transitional alias. (#811)
- `skills/bottom-config` (2026-11-01): btm-mt's `processes.split_command` (command drawn as `name ┊ cwd ┊ options`,
  expandable groups in grouped mode) replaces `processes.truncate_cmd_middle`. (#234)
- `skills/btww` (2026-11-01): `/btww` now names the TUI's native side-question command (or says it has none) instead of
  answering the question as an ordinary turn; the omp and Hermes CLI aliases still run the native `/btw`. (#238)
- `skills/btww` (2026-11-01): the remaining TUIs are resolved: Copilot (`/btw`, alias `/ask`), opencode, Qwen Code and Muse (`/side`)
  have a native side-question command; Cline and goose have none; Devin is not installed. (#239)
- `tests/test_paseo_bypass_watch.py` (2026-11-01) pins the two adversary round 4 gaps: `Auto`, `DEFAULT` and `autoSomething` seats are never flipped (exact mode match), and an `auto` seat whose set exits 0 but still reads `auto` fails the post-set verify (#216)
- `ade-self-session`'s `supported unattended successor mode unavailable` refusal now carries the planner's reason, and the modeless-Pi reason names the providers with a verified unattended mode (Codex `full-access`, Claude `bypassPermissions`, Antigravity `full-access`); `docs/self-session.md` states that a standing role needing unattended self-handoff must run on one of those. (#806)
- Closed queue Carried 82: the graft/Trail hook no longer imports repo-controlled JavaScript (fixed in #827); the queue entry moved to Done with its limits. (#829)
- A pull request now adds its changelog line as a `changelog.d/<branch>.md` fragment instead of editing the shared `## Unreleased` block of `CHANGELOG.md`, so a merge no longer makes every other open PR conflict there; AGENTS.md code review rule 10 names the single folder (the lead) and the trigger, and order 9's staged list marks "CHANGELOG fragments" done (#190).
- Documented verified attended Chrome freeze/resume and discard/recovery, replacement tab IDs, recovery limits, cleanup and Codex tool-loading observations. (#780)
- Tightened the chrome-use wording in `docs/mcp-servers.md` and `docs/threat-model.md` (Pi approval, "holds or drives the debugger", the Update CLI trigger) (#772).
- `load-watch`'s compressor-churn alert got its own threshold, `LW_CHURN_ALERT_PAGES_S` (default 50000), instead of sharing the `admit` gate's 20000 limit, which paged the watcher every hour for standing memory pressure that was never an incident; the gate itself is unchanged (#255).
- `load-watch admit` refused on compressor churn above 20000 only when load (over 1.5 per core) or pageins corroborate thrash, or above 60000 on its own (`LW_CHURN_THRASH_PER_CORE`, `LW_MAX_COMPRESSOR_HARD_S`); standing churn with idle CPU had refused nearly every agent launch, review and test run for hours (#255).
- `ade-claim` Paseo plugin: a true observe-only mode for **every** hook (#638 D1). Before, `ADE_CLAIM_DRY_RUN` gated only the W2 create; the archive release, owner bind and `agent.open.before` (`ADE_CLAIM_ID` export, `libexec/agent-bin` `PATH` prepend) still acted. Now one predicate (`resolveClaimMode`) is re-read on each hook call (no daemon restart): env `ADE_CLAIM_DRY_RUN` forces observe, else `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/ade-claim.mode` must say `enforce` (own-user plain file, no symlink, not group/world-writable, 64-byte cap); a missing or odd file means observe, so a fresh install observes. In observe every hook logs its planned argv or env delta and does nothing else (and never throws), and each call appends a line to `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/ade-claim-observe.jsonl` (0600, rotates at 1 MiB) for the soak. (#641)
- `bin/route_agent.py` and `bin/acp-dispatch` lost the Pi capacity gate, the `agy` model-family mapping, the agy report-path and resume special cases and the
  copilot, cline and zcode provider-map entries. (#297)
- Changed `load-watch`: it attributes a spike to a Paseo agent from `paseo ls -g --background --json` by working directory (the walk up stopping at the agent's own CLI), tells it with `paseo send --no-wait --json` (reading `.status`; `queued` is not delivered), sends work that several agents share or that it cannot attribute to the operator, and drops the relay.py channel and `watcher set --relay`/`--sid` (slim-down E5) (#297).
- Changed `context-budget-check`: `install`/`uninstall` removed (exit 2); it runs as a nightly Paseo schedule (J3) (#297).
- Changed `context-watch`: it no longer imports session-finder's `fleet.py` (slim-down E5) (#297).
- Changed the quota-guard and fleet-governor successor advice and the handoff skill: a successor is launched with `env -u PASEO_AGENT_ID -u PASEO_AGENT_CWD paseo run …` and archives its predecessor by id once it has answered; the context nudge leaves compaction to auto-compaction (slim-down E4) (#297).
- Changed standing order 10's mechanics: a sourceless send (deadline, load-watch) is delivered when `paseo send --json`'s `.status` reads sent or completed; an agent's send is accepted (exit 5, queued by the daemon) and proven only by the peer's `ack <id>`; `paseo send`'s own exit status is never read as delivery (slim-down E3) (#297).
- `bin/ade-backup-stores` backs up only the surviving providers' stores (zcode, crush, copilot, agy, qwen, cline, pi, muse removed; Antigravity's IDE and ACP stores kept); `bin/skill-everywhere`'s usage says to run `/reload-skills` after linking (slim-down E10). (#297)
- Changed the workspace claims: `ade_claims.py new` stamps `expires_at` (`--ttl`, default 72 h) and an expired claim guards nothing; `ade-git-guard claim ls` lists the registry; the uncalled `ade_claims.py show`, `find` and `set-state` verbs are gone (slim-down E8) (#297).
- Changed `acp-dispatch` into a shim over `paseo run --no-wait --json` and `paseo wait --json` (status field mapped to the exit codes: idle classifies the report, timeout 124, permission 4, error 3): agents are Paseo providers (claude, codex, antigravity, cursor, opencode, hermes, omp; `--model M` becomes `--provider P/M`, `--mode` always passed); `--perm read-only`/`deny` run Claude through `bin/review-launch` and are best effort for every other provider (its default mode, a brief note, `perm_note` in the record); the in-flight cap and reservation are re-judged under a lock after the `running` record is written (exit 5); `wait --once` is a non-blocking look (75 while unfinished); the `load-watch admit` gate stays, plus a Paseo-side cap on live `ade.via=acp-dispatch` agents (LW_MAX_AGENTS, default 14, exit 75); the frontier admission (agent_guard identity policy on the launch argv, estimate floor, in-flight reservation, per-wave cap) and `footer`, `check`, `outcome` stay; `tell` writes the inbox and reads `paseo send`'s status (queued is accepted, exit 5) (slim-down E6) (#297).
- Changed `skills/dialogue`: an `acp` route (now an alias of the new `paseo` kind) runs `paseo run --no-wait --json` plus `paseo wait` and reads the turn from a reply file the prompt names, instead of `acp-run` (slim-down E6) (#297).
- Changed `bin/route_agent.py`'s registry to `paseo provider ls --json` (enabled and available rows; `loading` rows retried twice over 6 s, then absent with a note) in place of `acp-run --list`, the headless TUIs and Orca; `services --check-models` checks every suggested model against `paseo provider models <provider>` (exit 1 missing, 2 unreadable); the `[discovery]` table folded into `[routing]` (`never_route`, `aliases`) (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `route_agent.py`'s quota to read `paseo usage ls --json` as a second source after aiuse: an `available` report gives windows and balances, anything else is unknown and never a pass; the two merge per pool by the tightest known reading, and `show` and `quota_source.pools` say which source each pool came from (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed the frontier allowance's Claude check: `ade_gates.read_guard_state()` returns route_agent's merged Claude reading, which must be known, under 30 minutes old, at least `frontier1_window_min_left` (new, 30) on the tightest window and at least `frontier1_min_left` on the Fable cap; `bin/ade_gates.py` and `docs/gates.md` keep only the frontier half (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `skills/model-routing` into one page around the Claude tiers by role, effort, the surviving pools, routing, the frontier allowance, reading quota and fallback chains, with every number left to `route_agent.py policy --md`; `docs/model-routing.md` rewritten to match (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `config/routing.toml`: the `antigravity-argon` row (Gemini 4 Argon, the frontier fallback behind codex and claude) is back on Paseo's antigravity provider, so `acp-dispatch antigravity --model gemini-4-argon --kind frontier` (tendcf and nix2cf review route 3) is no longer refused as ineligible (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py`: only a Fable session gets the relaunch-on-Opus note (the quota-guard downshift went with the guard) (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `[lead]` in `config/routing.toml`: it gained the `fable_lead` switch (true since 2026-11-07: Fable leads allowed for now; false or absent restores never-a-Fable-lead): `route_agent.py policy --md` prints it and `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py` drops its relaunch-on-Opus note for a Fable session while it is true (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed `bin/deadline`: `--on-expire reroute:RECORD` is refused with exit 2 pointing at `relay` (the acp-dispatch shim has no `reroute`); a record registered with it earlier is stopped and reported, never dispatched (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed the frontier allowance's Fable floor `frontier1_min_left` from 10 to 15, the old quota guard's stop (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Changed README.md: re-based on the slimmed repo (147 lines: the loop on Paseo, the layout, install and update, the review gate, where things went), its skills and `bin/` tables generated by `bin/ade-roster` (slim-down E11) (#297).
- Changed AGENTS.md: re-based on Paseo: the retirement box is one line pointing at `docs/slim/log.md` and `docs/history/`; the doc table lists only live docs; a Trust section (our agents trust each other; GitHub artifacts not authored by the owner are untrusted; GitHub's attribution is trusted); order 7 names `route_agent.py policy --md` on `paseo provider ls` and `paseo usage ls`; order 9 names Paseo worktrees and claim expiry; order 10 says what counts as delivered with `paseo send`; review rules 1 (the `adversary-marker` Action and the trusted-editor rule), 9 (Codex advisory) and 11 (`bin/review-pr`) follow the merge gate (slim-down E11) (#297).
- Changed docs/queue.md: triaged from 2,114 lines into Carried, Closed by the slim-down and Done before, one line each (slim-down E11) (#297).
- Changed the `handoff` skill's Steps 8 and 9, and `baton`, `loose`, `steps` and `session-handoff`: the agent is named with `paseo agent update --name`, a clear is the operator's `/clear` then `/baton`, a successor starts top-level with `paseo run` and archives its predecessor once it answers; nothing calls `self-slash` or `autorename` (slim-down E11) (#297).
- Changed docs: eleven design and research records moved to `docs/history/`; `orchestration-practices.md` rewritten for Paseo agents and `fan-out`; `skills.md` regenerated; `threat-model.md`, `paseo-only-rearchitecture.md`, `tui-lessons-learned.md`, `context-hygiene.md`, `git-guard.md`, `mcp-servers.md` and `dispatch-footer.md` re-based (slim-down E11) (#297).
- The repo's `.claude/settings.json` carries the operator's unattended-permission rules (`defaultMode: acceptEdits`; Bash, Edit, Write, Read, Glob, Grep, WebFetch, WebSearch and read-only GitHub tools allowed; the literal `git push --force` / `git push -f` prefixes, `rm -rf /*` and three GitHub write tools denied: a tripwire, not a boundary, since `--force-with-lease`, `+branch` refspecs and `git -C` forms pass) beside the graft status line; the operator accepted the risk the local review flagged (2026-11-07, `docs/slim/ledger-rulings.md`) (#297)
- Made `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=0` a provisioned default in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` and `bin/claude-settings-env`, so a new machine or rewritten settings file keeps the fork gate closed for Agent-tool background launches in interactive sessions, which keeps Claude Code's `agent_summary` background-agent label calls off (#251).
- Exit 69 from `deadline install` and `pr-policy install`/`uninstall` now means "no usable scheduler" (it was "not macOS"). (#291)
- Claude Code now starts with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1` and no longer sets `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS`
  (operator, 2026-11-21: stop using agent teams by default, use code mode by default), in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` and in the
  Codex `[shell_environment_policy.set]` block that Claude children inherit. (#844)
- The Codex template's `[shell_environment_policy.set]` now also carries `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL = "sonnet"` and
  `CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT = "0"`, as the Claude template and the live config already did, so `codex-env-policy-apply` no longer
  sees them as drift. (#844)
- Split the Codex routing pool by billing identity: `codex` now routes gpt-6.1-sol (judgment/code/research) and
  gpt-6-astra (frontier) on the OpenAI API prepaid credits (pool family `openai_api`, fail-closed when unknown or
  exhausted), while a new `codex-subscription` service routes gpt-6-luna (bulk/mechanical) on the ChatGPT subscription
  windows (pool family `default`). `canary_pool()` now also matches a service's `pool` family and `pool_family`, so the
  `openai_api` canary gates. (#776)
- `tests/test_codex_shell_env_policy.py`: the helpers `rendered`, `policy_toml`, `Policy` and `tool_env` now carry type annotations
  (typed dicts, `ClassVar` and `@override` on `Policy`, narrowed `CODEX` and pipe handles in `tool_env`, explicit `_ =` on ignored call
  results). No test behaviour changes; it clears every basedpyright error and unknown-type warning in that range. (#851)
- `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl`: removed the `claude-code-mods` marketplace and its `context-bar` plugin from Codex (a Claude Code UI mod, kept for Claude), added `project_doc_max_bytes = 65536` so the global + project instruction docs stop truncating at the 32768 default, captured the live-drift additions (`anthropic-agent-skills` marketplace with `document-skills`/`example-skills`, and `typescript-lsp@claude-plugins-official`), and documented the "trusted Git repository root escapes Codex home" root cause (the `~/.codex/.tmp` symlink escaping `~/.codex`, raised by git_policy.rs before git discovery; fix by making `.tmp` a real directory — not by converting the git marketplaces to local). (#781)
- Unified routing, gates, model tiers, install catalog and context budgeting in `config/ade.toml`, deleting the five legacy production config files and retaining one frozen repo-shaped fixture for explicit legacy readers and projection-drift tests. Added atomic, outside-byte-preserving `ade-model-tiers promote|set` writes to the unified tier subtree. Updated the active edit-surface documentation. (#766)
- `ade-backup-stores` dry-run resolves `ADE_BACKUP_HERMES` the same way as the real run; `seal_and_hash` opens files `O_NOFOLLOW`; scrub drops triggers before DELETE; CRED_RE covers authorization/cookie/jwt/refresh/session_key/pwd; exclusions add `.ssh`, `.netrc` and AWS credential paths; usage documents exit codes, dry-run schema limits and the schedule. (#402)
- Made `ade-github-setup apply --yes` refuse browser App create/install confirm
  (setup-plan S7) and refuse private-key / client-id store (operator stdin
  only; never prints PEM) (#462).
- Made environment deployment-branch-policies require exactly the configured
  branch (type=branch): `apply --yes` deletes leftovers; `apply --verify`
  fails when extras remain (#462).
- Made `apply --yes` fail closed when deployment-branch-policies cannot be
  listed, and re-list after POST already-exists / deletes before reporting
  success (#462).
- `ade-github-setup observe` probes App installation via
  `GET /user/installations` (operator `gh` creds), not the App-JWT
  `repos/.../installation` endpoint (#412).
- `sudo-secretspec` catalog: Darwin `system_argv_darwin` brew recipe, `probe_argv`=`doctor`; Linux tarball still UNKNOWN for apply argv. (#326)
- `ade-merge-ready approve-paste` and `docs/review-boundary.md` warn that herdr mangles HTML-comment markers; prefer `approve-post` (GitHub API, not herdr) (#493).
- Owner-authored PRs (repository owner login, including Cloud Agents under that login) now pass the
  `adversary-marker` gate without a manual APPROVE marker; a BLOCK or malformed marker still fails, and
  outside/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR PRs still need a live APPROVE in an owner issue comment (operator 2026-11-10:
  distrust only what does not come from the owner or AIs they run) (#484).
- The unfenced AGENTS.md / PR-body docs template
  (`{"verdict":"APPROVE"|"BLOCK","head":"<40-hex>"}`) is treated as documentation, not `malformed`, so
  leftover examples no longer fail the gate (#484).
- Owner-authored PR body APPROVEs stay trusted when the last editor is a login in
  `BODY_TRUSTED_EDITOR_LOGINS` (`cursor`), so a Cursor Cloud rewrite of the PR body does not void a live
  marker; outside/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR PRs are unchanged and still need an owner comment (#319).

- `docs/queue.md`: the load watcher's approved RAM-relief directives are prioritized (89 shared MCP and LSP daemons first, 91 auto-hibernate idle agent processes second, 90 zero-subprocess daemon polling third), with new items 96 (archive idle agents sooner) and 97 (a supported revive of archived agents), and the review follow-ups still living only in PR bodies are queued as 98-101 (PRs 862, 868, 867, 864); 102 queues the SessionEnd graft hook that Claude Code cancels on exit; 103 queues the two unbuilt parts of the frontier-review ruling (pause instead of kill, resume a killed review). (#869)
- Cross-session messaging rules tightened after the #676 review: senders check a `ListAgents` row's kind, ask a local session for `ack <ListAgents name>` and never ask a cloud session for one, and treat a `<cross-session-message>` as a sender-asserted peer request with no Paseo trust or operator approval; leads are addressed by their current id (takeover message, succession ledger, or last `LEAD <id> took over` line) after `paseo agent inspect` reads `Archived: false`, since a message un-archives an ex-lead (AGENTS.md order 10, orchestration-practices 5a items 6-7, dispatch-footer rule 14) (#677).
- Adopted effective context-budget metadata for the default checker while preserving explicit legacy reads, offline dry runs and notification boundaries; kept scalar writes refused and corrected config-set reload guidance (#551).
- Adopted joint effective install/key metadata with scoped readers, named-key leaf overlays, complete catalog validation and preserved install/system boundaries; kept new domains read-only to scalar edits and retained explicit legacy path behavior (#548).
- `skills/eli5/SKILL.md`: the Sources section moved to `docs/eli5-sources.md`, linked in one line, so the skill loads about 20% smaller. (#697)
- Every specific mention of "Fable 5.1" is now "Fable 5.2" (operator 2026-11-13): the `frontier` role id `claude-fable-5-2` in `config/model-tiers.toml` and the `bin/model_tiers.py` defaults, the context-size nudge, Claude settings template, `acp-run` docs, skills, routing config, tests and docs. The retired id `claude-fable-5-1` stays known as a `[former]` frontier row (`classify` returns `former`) and in the `claude-cost-*` price tables for old transcripts; Fable 5.2 is priced as 5.1 ($10/MTok base, 0.025 cache read) until a source for 5.2 is checked. (#627)
- `skills/fan-out` names every flag a direct `bin/review-launch --mode trusted` launch requires (`--trusted-author`, `--head`, `--schema`, `-C`) and a fresh `--evidence-dir` per attempt; a brief written from the old text omitted `--trusted-author` and the launch refused with exit 2; it also says the launcher only records the login (2026-11-14) (#667)
- GUI-hog `[freeze.auto] skip`: apps never frozen automatically, even if listed in `order` (shipped: Beeper Desktop, whose MCP API hangs while frozen and which uses almost no CPU, and CodexBar, whose freeze stops the aiuse quota readings); a manual Freeze stays possible, the overlay can add to the list but not remove from it. Docs: `docs/gui-hog-dialog.md` P7, and a "Deep freeze (open question)" section (P8). (#859)
- GUI-hog: Chrome's native-messaging hosts (1Password-BrowserSupport, chrome-use) run from outside its bundle and made Chrome read as hosting a build ("no actions"); they are now kept running by an absolute keep-running rule, so Chrome offers actions again (not yet exercised live). Docs: section P of `docs/gui-hog-dialog.md`. (#859)
- Retired automatic routing through uninstalled omp and OpenCode clients while preserving their pool metadata for the Pi migration. (#310)
- Routed qualified Z.ai and retained Cursor subscription profiles through native Pi/Paseo dispatch while preserving separate pools, vendor judgment/frontier gates and reserved services. (#315)
- Reused an existing Paseo workspace on ordinary `acp-dispatch` launches via `--workspace`, verified caller inspect, or `PASEO_WORKSPACE_ID`, validated against `paseo workspace ls --json` and `-C`. (#309)
- Required explicit workspace selection when caller inspection omitted workspace identity or failed, instead of silently falling back to an ambient workspace. (#309)
- `route_agent.py` never degrades a `frontier` slice from Fable to Opus (operator 2026-11-13: when Fable is out for a window, wait): `suggest_model` no longer swaps in Opus, `route`/`plan` do not offer claude for `frontier` while its Fable sublimit is under `frontier1_min_left` (before, only when a fallback pool listed claude), other frontier families keep their order, and with no frontier pool `plan` says `WAIT for Fable 5.2` and names the Fable window's reset when the quota reading carries one (new `sublimit_resets` on the claude pool entry; the later reset wins when readings merge, and the line says `no other frontier pool was eligible`). (#652)
- The context-size nudge (`claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py`) computes each session's real compaction wall at run time (`compaction_wall`: the lower of `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` / `modelSettings` / `autoCompactWindow` and the model's own window, less the 33k buffer; unreadable or unknown settings keep today's 267k) and derives every handoff tier from it: the "hand off at the next task completion" note fires at wall - 80k (187k on a 267k wall, was a fixed 120k, which made a lead hand itself off at ~150k with 117k left), the hard tier at wall - 50k by total only, Hermes paging at max(400k, hard tier). The `ASK_GROWTH` trigger, which fired the hard tier at ~175k, is removed. The messages print the computed wall and the tokens left, keep the 2026-11-14 "hand off yourself" ruling, and say a finished task alone is no reason to hand off or compact below the boundary tier. `bin/context-watch` now calls the hook's `compaction_wall` and `_tiers` (one copy), so its ceiling falls back to 267k, not a 200k guess, when nothing is known. (#862)
- `bin/ade-self-session finish` retires a predecessor in Paseo `error` status (out of quota) at once after the successor's
  acknowledgement, without the stable-idle wait (operator ruling 2026-11-21). (#852)
- Record versioned fork deployment baselines, exact recovery direction, reservation intent and uncertain release in the same Journal; require explicit rollback resume and release reconciliation authority. (#0)
- Preserve original held reservation context for confirmed settled failures and separately authorized same-process recovery; reject unknown outcomes without re-BEGIN or token persistence. (#0)
- Graft files in git is now ADE policy (operator, 2026-11-14): commit the portable `.ignore`, keep `/graft/` gitignored
  (its cache embeds local paths), forks keep their upstream's layout; the graft `.gitignore` papercut draft drops
  `/.ignore` from the exclude recipe and asks upstream for the `/.graft/` switch and a docs note on `.ignore`. (#702)
- `.gitignore` and `.ignore` list the six untracked paths `graft init` writes into every fresh worktree (`/.adal/`, `/.gemini/`, `/.grok/`, `/.kiro/`, `/.windsurf/`, `/.github/copilot-instructions.md`), anchored at the root, so a new worktree stays clean; `/graft/`, `.agents/` and the `.ignore` graft re-admit are unchanged, and `docs/queue.md` Carried 47 records what remains (#743).
- `skills/model-routing/SKILL.md` and `skills/tell-coding-bot/SKILL.md`: record the operator ruling (2026-11-21) that the Grok Bot "Coding" is not an ordinary delegation target — take coding work on yourself, in sub-agents where appropriate — and is only the try-first destination for tasks that need real UI/browser interaction. The route is not working today (nothing exists at `~/.local/bin/coding-bot`), so nothing is ever claimed sent. (#848)
- `tests/test_skill_frontmatter.py`: every `skills/*/SKILL.md` frontmatter must parse as YAML with a name and description (the `tell-coding-bot` description is now quoted, since an unquoted `: ` made it invalid). (#848)
- `skills/fan-out/SKILL.md` says what exit 2 means from a `load-watch` older than `--refused-since`, and the `release` exit-66 wording reads "released over 30 s ago, or expired" (#722).
- The GUI-hog trigger is live: a `would-show` decision now also starts the dialog detached when the operator owns the console and the bundle is fresh (single instance by flock;
  the build never waits on it). The dry-run log and `LW_GUI_HOG=0` are kept. A missing or stale bundle is one event line a day and no dialog. The sampler rewrites
  `gui-hog/snapshot.json` while a hold is active or the dialog runs. (#729)
- Swept docs and skill bodies for the 2026-10-31 retirement of herdr (bar its two bridges), Orca and BB from djbclark-ade: the `bigteam`, `diskfree`, `cfenginewatch`, `steps`, `loose`, `baton`, `peer-relay`, `ralph-tui-orchestration`, `model-routing` and `handoff` skills no longer route through them (or mark the passage `retired from djbclark-ade (2026-10-31)` where the `self-slash`/`launch.py` host branches live until D4/D6); `session-finder`, `helm` (and `auto.md`), `autorename/workspace-layout.md`, `docs/helm.md` and `docs/coding-factory.md` carry a retirement banner pointing at `AGENTS.md` for what is still live; `docs/model-routing.md`, `agents/README.md` and `zcode/commands/autorename.md` corrected; no code changed (#219).
- Set `network.force_ipv4: true` in the Hermes config template (dead IPv6 route to openrouter.ai) and queued the
  Paseo-schedule reconcile gap and the ade-claim rollout issue (#639).
- `bin/ade-worker-run` adopts the WORKER window registered to a predecessor whose fresh acknowledged self-handoff names the lead, when `paseo agent inspect` shows the predecessor archived, or idle/closed with no pending permissions; a running, unreadable, stale or other-successor record keeps the refusal and names the reason; `archive-window` follows the same adoption. (#755)
- `skills/fan-out`: a lead's background `wait-file` on a worker's done marker passes `--timeout 7000` (the 1200 s default exits 124 before most code slices finish; a 124 means re-check `STATUS:` and re-arm) (#837)
- `bin/load-watch`: `ADMIT_PER_CORE` rises from 2.0 to 5.0 (now env-overridable via `LW_ADMIT_PER_CORE`) and `ADMIT_BOOST_PER_CORE` from 4.0 to 5.0, per the operator ruling (2026-11-21) that bigmac's 10-core Mac stays usable at load 50. Runaway-process and thrash alerts are unchanged (`LW_MIN_CORES` 1.5, compressor churn 50k pages/s, Chrome 0.9 cores). The watcher's steer/ping line `LW_LOAD_PER_CORE` rises from 4.0 to 5.0 too (operator ruling 2026-11-21) so admit and steer agree at load 50; `LW_ADMIT_PER_CORE` has a 0.01 floor. The calm boost (5.0) now equals the default base, so it only matters to a caller that passes a lower base (bg's 3.0 until its own change lands). (#854)
- `skills/fan-out` section 3 and `docs/orchestration-practices.md` rule 4 now tell the launcher what the deleted dispatch shim enforced: `load-watch admit --wait N --max-agents` (N 300-600; exit 75 = launch nothing) before each `paseo run` and wave, a fresh `route_agent.py plan` per slice, `Choice.dispatch` unchanged. (#705)
- `bin/load-watch` docstring, comments and the `admit-log summary` text say that `bg` now calls `admit --wait CHUNK` and gets the starvation valve only after 300 s inside one chunk, not that it polls with `--wait 0`. (#705)
- `load-watch release` exits 66 (EX_NOINPUT) for a reservation that does not exist and keeps 1 for a lock or write failure; `load-watch admit --wait S --refused-since EPOCH` (env `LW_REFUSED_SINCE`, the flag wins; cap `LW_STARVE_REFUSED_SINCE_MAX_S`, 3600 s) starts the starvation valve's 300 s clock at the caller's own hold start, so `bg`'s 420 s chunks can reach it. Hard gates and the one-forced-admit-per-120 s gap are unchanged. (#712)
- `load-watch admit --help` for `--max-pageins` names the corroborated 1000/s line (`LW_MAX_PAGEINS_CORROBORATED_S`), `docs/orchestration-practices.md` quotes the 2.0 admit base, and comments note `bg`'s admit base is 3.0 since 2026-11-14 (#686).
- `load-watch admit` defaults retuned for this 32 GB, 10-core (8P+2E) Mac from its own measurements (2026-11-13: compressor churn p50 7.3k, p90 13k, max 25k pages/s; pageins p50 689, p90 1280, max 1916/s): page-ins limit 1000 -> 4000/s, with the old 1000/s line kept only when corroborated by free memory below the floor or churn above the admit limit (`LW_MAX_PAGEINS_CORROBORATED_S`; swap-ins are not measured yet); compressor churn limit 20000 -> 30000 (hard line 60000 kept); free-memory floor `LW_MIN_FREE_MB` 300 -> 1000; per-core base 1.5 -> 2.0 (boost 4.0 kept); agent cap `LW_MAX_AGENTS` 14 -> 20. The calm-boost line follows the churn limit (20000 -> 30000). The admit result and line report the 1000 line as `max_pageins` when it decided. Stale "16 GB, 8-core" comments corrected (#657).
- `load-watch admit` gained a swap-ins gate (`--max-swapins`, `LW_MAX_SWAPINS_S`, default 300/s; swap-ins above 20/s also corroborate the 1000/s page-ins line), a free-memory floor that scales with the RAM (35 MB per GiB above 4 GiB, clamped to 300..1000: 980 on 32 GB), a starvation valve for `admit --wait` (a caller refused for 5 minutes on soft gates only is admitted, at most once per 2 minutes machine-wide; hard gates never yield; prints `admit ok forced=starved ...`) and a tuning log (`admit-log.jsonl`, `LW_ADMIT_LOG`) with `load-watch admit-log summary [--days N]` (#695)
- `bin/local-review` asked `load-watch admit --wait` before starting Codex (`--load-wait SECONDS`, default 1800, env `LOCAL_REVIEW_LOAD_WAIT`; `--no-load-gate` skipped it, and the same LW_* thresholds acp-dispatch passes were passed through); a machine still too hot after the wait exited 75 and that run filed no result; a gate that could not run warned and the review started; a run that waited reported a peer's final result for the head instead of reviewing it again; the result recorded `load_gate` (#242)
- `load-watch ci decide DIR local "why"` now needs djbclark's fresh approval, the same `sudo-ask -k` Allow dialog and Touch ID as `bg approve-local`: a `local` decision (new, or replacing `ci`/`pending`) is refused with nothing written when it is denied, cancelled or cannot be shown (no GUI session, `sudo-ask` missing). `ci` and `pending` decisions, reads and an existing `local` row stay ungated; each outcome is logged in `~/.local/state/bg/approvals.log`. (#744)
- `bin/load-watch`: `ci decide DIR pending` is gated exactly like `local` (Allow dialog + Touch ID, logged in `approvals.log`); an existing row of the same mode only changes its note, `ci` stays ungated. Closes the ungated `pending` route (a `ci` row downgraded to run here, or a `DIR/*` glob) from the PR 744 review. (#758)
- `load-watch admit` fails toward waiting (2026-11-01; the load watcher's d8891c91 measured load 35-70 as memory starvation: ~64 MB free, ~12k compressions+decompressions/s, ~1760 pageins/s, 31+ agent processes, and the 4.0 calm boost let a slice start in every brief dip). It now refuses (exit 75) while free+speculative memory is below 600 MB (`--min-free-mb`, `LW_MIN_FREE_MB`), compressions+decompressions exceed 20000/s (`--max-mem-churn`, `LW_MAX_COMPRESSOR_S`; was 60000, sampled over 2 s) or pageins exceed 1000/s (`--max-pageins`, `LW_MAX_PAGEINS_S`). The live-`acp-run` cap (`--max-agents [N]`, bare = 14 or `LW_MAX_AGENTS`, 0 = off; one `ps`, exact argv match) gates only NEW agent starts: it is off unless the caller passes the flag, `acp-dispatch` always does and `bg` never does (a `bg` test run starts no agent, and at the cap it would starve the slices it counts). `acp-dispatch` reads `LW_MAX_AGENTS`, `LW_MIN_FREE_MB`, `LW_MAX_COMPRESSOR_S` and `LW_MAX_PAGEINS_S` from its own environment and passes them as flags (and `LW_BOOST_CALM_S` as an environment variable; every other `LW_*` is still scrubbed), and its exit-75 line and `load_gate.refused` name the gate that said no. The line and `--json` report each value plus `refused`. A `vm_stat`, `/proc` or `ps` read that FAILS where the tool exists (timeout, non-zero exit, unparsable) refuses (`memory_unreadable`, `agents_unreadable`); only an absent tool leaves its gate open, listed in `unreadable` (Linux: /proc/meminfo, /proc/vmstat). The 4.0 boost needs 10 unbroken calm minutes (`LW_BOOST_CALM_S`; `calm.json` under an flock, judged with the module defaults whatever flags the caller passed, fed by `admit` and the 20 s sampler; any hot reading resets it) and its churn line is the same 20000. `is_acp_run` also matches a framework `Python` and `-X`/`-W` options. Admissions under the cap (counted from a `ps` table that must contain the process itself, else the read fails and refuses) are reserved (`agent-reservations.json` under an flock; `admit --json` and the text line print the `reservation` id, `load-watch release ID` and acp-dispatch release it once the agent is countable by `ps`, when it ends, or on any exit after admission (a `--detach` caller hands it to the runner instead; the countable check includes the spawned root process), `LW_RESERVE_S` 1200 s is the backstop, `--no-reserve` records none but still counts the held ones) so concurrent dispatchers cannot all pass at 13 of 14 and a runner waiting for capacity keeps its place (`load-watch renew ID`, called by the dispatch every 60 s while it waits or starts, extends it by `LW_RESERVE_S`); every real reading feeds the calm clock even for a `--boost-per-core 0` caller; `acp-dispatch` ignores a zero `LW_MAX_COMPRESSOR_S`/`LW_MAX_PAGEINS_S` (admit exits 2 on it), treats an admit exit 2 as a refusal (never start anyway), and records the validated knobs in the job record (`load_knobs`) for detached runners. Docs: `skills/loadwatch` §2a (#210).
- Publish final NATS acceptance, execution history and implementation plan, and reconcile the historical rollout gates without expanding enrollment. (#779)
- Reconfirmed the lead's acceptance of the completed NATS implementation and bounded rollout, and recorded the final post-soak investigation of a self-contained Paseo plugin and widely available system-package dependencies without authorizing an immediate rewrite or retirement. (#809)
- `ade-net-resume` resumes a quota-stopped agent instead of only logging `would-resume-quota` (its launchd template now passes `--apply`), and gains a preventive `--transition-below PERCENT` pass that asks every agent whose pool headroom is nearly spent to hand off or switch account before it stops. Eligibility is uniform: the current lead counts exactly like a descendant, so a lead stopped by a provider window is no longer unrecoverable. (#769)
- Added `--quota` to the net-resume launchd template while keeping the scheduled scan dry-run without `--apply`, and pinned the rendered arguments and lead substitution in tests. (#615)
- `oc-go-key` skill: `use` reloads an OpenCode server only when it has no active session (a mid-turn reload left a Paseo seat stuck "running"); a busy server is deferred with exit 3, `oc-go-key reload`/`pending` and `--wait-idle S` finish it (#264).
- `--acp-arg=--meta` is no longer the documented example of a dash-leading value (it is refused for guarded agents); the docs use `--acp-arg=--tripwire-exempt-kinds` (#275).
- `docs/orchestration-practices.md` section 5a item 6: a local Claude Code session outside Paseo is reached directly with
  `ListAgents` + `SendMessage`, never through the operator's clipboard. (#676)
- Required native catalog/control evidence for ordinary agent YOLO creates, preserved explicit restrictive and external-security-critical scope, refused saved mode drift on resume, and stopped accepting prompting defaults in transitional launchers. (#568)
- Owner trust in the merge gate also requires that the operator pushed the head (`head_pusher_check`: the newest ref update of the PR branch, from the repository activity API, is to this head by the pinned operator `User`; otherwise the owner PR is judged like an outside PR) and pins the operator by GitHub user id as well as login (`TRUSTED_OWNER_USER_IDS`); `bin/review-pr` and `bin/ade-merge-ready` refuse an `is_bot` author. (#675)
- `ade-merge-ready approve-post --via auto` now always posts an issue comment (it edited the body of owner-authored PRs, which the gate ignores once someone else pushed the head); `--via body` still forces the body edit (#675)
- `bin/review-pr` gives the full-permission (`yolo`) runtime only to a PR the operator authored (login and numeric user id, `gh api users/LOGIN`) AND whose head the operator pushed (the merge gate's `head_pusher_check`, reused through `gh api`); a head pushed by anyone else, a fork head, or any unreadable answer is reviewed restricted, and `--runtime yolo` is refused with the reason (#683)
- The gate's branch-name check is ASCII-only and a full match (no `$`-before-newline, no Unicode letters); the `pr_merge` residual and `docs/code-review.md` rule 1 (owner trust and its pusher requirement) are written down; `approve-post`'s paste hint says every tip gets a comment (#683)
- Owner trust is keyed to the operator's GitHub user login (`TRUSTED_OWNER_LOGINS` in `bin/adversary_marker.py`, also used by `bin/review-pr` and `bin/ade-merge-ready`), not to whoever owns the repository, so the move to the `frdminc` org does not change who is trusted (but see the write-access precondition in `docs/review-boundary.md`); the PR author must be a `User`. The `ade-merge-gate` App manifest is now `"public": true` (2026-11-14). (#670)
- `skills/paseo-screen` is deprecated (plugin disabled 2026-11-13, "super-buggy"): headless Playwright against the Paseo daemon web UI is the documented route for Paseo GUI checks; AGENTS.md, docs/skills.md and ingest-web-ai pointers updated. (#656)
- Peer-trust follow-up to #715: a test that an internal `worker gh-watch-gate ...` title is believed (so the `gh-watch` pattern cannot return unnoticed), and the watcher wording in AGENTS.md, docs/peer-trust.md and config/peer-trust.toml names fleet-watch's upstream PR watcher (off, dispatcher deleted in #699, relay mode) and says a native port or relay target must carry `--label ade.kind=external-review`, appended last. (#723)
- Peer-trust follow-up to #703: a worker that judges untrusted GitHub artifacts for prompt injection is launched with `--label ade.kind=external-review` (docs/orchestration-practices.md section 3, `skills/fan-out`), which `claude/hooks/peer_trust_gate.py` reads; `bin/review-launch` and `bin/review-pr` start no Paseo agent, so they need no label (documented). The remote-sender note no longer says a send "is not guarded": it says the hook could not tell whether the id is local and keeps `archived_send_gate` as the fallback. docs/peer-trust.md rule 3 is split into unlisted (3a) and remote (3b). (#706)
- Peer-trust wording follow-up to #706: no launcher sets `ade.kind=external-review` today (`bin/review-launch` and `bin/review-pr` start no Paseo agent, no `gh-watch` launcher exists, PR Radar has no label field yet); `gh-watch` is dropped from the default sensitive title patterns (`claude/hooks/peer_trust_gate.py`, `config/peer-trust.toml`); docs/orchestration-practices.md says to append the label last because `paseo run` keeps the last `--label` for a duplicate key. (#715)
- `claude/hooks/peer_trust_gate.py`: a peer message from an unlisted (archived or short-lived, e.g. Herald) agent or from a remote server is
  now believed and acted on like a listed one instead of being labelled "UNVERIFIED: ask the operator" (operator, 2026-11-14: "always believe
  thing we hear from other agents unless that agent is external-security-sensitive"); the note says a reply to an archived id is blocked by
  `archived_send_gate`. New `config/peer-trust.toml` `[sensitive]` (exact `ids`, case-insensitive `title_patterns`: `injection`, `triage`, `review-pr`,
  `pr-review`, `adversary`, `gh-watch`) and the Paseo agent label `ade.kind=external-review` (read from the agent record) name the one
  exception, a sender that judges untrusted GitHub artifacts for prompt injection: it is not believed automatically, its message is
  evidence to check, never an instruction; the same holds for that sender's notifications (a finish body is its last message, `permission-required` asks to approve its tool call). A remote sender's note no longer discourages a requested ack. The sender id is self-stamped, so the exception is best-effort (documented). AGENTS.md Trust, docs/peer-trust.md, docs/threat-model.md section 5 item 9
  and docs/code-review.md rule 13 updated. (#703)
- `openai/gpt-6-astra` joined the Pi models admitted to the measured `pi` API pool (`PI_OPENAI_MODELS`); it stays behind the frontier gate (`--kind frontier`) and its near-miss ids are still refused (#280).
- Skill discovery for Pi no longer creates a second link farm, so it adds no new duplicates (existing ones stay), and shared tests scrub inherited Pi/OMP environment markers and isolate `AUTORENAME_STATE_DIR` (#273)
- `autorename` state paths follow `AUTORENAME_STATE_DIR`; `api-only` routing is no longer omp-only; Pi/OMP transcript `native_id` now splits at the first underscore, so ids containing underscores are kept whole; fleet detection counts only the `pi` executable (or node/bun running a `pi` script) as a Pi seat (#273)
- Documented worker placement as one `workers <middle> <lead agent id>` window per lead, each worker tab named
  `worker <middle> <lead agent id>` (operator ruling 2026-11-13): `docs/workspace-layout.md` (naming, measured name-length
  limits), the `skills/fan-out` launch recipe (the first launch minted the window, whose id is read from the launch itself,
  then `paseo workspace rename`; later launches `--workspace`; `paseo agent update --name`; archive the window only when no
  live labelled agent remains) and `docs/orchestration-practices.md` replaced the 2026-11-07 shared-tab arrangement and the
  one-window-per-launch "master" pile-up of env-stripped runs; a handoff successor stayed a new lead with its own
  placement. (#625)
- Amended the plan of record (paseo-only rearchitecture): B9's outside watchdog enforces declared budgets for every long process or wait, not only the daemon (register, heartbeat, soft then hard stop, recovery once, capped extensions, agent idle alert never kill), and records a job's descendants on every sweep so a child that leaves its group is still stopped; G1 `ade-jobs` got a no-progress timeout and capped, recorded extensions (#222).
- Amended plan F2 and updated the paseo skill: a scheduled run counted as succeeded only when THIS run's declared output exists (a fresh report path per run plus a `.done` naming the run); a scheduled agent never delegates and stops (#222).
- Codex's everyday-use quota gate `[routing].plenty_min` in `config/routing.toml` was lowered from 50 to 20 percent (operator
  2026-11-13: "change the codex gate quota from 50% to 20%"): codex takes judgment, code, bulk and mechanical slices
  while every codex window has at least 20% left (was 50%). The `[frontier]` floors in `config/gates.toml` are unchanged. (#628)
- `bin/codexbar-cpu-watch` became `bin/proc-cpu-watch --target codexbar|opencode` (2026-11-01, djbclark: "opencode serve seems to be having a CPU issue"): every subcommand takes a required `--target`; the new `opencode` target samples `opencode serve` and the commands its sessions run; one plist template (`launchd/com.djbclark.proc-cpu-watch.plist`) is installed per target as `com.djbclark.<target>-cpu-watch` with state in `~/.local/state/<target>-cpu-watch/`; every record carries its `target` and `report` shows only that target's records; an interpreter's first argument is named only when it is script-shaped (a script suffix, or a path right after argv[0]). The CodexBar job keeps its label and state directory; reinstall it after this lands with `bin/proc-cpu-watch install --target codexbar` (the old `bin/codexbar-cpu-watch` path is gone) (#226).
- Set `chosen = true` on the `ade-progress` row in `config/install.toml` (operator choice, 2026-11-13), so `just doctor` expects `com.djbclark.ade-progress` and reports it `missing` until the lead installs it (`docs/scheduling.md`). Merging installs and loads nothing. (#617)
- Changed `ade-sched reconcile` for an unchosen ADE-owned `ade-sched`/`paseo` inventory row (a `service` or dependency row is unchanged): a live label of the row is reported as `installed but inventory row ... is not chosen` instead of `foreign`, and a ledger-owned install of it is planned for removal with the reason `inventory row ... is not chosen` instead of `dropped from inventory`; `ade-sched diff` adds "installed but unchosen" when the label is live (any unchosen row with a label). Behaviour (report only, or unload when ledger-owned) is unchanged. (#617)
- Narrowed the wording in `docs/scheduling.md` and the `progress-1b-r2` changelog fragment: the installed-but-unchosen `reconcile` report applies only to ADE-owned `ade-sched` and `paseo` rows, and the remove reason only when the ledger id equals the row name. (#618)

- `docs/queue.md`: queues items 88-93: an unusable opencode:go account read as unmapped by the supervisor scan, the four RAM-relief items for the 32 GB Mac (shared MCP/LSP servers, CLI polling storms, idle-session pruning and daemon bloat, remote Linux workers), and the aiuse read timing out under load. - `docs/queue.md`: closes Carried 41, 46 and 48 (PRs 742, 740, 665 merged), drops the shipped parts of Carried 45 (PR 741), and queues the review Lows of PRs 740, 741, 742, 743 and 744 as Carried 50 to 55. (#746)

- `docs/ref-labels.md` Part B says what a Ref is (a unique script-issued id for one operator-facing post, never a hash, PR number or hand-typed text; growing from 1 to 3 characters at 1296), how the hook check works and what to do when it blocks; it drops the never-shipped `ref_audit.py`. (#863)
- `bin/ade-merge-ready` classes `review_bot_untriaged`, `review_bot_pending` and `review_bot_unknown` stop a tip that would be `merge_now`, and `merge-now` re-reads the bot gate on the exact head before `gh pr merge`; review rule 9 no longer treats Codex as advisory. (#713)

- `bin/review-pr`: the `yolo` runtime needs the newest ref update of the PR branch to be a direct push, force-push or branch creation by the operator (`head_pusher_check(allowed=DIRECT_PUSHES)`), so a head that arrived by merging a child PR is reviewed restricted; the author's login and numeric id now come from the PR's own record (`gh api repos/R/pulls/N`, `.user`), not from a lookup by login; the stale "owner-authored" wording in the `review-pr` docstring, README.md and docs/review-boundary.md now says the operator also pushed the head (#689)
- `bin/route_agent.py` and `tests/test_route_agent*.py`: clear every ruff finding with behaviour-preserving edits (import order, unused `re`, `removesuffix`, explicit `check=False`, `ClassVar` annotations, collapsed `if`s, unused unpacked names); the two `TRY004` and three `FURB162` findings carry a reasoned `noqa` instead, since their fixes would change behaviour. - `AGENTS.md` order 9: never `git stash` in a worktree; a repo's worktrees share one stash stack (two workers swapped WIP). (#571)
