- Adopted the versioned effective config for runtime router initialization and default reload, honoring unified/local routing overrides after the existing pure shape validation and reporting source provenance; preserved isolated legacy reads, shipped policy values, frontier bounds and quota/acceptance gates (#541).
- `bin/route_agent.py`: `--available` rows and Paseo usage reports of one pool are told apart by the account they read (aiuse `account`, Paseo `id`; a second family of one account stays fail closed on purpose), not by the pool key. A repeat of one account's quota keeps known-beats-unknown; any other second row folds as another account, fail closed, and an unreadable Paseo report of another account now leaves an `(unmeasured)` window instead of being dropped. `cap_only` is removed. `docs/model-routing.md` D3a and A2c record the rule and the no-`frontier_order` default (PR 708 Lows). (#735)
- Adopted one validated effective model-tier snapshot for router reporting, frontier tokens, sublimit accounting and judgment fallback, with coherent reload and preserved isolated legacy reads (#545).
- `ade-update-auto` gained `--apply` (S4 options 2–3, operator ruling
  2026-11-12): the scheduled apply now skips while any machine-wide Paseo agent
  is mid-turn (`paseo ls -g --background --json`; unknown fleet readings defer
  too) and records deferrals and the applying intent durably in
  `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/update-auto.json` (deferral exits 6), applies
  the checkout fast-forward plus dependency updates, and sends one
  hermes-ping per failed run (`ADE_UPDATE_HERMES_PING` overrides the tool).
  `ade-update --auto --apply` dispatches to it. No launchd/systemd/cron job is
  installed or loaded by the repository; the daily check job stays check-only.
  (#583)
- The context-size nudge (`claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py`, boundary and ask messages) now tells a lead whose work remains to run handoff Step 9 itself (`bin/ade-self-session handoff`, an acknowledged self-handoff) instead of telling the operator `/new` is ready, per djbclark's 2026-11-14 rule; the ~46k versus ~72k cost note stays. (#663)
- `ade-self-session finish` follow-ups from the PR 714 reviews: a long commit subject in `head_advanced` keeps its short sha (the end is cut, not the start); a proven descendant whose `git log` fails records `log_unavailable`; one stderr progress line and a handoff-prompt sentence tell the successor to read `head_advanced`; a stale `head_advanced` is removed when HEAD returns to the recorded head; any native failure during the wait or a re-check (including `ls -g`) is recorded as `native_error`; the post-refusal timeout message says `running (archive refused)`; `--poll` is capped at 1 h and `--wait-timeout` at 24 h so `time.sleep` cannot overflow. (#725)
- `ade-self-session finish` waits (bounded, read-only) for the predecessor to stay idle with no permission waits for a stable window (`--stable`, default 45 s; `--wait-timeout`, default 25 min; `--poll`) before archiving, and an `agent archive` that answers the structured code `AGENT_RUNNING` returns to the wait and retries inside the same bound; every other archive error is still recorded and not retried. A timeout records `PREDECESSOR_NOT_IDLE`, exits non-zero and keeps both sessions. The HEAD check before retirement tolerates a fast-forward (the recorded head is an ancestor of the current one, as when another lead fast-forwards the shared checkout after a merge) and records it as `head_advanced`; a rewind, a git error or a changed checkpoint still blocks. (#714)
- `bin/ade-self-session handoff` now gives the successor the predecessor's title (`agent run --title=`) and the labels `ade.predecessor`, `ade.chain` and `ade.role`, so `paseo ls` still names a chain's current lead; `accept` and `finish` read the name and labels back and repair a loss, a Paseo refusal is recorded in the handoff record's `identity`, never fatal. New options `--title` and `--role`. (#726)
- Refreshed the setup-plan progress docs (2026-11-12 snapshot): cleared stale pending markers for merged #553/#554/#556, updated the 2026-11-11 base and continuation sections, and linked it from `docs/setup-plan.md`. (#560)
- `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl`: refreshed from the live `~/.claude/settings.json` (queue Carried 40): `footerLinksRegexes` carries the graft card link regex, and the `ops/site-private/` and `ops/site-djbclark/` paths use the canonical `site-private-djbclark` and `site-public-djbclark` names. Machine-local live keys stay out; `tests/test_claude_settings_template.py` guards the template. (#754)
- Pathless `model_tiers` readers, `ade-model-tiers` and the `context_size_nudge` hook accept `config/ade.toml` before
  anything writes it; the legacy tier writer refuses while `ade.toml` carries `[model_tiers]`. (#602)
- Documented the trusted-review runtime split in the review boundary, review rule 11 and the ordinary-agent mode notes. (#574)
- `launchd/com.djbclark.load-watch.plist` now starts `bin/load-watch sample` through `bin/ade-job-run` (canonical `--lock-dir`),
  so once an existing install is migrated with `ade-sched migrate-wrapper`, `load-watch install` and `load-watch uninstall`
  work on the LOADED job through the locked record-first path; an activated `ade-sched uninstall` of a LOADED canonical-wrapped job now
  goes through `remove_launchd_locked` (recovery record and prior bytes before the `bootout`, the prior definition loaded again if the
  unlink or read-back fails, the record kept with a manual-bootstrap hint if that fails too) instead of a bare bootout and unlink. (#626)
- `ade-sched migrate-wrapper` also takes the OLD loaded definition's `LABEL.lock` exclusive and non-blocking, for the whole
  migration, when that definition is a wrapped job locking in a directory other than the canonical one: a run of the old wrapper
  makes it refuse with nothing changed. A pre-wrapper old definition keeps the documented idle-only race. (#626)
- `docs/scheduling.md` documents load-watch's emergency stop (`guard_stop`: `bootout`, `kill -TERM/-KILL`, `disable`, and
  the bootstrap/reset without admission) as the one deliberate exception to the admission lock and the no-kill contract, and how
  it interacts with reconcile and a locked reload. (#626)
- `docs/ci-runners.md`: a job moves to WarpBuild only if it runs over 10 minutes and a measured pilot shows at least 1.5x speed (operator, 2026-11-13); each project measures in `frdminc/warpbuild-pilot`; djbclark-ade (1.07x/1.22x) stays on GitHub-hosted runners. (#659)
- `docs/ci-runners.md`: the WarpBuild org pilot timings (frdminc/warpbuild-pilot, 2026-11-13): Linux about 7% faster, macOS about 18% faster. (#658)
- Recorded the operator's 2026-11-12 Wave 6 C3 historical waives for `cfengine` and `aiuse`
  (`docs/slim/log.md`, `docs/slim/c3-waives-2026-11-12/`) and updated the Wave 6 follow-up status. (#570)
- Windows are now at most three "boxes" per lead and repo, `LEAD <repo> (<work>)`, `WORKER <repo> (<work>)` and `OTHER <repo> (<work>)` (operator ruling 2026-11-14: "Close any unneeded tabs and any unneeded workspaces you or your predecessors have made. In the future, have at most LEAD, WORKER, and OTHER workspaces and put tabs in the appropriate box. The tabs can mention the worktree they are in."), replacing the 2026-11-13 `MAIN`, `WORKERS` and `USER` (still found, never duplicated, renamed to the current kind when a launch reuses the window). `bin/ade-worker-run` keeps one WORKER box per lead and repo, found by ownership evidence and reused whatever the slice or `--window-middle` (now only the `(<work>)` text of a box a launch opens, default `workers`, and a picker among several); a self-handoff successor reuses its predecessors' box; new `--placement worker|lead|other` (`--interactive` = lead, `--other`), `rename-lead` (alias `rename-main`), `archive-window --placement worker|other`, and tabs `worker <slice> <lead id> [wt: <worktree>]` (`--worktree DIR` or a `--cwd` that is a linked worktree). `bin/ade-tidy` names, renames and counts the three boxes, never archives a LEAD window, and reports windows beyond them in a new section H (`--apply` archives the empty or finished extras of the lead's self-handoff chain, never another lead's window, a running agent's window or a tab holding an active claim). The `ade-window-name` plugin names new windows LEAD or WORKER and reads `ade.placement` `lead|main`, `worker|workers`, `other|user`. Docs: `docs/workspace-layout.md`, `skills/tidy`, `skills/fan-out`, `AGENTS.md` and the other placement mentions. (#732)
- Window names are now `<KIND> <repo> (<work>)` with KIND `MAIN` (a lead's own window), `WORKERS` (the workers the lead starts
  itself) or `USER` (operator-started agents a lead was connected to), and carry no lead id (operator rulings, 2026-11-13);
  the lead id stays in the tab name `worker <slice> <lead id>` and the `ade.lead` label. `bin/ade-worker-run` finds the WORKERS
  window by exact name (or, without `--window-middle`, by the lead's labelled agents), refuses two matches or a window that
  holds another lead's labelled agents, and renames a legacy `workers <middle> <lead id>`, `WORKERS <middle> <lead id>` or
  `MAIN <middle>` window to the new form in place (same window id) before it launches. Because the name no longer proves
  whose window it is, the helper keeps a per-lead window record (`$ADE_WORKER_RUN_STATE/<lead id>.windows.json`) written when it
  mints, migrates or adopts a window; `archive-window` archives only a window it can show is the lead's, and a launch refuses a
  window registered to, or holding any agent (closed included) of, another lead. (#643)
- `docs/workspace-layout.md`, `skills/fan-out`, `skills/handoff`, `docs/orchestration-practices.md` and the AGENTS.md table row
  carry the new naming rule with the operator's words. (#643)
- Raised the outer `timeout` of the six Claude hooks wrapped in `bin/hook-deadline` from 5 s to 20 s in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` (2026-11-01, djbclark: permanent): at load 30-50 starting the wrapper alone outlasted 5 s, so Claude Code killed it first and printed "timed out after 5s" for `prior_art_nudge` and `quota_nudge` on every prompt. The timeout is only a backstop, so a 20 s ceiling adds no load; `tests/test_hook_deadline_timeouts.py` requires at least 15 s and above each inner deadline (#218).
- `session-find.py --all` now searches ended sessions of every agent in the SQLite history index (it covered ~160 Claude sessions) and needs keywords; its history child is started with `--` before the keywords; a finished ACP launch still listed by the fleet keeps its row and takes its history hit (its snippet, not the fleet's reply hint) instead of the hit being dropped as live; the launchd job `session-history.py --install` writes now carries `SESSION_INDEX_DIR` and logs in that directory, and its comment no longer contains `--` (invalid XML, so `launchctl bootstrap` would have refused it); the crush history adapter keeps every distinct database registered for one project and resumes a session with the data dir that holds it, and `session-find.py --all` compares ids as the history adapters report them (an omp launch's transcript-path id); a fleet row with unknown activity but a live process counts as running for `LIVE`/`LIVE?` labels; the installed launchd job carries every adapter-root variable set at install time (`CODEX_HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; adapters declare theirs in `ROOT_ENV`), so the scheduled refresh indexes the stores an interactive search does; `session-find.py --all` exits 2 (not 1, "no hits") when its history search could not run and nothing else matched (#191).
- Raised the load rules for this 16 GB, 8-core Mac (2026-11-01, djbclark: 3.5 per core "is actually not too bad"): `load-watch admit`'s calm-churn boost default 2.0 -> 4.0 per core (`ADMIT_BOOST_PER_CORE`, `--boost-per-core`) and the watcher's sustained-load alert `LW_LOAD_PER_CORE` 2.0 -> 4.0; churn limits (25000 boost, 60000 max) and `LW_MIN_CORES` unchanged; docs quote `bg`'s base as 2.5 (changed in site-private); `skills/bigteam` caps a lead at two test-running slices at once, after five parallel pytest runs reached load 222 on 2026-10-31 (#200).
- djbclark-ade `master` is PR-only (2026-10-31, djbclark): GitHub branch protection, enforced for admins, refuses a direct push, force push or deletion; AGENTS.md "Machine context" says so. Also merged the unbuilt "model tiers as data" design as `docs/model-tiers-design.md`, queued to build after D1 (#188).

- herdr (except the `herdr4paseo` and `paseo4herdr` bridges), Orca and BB are retired FROM djbclark-ade by djbclark's ruling of 2026-10-31 (they stay installed and configured on his computer, untouched; no machine-side uninstall is planned), and Paseo is the only harness (`bin/herdr-server-run` and `config/launchd/dev.herdr.server.plist` stay for the installed server and the two bridges; the live paths named in `AGENTS.md`'s retirement box stay until D4/D6): `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/skills.md`, `docs/paseo-only-rearchitecture.md` (capability table, retired from djbclark-ade by ruling) and `docs/apply-toolchain-prompt.md` (5.12: BB block and herdr/Orca items removed, `paseo-screen` added) say so; the `herdr-tidy` and `reorg-orca` skills and their command wrappers are deleted, `/autorename all` is refused in the skill body, and the `cow-workspaces`, `autorename`, `session-finder` and `paseo-handoff` descriptions no longer offer Orca or herdr routes (#180); README, AGENTS.md, `docs/skills.md` and the `autorename` skill agree that `/autorename all` is retired and refused in every host, and the README retirement box lists the live herdr/Orca paths instead of claiming none (#180).
- `skills/paseo-screen` and `docs/apply-toolchain-prompt.md` state that the plugin has no host-side authorization (every paired client and any local caller of the daemon's plugin RPC can see and drive the screen while it is enabled) and add the agent rules (never pair a client, never enable or reconfigure the plugin, disable it only on the operator's word); `paseo-screen` and `questions-please` install unpinned, tracking latest (djbclark 2026-10-31: nothing is version-pinned) (#180).
- `AGENTS.md` standing order 11 (2026-10-30, djbclark): djbclark-ade is the lead's project; a change to code or configuration in another repository or in installed files outside a repository waits for the operator's yes (own installs, memory notes and upstream filing of an approved change excepted), and findings there are reported as proposals (#170).
- `skills/paseo-handoff` (2026-10-30): an "in a worktree" handoff now gets an `isolation: "local"` workspace and a `git worktree add` task worktree instead of a Paseo worktree workspace, until the Paseo-only plan's D1.3 and D2 gate prove that archiving cannot delete unpushed work (plan rule D0.3) (#154).
- `skills/aws-rules` and `tools/acp-run/README.md` (2026-10-29): record that Bedrock inference access is off (the Bedrock API key's service credential is `Inactive`, with an inline `Deny bedrock:*`; root can still call Bedrock; gating is issue 141), that the `aws-agent` profile is the root user on purpose, and that goose-scout is off with its key deactivated (#142).
- `skills/aws-rules` (2026-10-29): the `aws-mcp` server is the uv tool `~/.local/bin/mcp-proxy-for-aws` called directly instead of `uvx mcp-proxy-for-aws@latest` (one process of about 50 MB at start instead of about 175 MB across the uv wrapper and Python); upgrade with `uv tool upgrade mcp-proxy-for-aws` (#139).
- The context nudge runs under `PostToolUse` as well as `UserPromptSubmit` (2026-10-29): `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` registers `context_size_nudge.py` after every tool call (matcher `*`, `hook-deadline 2`, timeout 5; at most one transcript check per `POST_TOOL_CHECK_SECONDS` = 20 s per session), the live `~/.claude/settings.json` gets the same entry by hand once this code is on the shared checkout (nothing applies the template), a `PostToolUse` call from a sub-agent (it carries an `agent_id` and the parent's session and transcript) is ignored so it cannot spend the top-level session's one-shot hard-tier and boundary notes, and the hard-tier text, which claimed it "runs only when a prompt is submitted, not between tool rounds", now says it runs on prompt submit and between tool rounds (#127).
- `relay.py`'s deferred deliverer now looks at a WORKING target on the card ladder with a 30 s cap (`BUSY_BACKOFF_CAP`: waits of 5, 10, 20, then up to 30 s with full jitter) instead of every 5 s, after the load watcher measured about nine deliverers each spawning `paseo agent inspect` every 5 s at load 39 (2026-10-29); a queued text may wait ~30 s past the end of a long turn, and a target that is not there or could not be asked is still looked at every 5 s, since those looks count toward the miss and error limits (#129).
- CI no longer runs the macOS `pytest` job on pull requests or pushes: it runs only on a manual `Tests` dispatch with the new `macos` input, so a slow macOS runner queue no longer holds a merge (operator ruling, 2026-10-29); the Linux leg and `context-policy` still run on every pull request (#125).
- The context nudge's hard tier now fires 50k+ tokens before Claude Code's auto-compaction wall (2026-10-28): `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py` sets `ASK_TOTAL` to the wall less 50k (217k; it was 300k, above the ~267k wall, so it never fired) and `ASK_GROWTH` to 140k (it was 200k, which at the median session floor of 71k also lay past the wall), so regrowth after a compaction (floor ~104k) reaches the hard tier by total; the hard text drops "at the next natural boundary" for what to do mid-turn (finish the current edit, hand off within the stated number of tool rounds) and says how many tokens remain; a compaction written mid-turn is found by searching every byte appended since the previous prompt (raw-bytes chunks, up to 64 MB; real heavy turns write >2 MB after the boundary), only a real `system` `compact_boundary` record counts (a tool input quoting the JSON does not), and the stored level is clamped against the stored peak, not the dipped total (#108).
- `self-slash` wording after the PR 103 review: the successor log line says it goes to the state-file workspace "unless --workspace names another", the dry-run text names `$PASEO_HOME/agents/*/<id>.json` instead of a fixed `~/.paseo`, the header and the `workspace_plan` comment describe the state-file workspace id and its 4th argument correctly (`paseo agent inspect` prints no workspace id, getpaseo/paseo#7592), and `skills/handoff` says when to use the partial-handoff recipe and when `/handoff paseo` (#109).
- The frontier allowance is a standing rule (2026-10-27, operator: "Frontier model use should still be open when required"): `config/gates.toml` `[frontier]` now carries the ruled caps as the BASELINE (judged use on for both frontier models, `frontier1_per_wave` 2, `frontier2_per_wave` 1, `frontier1_agent_calls_per_day` 4; the `*_min_left` floors unchanged), no profile or expiry needed, so the Agent-tool hook, `acp-dispatch --frontier-reason`, the quota guard's `watch` advice and `quota_nudge.py` (a daily `[gates] frontier allowance` line when no profile is active) apply it whenever the guard checks pass; `[floors.frontier]` bounds equal the baseline (`calls_per_day` 6 became 4) and bound the run-time baseline guard (`ade_gates.baseline_limit`), `bin/gate-profile validate` accepts a frontier baseline between the code default and its bound, `ade_gates.FRONTIER_DEFAULTS` stays all-closed so a missing or unreadable `gates.toml` means closed, the `away` frontier table stays valid and equal to the baseline, and the texts that said "away" now say "frontier allowance"; `route_agent.py plan`'s two-claude-slices-per-wave cap holds only while the same guard checks pass (a tight, critical, stale or missing guard state, or a codex-pool reading under `frontier2_min_left`, leaves the closed 1), and the daily nudge line needs a guard state under 30 minutes old (#97).
- The macOS `pytest` job's timeout is 45 minutes, not 30 (2026-10-27): four consecutive green runs measured 1446 s, 1588 s, 1615 s and 1702 s against the 1800 s cap, and the suite then crossed it — run 40533487414 was cancelled with "The job has exceeded the maximum execution time of 30m0s" while `pytest-linux` passed in 17m0s and `context-policy` in 14 s, so a slow runner reports a wall-clock timeout as a code failure and a PR head cannot be CI-green at all. `pytest-linux` stays at 30 minutes (1130-1150 s measured) with a comment saying when to raise it (#94).
- Changed refusal handling to preserve launched records while refreshing unlaunched attempts, and scoped effort/context inheritance to the same agent and model (2026-10-26) (#88).
- `relay.py` delivery watch on by default for an agent session (2026-10-25): a send that is only accepted or queued is watched 10 s more and exits 0 delivered, 1 not delivered or 3 unverified (a declared job run by launchd, such as the load and rumination watchers, keeps the old exits); `--no-ack` and `RELAY_NO_ACK=1` turn it off, `--json` adds `ack_default`; an `ack ID` reply (alone, or with a short remark in parentheses or after a colon) and a text whose last clause is "no reply needed" carry no first-contact footer and open no link; only a bare `ack ID` or a lone "no reply needed" refuses `--expect-ack`/`--needs-reply` (exit 2), and on any longer text an explicit flag wins; SKILL.md gains the requester steps for exit 1, exit 3, `OVERDUE` and `ONE-WAY` (#81).
- `relay.py` refuses to send when it cannot resolve the sender (2026-10-25): a relay from a process with no session identity (a launchd job, a script, a Hermes turn) and no `RELAY_SENDER_NAME` exits 4 with nothing sent, queued or recorded (`--json`: `{"sent": false, "error": "no_sender", "reason": ...}`) and names every variable and ancestor source it checked; `[relay from unknown]` is never sent. `bin/load-watch` and `bin/session-load-watch` now declare `RELAY_SENDER_NAME` for their relay calls (#74).
- `bin/ade-git-guard`, round 11 (2026-10-25): the reference-transaction hook reads `remote.origin.url` from the repository's own config only (`git config --local --includes`, caller's `-c`, `GIT_CONFIG_*`, HOME and XDG removed) and an unanswered question widens the claim rule instead of skipping it; an empty `argv[0]` no longer shifts the macOS `pack-refs` parent parse; the shim refuses `clone -c/--config` hook keys and every non-default template source on `clone` and `init` (`--template`, `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR`, `init.templateDir`) and exempts exactly acp-run's two `hook.acp-run-identity.*` environment pairs from its hook-key rule; a foreign claim now takes precedence over an unanswered config question, so the refusal names the owner (#59).
- `bin/ade-git-guard` is no longer a parser of git's argument grammar (O2 redesign, 2026-10-25, after nine rounds of argv bypasses): refs are guarded by git's own hooks (the entry above) and the shim keeps only what hooks cannot see; it dropped the fetch/pull/remote-update and push dry-run preflights, the `update-ref`/`branch -d|-D|-f`/`checkout -B`/`switch -C`/`worktree add -B` target rules, the output-file rules and the `--check` shell-string analyser (#59).
- Codex shell commands inherit their launching terminal environment, restoring multiplexer and runtime context while preserving the configured exports and keeping the shared app-server disabled (2026-10-25; direct integration).

- Handoff template (2026-10-25): `skills/handoff/SKILL.md` Step 4 gained `## Decisions` and `## Relations` in Basic Memory's observation and relation syntax (a `[ruling]` only for something djbclark said, quoted and dated; an agent's own choice is a `[decision]` with `decided_in`, whose targets resolve: file stems or PR links); stale Basic Memory paths and the false "Hermes reads the same store" line were fixed in the memory docs, `session-handoff` and `todo` skills (design slices W2 and W5; the pre-compact hook writes no Basic Memory note, so it is unchanged) (#52).
- Autorename docs (2026-10-24): Paseo tabs are named `<name>-pt` (17 characters including the suffix) and Paseo workspaces `-pw`, the `-p` suffix is retired for Paseo (`skills/autorename/SKILL.md`, `workspace-layout.md` principle 10, an `AGENTS.md` row); `docs/upstream-issues.md` gains draft 19 (terminal-tab rename, UI rename event stream, inspect workspace id) (#51).
- `autorename` names a Paseo agent's tab `<topic>-pt` (2026-10-24, at most 17 characters including the suffix, `paseo_tab_name` in `skills/autorename/autorename.py`; the sentence title stays the provider session's) and `--auto` no longer overwrites an agent name that already ends in `-pt` (the 0.4.4 overwrite bug); the names store that tells a user rename from a tool rename is a later slice; the reply line ends `(names store unavailable)` on both `--auto` paths (#53).
- Clarified bounded executor waits and required a registry claim before a worker binds any Mac test port, after a compatibility probe used an unclaimed port (2026-10-25; direct integration).
- AGENTS.md standing order 9 is now an absolute rule (djbclark, 2026-10-25): all development happens in an exclusively claimed task workspace (linked worktree or cow pasture), one writer at a time, with named exceptions; the shared-master commit allowance is gone, `skills/cow-workspaces/SKILL.md` accepts both worktrees and pastures, and `~/ops` keeps plain master until the ops-promotion pilot passes (direct commit).
- `relay.py` repairs after the adversary's round 1 (2026-10-25): the pane and launch routes type the sender header too (a Paseo-hosted sender included); a relay Hermes starts itself is sent as `hermes SID8` (from `HERMES_SESSION_ID`) instead of exiting 4, and `RELAY_SENDER_NAME` cannot be a `hermes xxxx` label; an `--expect-ack` closes only on its target's reply (a compaction summary or another peer's `ack` no longer does) and a first-contact `ack` is attributed by the header's exact ID word; `--expect-ack ID` is claimed before the send and released when nothing is sent; `where.env_of` reads every key from the env part of `ps eww` only (#74).
- `relay.py` repairs after the adversary's round 2 (2026-10-25): an `--expect-ack` or first-contact `ack` to a Hermes gateway session closes on that session's own reply (`[relay from hermes SID[-8:]]`, the last 8 characters of its session id; before, every question to Hermes went falsely OVERDUE); `--sweep` skips a record whose `tx`, `mark`, `sender` or `who` a hand edit broke instead of aborting; an answered `--expect-ack` no longer marks the link of a new session that took over the target's pane; a declared `RELAY_SENDER_NAME` whose first word is `hermes` in any case is refused like `hermes xxxx`; a Paseo send is verified by its whole text, not its first 40 characters, so one transcript line no longer certifies two relays that share a sender header, and the probe matches the raw whole text (a send with no source agent, which Paseo 0.13.0 stores unescaped) or the XML-escaped whole text (a Paseo-hosted sender), so a text with `'`, `"`, `&`, `<` or `>` is `delivered` again from every sender; a Codex session in herdr can send a question or `--needs-reply` again (its rollout is found by its session id before `--expect-ack` is required) (#74).
- Changed `load-watch admit` to raise the per-core limit from 1.5 to 2.0 while compressor churn is at most 25k pages/s (new `--boost-per-core`, `--boost-max-churn`; swap is not the signal); `bg` now defers to that decision (2026-10-25).
- codex takes `--kind code`, `bulk` and `mechanical` (djbclark, 2026-10-25: "if we have lots of codex quota and it is the best of available options we can use it for non-frontier") on `gpt-6.1-sol --effort medium` (code) and `gpt-6-luna --effort low` (bulk, mechanical), only while the tightest codex window (5-hour and weekly) has at least `[routing].plenty_min` (50%) left; below it, or with no fresh reading, codex is frontier-only again and `acp-dispatch` exits 2 naming the headroom. New `plenty_kinds` service key and `Service.eligible(kind, quota)`; `route`, `plan` and `acp-dispatch` read the same gate. `gpt-6-astra` still needs `--kind frontier`, and `[dispatch.estimate_floor]` keys may be `SERVICE:frontier` (codex 60 for astra; the everyday models get `codex` = 10).
- `relay.py` named a relay's sender from `PASEO_AGENT_ID` (its fleet row's name, else the agent's short id) when the caller had no Claude `self` row, so a Cline, Codex or omp agent under Paseo was no longer tagged `[relay from unknown]`; a busy-target send that was refused (exit 1) now queues (`queued: <id>`, exit 0) for a caller relay can identify, unless `--no-defer` (#57).
- `load-watch` passes `--no-defer` to relay.py for every incident brief to the load-causing session, job-guard line and "no longer the watcher" line, so a busy session that cannot take the line now is "could not reach it" (the hermes-ping and the `acp-dispatch cursor` fixer line) instead of "told it": a queued brief would arrive after the load spike it is about; only the `watcher test` registration check lets relay.py queue (#57).
- Developer search moved from the Mac router to Hyades (2026-10-24): `djbclark-fc-api` has `RESEARCH_PROXY_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3010`, a `devsearch` process in the `djbclark-fc-searxng` microVM (firecrawl-hyades `docs/developer-search.md`, image r11+) that answers from the GitHub search API through the same vps-primary and pastebin proxy legs as SearXNG, with a fine-grained public-repositories-read-only token, a 10-minute cache, stale-if-error and a background leg prober. `bin/firecrawl-router` no longer answers `/v2/search/developer` or `categories: ["developer"]` and no longer reads a GitHub token (`FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_DEVELOPER`, `FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_GITHUB_KEY`, `FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_GITHUB_SECRET` and `FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_GITHUB_BASE` are gone; the `DevSearch` code and its tests are deleted); `skills/firecrawl/SKILL.md`, `docs/mcp-servers.md` and `docs/threat-model.md` item 10 say where it lives now.
- Developer search now runs on a fine-grained public-repositories-read-only GitHub token (2026-10-24): site-djbclark's `shared_mcp` role sets `FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_GITHUB_SECRET=FIRECRAWL_GITHUB_PUBLIC_TOKEN` (stored with `sudo-secretspec`, probed: a private repo answers 404, search on it 0 results), which closes the private-code inference residual of #41 and #43 that the classic `GITHUB_TOKEN` default still had. Docs only here: `docs/threat-model.md` item 10, `skills/firecrawl/SKILL.md`, `docs/mcp-servers.md` and the router header say so; the router's default name is unchanged. The token expires and needs renewing.
- `load-watch` retried a `paseo send` to the registered watcher twice, 5 s apart and inside 30 s, when its `--json` error code was exactly DAEMON_UNREACHABLE, DAEMON_CONNECTION_LOST, DAEMON_NOT_READY or DAEMON_NOT_RUNNING (a Paseo daemon restart; the code is parsed, never matched as text), so the incident line was no longer lost in a short gap; once one send gave up, the rest of that process made one attempt each, the send passed `--json` and `--` before the target and text, and other failures and the relay channel still got one attempt (2026-10-23, load-watch send retry).
- `route_agent.py` reads the fleet governor's state: while its `level` is `tight` or `critical` and under 30 minutes old, `plan` and `route` never pick `claude` for `code`, `bulk`, `research` or `mechanical` slices (the reason is in the output) and `judgment` prefers omp `anthropic/claude-opus-5-5` when omp is eligible for it, ready and has credits (2026-10-23, #31).
- `fleet.py` lists every non-archived Paseo agent of any provider (a cline agent that `paseo ls` showed was missing, so peers thought it offline; `--all` adds archived ones as `ended`), `relay.py` resolves a Paseo agent id prefix of 6 or more hex characters (ambiguity refused with the candidates), and a send to a working Paseo agent whose provider cannot steer (cline, any ACP one: Paseo would replace its running turn) is refused with that reason instead of "only idle ones take typed text"; helm leaves Paseo agents with no Claude transcript out of its attention queue (2026-10-23).
- `relay.py` pane route: a message that only reached Claude Code's queue is reported "queued", not "delivered", and one the operator then pulled back into the box (Up or Esc, a queue `remove` with a stated reason other than a delivery, or `popAll`) is "unverified" and never submitted or retyped (a `remove` with no reason, and a `dequeue`, count as still queued or delivered, as real transcripts show); a short text (30 characters or fewer) whose copy we never saw in the box is claimed delivered only by a transcript line stamped from 2 s before our typing to 10 s after the end of its `--wait` loop (so not by an equal text the operator typed himself later), and that stamp never lets a recalled copy of an already-delivered text be submitted again; a different text is no longer typed while an earlier one to the same session is unproven (skipped, so the earlier one's retry cannot type it twice; an entry the operator pulled back and then emptied from the box is dropped); two relays never type into one pane together (a per-session lock, the second is refused), and the unsent-entries file is updated under a lock through a unique temp file, so concurrent relays no longer lose each other's entry. `fleet.py` lists an `agent_close` Hermes row only when the gateway can reopen it (keyed, not a subagent), so finished delegated children are no longer offered as live conversations (2026-10-23).
- Made Agent-tool sub-agents and teammates with no `model` default to Sonnet: `bin/claude-settings-env` now adds `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=sonnet` to the live `~/.claude/settings.json` when absent, leaving a deliberately different value alone (`--check` reports drift), and `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` carries it, so an Opus or Fable lead no longer spawns Opus or Fable sub-agents by inheritance; a definition's `model:` (adversary `opus`, fable-deep `fable`) or a model passed in the call still wins, and the built-in Explore and Plan agents are unaffected (#32).
- Routing: moved lead / orchestrator sessions to Opus 5.5 at high (Sonnet 5.5 at high only to drive a pipeline), never Fable (`config/routing.toml` `[lead]`, printed by `route_agent.py policy --md`); sent adversary rounds first to `acp-dispatch omp --model anthropic/claude-opus-5-5 --kind judgment --effort high` (omp became eligible for `judgment`), and had the Agent-tool `adversary` take a `model: "sonnet"` override when the guard says `tight` or worse (#29).
- Routing: a lead that only drives a pipeline now runs Sonnet 5.5 at medium (`[lead] pipeline_effort`, optional, default `high`; `[lead]` `effort` and `pipeline_effort` are validated against the Claude effort levels `low|medium|high|xhigh|max` (Paseo's `CLAUDE_EFFORT_LEVELS`), and an unknown `[lead]` key is rejected; printed by `route_agent.py policy` and `policy --md`), with high only for a judgment turn; `skills/model-routing/SKILL.md` item 12, `skills/bigteam/SKILL.md`, the model-routing doc and `handoff` Step 9 (`--thinking medium` for a pipeline successor) say so (#33).
- `self-slash`'s 30-minute gap check read any log line that merely contained `<host> <pane>: sent /compact`; it now matches only lines that start with the timestamp, and a value flag as the last argument is a usage error (exit 2) instead of an endless loop (#30).
- `handoff` Step 9 runs `self-slash --finish --successor claude/claude-opus-5-5 --thinking high` (Sonnet 5.5 when the quota guard says `tight`) instead of `/new` on Paseo when the session runs on Fable, `~/.local/state/ade/quota-guard.json` says `tight` or `critical`, or the operator asks for a model change, and the context-size hook names that form in its boundary and ask messages; a quota guard only moves a session down from a model known to be above the target, an unknown or lower model gets plain `/new` (#30).
- `loose` gains an "On Grok Bot" section (G1-G7) with pointers from Rule 2, 3 and 4: box paths, GitHub reads via `gh`/
  `GITHUB_TOKEN_DJBCLARK` or the connector, the box's own jobs instead of a Mac process list, a chat reply instead of
  `hermes-ping`, no `handoff`/`self-slash`/`/compact` there; still walks the findings with `/steps`. Other runtimes'
  rules are unchanged (2026-10-23).
- `steps` gains a Grok Bot branch: Prompt tool rule 5 and section 2d use Grok Bot's `SendToUser` question widget
  (context in a text message first, one-line numbered labels, recommendation `primary`, destructive `danger`,
  `allowCustom` for `stop`, a dismissal is a decline; no `/compact` or `self-slash` there). Other runtimes'
  rules are unchanged (2026-10-23).
- `acp-dispatch --detach`: a `waiter:` line above `rearm:` says to run the waiter in the background with a long tool timeout (Claude Code Bash: `timeout 7200000`); at the 30 min default a lead's harness killed it mid-slice. `rearm:` stays the last line (#28).
- `acp-run` removes `PASEO_AGENT_ID`, `PASEO_AGENT_CWD` and `ORCA_TERMINAL_HANDLE` from every agent's
  environment and sets `ACP_RUN_AGENT=1`; `self-slash --finish` and `--then /quit` on Paseo are
  rejected with `--then`, and `paseo agent archive` gets `--` before the id (#19).
- The context-size nudge tells a session to run the `handoff` skill (Step 9 does the `/new`) and, on
  Paseo, to offer the `loose` Rule 4 choices instead of archiving itself; the dispatch footer, `handoff`
  Step 9 and `loose` Rule 4 say lifecycle actions run only in the top-level session (#19).
- `handoff` ends in a new Step 9 that clears the session and starts `/baton` by itself; `loose`
  Rule 4 offers handoff and continue, finish and archive, or keep working; `baton` never clears
  a session itself (#19).
- `acp-dispatch check`: a slice whose recorded class is `killed:` or `lost` gets the `resume` hint (no
  "after the reset") and no `--escalate`; a missing agent binary is `NO-REPORT: not-installed` (exit 127
  plus acp-run's own line) with a re-dispatch hint; `acp-run` waits up to 90 s (`ACP_RUN_BIN_WAIT`, clamped
  to 0..600, junk is the default) for a missing binary and preflight allows for that wait; re-dispatching a
  footed brief under a new `--name` carries one delivery contract, cutting only a provably generated tail
  and saying so on stderr (#18).
- `route_agent`: pool state comes from `aiuse --available --json`, with the snapshot file as the
  fallback (#12).
- `steps` skill: prompt-tool table and §2c cover the `question` tool so `/loose → /steps`
  walks via `question` on OpenCode, not just `AskUserQuestion` (Claude class) /
  `clarify` (Hermes) / `request_user_input` (Codex). Universal skills `/loose`,
  `/handoff`, `/baton`, `/steps` were already reachable from every installed TUI
  (`skill-everywhere --check` all green); the change wires the chain on OpenCode.
- `bin/skill-everywhere --check` ends with a `== end` line, which `bin/ade-update` requires (a cut-short check
  is exit 4, "skill check incomplete"); `bin/ade-update` warns on stderr when it runs from a path other than
  the canonical checkout (#20).

- Deleted the dead and retired pieces: `bin/ade-scan-state*`, `bin/seat-name`, `bin/jev`, `bin/jev-eval` and the Jev shadow in `route_agent.py`, `claude/hooks/jev_effort_hook.py`, `skills/ralph-tui-orchestration`, `skills/skillopt-sleep-learned`, `plugins/herdr-sleeper` (Paseo's `agents.idleRuntimeCleanup` replaces it), `skills/cow-workspaces` and `bin/cow-pasture` (Paseo worktrees plus the committed `.worktreeinclude`), `vendor/gate-scripts` with the `openrouter` and `deepseek` routing rows; moved the `docs/ade-everywhere-*` series and `docs/apply-toolchain-prompt.md` to `docs/history/` (slim-down E2) (#297).
- Slim-down E9, the unconditional TUI retirements (agy, crush, pi, zcode, venicecode, qwen-code, goose, aider, kiro-cli, devin, muse, dsh, cline, copilot CLI):

- `acp-run` no longer drives copilot, qwen, devin, cline, pi, goose, zcode, agy, crush or kiro: their `AGENTS`/`YOLO_*`/`DENY_*` rows, strip and deny

  retired accounts are `never_route` in discovery, so an aiuse account for one is never surfaced as a dispatchable service. The `github` kind now has no pool. (#297)

- Removed `bin/proc-cpu-watch`, `bin/session-load-watch`, `bin/rumination-watch`, `bin/routing-recalibrate`, the `loadwatch` skill and the proc-cpu-watch, rumination-watch, routing-recalibrate, session-history-update and context-budget launchd plists (slim-down E5) (#297).
- Removed `skills/helm` and `/helm-all` (`paseo permit ls` and the asks / questions-please plugins are the operator inbox), `bin/self-slash`, `bin/paseo-self`, `bin/opencode-self-compact`, `skills/autorename` (its `workspace-layout.md` is now `docs/workspace-layout.md`), `bin/paseo-new-seat` (`paseo run`), `bin/paseo-bypass-watch` (`paseo agent mode`); `bin/watch` launches watchers with `paseo run --no-wait` (slim-down E4) (#297).
- Replaced `skills/session-finder/relay.py` by a shim over `paseo send --json` (targets are Paseo agent ids or unique shortId prefixes; exit 0 delivered for a sourceless send, 5 accepted for an agent's send, 2 not delivered, 3 unknown target, 4 anonymous sender, 64 a retired flag; `paseo agent inspect` refuses archived, pending-permission and non-steerable busy targets; the shim is removed in the follow-up PR after one live cycle) and deleted the rest of session-finder (`fleet.py`, `launch.py`, `where.py`, `session-find.py`, `session-history.py`, `nativeid.py`, `prompt_line.py`, the adapters, `skills/peer-relay`, `claude/hooks/relay_prose_nudge.py`, the `/session-finder-all` wrapper, 29 test files); `claude_identity.py` moved to `bin/` with a one-cycle stub at the old path (slim-down E3, E3b) (#297).
- Removed the nightly SkillOpt-Sleep cycle (`bin/skillopt-night`, `skillopt_sources.py`, the `skillopt-sleep` skill and its launchd job), `bin/failover.py` and its model chain (now `paseo run`'s), `bin/skill-get`, `bin/fix-skill-frontmatter.py`, the `skills-everywhere` skill (the `skill-everywhere` tool stays), the prior-art shadow hook `claude/hooks/prior_art_nudge.py`, `bin/npm_metadata.py`, and the redundant `/paseo-screen`, `/retro`, `/steps` command wrappers (slim-down E10). (#297)
- Removed `bin/watch`, `config/watch.toml`, `skills/watch`, `skills/paseowatch` and `skills/cfenginewatch` (fleet-watch's own tick still runs the upstream profiles; only the relay to a registered watcher session went), `bin/web-get`, `bin/web-digest` (with the web-access half of `skills/fallback-chains`), the pi and zcode leftovers (`pi/extensions/subagent`, `zcode/commands/`), the `.pi/`, `.kiro/`, `.windsurf/`, `.gemini/` and `.adal/` provider directories, and the six vendored `skills/ade-paseo*` skills (Paseo's own `paseo*` skills replace them) (slim-down Wave 3b) (#297)
- Removed `bin/pr-wait` (wait with `gh pr checks PR --watch --required`; the marker is judged by the `adversary-marker` Action) and `bin/local-review` (the local review is `bin/review-pr`), with their tests and the `--local-codex` flow (slim-down E8) (#297).
- Retired the grok CLI (the SuperGrok subscription is cancelled; grok-bot and its `grokbot` skill-everywhere copy stay): `acp-run` lost its `grok` adapter, the per-run `GROK_HOME` strip and deny machinery and `tests/test_acp_run_grok_deny.py`; `bin/skill-everywhere` no longer links `~/.grok/skills`; `bin/ade-backup-stores` lost the `grok` and `grok-db` stores (the `.grok/auth.json` exclusion stays); `bin/agent_guard.py`, the quota-guard advice, the handoff, hand, btww, research and dialogue skills and the skill-everywhere, hand-alias and agent-guard docs no longer name it as a route (slim-down E9c) (#297).
- Removed `acp-dispatch resume`, `reroute`, `auto-resume`, `extend`, `finalize`, `reap` (with `--install`/`--uninstall` and `launchd/com.djbclark.acp-dispatch-reap.plist`), the `cursor-cloud` dispatch route (`bin/cursor-cloud` stays), `--checkpoint`, `--no-preflight`, `--no-auto-resume`, `--acp-arg`, `--context`, `--wrapup-grace`, `--no-model-check`, `--acp-run`, the launchd runner scripts and `config/sudoers.d/acp-dispatch-pmset`; each retired verb exits 2 naming its replacement (slim-down E6) (#297).
- Removed the `bigteam` skill (and the zcode `/bigteam` wrapper) and 19 `tests/test_acp_dispatch_*.py` files; `tests/test_acp_dispatch_identity.py`'s policy checks are now `tests/test_agent_guard.py` (slim-down E6) (#297).
- Removed `bin/coding-bot` and its test: the code is re-homed to the peer-link repository; `tell-coding-bot` and `tell-chief-of-staff` are pointer skills (slim-down E6, E9j) (#297).
- Removed the Claude quota gates: `bin/claude-fleet-governor`, `bin/claude-quota-guard`, `bin/gate-profile` (gate profiles and the `away` profile), the `[governor]`, `[quota_guard]` and `[profiles]` tables of `config/gates.toml`, `route_agent.py`'s governor hold, and `bin/claude-cost-whatif`, with their tests; pausing at a quota limit and resuming after the reset is the session-guard and usage-auto-resume Paseo plugins' job (slim-down E7) (#297).
- Removed the `effort-routing` and `fallback-chains` skills: their effort levels and model-chain sources are sections B and F of `model-routing`, and the chain check is `skills/model-routing/scripts/chains-check.py` (slim-down E7) (#297).

The live `~/.codex/config.toml` was changed the same way (#237).
- Deleted `claude/hooks/quota_nudge.py` and `claude/hooks/cswap_quota_context.sh` with their template hook lines (R2-18): E7's session-guard and usage-auto-resume replaced the Claude quota gate; the nudge was inert without the deleted guard state file, and the SessionStart cswap table was that path's display companion. Live `~/.claude` hook lines remain an operator edit (`docs/slim/operator-batch.md` C5) (#457).

- `bin/wave6-delete-check`, `bin/wave6-mac-proof-capture`, their tests and `tests/fixtures/wave6-mac-proofs/`: the Wave 6 shim deletion they gated is done (queue Carried 1a). (#705)
- Removed Moshi hook entries from `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` and Moshi plugin registration from `agents/hermes/config.yaml.tmpl` (#408).
- Deleted the three Wave 6 shims: `skills/session-finder/relay.py`, `bin/acp-dispatch` (+ `acp-dispatch-wait`, its report schema) and `tools/acp-run`, with their tests, `bin/agent_guard.py` and `docs/agent-guard-contract.md`; the OpenCode Go enforcement tests that ran through them went too (`route_agent.py` still refuses costly Go rows). Agents launch with native `paseo run` / `bin/ade-worker-run` and message with `paseo send`; docs, AGENTS.md "Machine context", the threat model and queue Carried 1 now say so (#699).
- Retired herdr (except the `herdr4paseo`/`paseo4herdr` bridges and the live paths the plan removes in D4/D6), Orca and BB from djbclark-ade (2026-10-31, djbclark ruling; the tools stay installed and configured on the machine, untouched): removed `bin/orca-reorg-watch`, `bin/herdr-sleeper` (the `plugins/herdr-sleeper` plugin stays while `autorename`'s `relocate-pane.sh` calls it), `bin/herdr-ai`, `bin/herdr-jump`, `skills/herdr-tidy` and `skills/reorg-orca` with their `/herdr-tidy` and `/orca-tidy` wrappers, `docs/orca-integration.md`, `docs/herdr-{tidy,ai,jump}.md`, `vendor/orca` and their tests; `cow-pasture create --orca` and the Orca unregistration on `remove` are gone (`--orca` is now a usage error); `skills/fallback-chains/scripts/check.py` no longer reads `herdr-ai` (#180).
- `skills/autorename/herdr_place.py decline`, `check --auto` and `check`'s `already_answered` field (2026-10-30): they only stopped the nudge hook from asking about tab placement twice, and no path asks about placement on its own any more; "leave it" needs no command (#169).
- `bin/rumination-watch autoaccept` (2026-10-29): dead since the tool it wrapped moved to `bin/paseo-set-model autoaccept`, so a subcommand that looked live but mutated `~/.paseo/agents/*.json` by hand was deleted rather than kept beside the daemon-supported one (#134).
- Removed the OpenUsage wiring (2026-10-29): the canonical `opencode-plugins/openusage-telemetry.ts`, its handling in `bin/opencode-plugin-guard` (the guard now manages `rtk` only), the three `claude-hook.sh` blocks in `agents/claude/settings.json.tmpl` and the `--previous-notify` fanout in `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl`; an OpenUsage app relaunched from a stale `dist/` build is no longer fed by hooks or plugins (#119).

### Fixed

- `bin/ade-install doctor` no longer fails on a slow healthy sub-doctor: a `[[component]]` row in `config/install.toml` may set an optional
  `probe_timeout_s` (positive number of seconds, at most 3600, range-checked by the catalog loader (both schemas only type-check it); documented in `docs/install-catalog.md`)
  that replaces the global 15 s probe timeout for that row only. The `hermes` row sets it (`hermes doctor` runs ~44 connectivity checks and
  took more than 15 s). A probe that still times out stays `error`. (#646)
- `bin/ade-sched`'s generated-by comment names the plist canonically (repo-relative POSIX path under the ADE root, resolved
  absolute otherwise), so a relative install, an absolute install, a symlinked path and a worktree all render identical
  artifacts and `reconcile` no longer plans an install/reload of an otherwise identical job. Older installs differ in that
  one comment line; a loaded launchd job still defers untouched, and docs/scheduling.md says how to adopt by a comment-only
  rewrite. (#620)
- `ade-sched reconcile`'s live backend scan (`list_backend_labels`), `ade-sched status` with no label
  (`installed_labels`) and reconcile's whole-file byte-match reads no longer open a non-regular `*.plist`/`*.service`
  entry: a FIFO (or a symlink to one) planted in the agents or unit directory used to hang them forever. Entries are
  `lstat`-checked, opened `O_NONBLOCK|O_NOCTTY` with a re-check on the descriptor and read with a bound. A non-regular
  entry is listed as source `non-regular`, is not a live identity, and gets one scan note. (#621)
- `claude/agents/adversary.md` no longer tells the adversary to emit its `adversary-verdict` marker as a raw line "for the teammate to paste into the PR body", which contradicted `docs/code-review.md` rule 1 and AGENTS.md "Code review" item 1 and led a tendcf lead to paste a live APPROVE into a PR body with `gh pr edit`. The marker is now relayed inside a fenced block in the reply and in every report file (`bin/review-pr` also prints it inside a fence), the PR body carries the verdict as prose, and the operator or an owner-authored path applies a live marker. `docs/code-review.md` rule 11 and `docs/review-boundary.md` say the same (#724).
- Mitigated Antigravity stream interruption race (`replaceRunning: true` upstream issue https://github.com/getpaseo/paseo/issues/9498) by enforcing that completing workers end their turns immediately and leads idle-gate prompt acks alongside atomic disk acks (#573).
- Updated `docs/orchestration-practices.md`, `docs/dispatch-footer.md`, `skills/fan-out/SKILL.md`, and `AGENTS.md` standing order 10 with dual-route completion semantics (#573).
- Return failure when lead acknowledgement has no successful route and require `--done` for `--lead-ack` (#573)
- `adversary_marker_gate`: every JSON payload is also read leaf by leaf (a decoy fence or code span in a neighbour leaf can no longer hide an APPROVE marker leaf, and an escaped `\u0041PPROVE` verdict counts as APPROVE), `D=sub; cd $D` is expanded and the file is read under the event cwd too (a `cd` target set in another tool call stays a documented limit), and a word equal to a flag word is scanned as written too; `ade-worker-run archive-window` blocks on a closed agent with no readable state file and shows window names escaped in refusals; `ade-tidy` lists a corrupt claim file as a warning in a dry run (`--apply` still refuses); the approve-post scrubbed-env test pins PATH, HOME, USER and LOGNAME, and docs/review-boundary.md gains a manual probe for the live sudo-ask path (#734).
- Paseo's `antigravity` provider read `error` since the slim-down: it runs the `agy` CLI from PATH (Paseo.app `builtin-plugins/antigravity-provider`), C53 had uninstalled `agy`, and E7 restored the provider without it; the `agy-acp-bb` shim and `agy_acp_server.par` are not what it launches (2026-11-10). `config/install.toml` gains the hard Mac component `antigravity-cli` (`brew install --cask antigravity-cli`, probe `agy --version`) so `ade-install doctor` reports it missing and `ade-install apply --yes --system --apply-system --id antigravity-cli` installs it; the `antigravity` login check now records `agy` (run it once and sign in). (#537)
- The `antigravity-argon` frontier-fallback row dispatched `antigravity/gemini-4-argon --thinking high`, which Paseo refuses ("Unknown Antigravity model"): the provider folds the effort into the id (`gemini-4-argon-high`). The row now names `gemini-4-argon-high`, and `acp-dispatch` maps a bare antigravity id plus `--effort` (tendcf and nix2cf call `--model gemini-4-argon`) to that id with no `--thinking` (`paseo_model`). (#537)
- `route_agent.provider_models("pi")` (so `pi-default`, `bin/pi_defaults.py` and `services --check-models`) trusted Paseo's Pi list, which kept `antigravity/gemini-3.1-pro` (gone from Pi's catalog) and omitted `antigravity/gemini-4-argon` (which Pi runs). For a namespace Pi keeps a refreshed catalog for (a `models-store.json` key) it now takes `pi --list-models`; live extension namespaces (cursor) stay as Paseo lists them; Pi unreadable leaves Paseo's list alone. (#537)
- Blocked default component installation after unknown or failed presence probes, preserving known-missing installs and explicit `--force` repair under existing component/system authority and ledger checks. (#558)
- `archived_send_gate`: a `$(...)` or backtick is text inside single quotes (a markdown code span in a message was judged as a send, a false deny) and a command inside double quotes (`"$(paseo send X)"` is parsed as a send); a `#` after an escaped blank, a continuation or a closing quote is no longer a comment start; a target in a shell variable (`"$LEAD"`, `$id`, `{}`) no longer switches the backstop off; the backstop ignores `paseo send` and ids inside quoted message text (but still sees a string run by `eval`, `watch`, `tmux`, `osascript`, `-c`, `-e`, `--run=`, `--command=`, a quoted id or list of ids, a `"${VAR:-id}"` default, and every string when a shell is left outside quotes: `| sh`, `bash <<<`, `$SHELL`, `source <(...)`; a shell word inside message text does not count, and the scan is linear), a `#` glued to the end of a `$(...)`, backtick or `$((...))` is part of its word, and its denial says the hook could not parse the form instead of `Re-send to`. `ade-worker-run`: tests for the normalised-work, other-repo and MAIN-placement branches of window ownership, and the `find_window` docstring. (#692)
- A message to an archived or replaced agent no longer resurrects it (Paseo un-archives and runs an archived agent that is sent a message; the ex-lead 994234ba came back running that way on 2026-11-14). `bin/ade_send_guard.py` decides from the completed-handoff records: a send to an agent a self-handoff replaced goes to its successor, a send to an archived agent nothing replaced is refused (`ADE_ALLOW_ARCHIVED_SEND=<its full id>` resumes one on purpose). The new Claude hook `claude/hooks/archived_send_gate.py` denies such `paseo send` / `send_agent_prompt` calls and names the successor; `completion-notice`, `fable-wait` and `deadline` use the same decision. Also hardened from the #669 review: handoff records must be owned by the reader and not group/world-writable, `home` must be an absolute path, and `ade-net-resume` no longer reads a listing without string ids as proof a lead is retired. (#678)
- `ade-window-name`: a first agent whose `createdAt` is more than a minute in the future no longer counts as young (no equality-as-default), and a decision-script name with control characters is refused before it is written. (#674)
- `ade-window-name`: a run past its 25 s budget (or cancelled by Paseo) is aborted (python child killed, no late `setTitle`); the decision script's name is validated before it is written; a title equal to the first agent's own counts as Paseo's default only within 5 minutes of its creation. (#671)
- `ade-window-name` tests: the lone-surrogate and private-use branches of the decision-name check are now pinned by tests, and the cancel test reads its own log. (#679)
- `bin/ade-auto-permit` now also approves permission requests from agents whose Paseo labels include
  `ade.lead=<its --lead id>` (audit reason `lead-label`, checked by default via the exact `ls -a -g --label` filter), so
  a lead's env-stripped top-level workers, which are not descendants, no longer stall on prompts. The launch shapes in
  `skills/fan-out`, `docs/orchestration-practices.md`, `docs/scheduling.md` and `skills/handoff` document the label. (#623)
- `bin/ade-auto-permit`: the audit log (`auto-permit.jsonl`) now records each request's tool `name` (the name field only, never arguments; redacted unless the provider is classified and the name is an identifier that does not look like a credential) and the denylist `rule` that matched, in every request row. The question matcher splits names on word boundaries (`task_user` is no longer `ask_user`), and a well-formed `mcp__server__tool` name is held back only when the tool is itself a question or elicitation or acts on another agent's cards or mode (`respond_to_permission`, `set_agent_mode`, `update_agent`, `send_agent_prompt`, `send_terminal_keys`, `kill_terminal`, `cancel_agent`, `kill_agent`, `archive_agent`, `archive_workspace`, `create_schedule`, `update_schedule`, `run_schedule_once`, `resume_schedule`, `create_heartbeat`, held under any prefix or suffix, so OpenCode's `paseo_respond_to_permission` too), so `browser_select` and `ask_gemini` are approved as the tool prompts they are. `AskUserQuestion`, `ExitPlanMode`, `EnterPlanMode`, Codex/Pi/OMP/ACP question and elicitation names stay pending. (#662)
- `autorename` named the wrong or a missing session ("no transcript for session <id>") when a Claude Code session started from a predecessor's environment (`/baton`, `/handoff`) inherited its `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`; it now reads the session id from the owning Claude process's registry file (`~/.claude/sessions/<CLAUDE_PID>.json`, used only when its `pid` and `procStart` match the live process) and uses the variable only as a fallback (#259).
- `claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py`: denies an unquoted word that starts with `=` (zsh expands `=cmd`, so `echo ===; next` failed with `zsh: == not found` and never ran `next`; `[[ ]]`, `(( ))`, `=(cmd)` and quoted words pass), and a `paseo send` or `paseo agent send` command whose double-quoted argument holds a backtick or `$(` (the shell ran it before paseo saw the message); both reasons say what to write instead, and `docs/bash-habits-gate.md` lists the probed cases (#707).
- `load-watch ci decide` audit-log events name the decided mode (`lw-pending-approved`, `lw-pending-denied`, `lw-pending-unavailable`), not always `lw-local-*` (#760).
- The `archived_send_gate` backstop no longer judges an agent id that sits in a path component (`agent-reports/<id>/`) as a send target (#760).
- `skills/btww` (2026-11-01): the reminder quotes only the running TUI's own table row; Muse had been given Claude's `/btw` description
  instead of its own `/side` (alias `/btw`). (#240)
- Distinguished managed Go credential preflight failures without exposing subprocess output or trying another account. (#790)
- Gate routed Pi handoff acceptance on a successful post-ack durable real turn; retain failed-pool retry history and reconcile explicit operator-appointed custody without creating another writer. (#791)
- Reject failed successor runtimes and add an isolated named-recipient archive regression fixture. (#791)
- `scripts/github-ci-run` now prefers the remote-tracking `ci/base` ref over a local branch, so a stale local `ci/base` no longer shadows the fixed remote workflow; it warns to stderr when a local branch is strictly behind. (#800)
- Claim stack-rebase temporary worktrees before creation and release claims after cleanup on success or failure. (#77)
- Registered Chrome DevTools MCP for Codex alongside Claude Code, and extended the wiring doctor to check both clients' stdio commands, arguments, transport and enablement with client-specific, secret-safe repair commands. (#774)
- `ade-claim` Paseo plugin failed to load in the daemon (`Plugin failed to load: The "path" argument must be of type string or an instance of URL. Received undefined`, `paseo plugin install` rc 1, status `failed`; #638 D2). Paseo bundles `index.server.ts` to CommonJS and `eval`s it, so `import.meta.url` is undefined there and `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)` threw at module load (the daemon never hands a plugin its directory; `ade-yolo` works because it never asks). New `resolvePluginRoot` (returns root + source): a real `file:` URL still wins (plain Node ESM, the tests); otherwise Paseo's own install record (`~/.paseo/config.json` `plugins["ade-claim"].path`), otherwise the shared checkout `~/src/djbclark-ade/plugins/ade-claim`, using the account's home directory (`userInfo()`), never `$HOME`; the root and its source are logged once at load. The trusted `bin/ade_claims.py`, `bin/ade-git-guard` and `libexec/agent-bin` are found from it by the parent walk (env `ADE_CLAIMS_PY` / `ADE_GIT_GUARD` / `ADE_AGENT_BIN` override). Tests cover each source and load `hook.mjs` with `import.meta` replaced by `{}` as Paseo's bundle does. (#644)
- `bin/review-launch` runs its boundary self-check on the launch's own `--model` and caches it per model, so an unavailable default model no longer refuses every launch (#297)
- `acp-dispatch` reads `review-launch --json`'s `structured_output` envelope, so a Claude read-only or deny slice's report is no longer lost as no-report (#297)
- `acp-dispatch` keeps a slice's in-flight reservation while it waits for an agent slot and re-judges admission on a fresh quota reading before launching (#297)
- `route_agent.py` keeps the tighter reading per quota window when aiuse and `paseo usage ls` both report it (#297)
- `adversary-marker` now judges every open PR at the head on `workflow_dispatch` too, keys its concurrency on the head sha, and counts an APPROVE only in an owner-written PR's body or in an owner's own issue comment; a body APPROVE on any other PR never counts, an owner comment counts only if every edit in its history is the owner's (GraphQL `editor`/`userContentEdits`), the body's last editor is read from `editor`/`lastEditedAt` (the earlier `lastEditedBy` is not a GitHub GraphQL field, so that query errored), both shared-head lookups are paginated to the end, and a BLOCK in any comment fails (Argon review and the Codex review of PR 300, 2026-11-07) (#300)
- `bin/review-launch` refuses a worktree with an outward symlink (exit 2), resolves relative globs, adds `config/secret-deny.toml`'s patterns to its read-deny list, gives read-only mode no web tools unless the new `--allow-web`, and no longer reads the prompt in its bypass-flag scan (#300)
- `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl` no longer pre-trusts `~/Downloads`; `docs/scheduling.md` requires an explicit `--mode` on every `paseo schedule create` (#300)
- Rejected missing or malformed PR body editor attribution instead of inheriting owner trust when the edit timestamp was null (#300).
- Bind aiuse Schema 1.6 canonical Codex subscription metadata to its own routing pool while keeping API credits independent and unknown metadata closed. (#779)
- Reject conflicting parent families and child vendor/provider identities when inheriting canonical subscription metadata. (#779)
- Require Codex worker/probe briefs to preserve yielded command session metadata and recover existing process results without replay. (#779)
- Made `git-commit-own` publish after committing, support explicit publish targets and `--no-push`, and report rebase or push failures. (#610)
- Refused differing fetch/push endpoints and multiple push URLs before remote contact to keep history checks aligned with publication. (#610)
- `bin/completion-notice`: a short commit SHA in a `gh run list --commit` hint of a done notice's title or detail is expanded to the full SHA with `git rev-parse` (or the notice is refused, exit 2, when it names no commit), because gh matches only the full 40-character SHA and `gh run list --commit 5a2228d` prints `[]` with exit 0, so a lead's poll never found the run; `docs/dispatch-footer.md` rule 20 now tells workers to use `gh run list --branch BRANCH` or the full SHA (#704).
- `bin/completion-notice`: a 4-6 digit SHA in a `gh run list --commit` hint of a done notice is refused without asking git (a short prefix typed for another repository's commit can still resolve in the current one), so the floor is 7 digits; `docs/orchestration-practices.md` item 6 now puts the short-SHA sentence after its Why paragraph (#721).
- Routed manual ClinePass dispatches through configured Pi with fully namespaced model IDs, kept the pool manual-only, made unified tier span scanning linear and TOML-string-aware, preserved writer reason metadata and exact suffix bytes, and made projection drift check parsed tier equality as well as outside-subtree bytes. (#766)
- Gave `research` a profile on every live pool, including claude and codex, which had none: no vendor here lacks research capability, and the two strongest pools were the ones excluded. Codex's `research` is also `plenty_kinds`-gated like its other everyday kinds. Kept `ade-harden` and `ade-github-setup` usable when `config/ade.toml` exists without their domain, instead of refusing on mere presence. (#766)
- Said why a kind cannot route instead of "no pool has room" when no live pool profiles it (`github`, `realtime-web`, and `api-only` after omp was archived), gave claude a last-resort `bulk`/`mechanical` rank behind every other pool, and documented that frontier order is a cost ordering (astra before Fable) rather than a model-quality claim. (#766)
- Made claude's `bulk`/`mechanical` a structural last resort (`fallback_kinds`), because a weak rank alone let it win against a usable pool that was merely headroom- or pace-penalised; the unprofiled-kind diagnostic now ignores retired pools; and the Argon/Claude research policy comments and the frontier cost-ordering prose now match what the code does. (#766)
- Corrected Argon's research rank to 24 so it sits behind cursor/zai and cursor-cloud rather than ahead of them, and documented that `fallback_after` gates every requested kind, not only `frontier`. (#766)
- Made Argon's `research` a structural last resort (`fallback_kinds`) rather than a rank preference, so a usable pool that is merely headroom-penalised still takes the slice; corrected the fallback predicate description to say it blocks Argon only while **both** Codex and Claude can take the requested kind. (#766)
- `bin/acp-dispatch` classifies GitHub Copilot quota stops as `quota` (2026-11-01): the CLI's 402 `You have exceeded your monthly quota` / `You have no quota` / `quota_exceeded` / `"errorType":"quota"`, `Quota exceeded. Upgrade to increase your limit`, `You have exceeded your premium request allowance/limit`, the `weekly rate limit` cap and provisional AI-credit wording. A Copilot quota stop with no parseable reset (Copilot names none, aiuse has no copilot pool, quotas reset monthly) is a far reset: auto-resume is skipped and `check` prints a `reroute now:` line instead of a 30-minute in-place resume on the exhausted account; `retry_after_ms` is read as the reset, `reset on April 19, 2026 at 9:00 PM` parses, and `on <date without year>` never falls into the clock-time branch; the same error text inside a session-log event (JSON-escaped quotes) is read the same way (#212).
- `cow-pasture create` stopped handing out pastures whose dependencies were symlinks into the source checkout (2026-11-01: cow 0.1.10 links large dirs such as `node_modules/*` and `.venv/bin` into the source by default, and an `npm install` in a paseo pasture emptied ~392 package dirs of `~/src/paseo/node_modules`): it now passed `--no-symlink` to `cow create`, ran `cow materialise <source>/<name>`, and exited 1 without printing the path if any symlink still resolved into the source (or a recorded source of it), or an untracked one into another checkout `cow list --json` records (#228)
- `bin/cow-pasture remove` drops the pasture's seeded Claude Code trust entry from `~/.claude.json` (new `untrust` subcommand; an entry a
  session extended stays), and its usage names `--force` as the non-interactive removal. (#241)
- `bin/ade-worker-run`: `--cwd` is `expanduser`ed where it is parsed, and the window check and the post-launch `Cwd` check compare directories with one `same_dir` helper (`os.path.samefile`, else the textual `normcase(realpath)` compare), so a `~` spelling or a case-differing spelling on macOS's case-insensitive volume is no longer a false mismatch (cq-41). (#742)
- `claude/hooks/adversary_marker_gate.py` now denies a `gh` write whose JSON payload is nested past the parser's recursion limit when it mentions APPROVE (an escaped live marker could slip past the leaf walk); the old recursion test could not fail and is replaced by tests that do (#741)
- `ade-backup-stores --keep` only prunes after a successful run and only among successful generations (`ok` / empty errors); failed renames and failed runs no longer displace usable retained backups. (#402)
- `ade-backup-stores --clean-parts` validates `--dest` with `refuse_reason` and the cleanup parent with `refuse_cleanup_parent` before any recursive delete, so an invalid dest cannot remove sibling `*.part` under a live store. (#402)
- `ade-browser-task --root` accepted before or after the subcommand (just recipes
  and docs); `relay --send` and `--dry-run` are mutually exclusive (#454).
- Cleared unverified/foreign `ADE_CLAIM_ID` on `agent.open.before` when claim lookup or ownership fails; canonicalize claim cwd paths with `realpath` before compare (#500).
- Documented `--set __STATE__=$HOME/.local/state/ade` for installing `com.djbclark.ade-update-check` (unfilled `__STATE__` exits 2) (#359).
- `ade-merge-ready` takes `live_approve` / `merge_now` only from the `adversary-marker` check (attribution-aware `decide`), not from an unfiltered body or comment marker scan (#371).
- `stack-rebase` refuses fork/cross-repo heads and uses `git rebase --onto` with the old parent tip so squash-merged parent commits are not replayed (#512).
- Deadline / pr-policy install dry-run tests assert unreplaced `__NAME__` / `@NAME@` placeholders via the ade-sched pattern, not bare `__` (temp dirs with `__` no longer false-fail) (#495).
- Closed the remaining code-fixable `ade-update` LOWs from PR 20: `--check` casefolds collisions when `core.ignorecase` is true, `git status` uses `--ignored=matching`, `run()` decodes helper/git output as UTF-8 with replacement, `check_skills`/`link_skills` are pinned to `helper_env`, and `skill-everywhere`'s grokbot render cleans its mktemp dir and no longer uses `eval`+`&&` for the copy swap (#407).
- `bin/verifycmd` refused `register` / `run-once` on a shared/primary checkout or foreign /
  expired claim via `ade_claims` ownership helpers (exit 2; order 9). (#464)
- `bin/verifycmd` deadline names kept a sha256 id component and the attempt suffix within the
  100-char limit so sanitize collisions (`agent:a` vs `agent@a`) no longer share a live name. (#464)
- Push rejudge emits one `marker` matrix cell per head (concurrency `adversary-marker-<head>`) and scopes unreadable-PR failures to that head, so a sweep no longer races dispatch / Tests completion or poisons other heads (#484).
- `bin/bm-lint`: a config project path with a lone surrogate is listed under `skipped` instead of aborting the run, and a clean partial-read headline names the skipped count (queue Carried #19 / PR 55 LOWs) (#406).
- `tests/test_bin_modes.py` docstring matches the exemption of any `tests/` path; `tests/test_route_agent.py` pins `xhigh` and `max` as valid `[lead]` effort levels (#361).
- `bin/review-pr`'s `deadline run` wrap no longer passes `--heartbeat 300` (review-launch does not beat) (#361).
- `bin/ade-release-ready`: require Unreleased entries after a prospective in-memory fold (same dedup as `changelog-fold`), so leftover fragments that only duplicate older CHANGELOG sections are not READY (#445).
- `bin/gh-pr-checks` forwards SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP to its `deadline run` supervisor and falls back to raw `gh pr checks` when deadline refuses or exits before the watch starts (docs/deadline.md "Never costs the wait") (#448).
- Relative local `core.hooksPath` values are resolved from the process cwd after every `-C` (with a leading `~/` expanded), matching Git's hook-lookup semantics, not from `--work-tree` / `show-toplevel` or the common Git directory (#432).
- `hooks-path` no longer refuses plain `git branch <new>` when local `core.hooksPath` is non-ade; only force/delete/move/copy `branch` forms (plus push/fetch/other ref-writes) are refused, so lazy-install probes still get git's own exit (#432).
- `ade-harden` inventory lists private repos via authenticated
  `/user/repos?affiliation=owner` and `/orgs/{owner}/repos?type=all` (not
  public-only `/users/{owner}/repos`), and `_gh_json` parses a complete JSON
  document before falling back to NDJSON (#452).
- `normalize_export.py` fallback `conversation_id` includes export ordinal and content fingerprint so multi-conversation exports without ids/titles/timestamps no longer collide at the S1 locator (#439)
- `normalize_export.py` reads at most 32 MiB + 1 byte before refusing oversize input, so the size gate fails closed without allocating the whole file (#439)
- Copilot `{id_dotted}` now preserves the model-name prefix and only dots the numeric version suffix (`claude-opus-5.5`, not `claude.opus.5-5`) (#401).
- After `promote` reuses a shared alias, displaced same-family roles resolve to their canonical ids so judgment fallback does not keep returning the new frontier (#401).
- Aligned `tests/test_bin_modes.py` docstring with the nested `tests/` exemption and pinned that `[lead]` accepts `xhigh`/`max` effort (#391).
- `ade-sched` imports the install catalog only for `list`/`diff`, so other subcommands stay usable on Python 3.10 without `tomllib` (#433).
- `ade-sched diff` / doctor treat Paseo probe timeouts/launch errors and failed host-scheduler scans as unknown, not empty or missing (#433).
- Reconcile treats matching unit files as healthy only when the timer is active (completed oneshots without a timer may stay inactive); keeps implicit `--ade` so linked worktrees still refuse; preserves the old ledger identity across a label rename until removal completes (#461).
- Cron reconcile hash-match now compares the installed `# BEGIN ade-sched` block instead of a pseudo `Path("crontab")` file, and ledger removes refuse a different recorded backend (#511).
- `ade-sched` paseo reconcile: presence checks use only `backend=paseo` live jobs; delete failures keep `pending-remove` so the next run retries (#520).
- Mac B1 idle evidence capture now records `cmd:` + `verified: no match` (and exit status) so successful idle probes leave non-empty `mac_proofs` files; empty redirects still FAILCLOSE (#417).
- Wave 6 §C operator probes are copy-pasteable ERE with real `|` in a fenced
  block (markdown table `\|` escapes under-matched and falsely reported clean
  on bigmac 2026-11-10) (#533).
- `wave6-delete-check --inventory` (non-JSON) prints gated refs and `gate_exits` on success, not only on failure (#472).
- Restored `acp-dispatch check_report_name` parity in the inline footer fill: basenames
  Claude Code refuses to Write (`report`/`summary`/`findings`/`analysis`*.md) deny the
  launch instead of injecting an undeliverable contract (#344).
- Documented that native `paseo run` fan-out must re-plan per launch until in-flight quota reservation is native; corrected model-routing D5 (#498).
- Restored fan-out Collect teaching for `bin/completion-notice` / `done: <title>, <PR URL>` after the LIVE_RE scrub (#498).
- Hardened the `/loose` zero-tool reader: `--setting-sources ""` plus `disableAllHooks` so a hostile checkout's project hooks cannot run (#333).
- `bg` no longer sends false "expired ... Recover: rerun the unfinished part" notices for runs that never started or whose process group was
  killed: its run record is registered only once the run has started, and both its `bg-wait-*` and `bg-run-*` records carry a `--holder`. (#266)
- `docs/mcp-servers.md` no longer claims Beeper is always registered or that its OAuth works "from any session": it gives the user-scope `claude mcp add` command and says only the operator can approve the OAuth, by either `/mcp` → beeper → Authenticate in an interactive tab or `claude mcp login beeper` from a shell (`--no-browser` prints the URL), because headless and Paseo-launched Claude sessions get no `authenticate` tool; an agent runs `claude mcp login` only with the operator's yes (Claude Code 2.1.325, 2026-11-15). (#665)
- The top-level successor launch in AGENTS.md and docs/context-hygiene.md now also strips `PASEO_WORKSPACE_ID`:
  with only the two agent variables removed, `paseo run` still inherited the caller's workspace and ignored `--cwd`
  (live, 2026-11-12, ade-yolo canary r2). (#612)
- `skill-everywhere --remove paseo` removes the Hermes `paseo` link when it is ours (it was a silent no-op), `--remove` of other names still leaves it alone; `bin/ade-update`'s `linked now:` line names `paseo` when the Hermes link was made even if a stuck Paseo-owned row remains; docs/skill-everywhere.md gives the real `== paseo (managed by Paseo)` header wording. (#878)
- Corrected stale routing docs (2026-11-21): `docs/model-routing.md` B3 now says `codex` bills OpenAI API prepaid credits and adds B3a `codex-subscription` (the 2026-11-17 split); SKILL C3 no longer lists bulk/mechanical on `codex`; the C7 research model is `gemini-4-hydrogen-high` instead of `gemini-3.8-flash-high` (#873)
- Documented top-level ade-yolo canary launches, real-answer and Cursor persistence fixture gates, Cursor Auto selection, and scoped live canary results with rejected-fixture coverage omitted. (#611)
- Fixed `acp-dispatch` auto-resume scheduling across a DST change (2026-11-01): the schedule was computed on a fixed-offset `now`, so a `pmset` wake (and `not_before_iso`) that fell after the US fall-back or spring-forward was an hour off; it now computes in UTC (`now_utc()`, frozen by `ACP_DISPATCH_NOW` for tests) and shows local wall time only at the wake instant, in the real zone from `local_zone()` (`TZ`, else `/etc/localtime`); the wake tests are frozen-clock cases across both America/New_York transitions (#213).
- `tests/test_deadline.py::test_install_agents_auto_looks_for_paseo_on_the_job_path` no longer depends on a real `paseo` in `/opt/homebrew/bin` or `/usr/local/bin`: the test pins its copied plist's `PATH` to one empty directory, so `want_agents`' own `~/.local/bin` prepend is what the fake-home step proves (the code under test was right: it reads the plist's own `PATH`) (#728).
- `claude/hooks/agent_footer_gate.py`: a delivery-contract footer pasted inside a fence or `>` quote (a peer's brief) no longer suppresses the hook's own footer: only a `## Delivery contract` heading outside pasted text counts, and a pasted footer's delivery line is never taken as this launch's report path (#728).
