- `claude/hooks/agent_footer_gate.py`: the prompt's own `Report path:` line is also read behind the operator's `A1.` / `A1a.` / `B2)` labels; fenced code blocks (CommonMark fences, one linear scan), `>` quotes and 4-space-indented lines (a pasted peer brief) no longer compete with the brief's own report path; when the labels conflict (an own `Report path:` against a peer's `Worker report:`), the hook derives its own path instead of ranking them, so it never names a peer's report (#728).
- `tests/test_guard_keep_tags.py` runs the real git guard for both refusals and fails if `GUARD_KEEP_RULES` in `bin/review-pr` or `bin/ade-merge-ready` drifts from the rule tags `bin/ade_claims.py` prints (#728).
- `bin/claude-cost-mix` and `bin/claude-cost-whatif` priced Fable 5.2 as Fable 5.1 ($10/MTok base, unverified since #627);
  the published price is $8/MTok base (cache read 0.025x unchanged: $0.20/MTok), checked against
  platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing on 2026-11-13. (#631)
- Rejected malformed new/local config before normalization, preserved legacy routing
  validation/defaults and unusable-input errors, and merged raw gate/floor overlays
  before clamping in all read-only config commands. (#308)
- Rejected raw service duplicates before routing isolation, preserved explicit CLI
  root/home targets in every option position, and disabled local loading for diff baselines. (#308)
- GUI-hog auto-freeze (adversary round 1): it now requires launchd's sampler job to be loaded (a hand-run `load-watch sample` could leave a timed freeze that nothing thawed), "Ignore 1 h" pauses it, `[freeze.auto]` values are type-checked (the overlay can only switch it off), the app's start time is read in the tick, keep-running rules must name an exact executable inside a bundle, and an `OSError` is a refusal retried after 120 s. (#859)
- GUI-hog dialog: a quick second Freeze click on another row was swallowed because every redraw restarted one window-wide 0.7 s click grace. The grace is now per row, a row has its own in-flight state, freezes queue one after another, and a redraw that changes nothing is skipped. (#859)
- Preserved indexed history when a client, adapter or source log was unavailable or a JSONL transcript was damaged, instead of pruning sessions or replacing complete history with partial parsing. (#310)
- `bin/skill-everywhere` no longer exits 1 because a foreign real dir sits at the name of a third-party (chrome-use) skill such
  as `canvas`: that is a note, while a collision with a public, private or site skill name still exits 1. (#871)
- `bin/ade_claims.py`: a Claude agent that the ade-claim plugin rebound a claim to is the owner on its first launch, with no re-open (M1): `caller_ident` carries `PASEO_AGENT_ID` and `is_mine` accepts it only as the exact `owner.record` of a `bind-owner`-shaped claim, never over an explicit `ADE_CLAIM_ID`. (#843)
- `bin/ade_self_session.py`: `finish` records the predecessor's active claims before the archive and reports any that another agent took meanwhile as `claims_not_rebound`, so a race with the plugin's rebind neither loses a claim silently nor rebinds it twice (M2). (#843)
- `adversary-marker`: read the PR body's last editor from `userContentEdits(first: 1)` (connection is newest-first, live probe 2026-11-07) after GitHub removed the GraphQL `lastEditedBy` field, which had made every gate run fail with `undefinedField`; PR 300 then replaced this with `editor`, `lastEditedAt` and a full edit-history check (#302)
- Agent-tool sub-agents following the delivery footer no longer fail with "Agent not found: main": footer rule 22 tells them to skip the Paseo completion notice (the Agent tool already returns their final message), and `bin/completion-notice --send --requester main|team-lead` sends nothing and exits 0 with a clear message (#648)
- Stopped unconditional `--new-workspace local` on every child dispatch so advisors reuse the task workspace instead of minting one per slice. (#309)
- `bin/git-commit-own` publishes to a `forgejo.mit.edu` https remote through `git-forgejo` (ls-remote, fetch and push), so the publish step no longer fails with "could not read Username"; a Forgejo remote with no `git-forgejo` on PATH exits 3 naming it, and other remotes keep plain git (#857)
- Imported the assigned worktree as an explicit local Paseo workspace when dispatching child agents, preventing inherited parent directories from overriding `-C` (#304).
- Preserved the returned `paseo_cwd` and warned about missing or mismatched placement without stopping the paid agent (#304).
- `tests/test_ade_jobs_inventory.py`: both `doctor()` calls pass `skip_credential_bak=True`, so the suite no longer
  reads this machine's `~/.local/state/ade/credential-bak` and no longer fails on the vault residue's passed
  `delete-after` (2026-11-19) instead of on job drift. (#807)
- `route_agent.py` records `age_s` of the account whose reading a multi-account pool keeps, not the last-processed account's, and the oldest age when one account has no usable reading, so a pool of two accounts, one older than the stale limit, reads as old to the frontier gate in either account order (the wider gate gap is queue Carried 84) (F39, CodeRabbit on omercnet/paseo-plugins#618; adversary M1) (#834)
- `pr-radar-route` and `docs/model-routing.md` no longer claim pr-radar's example is a byte-identical copy; `docs/dispatch-footer.md` item 16 gives `load-watch` its full path `~/src/djbclark-ade/bin/load-watch` (#834)
- `bin/load-watch` no longer stamps a Hermes cooldown after a failed ping, so a failed alert is retried on the next cycle instead of being suppressed for the cooldown period. (#803)
- `bin/acp-dispatch` recognized positively identified Anthropic credit-balance refusals on failed runs, excluded exit-0 tool output, and capped row-derived or refusal-text retry timestamps at six hours so healthy pools are not poisoned and exhausted pools are rechecked (#287).
- `bin/opencode-plugin-guard` can now clear Paseo's blank OpenCode model list: run without `--check`, it patches the `paseo-managed-bridge` plugin's stale-binding check (OpenCode's Bun reports a refused connection as `error.code === "ECONNREFUSED"`, which the plugin missed, so every bind returned HTTP 500 after a Paseo daemon restart). On demand only, no restart; upstream getpaseo/paseo#8780. (#281)
- Quoted YAML frontmatter in the OpenCode `/hand` command so OpenCode loads the command when the description contains a colon (#286).
- `route_agent.py` read every pool as stale whenever aiuse's top-level `fresh` was false, so one rate-limited pool
  (antigravity, queried at most every 15 minutes) made `plan --kinds code` find no slice for minutes at a time.
  When the payload's `stale_pools` names the stale entries and the snapshot itself is young, only those
  (provider, pool_family) rows read as stale; `fresh: false` with no usable list, a malformed entry, or an unknown
  or old snapshot age still reads the whole payload as stale (fail closed) (#650)
- `bin/ade-self-session handoff` now works from a session whose cwd is in no git repository (for example `~`): the transaction records
  `head: null`, the successor attests `head: null`, and `accept`/`finish` still fail if the cwd became (or stopped being) a repository. Only
  git's "not a git repository (or any ...)" discovery failure counts, and only when no `.git` entry (file, directory, symlink, unreadable)
  exists in the real cwd or any ancestor; a timeout, unborn HEAD, broken linked worktree or gitfile, unreadable `.git` or any other git
  failure still refuses, and git runs without any inherited `GIT_*` variable. (#649)
- `bin/ade-self-session finish` now repoints the supervisor's lead file (`$ADE_SUPERVISOR_STATE_DIR/lead`, default
  `~/.local/state/djbclark-ade/supervisor/lead`) from the archived predecessor to the successor, atomically, and reports
  `supervisor_lead` (`repointed`, `already-successor`, `unchanged-not-predecessor`, `unchanged-not-regular`, `absent`,
  `error`); a file naming another agent, a symlink or a missing file is never touched (#870)
- `bin/ade-update` reads `skill-everywhere --check`'s `error: paseo skill is <state> on <tui>` lines as rows of a `paseo` pseudo-skill instead of a helper failure (exit 4): a missing Hermes link is a fixable link gap (exit 10 on `--check`, repaired on apply by the new `skill-everywhere paseo`), and `paseo` missing from a claude/codex/pi dir that Paseo's daemon syncs is a stuck row (exit 10, documented for hosts that never sync Paseo's skill). docs/skill-everywhere.md section 3a now says the Hermes link and the check need `paseo` selected. (#874)
- Dropped explicit worker mode flags for modeless providers before launch, retained compatibility with label-only catalogs, and archived confirmed empty workspaces minted by failed agent creation. (#789)
- `agent_footer_gate` denies launching an Agent-tool sub-agent whose report path already exists without its `.done` (an interrupted earlier sub-agent) and says to resume it with SendMessage; a deliberate fresh start adds a `Relaunch-reason: <why>` prompt line (allowed, logged). docs/orchestration-practices.md section 5 rule 10. (#691)
- Separate stable release records from authenticated reattestations, hold malformed discovery metadata, and transact selected records under current qualified-latest consent. (#796)
- Carry fresh exact-attempt authentication into selected transactions so obsolete reattestations cannot obstruct deployment, while retaining corruption and current-policy refusal. (#796)
- Permit authorized failed-target recovery inspection without inventing a loaded receipt, preserving complete non-target cohort and strict post-recovery verification. (#796)
- Report truthful read-only Journal/native/cohort status instead of a universal unsupported placeholder, and show a separately authorized standing apply schedule source for automatic promotion. (#796)
- Require the independently reviewed exact packaged namespace-helper bytes before import; remove the generic AST safety predicate and refuse matching-manifest rebinding/subscription variants before compilation. (#796)
- Limit fork publication to its candidate ref, retain crash-safe flock ownership, and recheck unattended App authority at each write. (#784)
- Permit clean detached exact-base workspaces when staging integrations, while retaining fatal Git branch lookup failures as holds. (#784)
- Bind plugin qualification evidence through the release core canonical toolchain string and enforce Git streaming deadlines with exact-child cleanup. (#793)
- Bind managed plugin helpers to the native candidate source directory and preserve regular materialized Git shim helper layout, with CI closure fixtures. (#793)
- Supply the explicit cold CJS fixture compiler in both existing full-suite CI jobs without skipping candidate binding assertions. (#793)
- Keep ordinary checkout plugins working when hosts expose source directories, and use explicit native managed-source classification for sealed helpers. (#793)
- The Bash gate's gh watcher rule also sees `rtk proxy gh run watch`, `env -i VAR=x gh ...` and `nice -n N gh ...`; dispatch-footer item 26 names the GraphQL rate-limit bucket, and the remaining undetected shapes are listed in docs/bash-habits-gate.md. (#684)
- The Bash gate's gh watcher rule also sees `env -i "VAR=$X" gh ...` (quoted assignment), `env -iu X`/`sudo -Hu me` (combined options), `nice --adjustment N`, `command -p gh`, `exec -a NAME gh` and `gh>log run watch 9`; a test now pins the four-candidate limit. (#694)
- `bin/ade-git-guard`: `git worktree remove` is refused (`worktree remove: live process`, exit 77) while another live process has its cwd or an open file inside the target, claimed or not; the refusal lists pid, command and path. It fails open when `lsof` is missing, fails or times out; `--force` does not bypass it, `ADE_ALLOW_LIVE_WORKTREE_REMOVE=1` or the operator override does (#693).
- Stopped `ade-github-setup apply --yes` before App ID discovery and ruleset operations when environment or deployment-branch-policy setup failed, preserving the prerequisite failure and reporting `refused-prerequisite`. (#556)
- Clarify bounded exact-file discovery when graft falls back to a parent index, excluding generated bundles before raw searches. (#779)
- `bin/ade-update` now detects drift in `~/.claude/helpers/graft-hooks.cjs` and installs an atomic, backed-up executable copy from the canonical checkout (step 3a), so a merged hook change can no longer age out silently; other checkouts stay report-only. (#826)
- `claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py`: the `paseo send` backtick check also reads a send after a `case` arm (`x) paseo send ...`), `ssh host`, `gtimeout`, `noglob`, `nocorrect`, `builtin`, `coproc`, `flock`, `watch`, `script`, `direnv`, `secretspec run` and `op run`, and `{paseo send ...;}` without a space after the brace (the #707 adversary's Lows); `bin/completion-notice`: two over-long lines wrapped and the `build_message` docstring now says a short SHA can be refused (the #721 lead review's Lows) (#733).
- `claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py`: the `paseo send` backtick check (gs-03, #733) also reads a send after `sudo-secretspec run --`, `doas`, `genv`, `gnohup`, `gstdbuf`, `gnice`, `unbuffer`, `chronic`, `mosh host` and `uv run` (the second wrapper batch of #733, which the changelog fragment of that PR did not list); `genv`, `gstdbuf`, `chronic` and `mosh` now have deny rows in `tests/test_bash_habits_gate.py`, so dropping one fails a test (#737).
- `gui-hog status` no longer raises when a window entry's RSS is a float; `window_means` rows carry `present`, `cpu_now` and an integer `rss_mb`. (#729)
- GUI-hog agent detection skips leading option words, so `node --max-old-space-size=4096 /opt/bin/pi` is recognised as the `pi` agent CLI. (#729)
- A hold file with pid 1 is no longer accepted; stale `.hold-*.tmp` files older than 2 minutes are swept; the start times of all holds come from one `ps -p a,b,c` call. (#729)
- GUI-hog load-source labels never carry command-line text: a process is named from the kernel's executable path, asked for every process (the first word `ps` prints can be argv[1] or an environment string when argv[0] is empty, and an argv[0] with a space is cut inside its path), and `?` when that path is unreadable because the process exited; the earlier "never from argv" wording in the docs and changelog is corrected. (#875)
- GUI-hog keep-running rules name one exact executable (matched on the kernel path, never as a prefix; an absolute directory is refused), the auto-freeze reads its start times in one batch call and only while a build is held and leaves out an app that started after the table was read, and the stale `config/gui-hog.toml` comments are corrected (the #859 adversary lows). (#872)
- GUI-hog load sources label a process by its executable name only, never its arguments, also inside an app bundle (`clang -c a.c -DPASS=...` no longer leaks); an always-freeze app is hidden from the dialog only while it is frozen or the sampler will freeze it now, so a Resumed, refused or idle one is an ordinary row again; `gui-hog suggest` cleans bundle ids and survives a bad `[suggest]` value, and `status --json` survives a corrupt sources record (the #872 adversary review lows and notes). (#872)
- GUI-hog load-source labels inside an app bundle come from the kernel executable path (an argument holding `/Contents/MacOS/` no longer reaches the label), and an always-freeze app is hidden only while the sampler may act (a live hold, no Ignore snooze) or it is already frozen, so `gui-hog show` lists a busy one (the #872 re-review lows). (#872)
- `macos/gui-hog/UI.swift`: the window title now reflects the hold state on every refresh, matching the body. It was set once at launch to "A build is waiting" and never updated, so after the last hold cleared the title still said a build was waiting while the body said "No build is waiting now." (operator 2026-11-21). (#849)
- Clarified that a Bash habits gate denial executes no part of a command and requires re-running the whole corrected command. (#608)
- The Bash habits gate (`claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py`) no longer reads the text after the `)` of a `$(... | tool ...)` group as arguments of the last tool inside it, which denied valid commands such as `gh api x $(echo $r|cut -d/ -f1):master --jq .y` as `cut --jq`; it now checks the outer command (substitutions replaced by a placeholder) and each substitution body as separate commands, so a GNU option in either is still denied, including in a lone `$(date -d yesterday)` that it used to miss; escaped nested backtick commands inside arithmetic expansions remained checked rather than being discarded (#254)
- Serialized live component planning, execution and install-ledger read-modify-write to retain concurrent evidence, with fresh observations after waiting and unchanged dry-run/system authority (#561).
- Six minors from the 2026-11-01 second-opinion review of PRs 171-185: `go-status` reads a Go/Console wall (`go-limit`, `go-429`, `no-funds`, `region`) as OpenCode's own even when the record names a foreign `provider=`/`model=` (it was printed `(foreign)` with exit 0, a silent quota outage); `route_agent.py --apply` exits 7 (cannot list), not 3, when `paseo-set-model list` prints valid JSON that is not a list; `self-slash`'s Paseo provider gate accepts a one-element list around the inspect JSON (as `rumination-watch` does) and strips leading whitespace before matching `/new`, `/clear`, `/reset` and `/compact`; a redundant test of the `rumination-watch` auto-seat lost-start guard was cut to one row; `docs/failover.md` says `sweep_tmp_dirs` checks every entry at any depth (#220).
- A completed `ade-self-session` handoff now carries every lead-id binding to the successor: `ade-worker-run` adopts the archived predecessor's windows and `ade.lead` workers instead of refusing them as another lead's, the installed `ade-net-resume` and `ade-auto-permit` jobs follow the chain to the current lead, and `fable-wait` delivers to it. `ade-net-resume` with a retired lead and no successor now logs `skip-lead-retired` and exits 0 instead of crash-looping with exit 2; a cyclic ledger is refused loudly (`ade-worker-run` exit 1, `ade-net-resume` exit 2), never read as "no predecessors". (#669)
- Follow-up to the load-gate-50 change: the `admit` test helper and `load_module()` now scrub `LW_ADMIT_PER_CORE` and `LW_LOAD_PER_CORE` from the host environment (a host that exports one no longer fails the default-limit tests), a churn test is renamed and gains a corroborated-churn refusal case, and stale wording in `bin/load-watch` and `docs/gui-hog-dialog.md` now names site-public-djbclark `f536056` as bgrun's 5.0 base and 5.0 as the steer line. (#868)
- `bin/review-pr` and `bin/ade-merge-ready` no longer `shutil.rmtree` a temporary worktree after ade-git-guard refuses `git worktree remove` because a live process uses it or another agent claims it (exit 77, rule tags `worktree remove: live process` / `worktree remove of a claimed workspace`): the directory stays, the refusal is reported, and the result carries `worktree_left`; the guard's routine `--force`-on-an-unclaimed-tree refusal still cleans up. `ADE_LSOF_TIMEOUT=inf`/`nan` no longer crashes the guard's live-process check (it uses the default; values above 120 s are cut), and tests pin that only `ADE_ALLOW_LIVE_WORKTREE_REMOVE=1` overrides it (#701).
- `claude/hooks/agent_footer_gate.py`: the prompt's own `Report path:` line names its report, so a peer's `Worker report:` listed first is no longer denied (or overwritten via `Relaunch-reason:`), also when the label carries markdown or a list marker; two labelled reports with no `Report path:` derive the hook's own path; `Relaunch-reason:` counts only at column 0 (#701).
- `claude/hooks/bash_habits_gate.py`: an unquoted assignment before a quoted one (`env -i A=1 "B=$X" gh run watch 9`) no longer hides a `gh` watcher, and the `<` redirection forms are tested (#701).
- `load-watch`: the registry lock wait is bounded (30 s, `LW_REGISTRY_LOCK_TIMEOUT_S`) and refuses clearly on expiry; a damaged registry row refuses before the `local` approval dialog is shown; the `helper_env` TMPDIR comment is corrected; `docs/bash-habits-gate.md` names the `x) echo` case-arm false positive (rv-744 Lows, gs-04). (#750)
- `bin/ade-merge-ready` (`classify`, and `merge-now --prs N --yes` for the named PRs only) re-dispatches `adversary-marker.yml` itself, once per head, when pytest-linux passed more than `--marker-grace` seconds (default 180) ago and the `adversary-marker` check never appeared (PR 587: a `workflow_run` run reports master's head, so a force-pushed tip got no check until someone dispatched by hand); the head is claimed in `~/.local/state/ade/merge-ready/marker-dispatch.json` (flock, atomic write) before the dispatch and a state file that cannot be written stops dispatching, a pending or failed check is never touched, no marker is written, `--no-marker-dispatch` opts out; documented in `docs/review-boundary.md` (#600)
- `ade-install doctor` TUI wiring check: stderr warnings attach to a TUI structurally (`warning: <tui-id> …`), every stderr line is kept (deduplicated, capped at 200 distinct), cursor-agent reads `unknown` when claude is not installed, and the inherited `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_GROKBOT_NOTE` (plus `OPS_ROOT`, `CODEX_HOME`, `HERMES_HOME` when HOME is pinned) is scrubbed (#635)
- `tests/test_ade_install.py` runs with a temporary HOME: two doctor tests failed only on a Mac whose real credential-bak vault was inside its delete-after window (#635)
- `load-watch admit`'s memory gate counts memory available without compressing or swapping on macOS (free + file-backed + purgeable pages, the file-cache term of Linux MemAvailable), not only free + speculative pages, which read 82 MB on an idle Mac and refused every launch for ~1.5 h (#230).
- `load-watch admit`'s free-memory floor (`--min-free-mb`, `LW_MIN_FREE_MB`) defaults to 300 MB, down from 600 MB, now that it measures available memory (#230).
- Match the live plugin open-hook schema and bound executor termination with escalation. (#779)
- Serialize launch admission with all drain/resume phases, load isolated siblings from trusted absolute paths, and route enrolled PATH through shipped guards. (#779)
- Retain per-attempt handoff history with one unfinished selector and idempotent projection recovery across successive transfers. (#779)
- Permit explicitly resource-free cross-host role succession while retaining retirement/operation/checkpoint fences and rejecting resource migration. (#779)
- Acquire current network-consumer credentials through the managed boundary after durable offline buffering, narrowing injected environment to the selected credential. (#779)
- Add opt-in durable coalesced machine wakes with local-daemon/owner fencing, busy deferral and explicit unknown-outcome reconciliation. (#779)
- Fence resource reconciliation and identity binding against concurrent launch state changes; reconcile failed consumed launches without re-spawning. (#779)
- Refuse legacy replacement claims for enrolled resource scopes before any registry publication, preserving enrolled projections. (#779)
- Track the managed NATS credential helper as executable, matching the repository shebang invariant. (#779)
- Separate pending disposition intent from confirmed processing and verify or recover artifacts when adopting committed reports. (#779)
- Preserve an explicitly configured public NATS target after fresh managed environment injection, retaining the selected credential and safe child environment. (#779)
- Refuse unsupported worker prompt-file flags before native side effects, and document positional brief delivery. (#779)
- Return actionable coordinator CLI errors for missing JSON stdin and flattened inbox snapshots; document a subprocess request example. (#779)
- Place NATS plugin implementation inside the native server module boundary and resolve the versioned runtime through the trusted Paseo install record after CommonJS bundling; exercise the released compiler/evaluator in Linux CI. (#779)
- Use a hoisted default plugin contribution so the real CommonJS evaluator retains it during eager export copying. (#779)
- Recognize maintained nats.js typed missing-stream errors during explicit broker provisioning; refuse unsafe existing streams and exercise authenticated empty-broker provisioning and repeat calls in Linux CI. (#779)
- Preserve published first-real-turn handoff evidence alongside NATS retirement gates, and prevent legacy operator appointment from bypassing enrolled role retirement. (#779)
- Record executed native coordination, offline delivery, recovery and client rollback evidence with remaining activation gates; clarify plugin restore and CI workflow discovery instructions. (#779)
- `ade-net-resume` resumes Claude agents stopped by the transient Cloudflare page `[System Error] Failed to authenticate. API Error: 403
  Attention Required! | Cloudflare` (exact line only; a generic 403 or "Failed to authenticate" still counts as a real logout and is
  skipped), and for Claude ignores a trailing `Warning: claude.ai MCP servers blocked by enterprise policy` line when choosing the
  deciding line; a persistent 403 is held to the existing two-per-hour cap and reported as `capped`. (#624)
- Honor explicit exhausted-state overlays despite unchanged quota numbers, and hold identity-less legacy requests without inferring source recovery or healthy transition outcomes. (#769)
- Bind canonical ceiling windows, validate raw quota rows, retain unresolved semantic requests across account churn, follow completed worker successors, and validate current acknowledgements without rejecting telemetry drift. (#769)
- Validate preventive quota identity, family, freshness and thresholds per scan; reserve durable requests and distinguish submission, acknowledgement and observed outcomes; complete scheduler recipes. (#769)
- relay.py told a program that merely had `omp` as an argument (`vim omp`) from the omp TUI of a herdr pane by basename, so it could pin vim's pid and a later poll reported "target changed"; `named_hits` and the Hermes walkers (`descends_from`, `turn_is_above`) now use one exact-executable table (`fleet.EXACT_EXECUTABLES`: omp and Pi first, every other agent unreviewed and unchanged), so an omp started inside a Hermes turn no longer inherits the turn's identity or hop count (#274)
- session-history showed a live omp session outside herdr as `ended` with a resume command: omp hits are now checked with `omp session lease --json` and print `LIVE?`, never `ended`, when the check cannot run (#274)
- The omp history adapter indexes the default store and every named profile (`~/.omp/profiles/*/agent/sessions`; `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` and `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR` are not followed for omp because Pi reads the same variables and its sessions must not be indexed as omp), keys sessions by file so equal ids and same-named files do not collide, takes the id of a noncanonically named file from its header, no longer hangs on a FIFO named `*.jsonl` and no longer loses a session that holds a non-object JSON line; Pi and omp share one reader (`skills/session-finder/jsonl_session.py`) (#274)
- autorename `clean()` and the Pi/omp bridges strip the invisible bidi marks, zero-width space, word joiner and BOM (Unicode categories Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp) from titles, but keep ZWNJ and ZWJ, which are part of Persian, Devanagari and emoji-sequence text (#274)
- A legacy filename-keyed omp history row (`omp:daily`) is retired when its file is re-indexed under its header id, and a fleet-listed omp transcript joins its history hit and its liveness by file (the header id for a noncanonical name such as `daily.jsonl`; the same id in two stores, e.g. a profile, stays two sessions), in `session-find.py --all` and `session-history.py`; `session-find.py --all` lists a history hit that is live by `omp session lease` when the fleet has no row for it (an omp outside herdr), with the hit's own fields, instead of dropping it (#274)
- When one omp session id is indexed in two stores (the default one and a profile), `omp session lease` cannot say which copy runs, so history shows both as `LIVE?` with their resume hints instead of marking both exactly live (#274)
- `acp-dispatch` maps `omp` to its own pool in both maps (`bin/acp-dispatch`, `bin/route_agent.py`): the record carries `service: omp`, `--min-headroom` is checked, a resumed or legacy omp record is rechecked against its minimum in the runner, and `reroute`/`--escalate` exclude the pool; no fresh omp reading still admits, like any ordinary pool (#275).
- The fleet scan no longer classifies the npm-installed OMP (`@oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent/dist/cli.js`) as a Pi seat; Pi's `cli.js` rule requires the `@earendil-works` scope (#275).
- Restored default Pi native session/history backup coverage with credential exclusions and existing archives preserved; added complete JSONL fixture restoration and history-reindex validation (#566).
- Installation doctor detected missing explicit Pi extension paths left behind by removed task worktrees; documented durable installation paths. (#767)
- `bin/ade-pi-stuck`: a pool that bills several accounts (OpenCode Go, Clinepass) now maps each aiuse reading to its named sub-account, so `clinepass-t-e` and `opencode-go-djbclark` are judged on their own headroom instead of reported as having no quota mapping. An account aiuse has no reading for (a bare `clinepass` or `opencode-go` provider, or `xai-oauth`) stays unmapped and is still reported rather than assumed healthy. (#817)
- `bin/pr-radar-route`: a router failure now shows route_agent's JSON `reason` ("no eligible service with headroom" is printed on stdout) on stderr, with the last stderr line after it, instead of only the exit code or an aiuse note; a template dispatch (`<id from: …>` in a provider, model, thinking or mode value, or a launch line with `<`/`>` and no `--provider`, as cursor-cloud's) is refused instead of becoming a provider/model override (#688).
- `bin/route_agent.py`: several accounts of one pool keep every window, model sublimit and sublimit reset (the last account's used to overwrite the others); a copy of the file without the ADE modules fails with one stderr line and exit 2, not a traceback (#688).
- Follow-ups to #126 and #127 that no review round had filed (2026-11-01): `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py` no longer says the hook "runs only at prompt submit" (it also runs on `PostToolUse`) and names in-process agent-team teammates beside sub-agents where the `agent_id` guard skips them; `docs/context-hygiene.md` names the owner of the live `~/.claude/settings.json` hand-edit (the merging lead session); `skills/handoff` Step 9's partial-handoff recipe spells out the codex and grok model, mode and first prompt (`$baton PATH` for codex) at quota-guard `critical`, and says the exit-4 note is for claude only (#215).
- `ade-install doctor` probe detail now skips box-drawing and blank lines instead of printing the first output line, which for
  Hermes was a border. `apply` treats a `warn` component as present, not blocked; `update` counts it healthy. (#642)
- `AGENTS.md`, `skills/loose/SKILL.md`, `skills/steps/SKILL.md`, `skills/eli5/SKILL.md`, `claude/agents/adversary.md`, `skills/research/SKILL.md`, `skills/dialogue/SKILL.md`: correct stale facts found by the read-only prompt audit (2026-11-21) — self-compaction now uses `bin/ade-self-session compact` (the old self-slash was removed), the GUI-hog window and Freeze PRs are merged, the handoff skill is a compatibility alias, the ade-claim hook implements claim creation (observe-only), standing order 5 uses Roman sections, the retired shim tests are deleted, and the Cursor grok model is not the never-routed grok/grok-bot providers. (#851)
- `bin/route_agent.py` no longer binds a stale exhausted (or 0%) quota reading when the window's `resets_at` is already in the past: such a window reads unknown (re-probe) instead of exhausted, at both the row and window fold, so a pool that refilled overnight is not treated as out of quota. (#840)
- `bin/quota_nudge.py --reset` names every pool whose binding window has already refilled, and `skills/model-routing/SKILL.md` D5b records the rule to re-probe before a quota-sensitive dispatch. (#840)
- Changed `bin/ade-net-resume --quota` to skip a malformed `quota-wait` ledger row (missing or mistyped field, or an unparseable `resets_at`/`updated_at`) with one keyed `quota-ledger-invalid` warning instead of ending the whole run with exit 2; a wholly unreadable `events.jsonl` still exits 2. (#614)
- Corrected `docs/net-resume.md` and the `--quota-per-pool` help: the flag only changes dry-run counting because the 600 s pool gap limits `--apply`; ledger rows are keyed per error event or per clock-aligned ten-minute bucket as listed. (#614)
- Added tests for `updated_at` validation, the skip-and-warn behaviour (the network pass still ran) and the future-`UpdatedAt` `stale` note carrying no `stage`. (#614)
- Match shell heredoc delimiters exactly, preserving quoted Python/YAML data while still rejecting unterminated bodies and live unsafe commands after them. (#797)
- Preserve quoted subshell heredoc boundaries during watcher inspection and match deeply nested substitutions without Python recursion. (#797)
- Fixed the sibling `RecursionError` readers of #98 (2026-11-01): `guard_level`, `read_state`, `read_snapshot` and the `paseo-set-model list` read in `bin/claude-fleet-governor`, and `load_state`, `read_snapshot` and the aiuse output read in `bin/claude-quota-guard`, treat a deeply nested JSON file as unreadable (the usual fail-safe: level `ok`, empty state, no reading, or the existing unreadable exit 10) instead of crashing; the governor's `read_snapshot` also reads a snapshot that parses but is too deep for its recursive window walker as no reading, so `check` no longer ends in an internal error, rc 12 (#214).
- `bin/ade-pi-stuck`: any agent in a hard `error` is flagged stuck, on any provider and Pi included (the queue item 82 `xai-oauth` 402 case was invisible: `error` was never stuck for Pi). A Codex or Claude agent in `error` gets no Pi alternative: `alternative` is null and `family` names its provider, since the candidates are DeepSeek models on other vendors' accounts. It covers all agents, not just leads, because `paseo ls` carries no role. A healthy non-Pi agent is still ignored. Detection only: re-handing a non-Pi agent needs the native fallback (Carried 104). (#832)
- `bin/ade-supervisor-scan` no longer drops `error` entries: a Pi agent in error is told with its Pi alternative, a non-Pi one as a notice with no alternative (Carried 104). `skills/watch/SKILL.md` section 5, `docs/scheduling.md` and the `ade-pi-stuck` docstring and summary line say so. The scan tests drop BASH_ENV so a local run cannot reach the real `paseo` or `hermes-ping`; the supervisor job notes in config/ade.toml name the error notices. (#832)
- `relay.py` no longer refuses with "cannot tell who is sending" for an agent in a herdr pane whose `HERDR_PANE_ID` is stale after the pane was moved (seen on an omp in w20:p3): the pane is found by its own process tree (#248)
- A Claude Code session started by /baton, or an agent started from a Claude shell but not running under it (its own pane), is no longer taken for the session whose `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` it inherited: `relay.py` (sender), `fleet.py` (`self` row) and `session-find.py` share one resolver, `skills/session-finder/claude_identity.py` (moved from autorename), that reads the Claude registry of CLAUDE_PID when it is an ancestor and trusts the env var only as the fallback (and not beside a CLAUDE_PID that is provably not an ancestor). Owners (`ade_claims` claims and `caller_ident`, `acp-dispatch` job records, the baton and handoff skills) use the same registry id but read the registry id of the live CLAUDE_PID (an ancestor or a detached job's living Claude) and fall back to the env var, so an orphaned `nohup` job still owns its claim; a herdr row whose session id differs from the caller's own is no longer its `self` row (#248)
- `relay.py` no longer refuses a session as ambiguous with itself: rows of one session (same session id or Paseo agent id) are one recipient, so a full Paseo agent id or session id always resolves, and a short prefix of two different sessions is still refused; `fleet.py` lists a session once when a resume leaves two live Claude processes (registry files) of the same session id, keeping the entry updated last (#225)
- `relay.py ID -` no longer sends the one-character text `-` and reports it delivered (a peer saw an empty message, 2026-11-02): a bare positional `-` is a usage error (exit 2, nothing sent) that names `--message-file -`, the only form that reads stdin (#267)
- research skill: `research_check.py fetch` refuses an EZproxy "has not been configured for access" page (and, on the auto-retry, a login/paywall stub) instead of saving it as a `via_proxy: true` source, and no longer follows a proxy hand-off to a non-http(s) address (new `tests/test_research_check_proxy.py`); `references/literature.md` item 6 records what one 2026-11-13 session observed from an institutional address; `docs/mcp-servers.md` explains the harmless Firecrawl "tool discovery" warning. The Mac shell variable is now `RESEARCH_EZPROXY_PREFIX` (`RESEARCH_PROXY_URL` is also Hyades' developer-search URL); `research_check.py` reads the new name first and still accepts the old one. A proxied body counts as a login/paywall stub only when it is short (under 200 words, or under 1000 with paywall wording), so long articles that say "get access" are kept. (#637)
- `bin/review-launch`: a review may read a regular `toolu_*.txt` in its own session's oversized-output spool, which lives outside the worktree, instead of being killed for it. Only that exact file is exempt, and the spool is looked up under `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set (else `~/.claude`). A Read of a symlink that lies outside the worktree trips even when it resolves inside, because `realpath` follows it; a link inside the worktree (`CLAUDE.md -> AGENTS.md`) is still read normally and judged by where it resolves. This stops a frontier review losing paid work for touching the harness's own spool (operator ruling 2026-11-20). docs/review-boundary.md describes the exception, and the long-path project folder name is tested against an independent JS encoder. An inside link is recognised through any spelling of the worktree path (a symlinked alias, /tmp for /private/tmp). (#835)
- `bin/route_agent.py`: an aiuse `account` label alone is no quota identity (one email in two organizations folds as separate accounts); a row is identified only with an organization field, which aiuse does not emit today, so its rows fold fail closed. `docs/model-routing.md` D3a states only what the code checks; a `[a, b, b]` test kills the `seen[-1] == who` mutant of `_folds_as_accounts`. (#751)
- Removed generated inventory counts from README roster headings so unrelated tool and skill additions no longer conflict on those lines. (#593)
- Publish the skill and tool roster automatically from master on a separate branch, so inventory changes no longer conflict with or stale open PRs. Migrate diagnostic coverage and contributor/release guidance to generated output, and track the publisher as executable. (#607)
- `bin/route_agent.py`: two `--available` rows of one pool (two accounts) no longer let the measured one open the plenty gate while the other reads expired: windows fold per account (`_merge_windows`) and an unknown row adds an `(unmeasured)` None window; an `error` account of the snapshot fold (a collection or auth error) now counts as an unmeasured account instead of being skipped (the `--available` fold gets the same guard, defensive: aiuse emits no error row there today). (#708)
- `ade-config check` now refuses a bad `[dispatch.repo_overrides.REPO] frontier_order` (unknown or repeated service, empty or non-list value, upper-case repo key) the same way the router does, and the router's runtime-load error names the local overlay as well as the base file; both go through one `ade_config.validate_frontier_orders` (parity-tested against `route_agent.py`'s pure file reader). (#720)
- Docs: the `frontier_order` comment in `config/routing.toml` and `skills/model-routing` D5a say the order does not apply model-family independence (pass `plan --exclude codex` for a Codex-authored slice); the queue line says a missing window closes only codex's plenty kinds. (#720)
- `bin/route_agent.py plan` no longer refuses research as "no pool has room" for about a third of every quarter hour while Gemini is at 100%: an antigravity pool aiuse lists in `stale_pools` only because its reused reading passed the 600 s fresh limit (the pool is queried at most every 900 s and `--live` cannot beat that gate) keeps its reading when the row is at most 20 minutes old, shows at least 50% headroom and no Paseo agent on antigravity is live or was created since the reading, so a dispatched wave cannot reuse a reading that predates it (`STALE_REUSE_*`, `ROUTE_AGENT_PASEO_LS_JSON`; `docs/model-routing.md` D1). Every other stale, low, expired or unverifiable reading still reads unknown. (#864)
- `bin/route_agent.py`: in the aiuse snapshot-file fold, an unmeasured second account of a provider (an expired reading, or an account that lists no window for any of the provider's pools) no longer lets the measured account open the pool for the plenty gate: a window either account could not measure stays unknown when accounts are folded, so the pooled service stays closed; `remaining` and the Fable cap keep the known reading (#698).
- `bin/route_agent.py`: the standalone import guard (one stderr line, exit 2) now covers only a missing sibling module (`ade_config`, `ade_gates`, `model_tiers` absent beside the file); an ImportError raised inside the repo copy keeps its traceback (#698).
- `bin/route_agent.py`: `plan` and `adjust` print an `ade-worker-run --lead … --slice … [--window-middle …] [--repo …] [--cwd …] -- --provider … --no-wait` line instead of a bare `paseo run … --no-wait`, so following `Choice.dispatch` as-is places the worker in the lead's WORKERS window (a bare `paseo run` carries no workspace and Paseo ignores `--cwd`, getpaseo/paseo#9699: a worker landed in the caller's own workspace, 2026-11-14). New `--lead` (default `$PASEO_AGENT_ID`), `--slice` (once per kind), `--cwd`, `--window-middle`, `--worker-repo`; a missing lead or slice keeps a quoted `'<lead>'` / `'<slice>'` placeholder, a lead or slice `ade-worker-run` would refuse is flagged, a missing `--cwd` is advisory, a pool with no model profile keeps a quoted `--provider` placeholder (the line parses in bash), a `--lead` other than `$PASEO_AGENT_ID` is flagged, and each is named in each slice's new `dispatch_warnings` (`--json`) and on stderr. `route` keeps the bare template (PR Radar's adapter) and says so in `dispatch_warnings`; the old JSON keys are unchanged. `skills/fan-out`, `skills/model-routing`, `skills/research` and `docs/model-routing.md` follow. (#727)
- Clarify router plan comma-separated kinds and route argument syntax after a rejected invocation. (#779)
- Match route quota to explicit pool families so Cursor Grok Bot cannot fund Composer/Auto, preserve parent constraints and Claude model caps, and reject familyless fallback readings. (#581)
- Cap Antigravity and Pi OpenAI API/Z.ai waves at one canary per pool until the lead observes a successful turn. (#581)
- Print provider-native unattended modes in dispatch commands so Claude, Codex and Antigravity workers do not inherit Default permissions; omit mode for Pi, which reports none. (#581)
- Load named-family quota before dispatch eligibility checks, map models to their explicit policy families, and align gate fixtures with fail-closed unknown quota. (#581)
- Align the Pi Cursor contract test with its explicit policy family and isolate the load-cap burst test from quota starvation using one-percent estimates. (#581)
- `bin/ade_send_guard.py` redirects a send addressed to a self-handoff predecessor to its successor from `acknowledged` on, not only after `finish`, so peers stop waking a predecessor that `finish` is waiting to see idle; the hop needs a successor Paseo reports live and a fresh record (`accept` now stamps `acknowledged_at`; the redirect ends an hour later or when `finish` records a failure other than waiting; `archive-unknown` after an `AGENT_RUNNING` refusal still counts, and the first `finish` check is recorded so drift ends the redirect at once), and every other consumer of the succession ledger still follows completed links only. (#736)
- The self-handoff prompt tells the successor to read lead-log / LEAD-STATE lines newer than the checkpoint (they fall outside the checkpoint hash) and to announce its takeover right after `accept`. (#736)
- `bin/route_agent.py`: `route` and `plan` skip a pool that has no model profile for the kind (naming it under `skipped`), so a bulk slice is no longer routed to `herdr-generic` with a blocked `--provider` placeholder; the placeholder remains only when no capable pool has a profile. The plan and route JSON gain an additive `skipped` key, and a queued slice's reason names the skipped pools. (#756)
- Admitted native Paseo schedule creation and ledger adoption independently of launchd while deferring removal without active-run safety evidence. (#770)
- Fixed pre-existing split printf format strings that prevented Just from
  parsing the Hermes setup recipe. (#540)
- `bin/ade_self_session.py`: `parse_envelope` keeps a native error code that matches `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,64}` (full match), so `host_offline` and `commander.excessArguments` are no longer recorded as `INVALID_CODE`; spaces, control characters, newlines and codes over 64 characters still are, as is a code the message scrub would redact: a bare token (`ghp_...`, `sk-...`), a `keyword:value` or `cookie:value` secret (`password:hunter2`) or a run of 16 or more letters and digits holding a digit. (#740)
- `ade-self-session` no longer hides why a native Paseo command failed: `native()` reads Paseo's `{"error": {"code", "message"}}` envelope from stderr (or stdout) and raises with the code, message, exit status and a bounded, secret-scrubbed stderr tail; "outcome unknown" is kept only for a timeout, an OS error or a nonzero exit with no JSON on either stream. The reason is stored as `native_error` in the transaction record (`creation-unknown`, `archive-unknown`, `send-unknown` are diagnosable from the file; a later success moves it to `resolved_native_error`). 2026-11-14: a self-handoff lost both `WORKSPACE_NOT_FOUND` and an empty-stdout rc 1 archive error. (#668)
- `handoff` checks `paseo workspace ls --json` before saving the intent; when the predecessor's workspace is gone the successor is launched with `--new-workspace local` (recorded as `workspace_placement`), not `--workspace`. `finish` raises a specific error when the archive error names an agent that is not the predecessor ("Paseo resolved archive to a different agent ..."), never retrying or forcing, and refuses before archiving when the predecessor is absent from `paseo ls -g --json` (the listing `agent archive` resolves from) (its workspace was deleted: Paseo then resolves the id to a partial title match such as a live worker, getpaseo/paseo#9668). A successful archive must name the predecessor in its receipt, else `finish` reports the agent actually archived. `docs/self-session.md` C0 documents both manual fallbacks (creation starts with `reconcile`, so a timeout never yields a second successor). (#668)
- Detected routed Pi transcript failures before acknowledgement and preserved retry history and pool exclusions across creation refusals (#791)
- Checked Pi entry structure and timestamp order before classifying terminal failures, preserving recovered candidates and rejecting malformed or unsupported tails; retained saved-proof identity and digest checks on the success path (#821)
- Self-handoff accepts native Paseo workspace IDs (`wks_` plus 16 lowercase hex digits), rejects malformed workspace IDs before creating an intent, and retains UUID checks for agent IDs. (#606)
- `archived_send_gate` (follow-up to #692): the backstop mask no longer re-joins a word at every glued quote (`''''...` was quadratic), an ANSI-C `$'A\nB'` list of ids is judged, a redirect operator and its operand (`<<<`, `>`, `>--home=x`) are no agent and no daemon selector, a lone `-` is an operand, and `NO_RUN` has one definition. `libexec/agent-bin/paseo` (follow-up to #696): the re-entry counter counts only an exec chain (a value a daemon passes to its agents no longer ends in exit 127), `08` is eight, and the real paseo's path is named to the helper alone, with a second always-inherited hop counter (cap 32) for a wrapper that forks between two shims. Tests pin the surviving mutants of both reviews and the docs say what is still not seen. (#711)
- `libexec/agent-bin/paseo` (follow-up to #711): the shim's hop count (cap 32) is anchored to the pid of the shim that set it (`ADE_PASEO_SHIM_HOPS_PID`, counted only while alive), so a forking wrapper is stopped on every command, `daemon` included, and a daemon's agents count from zero, and a total (`ADE_PASEO_SHIM_TOTAL`, cap 256, counted inside a 10-minute window `ADE_PASEO_SHIM_TOTAL_T`, so a daemon restart cannot grow it for good) ends a wrapper that starts the next shim in the background; the exit-code and helper header lines name the 32-hop cap and the helper-only `ADE_REAL_PASEO`. `archived_send_gate`: `\c` + a non-ASCII character is no blank, a crash in the backstop no longer un-judges a send the parser found, a redirect after `--` is skipped like one before it, an ANSI-C id list split by `\x0a`, `\u000a` or `\012` is judged, and the surviving mutants of the #711 review (hop cap 32, fd skip bounds) have tests; the quoted `'>'` and `2 >x` reads are documented. (#716)
- Self-session send redirect: an `AGENT_RUNNING` archive refusal naming another agent is recorded as `ARCHIVE_RESOLVED_OTHER_AGENT` and ends the guard's redirect at once; a non-waiting earlier `native_error` is cleared after a re-run's passing first check (a waiting one stays); one send decision inspects each agent once. (#753)
- `tests/test_ade_client_wiring.py`: the four tests that name Hermes' per-server MCP rows skip with a reason when PyYAML
  is absent (`needs_yaml`), instead of failing on the documented `hermes/*` `unknown` fallback; CI keeps installing
  PyYAML (`uv run --with pytest --with pyyaml`), so nothing is skipped there and the fallback stays covered by
  `test_mcp_hermes_unknown_without_pyyaml`. (#808)
- Keep install/doctor empty arrays safe under stock macOS Bash 3.2, preserve
  never-installed Hermes classification, and restore the checklist test docstring (#591).
- Made whole-session handoffs launch and verify their own successor, replacing operator-typed resets with the acknowledged self-session protocol and an agent-run fallback. (#588)
- Prevented network recovery from resuming errors older than a configurable 30-minute default window or agents whose latest assistant output already delivered a done line. (#601)
- Defer disruptive scheduler reconciliation without shared admission, per-job idle and safe-drain evidence; persist intent and preserve backend migration ownership. (#595)
- Identify deferred Paseo intent by its target backend so retries across host backend selections retain one record. (#595)
- `ade-sched reconcile` refused a job whose catalog `label` differs from its plist `Label` at plan time and again before `install`, and a blocked logical row (missing or unrenderable source, label mismatch) no longer plans the removal of its current or prior identities, so a broken replacement cannot delete the last valid definition (#619)
- `ade-sched`: the two lows left on #626 and three earlier ones. An uninstall of a LOADED canonical-wrapped job now proves under the lock
  that the bytes it would restore match the loaded definition (wrapper head, lock dir, full argv, program, source path; a file edited
  after the load is refused, nothing changed; environment/schedule/KeepAlive keys of the file are not compared); a disk error
  writing the recovery record leaves no half record and refuses before any `bootout`; the KeepAlive `launchctl print` read catches a
  newline-forged `properties` line; a renamed `ade-job-run` inside an `sh -c` string counts as wrapped; `--restart-service` on any
  subcommand but `install`/`uninstall` is a usage error (exit 2). (#636)
- G3 documentation corrections: update `bin/review-pr` docstring for owner-trust policy, record `watch`/`loadwatch` restoration in `docs/skills.md`, and fix citation in `changelog.d/feat-watch-load-skill.md` to #539 (#589).
- Preserved per-attempt review evidence and unresolved findings, prevented duplicate reviews, and added timeout-only reviewer replacement. (#590)
- Isolated aiuse windows by family and model scope, and kept missing Cursor included pools visible alongside Grok Bot headroom. (#579)
- Restore native Claude Paseo quota ingestion when family metadata is absent, while preserving explicit family isolation and freshness checks; pin the CLI fixture clock and verify both quota-merge directions. (#603)
- `ade-worker-run`: `--cwd` was silently dropped when a worker was placed in an existing window (`paseo run --workspace` takes the agent's directory from the window and ignores `--cwd`, upstream getpaseo/paseo#9699), so a worker meant for a claimed worktree started in the window's repo. The launch is now refused when `--cwd` differs from the window's directory (the refusal says to pass the window's directory and give the worktree as an absolute path in the brief), and after every launch with `--cwd` the agent's `Cwd` is read back and a mismatch interrupts the fresh worker (`paseo agent stop`) and is reported loudly. (#687)
- `ade-worker-run`: a successor lead that inherited several WORKERS windows from its predecessors is no longer stuck. A `--window-middle` for new work opens a new window beside the inherited ones (the "second window" typo refusal now applies only to windows the lead itself placed or has launched into), and with no `--window-middle` the repo (`--repo`, or the one derived from `--cwd`, else the current directory) narrows several owned windows to that repo's (launch only; `archive-window` narrows by an explicit `--repo` only); several of one repo are still refused, and the refusal says to pass `--repo` or `--window-middle`. (#680)
- `ade-worker-run`: one WORKERS window per lead and repo. The typo guard ("no WORKERS window is named for work ..., not a second window") counts only the lead's windows of the launch's own repo (the name's repo, else a legacy window's cwd repo; an unreadable repo still counts; `repo` None keeps every window), so a launch for a repo the lead has no window for opens one instead of being refused into another repo's window. A window the launch opens is created in the repo's primary checkout (`ade_ws_names.primary_checkout_of`: a submodule resolves through its superproject, a repo whose git dir is not a `.git` through `git worktree list`; a bare or separate-git-dir repo has none recorded, so the window opens in `--cwd` with a stderr warning), not in the claimed worktree `--cwd` names; the launch is refused (before anything is renamed) unless the brief names that worktree as a whole absolute path (never for `--placement main`, which keeps refusing a `--cwd` other than its window's), prints `cwd`/`requestedCwd`, and a repeat of the same `--cwd` into the window just minted is placed at the window's cwd instead of being refused; the post-launch `Cwd` check compares with that. The same brief check, stderr note and `cwd`/`requestedCwd` apply when no `--cwd` is given from inside a linked worktree (its refusal then asks for `--cwd <the worker's own worktree>` and never advises handing over the worktree the lead stands in); a brief naming the worktree's top directory satisfies the check for a `--cwd` in a subdirectory. The typo guard compares repo names without case, also counts a window of the same checkout named with another `--repo`, and counts a legacy window whose directory is gone (a directory that is no git checkout never aliases two repos; a checkout match under another name says to pass that repo's own `--cwd`); a brief path followed by `.old` or preceded by a prefix does not name the worktree. The paseo#9699 refusal for any other `--cwd` in an existing window is unchanged. (#709)
- `bin/ade-worker-run`: the cwd compare uses the one comparator `ade_claims.same_path` (now public; it also returns False for a path with an embedded NUL or lone surrogate), a case-variant spelling of the primary checkout no longer refuses a new-window launch, and an unusable window or `--cwd` path is refused before the launch (never a traceback after it); the redirect site has tests (wr-50, rv-742 Lows). (#747)
- `plugins/ade-yolo`: attach-only Herdr providers (`herdr-generic`, `herdr-omp`) no longer refuse every chat open and session import; a separate exact-id `ATTACH_ONLY` allowlist skips the mode checks (Paseo launches nothing there), keeps the snapshot identity check where a snapshot is readable, and logs one line per exemption (#654)
- `plugins/ade-yolo`: a ready, modeless Pi catalog is admitted as unattended by construction (Pi never raises permission requests), so ordinary Pi creates no longer refuse; the allowlist is Pi only (#605)
- `plugins/ade-yolo`: unstamped legacy agents (Hermes included) resume without mode mutation or catalog lookup even with saved mode/control drift or missing evidence; a warning names the agent id, provider and saved mode. Stamped-agent drift and missing evidence still refuse (#605)
- `plugins/ade-yolo`: document and test that Pi with a unique catalog-marked `isUnattended` mode is accepted like any other provider (#605)
- Fixed adversary marker edge cases, comment boundaries, strict JSON-string protection with documented fail-closed recovery limits, root-array examples containing comment delimiters, top-level verdict/head matching with fail-closed recovery of broken structure, and bounded discovery for empty marker starts, nested identity guard recovery and exact quoted hook exemptions in bin/ade_claims.py, and dispatch completion consistency (#175).
- A Cursor session present under two project directories no longer re-indexes itself on every run; printed `resume:` hints quote the session id and directory (#191).
- Automatic callers wake a closed Paseo agent (djbclark, 2026-10-31: automatic watchers SHOULD wake closed seats; relay.py itself already sends to a collected `closed` agent as to an idle one, #192): `rumination-watch` delivers its line to a closed seat (one send per 300 s pass at most, a resumed turn), counts `closed` as deliverable and healthy, lets the next-move nudge reach it, treats it as asleep, never `gone`, and starts no successor over it; only an archived seat, or a record-proved closed one that is not persistent, still proves absent (#177).
- An `--expect-ack` stayed open and went ASSUMED LOST for 28 h although the target answered and relay reported `delivered` (2026-10-31, expectation `aff7-attach`): Claude Code logged an idle session's peer message only as a user-role `api-request-blob` record, which the transcript scan did not read. `--sweep` now reads a user-role text block of such a blob when it carries a `<cross-session-message ...>` envelope; only a text block that STARTS with the harness prefix ("Another Claude session sent a message...", optionally inside a `<system-reminder>`) counts, and only the envelope that follows it: a tool result, an assistant blob, plain prose, hook output, a nested-memory or instruction reminder, a skill body, a task notification and a compaction summary in a blob count for nothing, as on the `user` route (#177).
- Fixed `/bigteam` caller-discovery, commit-preparation, and single-run waiter checks, including unindexed scripts and GNU-safe source excerpts (#195).
- Fixed `watch` handling of uncertain lifecycle reads, forced replacements after failed probes, watchers in background workspaces, and generation reuse after title-list failures (#195).
- Fixed `watch up` replacing watchers after Paseo collected their idle runtimes; retained saved sessions without periodic model wake-ups, let real events resume them through the relay, and refused duplicate launches when daemon state was unknown (#192).
- Fixed rumination-watch duplicate paid sends and starts, bound retained-ID reconciliation to each start attempt, preserved unresolved IDs across owner changes and absent IDs for fresh diagnosis, and added operator notices, isolated watcher tests, and parsed canonical workspace SHAs. (#174)
- Fixed Paseo self-slash clear and compact refusing unproved providers and directing them to successor handoffs (#179)
- `failover.sweep_tmp_dirs()` (2026-10-30) removes stale `failover-*` scratch directories that killed runs leave in the temp dir: only real directories (never symlinks) named like `TemporaryDirectory(prefix="failover-")`, owned by the caller, whose own and children's mtimes are over a day old; errors never propagate (#176)
- `bin/route_agent.py substrate` (2026-10-30, review of the DeepSeek-written substrate): the plain text form no longer crashes with `NameError` (it printed a deleted `DANN_ESCALATION`); a pool in aiuse's `[disabled_services]` is never chosen (only the `[substrate] lifeline` in `config/routing.toml`, `clinepass`, may be disabled), and with nothing usable, disabled or exhausted, it prints `no usable substrate` and exits 6; it applies `load_snapshot`'s guards (stale or errored rows read as unmeasured, the fresh `--available` reading can only tighten) and `_pct` on every percentage; `--apply AGENT` now exits non-zero on failure (1 and 4 from `paseo-set-model`, 2 empty or ambiguous agent, 3 no such agent, 5 cross-provider, 7 cannot list) and sets the same unique agent id it checked; a disabled lifeline that `aiuse --available` omits is listed as unmeasured instead of read as exhausted; and (djbclark, 2026-10-30) `opencode-go` lost its `code` rank and model in `config/routing.toml`, so `route`/`plan` never send a code slice there (bulk and mechanical stay; DeepSeek V4.1 Flash remains the main-loop substrate model) (#172).
- `skills/oc-go-key/scripts/go-status` (2026-10-30): no upstream error text is printed any more (it could carry an `access_token` or a lowercase `bearer` token), only a fixed explanation per failure class; an auth failure naming another provider no longer advises rotating the Go key, and one with no provider recorded exits 3 instead of 2; the Go wall, no-funds and region errors still exit 2; a timestamp without a time zone is skipped instead of crashing the scan (#171)
- `/autorename` with no argument renamed more than the operator asked for (2026-10-30): in Paseo it also renamed the workspace to `<slug>-pw`, and in herdr it offered to move the tab to another workspace. It now titles the session and names only this agent's own tab (the Paseo tab or the herdr tab label); the nudge hook does the same. `/autorename all` stays herdr-only and in a Paseo-hosted agent replies `autorename all is not yet supported in Paseo` (`herdr_place.py config` exits 2 there), and a Paseo-hosted agent never touches herdr even when the daemon passed a herdr environment down to it (#169).
- `bin/pr-wait` still reported `security-marker-unverified` on a head whose Codex security review was clean
  whenever Codex had EVER found a security problem on an earlier head (2026-10-30, PR 156): the explicit null
  pair is a statement only beside the evidence that `this review found none of that kind`, and `unresolved_stated`
  took that evidence to be `finding_comments` — every Codex finding comment of that kind, on EVERY head, all the
  way back. After a round of fixes that is the normal state, not a contradiction: the finding was fixed on a new
  head and its thread resolved, and Codex's review of the new head found nothing. So `merge_ready` was unreachable
  for exactly the pull requests that had had a finding and then fixed it (PR 156: the security P2 fixed on
  `a5c44a11`, four historical security comments still in the pull request, clean security review on the fixed
  head, blocked forever). The contradiction is now THIS head's findings (`review["for_head_all"]`) plus any open
  thread of that kind, so a finding on an earlier head cannot block the fix that produced this one, while a
  finding on this head whose thread someone resolved WITHOUT a fix still does — a resolution is not a fix. The
  evidence comes from `for_head_all`, unwindowed, so `--since` (which narrows the summary's counts) cannot wave a
  finding on this head through the gate (#165).
- `relay.py --expect-ack` from a Paseo-hosted agent recorded a `pid*` process-scan row as the sender (2026-10-29, an Antigravity agent): `fleet.sessions()` lists `_proc_scan` rows before `_paseo_rows`, and a process-scan row is `self = pid in ancestors`, so a Paseo agent whose tools run under a process fleet recognises got a `pid*` row for its own ancestor TUI, already `self`, listed FIRST; `next(x for x in listed if x["self"])` took it, `timeline_base` returned None without a `paseo` id, and the record was `sender: pid19798`, `sender_paseo: ""`, `tl_base: null` — an expectation that could never close (nothing watches a pid) whose footer told the peer to `ack pid19798`. There is now ONE row-picking rule, `fleet.sender_row()`: among `self` rows prefer a transcript (`--ack` watches it), else the row for the NEAREST process of this caller in `ancestry()` — a Paseo row counting as the process Paseo launched for that agent, and at one depth the row that can prove the ack (`timeline_ack` watches it) over a `pid*` row for the same process. So, among rows with no transcript, one `self` only by an inherited or exported `PASEO_AGENT_ID` — which marks a row `self` on its own and names no process of ours — cannot lend its row the name and send a genuine answer to another agent while a row that does name one of our processes competes (adversary B1; rows that all name none keep fleet's order, and a lone `self` row is returned unchecked), and a Paseo agent that started a different agent under it (`omp` running `codex`) no longer takes the nested agent's place when the nested agent's row has no transcript (local review of 2026-10-30; a transcript still outranks any depth, `docs/queue.md` item 9); a transcript row gets no second vote for also carrying a Paseo id: `_claude_sessions` gives a daemon-spawned Claude both, but a transcript already proves the ack and neither `ack_proof_note` nor `timeline_base` uses that id to prove an ack for a transcript row, so it adds nothing to the proof (local-review of 2026-10-30). `relay.sender_row` delegates to it and `fleet list`'s link annotation uses the same row, so the links relay writes and the links fleet shows agree. And a sender left with no watcher is TOLD at the send: `relay.ack_proof_note()` reads the artifact relay actually wrote (silent with a transcript, silent for a transcript-less Paseo sender only when its timeline was read) and otherwise warns, and only when a footer actually asked for the ack, so a linked peer's plain send stays quiet (B2): a row with no transcript and no keeping route (a `pid*` row, a native pane TUI) is told the expectation may not close on its own and given `--retire`, never an `ack` route it cannot reach (adversary B3), while a sender whose answer can arrive over a route that KEEPS the text (a Paseo agent, an ACP launch, a dispatch inbox) is closed by the target's `ack ID` through relay.py and is not warned at all, and a first-contact link, which opens no record, is told the reply to that first contact cannot mark it acked instead of being promised an `ack` that `ack_sweep` could never read (Codex review of #162) (#162).

- `bin/rumination-watch` resolved the role to a STOPPED seat and never looked further: a closed Paseo row is `usable()` (Paseo keeps the record and `ensureAgentLoaded` resumes it on the next send, so relay.py still reaches it) and `owner_candidates()` puts the comm registry — a row the seat itself writes — ahead of peer-mail, so a seat that stopped left a closed id at the head of the list and the live successor named further down was never written to, while #164's own closed-as-absent probe read that same closed id as `gone` and would have licensed a DUPLICATE start over a successor that was answering (adv-seat D3, and the Codex finding on this PR; 13 of the 25 Paseo agents on this Mac were `closed` on 2026-10-30, so this is the common shape rather than an edge). `owner_session()` now PREFERS a candidate whose row is not closed and keeps the closed one only as a fallback, which is the ordinary finished-session case the feature exists for — a lone closed seat is still resolved, still probed and still replaced — and `Archived` from `paseo agent inspect` must now be `is True` rather than merely truthy, because `"false"` is a truthy string and a record that says it is not archived must never prove a live seat absent. Pinned by `test_60`-`test_63` (`test_60` fails on every older head) and by `test_53[archived-string]`; `test_56`'s wrong-id reply now carries a proving `Status`/`Archived` pair, so the `Id == ident` guard is the only thing that keeps a reply about another agent from proving this one absent (#164).
- `bin/rumination-watch`'s opt-in auto-seat now starts a seat when the previous one is provably absent but Paseo still holds its record: a finished session usually goes `closed` rather than vanishing (13 of the 25 Paseo agents on this Mac were `closed` on 2026-10-30 against 6 `idle` and 5 `running`), and relay.py rightly calls that `refused` for SENDING, so the watcher waited for an id that never disappears — it paged djbclark and the entity stayed dark, which is the exact failure the feature exists to prevent. A `refused`, `archived` or `error` diagnosis now gets ONE direct `paseo agent inspect`, and only a record that names the SAME UUID and says `Status: closed` or `Archived: true` proves absence; a pending card, a live-but-unsendable seat, a broken read, an unreadable body or a reply naming a different agent still never starts one, and with `RW_AUTO_SEAT` unset nothing is probed at all (#164).
- `bin/rumination-watch`'s opt-in auto-seat now gives the seat it starts a Paseo Desktop tab, and no longer makes it a subagent of whoever ran the pass: after `paseo agent inspect` confirms the new agent it calls `paseo agent open <id>` — the deep link #159 uses, WITHOUT that PR's Desktop-process wait, so what it logs is `sent <id> to Paseo Desktop (a tab is not confirmable from here)` and never that a tab was seen — because a `paseo run` agent has NO tab and an auto-started seat that hits a `card:*` — which the switch correctly never answers — would be alive, reachable by relay and invisible, the same darkness one level down (2026-10-30, djbclark's ruling); unlike `self-slash` a failed open is LOGGED, put in the page to djbclark, and never fails the start, since there is no old agent to fall back to and the seat is reachable either way, and `RW_AUTO_SEAT_NO_OPEN=1` skips the attempt on a host with no desktop. A seat that comes alive by RECOVERY instead — its start's confirmation was lost, or the id came from the Paseo listing — was adopted on a later pass and never opened at all, so the recovery path now makes ONE attempt per id too, and pages him if it fails; a page no send accepted is carried and retried rather than lost to a single failed `hermes send`: the tab reason in `undisclosed_tab` (waiting out the shared page cooldown, one slot), and the notice that a seat was STARTED in `undisclosed_start` with no cooldown at all, because once the seat exists the gone page is never composed again and that notice is the one thing this feature exists to deliver - a newer notice replaces an older one, and a retried one is prefixed `(delayed)` with its date so it is not read as this minute's news. The `paseo run` now runs as `env -u PASEO_AGENT_ID`: with that variable set the daemon parents the new agent to it, and a measured probe started from inside an agent seat came back with that seat as its `ParentAgentId`; a pass run by hand can only reach a start through an `RW_SESSION` override pointing at a gone seat, so this is defence in depth rather than a hole a live caller falls into (adversary, 2026-10-30). The §L L7 field check the README required is also measured now: a seat started with this exact argv appeared in `fleet.py` with a real `chan`/`reach`, `relay.py --why` read its own reply, and a real relayed line was delivered and answered, so the "start a seat it cannot speak to" risk did not reproduce (#161).
- Fixed `go-status` to redact credentials in failure details, disclose incomplete log-window coverage, recognize HTTP auth failures precisely, and name listed accounts absent from its snapshot table, with regression coverage for classification precedence and exit codes (#163).
- `self-slash --finish --successor` now opens the successor in Paseo Desktop (`paseo agent open <new id>`) before it archives the old agent, and keeps the old agent (exit 11 in the log, `could not be opened in Paseo Desktop`) when that fails: an agent started by `paseo run` has no tab, so a lead handed over that way was invisible and its question card went unseen for 13 hours (2026-10-30); it then waits up to `SELF_SLASH_OPEN_WAIT` (20) seconds for a Desktop process (`SELF_SLASH_DESKTOP_PROC`, default the macOS app) and keeps the old agent when none runs, because `agent open` cannot confirm a tab; `SELF_SLASH_NO_OPEN=1` skips both on a host with no desktop (#159).
- `bin/acp-dispatch`'s `classify()` read a FINISHED slice as `partial`: it accepted only `DONE` as the report's last non-empty line, but a brief may ask for one machine-readable line after it (a verdict such as `AC_DONE: adv-e-152-delta APPROVE`, so the lead can grep it), and on 2026-10-30 the #152 scoped delta re-gate followed its brief exactly — a complete APPROVE with both mutation results in it — yet `acp-dispatch check` printed `partial — no DONE line yet` (exit 1) and `acp-dispatch-wait` said the same, so a delivered verdict had to be argued with before it could be acted on. `classify()` now treats exactly `DONE` followed by exactly one `AC_DONE:` line as done; anything else after `DONE` (trailing prose, a second paragraph, an `AC_DONE` with no `DONE` above it) is still `partial`, because that is the shape a truncated or permission-refused slice leaves behind, and `BLOCKED:` on the first line still wins. `tests/test_acp_dispatch_classify.py` pins the two-line shape and the four refusals (reverting the tolerance fails `test_a_done_line_followed_by_a_verdict_line_is_done`) (#155).
- `bin/rumination-watch` never told an idle seat that it had declared work nobody had started, because every line it speaks is a CHANGE and "idle with a full `- Next steps:` list" is not one: from 2026-10-26 00:30 to 18:37 the log holds 216 consecutive `quiet (nothing new); seat idle` passes (18.1 h, a healthy watcher and a seat nobody woke; three more runs of 3.8-6.8 h on 2026-10-25, 28 and 29), and `escalate_dark()` cannot see it because `seat_dark("idle")` is `""` on purpose. `_pass` now adds one keyed, state-like item, `next move: N declared step(s) still open in the Tier 1 log and this seat is idle`, when the seat's single `--why` probe per pass says `idle` (never `working`, `silent`, `card:*`, `unreachable` or an unreadable probe), `declared_next_steps()` counts at least one indented bullet under the log's `- Next steps:` line (the COUNT only: several chains write to one pointer, so naming a step would send this seat to do another seat's slice), and the last nudge settled more than `RW_IDLE_NUDGE_S` (default 10800, 3 h) ago, stamped in its own `state["nudged"]` map only after a send that settled. A bullet that says there is nothing to do (`- None`, `NOTHING IS PENDING ...`) is not a step and is not counted: 12 of the 135 real chain logs wrote one, and counting it would nudge a finished seat every cooldown forever with no ack that could end it (for one chain it was the only bullet). Numbered (`1. `, `1) `), starred (`* `) and plus (`+ `) bullets count as steps alongside `- `: a live chain wrote its open work as a numbered list and was the one chain a dash-only count missed (the total is 128 of the 135 chain logs either way, but the SET differs: dash-only counted a finished chain and missed a working one). The line goes to the seat, not djbclark (the tool's standing `unverified` disclosure of an admitted loss still applies to any line), and #152's keyed drop retires a queued copy (`pending: dropping resolved next move`) with no new drop path. The Tier 1 redirect logic moved out of `chain_staleness()` into `resolve_pointer()` so the staleness check and the step count always read the same file, and an undecodable pointer now reads as "nothing" instead of raising. `RW_IDLE_NUDGE=0` turns the item off (#157).
- `acp-run` lost the git identity guard, and every counted `git -c`-style setting a caller passed, in any agent that filters its environment by variable NAME: the guard went in as `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` + `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_n`, and a launcher that drops names containing `KEY`/`SECRET`/`TOKEN` (codex's `[shell_environment_policy] inherit = "all"` default excludes) kept `COUNT` and the values but deleted every key, so `git` read a count it could not satisfy and the guard's `reference-transaction` hook never existed — measured 2026-10-30 with `codex exec`: `COUNT=2` and both `VALUE_n` present, zero `KEY_n`. It blocked the `~/ops` pair-rename slice, which `acp-dispatch` refused with exit 4 rather than run a rename with no identity guard.
