Counted config is now moved into `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` (Git's quoted transport, immune to name filtering — verified surviving the same codex probe and readable back through `git config --get`), inserted between the `COUNT` layer and the caller's own parameters so a caller's own quoted config still wins; the counted group is validated before it is converted (a `COUNT` with missing or non-contiguous `KEY_n`/`VALUE_n` is refused with exit 2, naming the incomplete config, instead of launching an agent with mangled config), a legacy `COUNT`-only guard pair from an older launch is recognised and converted rather than duplicated, and `bin/ade_claims.py` exempts the new transport's own two guard pairs from its hook-config refusal while still refusing any other `hook.*` key, `-c`, `--config-env` and extra `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` entries, and a counted key that embeds userinfo in a URL refuses the launch by INDEX only, never by value, because the name filter had been keeping that token out of an agent's tool environment and the quoted transport would hand it over — every URL-scoped family, not just `url.*`, because git resolves `http.<url>.*` and `credential.<url>.*` for the matching URL too (`git config --get-urlmatch http.sslVerify https://user:token@host/x` answers the setting configured under that userinfo-bearing subsection and nothing for another host), so the check is the shape anywhere in the key: every `://` must be followed by an authority without `@`, in any section or scheme case and past any run of slashes, username-only userinfo refuses too, since a username cannot be told apart from a token without decoding it; and a `credential.` subsection that is a partial url with no scheme - the one form git resolves as a credential, measured with `git credential fill` - refuses whenever its authority carries `@`, no colon required, because a PAT is used as a username and git url-decodes `%3A`, while a port or a plain partial url still travels. Codex's security review of that same head then showed a URL's QUERY is the same hole: `http.https://example.org/repo?access_token=TOK3N.sslVerify` was accepted with no `@` anywhere in the key, matched `git config --get-urlmatch http.sslVerify 'https://example.org/repo?access_token=TOK3N'` and not the same URL without the query, and a loopback `git ls-remote` to it sent the token in its request target, so a `?` or `#` after a `://` now refuses too, as does either character in a schemeless `credential.` subsection (the one exception, an `includeIf.hasconfig:` glob pattern with a `?` wildcard, cannot travel through a counted config); and because the name filter hid a token in ANY counted key name whether or not git resolves the key, a known token shape (`ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `glpat-`, `xox?-`, `sk-`, `hf_`, `npm_`, `pypi-`, `AIza`, `AKIA`/`ASIA`, an `eyJ` JWT, each with a length floor) is refused wherever it appears in a key - a subsection (`remote.<token>.url`), a URL path - while a path segment that is ordinary data (`@scope`) and a name that only resembles a prefix (`remote.sk-1.url`) still travel, an unknown shape in a path being the check's documented limit; the shape test then requires a whole word (no match starting mid-word), matches each prefix in the case its issuer uses, and floors each body just under the real token length, because the first version's `re.IGNORECASE` with no word boundary also refused ordinary names - `branch.task-scheduler-refactoring.remote` (`sk-` inside `task-`), `remote.sk-learn-upstream-fork.url`, `remote.ghp_mirror01.url` and `url.https://asiapacificmirror.example.org/.insteadOf` (`ASIA` inside `asia`) - and turned a plausible launch into exit 2; the `sk-` floor is then 20 rather than just under OpenAI's length, because LiteLLM issues a WORKING key for this machine's proxy as `sk-` + 22 url-safe characters (`token_urlsafe(16)`, its default), which a floor of 32 left travelling; and BOTH checks also run on every percent-DECODED spelling of the key (decoding repeats until the text stops changing, so a double encoding is covered, while invalid escapes and a `%` in ordinary path data stay as written), because the exposure is the name rather than the setting's meaning: `git config --get-urlmatch http.sslVerify <url>` answers a setting written under `http.https://host/ghp%5F<20+>.sslVerify` for BOTH `https://host/ghp_<20+>` and `https://host/ghp%5F<20+>` on 2.56.0, so git treats the spellings as one subsection, and an agent reading the converted key can decode it exactly as well (codex security review, 2026-10-30); a key that only spells a credential once decoded (`credential.user%40host.helper`, `url.https%3A%2F%2Fuser%3Atoken%40host/x.insteadOf`) now refuses too, which is the intended trade (#156).
- `bin/rumination-watch` replayed a queued finding as if it were live after the condition behind it had been fixed: `state["pending"]` held plain text, retried verbatim every pass until a send succeeded, so the `stale chain log: Tier 1 log records 067a9868b010, repo is at 58a1a7743130` line queued at 21:41 on 2026-10-29 was logged again at 21:47, 21:52 and 21:58 (the seat was mid-turn) although the pointer had been corrected at 21:47 and `rumination-watch status` said `stale: no`. A `pending` entry is now `{"key", "text", "queued_at"}`; only the lines `_pass` recomputes from scratch every time are keyed (`stale chain log`, `job(s) finished but not closed`), and at retry time a keyed entry whose key the pass no longer produces is dropped with one `pending: dropping resolved <key>` log line, while one still true is replaced by this pass's fresh sentence and keeps its first `queued_at`. Event lines (new mail, new jobs, the new-PR announcement) stay keyless, because they are diffs against `state["last"]` and cannot be recomputed, so they keep the at-least-once retry. `load_state()` accepts the old bare-string queue and the mapping form, ignores unreadable entries (counted in a `pending: ignoring N unreadable entries` log line, no longer coerced to text with `str()`), and the `spoke session=... pending=N` line says `(retry, oldest 6m)` when it replays a queued line (#152).
- `bin/rumination-watch`'s `chain_staleness()` compared the Tier 1 pointer's recorded `head_sha` to the repo's `HEAD` by EQUALITY, so it disagreed on every ordinary commit by the owner or a peer seat — on several days in late October 2026, 82-98% of passes announced "stale" for the same handful of perfectly-fine snapshots (895 of 1,431 passes, three shas accounting for 746 of them), making the watcher's one voice functionally dark. It now compares by ANCESTRY: the recorded sha being HEAD or an ancestor of it is silent (the normal case), a sha absent from the repo entirely is reported as "not in this repo", and a sha that is a descendant of HEAD (the repo rewound under the pointer) is reported as "repo is behind the pointer"; an unreadable pointer, a missing `head_sha`, a redirect loop, and a `git` that could not answer at all (a timeout, an OSError, any return code git does not define for these two commands) all stay silent, because a check that did not learn the answer must not state one (#151).
- A merge into this repo did not reach the launchd jobs that run its tracked files, and the rumination watcher paid for it: the plist executes `~/src/djbclark-ade/bin/rumination-watch` from the SHARED checkout, which sat five commits behind `master`, so the job ran pre-#131 code for hours AFTER the fix for "the entity never hears the watcher" was merged — `last pass` looked healthy the whole time (2026-10-29, issue #143). Each pass now brings ITS OWN checkout forward: fast-forward only, and only when that checkout is safe to move (no uncommitted tracked changes, no merge/rebase/cherry-pick/bisect in progress, not a detached HEAD, local commits reported rather than merged or reset, and `--no-overwrite-ignore` so a revision that starts tracking a path this checkout holds as an ignored local file is refused instead of silently overwritten — `git merge` rewrites ignored files by default, and the clean-tree check cannot see that collision because the file is ignored), logging `deploy updated <old> -> <new>` on success and the reason once per cooldown when it cannot; `rumination-watch status` now prints the sha actually running, not just when the last pass was, because "when" was the field that lied. `RW_NO_DEPLOY=1` turns it off (#148).
- `relay.py` deferred a message and then refused it at re-send whenever the text asked a question: `deliver()` runs the inner send under `SENDER_OVERRIDE` (so the message speaks as the stored label) but the deliverer's environment also carries the SENDER's `PASEO_AGENT_ID`, so `fleet` marked that sender's own Paseo row `self`; `_main` read it as this process's session and re-ran the queue-time reply rule on text whose footer already said `[reply through relay.py to <sender>: ack <id>]`, exiting 2 "this message asks for a reply or action, so it needs --expect-ack ID" (2026-10-29: a queued question to Dann 7 never arrived, record `20261029-200457-a2c6ff`), and a re-send that did pass would have appended a second, self-addressed footer and written a duplicate link. Two layers: a run under `SENDER_OVERRIDE` (a deliverer's or a notice's own send) resolves no `self` row at all, and the deliverer's re-send now says `--requeued [EXPECT_ID]` — internal, exclusive with `--expect-ack` — so a re-send is never re-gated, re-footered or re-reserved whatever identity it runs under, and writes no second link or expectation; the queueing run stores the shape it sent under (`shape`: expect, fyi, needs_reply) and a record queued before this carries none, so its re-send passes the bare flag. `--requeued` is internal: an ordinary send is refused (exit 2), because taken from a session it would send a question with no footer, no link and no expectation, which is what the reply gate exists to prevent; and the stored `shape.expect` is checked before it can become an argument (a record is a plain JSON file — `--requeued --pending` would have listed the queue, exited 0 and been marked delivered), so a malformed record is failed "the record's stored shape.expect is malformed, so it was not re-sent: requeue the message" and is never sent, at once rather than after it expires, since it is the head of its target's queue; a valid value is bound as `--requeued=VALUE`. `tests/test_relay_requeue.py` pins both layers (9 of its 10 cases fail on the old code, the tenth is the unchanged queue-time gate) (#147).
- `bin/pr-wait` reported `security-marker-unverified` on EVERY pull request whose Codex security review was clean (2026-10-29): Codex writes `has_any_findings: null` / `has_unresolved_findings: null` with `status: completed` when that review finds nothing of its kind (PR 132 and 134, same shape), and the waiter read an explicit null the same as a MISSING key, so `merge_ready` could never be true for the commonest PR on the repo and the gate had to be talked around by hand. A null is now a statement — "this review found nothing of that kind" — but only beside the independent evidence: no Codex finding comment of that kind and no open Codex thread of that kind (`unresolved_stated`); a missing key, or a value that is neither boolean nor null, still blocks, and a null beside an open thread of its kind still fails closed (#146).
- Fixed Chrome CPU alerts to match Task Manager PIDs and distinguish browser/GPU processes from identified busy tabs before suggesting a tab action (PR pending).
- `bin/rumination-watch` could not reach the entity AT ALL, and had not since it was installed: `to_session` resolved the target from `RW_SESSION`/`PASEO_AGENT_ID` only, and a launchd job has neither, so it returned False every pass — 870 consecutive `spoke session=False` lines in `~/.local/state/rumination-watch/rumination.log`, zero deliveries, its whole purpose void (2026-10-29). The target is now resolved at RUN TIME and checked before use: the env override, then the comm registry's `paseo` id for the role, then `peer_mail.py resolve --as <role>`, each verified against `fleet.py` so a stale row cannot send into a dead session, with the unresolved case LOGGED (`cannot speak to session: ...`) instead of swallowed, and the relay outcome logged as delivered / queued / skipped / FAILED (`spoke session=<outcome>`, not a bool that reads like a status). No live session at all now also pings djbclark, once per cooldown and even on a quiet pass: a seat that is GONE stops producing the changes this watcher speaks about, so without that line the watcher would sit silent while the entity was dark — the exact outage it exists to prevent. Two assumptions in the same file were also wrong and are fixed: `--idle` was removed because it BLOCKS relay.py's deferral (a busy target got a refusal and no queue record, so the line was dropped), and a real launchd job cannot defer at all (no sender identity), so a busy target is now logged as skipped rather than retried into a void. Passes are serialised with a non-blocking flock, because launchd does not prevent a 300 s pass from overlapping the next and a second pass would re-announce the same line (#131).
- `bin/rumination-watch` called every busy seat healthy: a refusal read as the single word `skipped`
  whether the seat was mid-turn or parked on a question card only djbclark can answer, so a seat
  waiting on a prompt and a seat wedged with nothing new for 15 minutes both logged the healthy word
  while the entity was dark. It now asks `relay.py --why <id> --json` and keeps the answer (2026-10-29):
  `working` is healthy and never paged, `card:question|permission|plan` and `silent` (a hung turn) are
  darkness, and after `RW_STUCK_PASSES` (default 3, ~15 min) passes in a row of one class it tells
  djbclark once per cooldown with what the obstacle is and how to clear it. A quiet pass probes too —
  a seat that stops working stops producing changes, so without that the passes go quiet one after
  another and an outage reads as "nothing new" all the way through — and an unreadable `--why` pages
  nobody rather than guessing. `spoke session=` now carries the word (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` lost a line whenever the seat refused it: `state["last"] = cur` had already
  consumed the change, so the announcement was gone for good — and mail tends to arrive exactly
  while the seat is busy, so this was the everyday collision, not an edge case. A refused line is
  now kept in `state["pending"]`, prepended to the next pass and retried, cleared only by a real
  send (`delivered` / `accepted` / `queued`, or an unrecognised rc 0 shape read as `sent`), and
  bounded by `RW_PENDING_MAX` (clamped to at least 1, newest lines kept, every eviction logged). The
  logged word is relay.py's own: `accepted` is no longer reported as `delivered`. Since 2026-10-29 it
  is read from relay.py's `--json` report (`--json` is passed on the send) rather than its prose —
  a reworded "do not resend" would have been retried forever and a reworded refusal never explained
  — with the status line kept only as the fallback for an older relay.py that prints no report, and
  the prose a peer's untrusted reply preview can carry is never consulted for the outcome. `_pass`
  says plainly that delivery is AT-LEAST-ONCE on purpose: a crash between the send and the state
  write re-sends a line, because journaling first would instead lose one, and a duplicate is visible
  in the log while a lost line is invisible (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` reported a `gh` page size as a count: `--limit 100` printed "100 open
  PR(s)" whether there were 100 or 200. At the cap it now says "at least N", and a FAILED `gh` read
  is "PR count unknown (gh unreadable)" rather than a confident 0 — the same class of unearned
  precision as calling `accepted` "delivered" (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` read both subprocess streams as one string, and `fleet.py` prints a warning
  on STDERR on its successful `list --json` path: a healthy read could parse as an empty fleet and
  page "Nothing is listening" — a false dead-seat page on a machine whose seat was running. JSON
  reads (`fleet.py`, `peer_mail.py resolve`, `gh`, `relay.py --why`) now use stdout alone, and an
  rc 0 body that is not a fleet is a broken READ, not an empty one. Three more honesty bugs from the
  same review round: relay.py's rc 1 "it may have reached the daemon" / "do not resend" shapes are
  `unverified` (settled, never retried, and djbclark is told the line may have been lost) instead of
  `FAILED` and resent every 5 minutes; `card:picker` and `card:unknown` are darkness like the other
  cards, so a seat parked on a picker is paged; and a FAILED, unverified or `skipped` pass no longer
  resets the darkness streak (only a proven send or `working` does), because a flaky route used to
  hide a parked seat. The knobs are clamped (`RW_PENDING_MAX=0` used to keep everything), and a
  hand-edited state file of the wrong shape no longer kills the pass (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` three round-two honesty bugs (2026-10-29). A failed page to djbclark used to
  start a six-hour cooldown anyway: the `told` stamp was written inside `should_tell_djbclark`,
  BEFORE `to_djbclark()` ran, so one failed `hermes send` — or an `inbox-target` that could not
  resolve — silenced that page for the whole cooldown with nothing retrying it, on the one channel
  whose job is saying the entity is dark. The stamp is now a separate `djb_page_sent` called only
  after a send that actually returned True, at all four page sites (no live session, a dark seat, an
  item that needs his decision, an unverified send), so a failed page is retried on the next
  5-minute pass. A healthy `idle` seat resets the darkness streak: `idle` is `deliverable: true` in
  relay.py and is what a quiet healthy pass sees every 5 minutes, and leaving it out let three card
  episodes spread over a day page him "unable to take text for 3 passes in a row" — false in both
  clauses. The hand-edit guards cover VALUES as well as shapes: a `told` timestamp written as a word
  or a `stuck` count written as a string killed the pass at `float()` / `int()`, and such an entry is
  now dropped while the rest of the state is kept. Two more glued-stream reads are split the same
  way the JSON ones were: `inbox-target` warns on stderr and still exits 0, so a warning glued onto
  the target paged a destination that does not exist, and a git notice on stderr became part of
  `git rev-parse HEAD` and reported the chain log as permanently stale (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` round three: the darkness rules, the send contract and the disclosure
  retry (2026-10-29). A `card:*` this table has not learned is darkness: membership-only made
  `card:slash_menu` (relay.py's `pane_look` gained it after `DARK_MEANING` was written) log as
  ordinary status, reset the streak and stay mute — the same bug as `card:picker`, in a different
  key — so the PREFIX is now the rule and `DARK_MEANING` is only the wording. The streak counts
  CONSECUTIVE darkness, not one tally per class: a seat alternating `card:question` and `silent`
  (a permission prompt whose turn times out, then re-prompts) counted to one under each key
  forever and was never paged, though it had been unable to take text the whole time; the class is
  kept beside the count only to word the message, and the page item is class-independent so a
  flipping class cannot re-arm the six-hour cooldown and page him every three passes. A refusal
  whose prose says "blocked on a card" and whose `--why` cannot name the kind is now
  `card:unknown`, not the healthy `skipped` it used to be filed as, and `why_blocked`'s `detail` is
  no longer logged: for a card it is relay.py's `paseo_card_detail`, the agent's own tool input,
  which relay.py documents as shown by `--why` only and "never filed on a record or logged" — a
  permission prompt can carry a command with a token in it. The send's argv is built by
  `send_argv` and pinned by a test (`--no-ack --json`, and the pinned argv is the one `to_session`
  passes): a source-wide `"--json"` match in relay.py also matched four of its own internal
  `paseo_run` calls, so renaming the SEND parser's flag — argparse exit 2 on every watcher send,
  logged FAILED and retried forever with nobody paged — would have left the contract test green.
  And an unverified send's ADMISSION that the line may be lost is now carried in
  `state["undisclosed"]` and retried until a send actually returns True, instead of being
  attempted once: the line is still dropped on purpose (relay.py says "do not resend"), but one
  failed `hermes send` at that moment used to consume the trigger and the double failure ended in
  silence (#134).
- `bin/rumination-watch` round four: a settled-but-unproven send is not health (2026-10-29).
  relay.py's `accepted`, `queued` and `sent` keep a pending line out of the retry list, but they say
  nothing about the SEAT: a hung Paseo seat that keeps accepting text cleared its own darkness count
  on every busy pass, and a seat that produces a line every pass never has a quiet pass, so the
  `silent` probe could never run — the streak never reached `SEAT_STUCK_PASSES` and nobody was
  paged. A pass that gets one of those words now asks `relay.py --why` before touching the streak,
  so the observation that counts is the seat's, not the route's; `delivered`, `working` and `idle`
  alone reset it, and a probe that cannot be read (`unknown`) leaves the streak where it was rather
  than guessing either way (#134).

- The quota guard's `critical` advice (2026-10-29) is scoped to the Claude Code subscription pool: "No new Claude work of any kind until the reset ... hand off, stop" read as a stop order by idle omp sessions, so it now says "No new Claude Code subscription work (Agent-tool sub-agents of a Claude Code session included) until the reset", states that it is not a stop order for codex, grok, agy, zai and opencode sessions, and that an omp session keeps working only while `aiuse --available --json` lists omp (omp is off the subscription's dollar pool but shares the Claude weekly rate window, which aiuse gates when every weekly window is exhausted); a Claude Code lead is sent to an omp successor on Paseo (`self-slash --finish --successor omp/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5`), told to start omp by hand on herdr and Orca (where `self-slash` refuses an omp successor), or to a codex or grok successor when omp is not listed (agy is not a Paseo provider), instead of another Claude Sonnet session; `skills/handoff` Step 9 keys the same exemption on the state file's `level` being `critical` and drops its old `critical` branches (a Fable lead to Opus, an Opus or Sonnet lead to plain `/new`) (#126).
- `bin/paseo-new-seat` (2026-10-29): a claude seat created with the shield on no longer prints "auto_accept: NOT verified - the shield may be grey" and exits 4 once its mode is verified. Claude never publishes an `auto_accept` feature (its yolo is the permission mode), so its empty feature list is now "not offered" rather than "unverified"; other providers with an empty list still report unverified (#122).
- `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py` read a zero-usage transcript record (the `<synthetic>` stub Claude Code writes for a session or weekly limit notice) as a context size of 0, which counted as a shrink and made the next prompt's whole context look like growth, so the nudge asked early or twice in a row; a zero total is now not a reading (2026-10-28) (#114).
- The Linux CI job runs on ubuntu-26.04 (uutils coreutils) again and `ubuntu-latest` is unpinned: the g-prefixed GNU tool shims are now `exec` wrapper scripts instead of symlinks (a uutils multicall binary refuses to run under another name), and `test_r11_f3_proc_args` varies the exec path's length through the directory, not the link name, and no longer passes an empty argv[0] to `posix_spawn` (Python 3.14 rejects it) (2026-10-28) (#105).
- `ade_gates.read_guard_state` read a quota guard state file of absurd nesting (under the 64 KiB cap) as a `RecursionError` crash in `route_agent plan` on Python 3.13 and older (3.14 parses the nest, and `acp-dispatch` already treated it as closed); it now reads it as unreadable, the same as bad JSON, and the allowance stays closed (2026-10-27) (#98).
- `relay.py` reads `ack ID.` (a sentence-final period) as the ack when whitespace or the end of the text follows it, so two real acks are no longer missed; `ID.more`, `ID.-x` and `ID..x` stay longer ids (#101).
- `relay.py --expect-ack` can be closed by relay itself for a sender with a fleet row but no readable transcript, but ONLY one reached by a route that proves delivery or keeps the text (a non-Claude Paseo agent, an ACP launch or an acp-dispatch slice); a native OMP, Hermes TUI, opencode, gemini or pi pane sender with no transcript can open one but it ALWAYS goes OVERDUE (the safe direction: a missing ack, never a false one) (2026-10-26): the record stores `tx: null`, `--sweep` reads no transcript for it, and the expectation is closed by relay itself when the target answers through relay.py with a text that begins `ack ID` to the session that opened it (identity from the fleet; only for a record with no transcript, and only for an answer whose delivery watch ran and whose route to the session that opened the record proved delivered or handed to a Paseo agent or an ACP launch or dispatch inbox that keeps the text: never a `--no-ack`, watched-and-unseen, pane "queued" or deliverer send; a sender with a transcript keeps closing by that transcript alone); a caller with no session (a launchd script) is still refused `--expect-ack`, now with a message saying so (#87).
- `relay.py`: a Hermes gateway conversation's `ack ID` sent through relay now closes a transcript-less sender's expectation: relay reads the Hermes caller from `HERMES_SESSION_ID` and the process tree (below the pid in the conversation's lock file or in `gateway.pid`), and a relayed Hermes turn no longer treats the asker's herdr pane as its own session; it is a same-user bound, not unforgeable: any same-user process that sets the variable and writes those files (or descends from a reused pid recorded there) can pass as that conversation, as `HERDR_PANE_ID` can for a pane; a recognised Hermes caller can no longer defer a send to a busy target (exit 1) (#87).
- Fixed dispatch footer stripping to accept blank separator lines between generated footer items, keeping any user-written line between them (2026-10-26) (#88).
- Fixed rerouting after a preflight refusal by retaining task and permission intent and supporting explicit legacy recovery (2026-10-26) (#88).
- Fixed `pr-wait` re-requesting a terminal code review while security review was still pending; security completion remains required (#86).
- Fixed the gate profile after adversary round 2 on #84: a deeply nested `gates.toml` reads as unreadable (code defaults) instead of crashing the governor and the guard; an unusable lock path (a link, FIFO or directory at `gate-profile.json.lock`) no longer blocks `gate-profile clear` (it names the path and clears without the lock) and no longer silences the notices without a log line (`set` and `extend` refuse naming it); `gate-profile extend` no longer carries `expired_notified` into the revived profile, so its real expiry pings; docs/gates.md no longer claims that keys without a `[floors]` entry are guarded (2026-10-27) (#91).

- Fixed `self-slash` to refuse Cline hub discovery for Codex sessions with missing host variables, preventing lifecycle commands from targeting another agent (#71).

- `session-finder` finds the TUI behind Kiro's nested PTY (2026-10-25): `fleet.pane_procs` answers a pane whose only foreground process is the `bash (kiro-cli-term)` shim with the foreground process group of its single inner shell's tty (`nested_pty.py`, one `ps` snapshot incl. `tpgid`; ambiguous, stale, tty-less or shell-only chains keep herdr's plain-shell answer), and `_tui_of` recognises `goose session` and `node .../mastracode` as `goose` and `mastra`. ([#78](https://github.com/djbclark/djbclark-ade/pull/78))

- `self-slash --finish` (and a mapped `/quit`) on Paseo now refuses with exit 12 when the Paseo agent holds a watch role in `bin/watch status` (same prefix match as `watch`), so a session that is the load, CFEngine or Paseo watcher is not archived by accident; `--successor` is not refused, the check fails open when `watch status` fails or exceeds `SELF_SLASH_WATCH_TIMEOUT` (20 s), and `SELF_SLASH_ALLOW_WATCHER=1` overrides. The baton and loose skills say how to pick the chain and not to offer finish for a watcher (2026-10-25, #72).
- Codex automatic tab naming reads Herdr’s `result.pane` response and keeps missing titles absent instead of turning JSON null into `none-t` (2026-10-25).

- Clarified dispatch waits: finish downloads before reading dependent artifacts, and use the documented load-watch precheck instead of starting the governor daemon (2026-10-25; direct integration).

- Fixed unsafe OpenCode RTK automatic rewriting by disabling both entrypoints, safely migrating the exact former managed copy, refusing unsafe canonical sources and flagging unknown V2 copies (#68).

- Fixed Codex `self-slash` lifecycle submission in Herdr, preserved shaded multiline composer drafts and blocked/working states, and detached waiters from the launching tool's process session (2026-10-25) (#69).

- Fixed `self-slash` sending Codex panes `/baton` instead of the `$baton` skill, and reporting it sent without an exact rollout receipt (2026-10-25) (#69).

- Fixed Herdr peer relays leaving long messages unsent in Codex: used atomic prompt submission and verified the exact new user message in its session rollout before reporting delivery (2026-10-25).
- Fixed Codex tools running inside Herdr losing the pane identity: with the launching-terminal inheritance of `inherit = "all"` (listed under Changed), `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl` now also keeps Codex's default `KEY`/`SECRET`/`TOKEN` name exclusions on (`ignore_default_excludes = false`); there is no `include_only` allowlist (operator ruling, 2026-10-25). `acp-run` also drops `HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID` and `HERDR_TAB_ID` from every agent it starts, as it already did the pane id. New command `bin/codex-env-policy-apply TEMPLATE TARGET [--write]` recognizes table headers with trailing TOML comments and merges only the policy tables into the live `~/.codex/config.toml`: no flag is a dry run that prints a policy-span-only diff with non-allowlisted assignment lines (including keys), operator comments and continuations hidden, `--write` applies it atomically (does nothing if already applied, refuses if the file changed meanwhile, keeps a private 0600 backup), exit 0 done or nothing to do, 1 refused or failed, 2 bad arguments (2026-10-25; #71).

- `acp-dispatch` no longer finalizes a live dispatch as "lost" while it waits in the load gate: `launchctl print gui/UID/<label>` cannot address a label that carries the absolute `--task` path (every `--task` dispatch), read as "not loaded" after 120 s, and the reap sweep, `check` and `wait` finalized the still-loaded job (2026-10-25: three slices); a label containing `/` is now looked up with `launchctl list <label>` (#62).
- `self-slash` on herdr (2026-10-25, regression from 1eda130): the herdr branch required `PASEO_SELF_TRUST_HERDR=1`, which nothing sets, so a plain herdr session fell through to `paseo-self`, which named another Cline agent from the hub's newest `tool.started` row; a herdr handoff would have sent `/clear` and `/baton` to that stranger. herdr is the host again whenever `herdr pane get $HERDR_PANE_ID` finds the pane (a Cline tool shell's inherited pane does not exist), and a Claude Code session (`CLAUDECODE=1`) is never discovered through the Cline hub.
- `relay.py` deferred send, PR 57 round-4 residuals (2026-10-25): a queued message to a TUI restarted onto the same session id (a `--resume`, a crash and relaunch) is no longer failed "target changed" for its new pid (the pid is checked only for a target with neither session id nor transcript; `/clear`'s new sid still fails it); a herdr pane row that carries fleet's own pid (zcode, muse) is judged by membership among the pane's foreground pids like every other herdr row, and one with several same-named processes is refused at queue time (exit 1) instead of storing a possible child's pid (defensive: fleet reports undetected rows as `unknown`, which are never deferred today); `deliverer_env` passes `RELAY_DEFER_DIR`, `RELAY_UNSENT` and `RELAY_HERMES_DIR` resolved, so the deliverer's `chdir("/")` cannot redirect a relative value (#60).
- `bin/ops-post-checkout-guard` now has a tracked source (it was three untracked copies in `~/ops/{site-djbclark,site-private,stayturgid}/.git/hooks/post-checkout`) and stays silent in a linked worktree: git hooks live in the common git dir, so `git worktree add -b X` ran the guard inside the NEW worktree and printed "deploy checkout is now on 'X', not master" although the deploy checkout never moved (three false alarms on 2026-10-25), and with a merged PR it could have tried to heal the wrong tree. Only the main working tree is guarded; `tests/test_ops_post_checkout_guard.py` covers the main checkout still warning, `worktree add` and a checkout inside a linked worktree (2026-10-25).

- Queued LOWs from the 2026-10-24 landing rounds (2026-10-24): `bin/skill-everywhere`'s usage text and `docs/skill-everywhere.md` now say that a report-only `ade-update` run skips the Grok Bot copy and what the symlink refusal means (the usage printer also shows the symlink line it stopped before); the no-origin shallow restore recipe in `bin/ade-scan-state-doc` is `git init --bare -b <branch> R` (git 2.28 or newer), so a clone of the restored repository no longer warns `remote HEAD refers to nonexistent ref`; new tests pin the `.git`-directory row's plain git-dir command, the borrowed-twin age rule of `bin/claude-quota-guard` for a reported `resets_at`, `weekly_resets_at` and `pace`, the in-tree directory-link refusal of `bin/skill-everywhere` and the dropped inherited `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_NO_COPY`; a stray blank line no longer makes the `### Changed` list loose (#56).
- `acp-run zcode --perm read-only` and `--perm deny` now remove the disallowed tools for real: zcode's backend ignored `--disallowed-tools`, so a read-only run was offered Bash, Edit and Write and ran `ls` unasked; `tools/acp-run/zcode-denylist-shim.cjs` adds `toolDenylist`/`toolAllowlist` to the backend's session requests, and the run fails with exit 1 when zcode's entry is not found (2026-10-24).
- `--model GLM-5.3` (and `GLM-5.3-Flash`) now resolves to the coding-plan id `builtin:zai-coding-plan\<name>` in `acp-run` and `acp-dispatch` instead of being rejected as unknown; full ids still work (2026-10-24).

- `bin/skill-everywhere`'s Grok Bot copy no longer dereferences a symlink that leaves the skill (an absolute link in a PR branch was `cp -rL`-copied into a temp dir by `ade-update --check`): a skill holding a link that is not to a regular file inside it is refused (`--check` row `unsafe source symlink`, stuck; link mode exit 1, nothing copied), and a non-canonical (report-only) `ade-update` run sets the new `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_NO_COPY=1` so the helper checks link targets only (2026-10-23) (#37).
- `acp-run` named `env` instead of `crush-acp` for the crush wrapper, so a missing `crush-acp` skipped `ACP_RUN_BIN_WAIT` and exit 127 (and `--list` showed it `ok`); `acp-dispatch` kept the not-installed probe result per model, so every model of a missing agent paid the 90 s wait again, and now keeps it per agent; the detached `waiter:` hint always said `timeout 7200000`, and now says when the wait is longer than that 2 h maximum (or unlimited) and to re-arm the same line (Codex 4322594869, 4323297404, 4324164145) (#38).
- Restored the executable bit on 12 tracked scripts with a shebang (d618809, 2026-10-23): `claude/hooks/context_size_nudge.py`
  had been 100644 since 836b306 (2026-10-19), so under `hook-deadline` it exited 127 and no context-size nudge ran for
  four days; `bin/claude-settings-env` had lost it on this branch. `tests/test_bin_modes.py` now fails on any tracked
  file with a shebang outside `tests/` that is not 100755 (#32).
- `bin/ade-scan-state-doc` follow-up 2b to #39 (2026-10-24): the git-dir command of a row whose scan saw a gitfile naming a git directory outside the checkout now fails (nonzero, `gitdir capture FAILED: .git is not a file` on stderr) when `.git` is no longer a file at paste time, where it exited 0 having archived nothing; the ordinary-checkout command is unchanged (#54).
- `relay.py` pane route: a relay that failed the pane lock and read an empty hash (the holder releasing, or slow to write it) skipped to the socket, which put the holder's already recorded text in a second time; it now asks for the lock up to 5 times and takes the pane route under it once it is free (queue L-D, 2026-10-24, #49).
- `bin/ade-scan-state-doc`: every bundle row (branch, detached HEAD, local-only, origin unreachable, tags) of a shallow checkout that has an origin now say the bundle cannot be cloned alone and restores through a bare clone of origin and a forced fetch into `refs/bundle/*` (2026-10-24, #47).
- `relay.py`: a send to a WORKING codex, omp, opencode or pi Paseo agent was refused unless `--steer` (a new flag; `--idle S` still waits for idle), because Paseo replaces the running turn when the provider declines a steer (a Codex review turn, an omp steer error) and relay cannot see that beforehand; claude is steered as before. A Paseo agent with no Claude registry row stopped being found by its workspace title (its id, Paseo id and hex prefix still are). `fleet.py` marked a running Paseo agent with a pending permission or question card `blocked` (one `paseo permit ls --json`), `relay.py` refused it naming the Paseo UI, and helm listed it as a `permission` item with every card and its own `paseo permit allow|deny <agent> -- <request id>` (or the Paseo UI) and pressed no keys (2026-10-23, queued lows, #40).
- `relay.py` pane route: a queue `dequeue` counted as delivery only when the next prompt the agent got (a user line or a `queued_command` attachment) is our text, otherwise ours stayed queued (this corrected "a `dequeue` ... counts as delivered" above); a second relay to a pane another relay was typing into skipped the pane route, so the socket could still deliver, instead of ending the run "not sent"; each unsent-text entry recorded its transcript file, and an entry whose pane had another transcript (/clear, a resume) was never pressed, retyped or proven (2026-10-23, queued lows, #40).
- `relay.py` hermes route: a thread Hermes refuses to reload (`This session is too long to reload safely`) was reported "not sent: that Hermes thread is too large to reload" instead of "unverified"; RELAY_HOPS and RELAY_SENDER were ignored by a relay with another agent between it and the relay that started the Hermes turn (`RELAY_HOP_PARENT`, the pid of that relay, marked the boundary); the refusal while a turn held the conversation's lock named its pid; lock files idle for 7 days were pruned; `--json` results of a timed-out or failed turn carried `reply_file` (2026-10-23, queued lows, #40).
- `relay.py`, `fleet.py`, `helm.py` (adversary repair of the queued lows, 2026-10-24): a relayed Hermes turn's relays counted their hops by walking up to `RELAY_HOP_PARENT` (set to the starting relay's pid), not by recognising a Hermes process by name, so a relay Hermes made inside a relayed turn was tagged `hermes ...` and counted (covers relays started through Hermes's terminal tool and background processes, which keep the `RELAY_*` variables; a relay started from Hermes's `execute_code` sandbox, which strips them, is NOT covered: open in `docs/queue.md`); `fleet.py` kept every pending Paseo card with its request id and helm printed `paseo permit allow|deny <agent> -- <request id>` per card (an id-less `allow` approves every card, `deny` without one fails) and re-opened a skipped item when a card is added; one `fleet.clean_text` stripped escape sequences, control, bidi and zero-width characters from relayed replies and card text; the pane lock file carried a hash of the text in flight, so a second relay of the same text stopped while the first was sending it (a different text still fell to the socket); a refused Hermes lock on an inode a prune just unlinked was retried instead of reported as a running turn; a moved unsent entry blocking another text said it could not be proven; a tool_result-only line did not end the wait for the prompt a `dequeue` handed over (2026-10-24, queued lows repair, #40).
- `relay.py`, `fleet.py`, `helm.py` (adversary round 2 of the queued lows, 2026-10-24): a Paseo request id had to start with a letter or digit (an id of `--all` or `-h` would have turned the printed command into "approve every card") and helm printed `paseo permit allow|deny <agent> -- <request id>`; `RELAY_SENDER` was accepted only as `hermes <up to 8 id characters>` (any other name set inside a turn was ignored and the normal sender was used); the dequeue scan skipped a user line that has a `tool_result` block and no text block (an AskUserQuestion answer with a pasted image too), so an image-only or document-only prompt counted as someone else's prompt (#40).
- `tests/test_acp_dispatch_quota_d2.py::TestExit0Quota::test_agy_exit0_quota_in_log_is_a_quota_stop` stopped failing from 2026-10-24 18:18Z onward (2026-10-24): its agy fixture hard-coded `quotaResetTimeStamp:2026-10-24T18:18:05Z` as "6 days out", so once the clock came within the auto-resume window the code correctly scheduled a resume and master CI went red on Linux and macOS; the stamp is now built from `now + 148h22m36s` and the aiuse `--resets-at` assertion uses the same value (#42).
- `bin/self-slash` (2026-10-24): a successor start with no `--mode` expanded an empty `"${mode_args[@]}"` under `set -u`, which is "unbound variable" in bash < 4.4 (macOS `/bin/bash` 3.2, the `env bash` of the GitHub macOS runner), so it failed there and `test_successor_to_a_non_claude_provider_from_another_provider_passes_no_mode` kept master CI red on macOS; it is now `${mode_args[@]+"${mode_args[@]}"}`.
- `firecrawl-router` developer search, follow-up to #41 (2026-10-24): with `types` set, public hits of a kind the caller did not ask for no longer count as "found", so the fewer-terms retry now runs (a `types=["doc"]` search whose first code page held only source files used to return nothing). `dev_found` replaces `dev_public_hits` and counts what `_shape` would show, of the requested kinds.
- `firecrawl_developer_search` no longer returns a 404 (2026-10-24, #41): with no commercial fallback configured, `bin/firecrawl-router` answers `GET|POST /v2/search/developer` and `POST /v2/search` with `categories: ["developer"]` itself from the GitHub search API (issues, merged pull requests, READMEs, docs, code files of public repositories, best effort until a fine-grained public-read token replaces the classic PAT behind `GITHUB_TOKEN`: `is:public` on issue and PR queries, private code hits dropped, code search always takes the 100-item page, residual in `docs/threat-model.md` item 10; round-robin ranking, `source: "github"`, per-kind `coverage`, rate-limit buckets closed from GitHub's headers, HTTP 429 with `Retry-After` when all are closed or two searches are already running, one 35 s deadline). Token via `sudo-secretspec get` (`FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_GITHUB_SECRET` names another), held in memory and read again at most once a minute while none is held; `FIRECRAWL_ROUTER_DEVELOPER=off` disables it. `skills/firecrawl/SKILL.md` drops the 404 note, `docs/queue.md` its "Resurface" item, `docs/mcp-servers.md` gains the firecrawl row and section, `docs/threat-model.md` item 10 the GitHub egress.
- `bin/ade-scan-state` follow-up to #27 (2026-10-24): a bigteam task's `CLAIM` or `jobs/*.json` that is a FIFO, a symlink or over 1 MiB was read bounded and non-blocking and is `scan incomplete` / `(unreadable)` instead of hanging the scan (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state` (2026-10-24): a shallow checkout with no origin got its `shallow` file copied beside `all.bundle` with the restore recipe, since a bundle alone cannot be cloned (record key `shallow`) (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state` (2026-10-24): a hard-linked gitfile no longer passed the git-dir back-pointer test; the back-pointer's directory is compared (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state-doc` (2026-10-24): `--separate-git-dir` is no longer described as having a back-pointer (it is skipped, as before) (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state` (2026-10-24): the replaced-tracked-file check lists `ls-files -m -d` only, with the gitlinks from a separate listing and `status`'s time bound (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state-doc` (2026-10-24): the FOREIGN row no longer said the gitfile's git dir is archived nowhere; its git-dir command archives it (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state` (2026-10-24): one directory under two spellings (case-insensitive APFS) is one embedded repository, not two (#39).
- `bin/ade-scan-state` (2026-10-24): `extensions.relativeworktrees` is allowlisted (#39).
- `self-slash` on a herdr pane running omp (2026-10-24): `--finish` sent to this session polled the screen 9 times for a `❯` that omp never draws and gave up with nothing sent, while the caller was told "queued". `prompt_line.py` now reads omp's input row (the box's last row, `╰─ `, minus its ` ─╯` border and the `⇧⇥ to change thinking effort` hint); `wait_idle` takes omp's busy state from the terminal title's braille spinner, because herdr has no omp detector and reports "idle" mid-turn; a herdr agent the script cannot read (not claude, cursor or omp) now draws a warning at queue time. Smoke-tested on a second omp pane: queued mid-turn, waited for the turn to end, sent `/quit`, omp exited.
- `bin/claude-quota-guard` (2026-10-24): it aged the decision by every snapshot row with a Fable window instead of the one whose window `_lowest` picked (a fresh 10% reading beside a 5 h old 90% row sent a false collector-down ping), and ignored a summary-only Fable (or default) row whenever another row had `windows[]`. Only the snapshot row that supplied a reported value (a shown window, or a twin that filled a shown reset or pace) now ages the decision; every windowless row with a headroom or `usable_now` is judged beside the detailed rows, a snapshot window that is neither judged nor shown (a display fill beside detailed rows, or a borrowed twin with no reset and no pace) no longer ages it, and a tie at the lowest Fable value goes to the fresher snapshot row (#36).
- `self-slash --finish --successor` after its first live run (2026-10-23): reads the agent id behind Paseo 0.13's
  `Using workspace <id>` line before the JSON (the successor started but this agent was not archived); copies the old
  agent's `--mode` only to the same provider (an omp `full` made `paseo run` refuse a claude successor), else
  `bypassPermissions` for claude and no `--mode` otherwise; new `--baton PATH` names the chain log for a lead whose chain
  lists the repo as a side workspace (a bare `/baton` resumed another session's chain).
- `self-slash --finish --successor`, adversary review adv-selfslash-1 (2026-10-23): `--baton PATH` is refused (exit 2) unless it is an absolute path of printable characters to an existing regular file (a newline in it forged a log line, a relative path resolved in the successor's cwd); an empty `--mode` on bash 3.2 (macOS `/bin/bash`) no longer aborts a cross-provider successor with exit 11; the dry run prints `--mode plan`, not `--mode planplan`; the JSON of `paseo run` and of both `paseo agent inspect` calls (this agent's, the successor's) is read from stdout alone, stderr (the `Using workspace` line, Node warnings) kept apart in one per-pane file that is removed after each call and shown only in a failure message, so a bracket in a warning on those calls no longer leaves the successor running and this agent unarchived, and an inspect of this agent that is not exactly one JSON object, or (without `--mode`) lacks a Provider or a Mode value (key missing, null or not a string) or gives one that is not a plain id, stops with exit 11 and nothing started instead of falling back to a default mode (a `plan` agent got a `bypassPermissions` successor); the same provider gets the old mode as it is, an empty one passes no `--mode`; stderr on `workspace ls` and the other calls is unchanged; the usage text and README say what `--baton` and the `--mode` default do (#35).
- `bin/ade-update` exits 10 naming a stuck link on the first apply instead of 4 with an empty error, and 4 (not 10) when the canonical checkout has no `skill-everywhere` helper at all (#20).
- `bin/ade-update` reads the Grok Bot copy-target rows of `skill-everywhere --check` (`not installed (no <dir>)`, `copied (<path>)`, `missing (<path>)`, `stale copy (<path>)`) as ok or fixable, not stuck, and drops an inherited `SKILL_EVERYWHERE_ONLY` from the helper's environment, so a check never reports on a subset of targets (#20).
- `bin/skill-everywhere`: the Hermes plugin nudge no longer aborts the script when the Hermes wrapper has no `exec`
  line; its `g*` tools reach plain GNU ones through master's alias loop (#20).
- `acp-dispatch` refuses `--perm read-only` or `--perm deny` (exit 2, before any record or preflight) for an agent
  whose `acp-run <agent> --info` `perm_modes` lacks that mode; before, acp-run's own refusal came after the
  record was written and surfaced as a no-report (an omp adversary round, 2026-10-23).
- Paseo's start-up skill sync no longer overwrites the six vendored `paseo*` skills in this repo through the
  `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.agents/skills` links: the daemon's skill selection is now `custom` with no skills;
  how it works and what to avoid in Paseo's settings are in docs/skill-everywhere.md section 3a (2026-10-23).
- `acp-run` classifies zcode's `token_login_failed` error code (inside its "Internal error" envelope) as `auth`, never retried, instead of `transient` (#26).
- `diskfree` skill: the idle check for deleting a cache runs in the same command as the delete; a
  `target-shared` delete approved minutes earlier broke a live maturin build (2026-10-20).
- `load-watch` job guard: a slow crash loop (60 failed restarts in 2 h) now trips too; litellm restarted
  every ~32 s for 19 h, just under the 20-in-10-min rule, and was never stopped (2026-10-20).
- `load-watch`: the registered watcher hears an unexplained sustained load at most hourly; Hermes keeps
  its 6 h cooldown (2026-10-20).
- `skill-everywhere`: the Hermes gateway nudge follows `hermes` wrappers to the real python; since `hermes`
  became a `/bin/sh` wrapper it ran the snippet under sh, where `import os, sys` was ImageMagick (2026-10-20).
- `acp-dispatch`: re-dispatching a footed brief cuts a footer only when each of its items is a line of the
  footer template and its heading is the template's own, so a constraint a lead added to or edited in a
  pasted footer or its heading is kept, in linear time; a `-f` file that is
  only a footer is refused ("no task is left"); the preflight probe and the slice's waiter deadline allow for
  acp-run's binary wait plus its settle polls (`bin_poll_seconds`); a probe that finds an agent not installed
  is remembered for 120 s, and an `ok` entry dated in the future is not trusted; `check` gives an `auth` stop the login hint, not `--escalate` (#22).
- `tests/conftest.py` pins `ROUTE_AGENT_OMP_READY=0`, so the route_agent suite passes on a host with
  an omp login as it does on CI; the omp-ready roster has its own tests (queue item 6) (#17).
- `acp-dispatch`: an unmeasured (stale) quota window is skipped instead of crashing the dispatch
  with a `float(None)` error (#13).
- `self-slash` (herdr): a slash command left on the input line after the first Enter is Entered again, up to 3 times,
  for every TUI and not only cursor-agent; if it never leaves the line the waiter exits 8 and logs "NOT confirmed
- `firecrawl-router`: tokenless serving answers only a `Host` header that names the bound
  loopback server (DNS-rebinding allow-list), and the bearer compare is constant-time
  (`hmac.compare_digest`) (2026-10-22).

  run" instead of "sent" (2026-10-20).

### Security

- Added a secret-file read-deny policy, `config/secret-deny.toml`, rendered by `bin/render-secret-deny` into the Claude
  template's `permissions.deny`, plus the PreToolUse gate `claude/hooks/secret_read_gate.py` for file tools, Grep and Bash
  command text; a Grep whose root holds a denied file (a project `.env`, anything at or above `$HOME`) is refused until
  a narrower `path`, `glob` or `type` excludes it (#289)
- `ade-browser-task` refuses PEM / secret flags on argv and refuses
  `widens_access` templates / known widen task ids (S7 never-through-bot) (#454).
- `ade-claim` resolves `ade_claims.py` only from `ADE_CLAIMS_PY` or the plugin install tree, never from the new worktree's checkout (#336).
- `ade-github-setup` refuse messages for equals-form secret flags
  (`--token=<value>`) report only the normalized flag name, never the value
  (#412).
- Required `sandbox.allowUnsandboxedCommands: false` and `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true` in the
  Claude denyRead trial plan so a `bypassPermissions` unsandboxed retry cannot bypass `denyRead`
  via `dangerouslyDisableSandbox`. (#451)
- Wired Codex `secret_read_gate` (`PreToolUse`) and `post-edit-check --hook` (`PostToolUse`) into
  `agents/codex/config.toml.tmpl`; docs and queue updated (OpenCode skipped; live `~/.codex` apply still needs
  operator yes). (#390)
- `bin/ade-release-ready`: run `changelog-fold` and `ade-roster` from the trusted script directory, never from `--repo` (#445).
- `bin/ade-roster` `tracked()`: disable `core.fsmonitor` / `core.fsmonitorHook` when inspecting `--root`, so an untrusted tree's hook cannot run via `git ls-files` (#445).
- `_FORMAT_PATCH_VALUE_LONG` includes git 2.56 required-value longs `--commit-list-format` and `--range-diff-notes`, so a following `-o .` is not treated as an in-claim output directory while git still writes into `format.outputDirectory` (#496).
- `ade-git-guard` `hooks-path` refuses a local/worktree `core.hooksPath` of `%(prefix)/…` (and any `%(…)` interpolation) instead of joining the literal onto cwd, matching Git 2.43's expansion rather than allowing planted stubs under `<cwd>/%(prefix)/…` (#497).
- Relative `core.hooksPath` while `--work-tree` / `GIT_WORK_TREE` is set is refused as ambiguous: Git joins relative hooks to cwd for `update-ref` but to the work tree for `commit` / `checkout` / `merge` (#497).
- `filter-branch` joins `_HOOK_GUARDED_SUBS`, so a non-ade local `hooksPath` (e.g. `/dev/null`) can no longer rewrite claimed branch tips past the claim hooks (#497).
- `ade-git-guard` refuses `push`, `fetch`/`fetch-pack`/`remote update`, and ref-writing subcommands when the repository's local/worktree `core.hooksPath` is set to `/dev/null` or any path that is not the ade-git-guard stubs directory (rule `hooks-path`, exit 77; §10 residual). An ade-stub `hooksPath` stays allowed only when the directory holds the canonical installed stubs (they must invoke `HOOKS_SRC`; a forged marker+exit0 script is refused); ordinary non-ref-writing calls with a non-ade local `hooksPath` stay allowed (#432).
- `ade-git-guard` refuses `stash show --output*` / `--out*` and `format-patch` `-o` / `--output*` / `--output-directory` (and plain `format-patch`, whose default is `format.outputDirectory` when set via `-c` / `GIT_CONFIG_*` / repo config, else cwd) when the output target is not inside the caller's active claimed workspace (O2 residual; rule `output-file`, exit 77). Unresolved `format.outputDirectory` fails closed. `format-patch --stdout` stays allowed. (#400)
- `output-file` validates every `--output` / `-o` destination independently (duplicate flags and `--output` + `-o` combinations), so an earlier foreign path cannot hide behind a later in-claim one (#400).
- `format-patch` short clusters honor value-taking shorts (`-v`/`-S`/`-I`/`-G`/`-O`): `-vo.` is reroll-count `o.`, not `-o .`, so `format.outputDirectory` is still checked (#400).
- `format-patch` value-taking long options (`--reroll-count`, `--suffix`, `--subject-prefix`, `--to`, `--cc`, `--add-header`, `--signature`, `--in-reply-to`, `--description-file`, `--range-diff`, and kin) consume the next argv token when not `--opt=value`, so `--reroll-count -o .` cannot fake an in-claim `-o` while git writes into `format.outputDirectory` (#400).
- The `format.outputDirectory` probe clears legacy `GIT_CONFIG` (format-patch ignores it; `git config` does not), so `GIT_CONFIG=/dev/null` cannot hide a repo/global value (#400).
- Relative `stash show --output` / `--out` paths resolve against the effective work tree (`--work-tree` / `GIT_WORK_TREE` / `core.worktree`), not only the call cwd (#400).
- `ade-harden` enum-validates `interaction_limits.expiry` at load and renders
  `just harden` remediation hints with `shlex.join`, so an attacker-controlled
  `--root` tree cannot inject shell metacharacters into displayed commands
  (#452).
- `normalize_export.py` writes event JSONL to an opaque file by default (`--output` or `<input-stem>.events.jsonl`) and prints only a metadata summary (counts/paths/role tallies) on stdout, so agent tool transcripts no longer receive raw export message text (#439)
- `secret_read_gate` no longer fails open when crafted slow Bash globs exhaust the analysis budget: a shared first-pass
  cap and a cached set-aside retry keep later words reachable; deferred words get a pattern-only check then a real
  walk (or fail closed) so walk-dependent secrets (`id_*`, `*`) are never allowed on pattern-only alone after
  set-aside, a deferred walk truncated by `GLOB_S` / the entry bound fails closed, a first-pass relative/cwd or
  absolute/tilde walk truncated the same way fails closed (absolute deep globs keep the pattern fallback only when
  that shared budget did not truncate), and a second timeout fails closed (issue 284 / queue Carried #1 residual)
  (#413).
- `secret_read_gate` keeps `_glob_budget_hit` sticky across brace alternatives (so `{,id_*}` / `{,*}` cannot clear a
  prior truncate) and latches the same fail-closed path when `MAX_GLOB_HITS` truncates a walk (#413).
- Held generic ADE install, forced repair, system install, update and retry commands for enrolled forks; unavailable fork policy failed closed. (#782)
- Restricted graft hook imports to install roots by default; required `ADE_GRAFT_ALLOW_PROJECT_HOOKS=1` to load project packages or bare `dist/claude` helpers for Claude, Cursor, and pre-bash. (#827)
- Rejected canonical project targets from every hook discovery source, including PATH, remembered records, node libraries, global packages, and symlinked modules; rejected relative `TRAIL_HOME` without explicit project opt-in. (#827)
- Compared ancestor device/inode identities to block case-aliased project hook targets, refused unresolved ancestry, and documented pathname-containment limits. (#827)
- Closed the `--acp-arg` identity bypass for the guarded agents `pi`, `omp`, `codex` and `claude`: `acp-dispatch` refuses `--model`, `--meta`, `--agent-arg`, `--agent-env`, `--auth`, their `=` forms, abbreviations and packed tokens (and `--set` unless its key is a reasoning-effort option) before any gate or probe, and the runner refuses a stored command or `acp_arg` carrying one or a `--model` other than the admitted one, for normal, deferred, resumed and legacy records; the frontier and fable-xhigh gates and the codex dry run can no longer be bypassed by a replacement model. One policy table, `bin/agent_guard.py`, serves both; every other agent is an explicit `unreviewed` row (`docs/agent-guard-contract.md`) (#275).
- `acp-run omp` requires the `anthropic/` provider prefix (`github-copilot/claude-...` no longer passes), no longer exempts `--resume`, and refuses model, provider, profile, role-model, key and config flags in `--agent-arg` (`ACP_RUN_OMP_ANY_MODEL=1` stays the operator override) (#275).
- Blocked non-public targets and redirects, pinned direct connection addresses, and marked JSON web output as untrusted data. (#582)
- Added the S5a advisory Jev injection gate with fail-closed escalation and a payload-free measurement harness; reader adoption remained gated on measurement and review. (#576)
- `relay.py`: any Paseo agent could close another peer's `--expect-ack` or first-contact link on a transcript-less Paseo sender (a forged ack), because `--sweep` took the peer's identity from rendered `paseo logs` text: a `[relay from … PEER]` header, a `[Message received] PEER` line inside a multi-line message body, or an exported `PASEO_AGENT_ID` all passed for the peer. The Paseo timeline is now a delivery witness only and never closes an ack (`timeline_ack`, `timeline_base` and the records' `tl_base` are gone). Such a sender's expectation and first-contact link close only when the target answers through relay.py, identified by the fleet and delivered or kept by the route (`ack ID` closes the expectation, `ack <sender id>` closes the first-contact link, which also fixes those links staying one-way for a non-Paseo peer, including an answer that arrives before the asker's own run has written the link), including an answer relay queued for a busy Paseo recipient, resolved at delivery from the caller recorded when it was queued. A same-user process can still forge the fleet's identity (an exported `PASEO_AGENT_ID` on a lone `self` row, `docs/queue.md` item 9(b); an exported `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` naming a peer's session, for a caller whose own Claude has no registry entry, adversary PR 173 C2); a direct send from an agent nested under a Paseo agent still goes out under the parent's title (`docs/queue.md` item 9(d)). Also fixed: a transcript-holding outer session no longer outranks the nested session that actually runs relay as the sender (the reply, its footer and the `--retire` self-check named the parent), and a queued send from a nested agent no longer takes its parent's inherited `PASEO_AGENT_ID` as sender, failure-notice destination and Paseo attribution (#173).
- `firecrawl-router`: the serve-only form refuses a non-loopback `--listen` like `--open` does,
  and the address actually bound is checked before the first request is served (#14).
- `cursor-cloud` and `firecrawl-router`: endpoint variables must be https or loopback, bearer
  keys never follow a redirect or a client-chosen request target, and `cursor-cloud` runs under
  `python3 -I` with proxy, CA and `PYTHON*` variables scrubbed from the key-holding process (#15).
